## Dedication / 献词

===Original===
To Borys & Christina

===Chinese===
献给鲍里斯与克里斯蒂娜

## Epigraph / 题词

===Original===
> “Sardanapalus builded seven cities in a day. 

 Let us eat, drink and sleep, for tomorrow we die.”

===Chinese===
> “萨达纳帕勒斯（Sardanapalus）一日之间筑起七座城。

 让我们吃喝安睡吧，因为明天我们就要死去。”

## I / 第一章

===Original===
“Ideas,” she said. “Oh, as for ideas﻿—”
“Well?” I hazarded, “as for ideas﻿—?”

===Chinese===
“观念嘛，”她说，“哦，至于观念——”
“哦？”我试探道，“至于观念——？”

===Original===
We went through the old gateway and I cast a glance over my shoulder. The noon sun was shining over the masonry, over the little saints’ effigies, over the little fretted canopies, the grime and the white streaks of bird-dropping.

===Chinese===
我们穿过古老的大门洞，我回头瞥了一眼。正午的太阳照在砖石上，照在一尊尊小小的圣徒像上，照在一方方小小的镂刻华盖上，照在尘垢和鸟粪留下的白色条痕上。

===Original===
“There,” I said, pointing toward it, “doesn’t that suggest something to you?”
She made a motion with her head﻿—half negative, half contemptuous.
“But,” I stuttered, “the associations﻿—the ideas﻿—the historical ideas﻿—”
She said nothing.

===Chinese===
“瞧那儿，”我朝那边指着说，“这难道没让你联想到什么吗？”
她的头微微一动——半是否定，半是轻蔑。
“可是，”我结结巴巴地说，“这里的联想——观念——历史观念——”
她什么也没说。

===Original===
“You Americans,” I began, but her smile stopped me. It was as if she were amused at the utterances of an old lady shocked by the habits of the daughters of the day. It was the smile of a person who is confident of superseding one fatally.

===Chinese===
“你们这些美国人，”我刚起了个头，她的微笑就让我住了口。那神情，仿佛一位老太太对时下女儿们的做派大惊小怪，而她听着直觉得好笑。那是一种人的微笑——此人确信自己终将取人而代之，且置人于死地。

===Original===
In conversations of any length one of the parties assumes the superiority﻿—superiority of rank, intellectual or social. In this conversation she, if she did not attain to tacitly acknowledged temperamental superiority, seemed at least to claim it, to have no doubt as to its ultimate according. I was unused to this. I was a talker, proud of my conversational powers.

===Chinese===
但凡一场稍长的谈话，双方总有一方要占上风——地位上的、智识上的或社交上的上风。在眼前这场谈话里，她即便还没能占到那种心照不宣的性情上的上风，至少也俨然以此自居，并且毫不怀疑这上风终将归于她。这我可不习惯。我是个健谈的人，向来为自己的谈锋自负。

===Original===
I had looked at her before; now I cast a sideways, critical glance at her. I came out of my moodiness to wonder what type this was. She had good hair, good eyes, and some charm. Yes. And something besides﻿—a something﻿—a something that was not an attribute of her beauty. The modelling of her face was so perfect and so delicate as to produce an effect of transparency, yet there was no suggestion of frailness; her glance had an extraordinary strength of life. Her hair was fair and gleaming, her cheeks coloured as if a warm light had fallen on them from somewhere. She was familiar till it occurred to you that she was strange.

===Chinese===
此前我已打量过她；此刻我又侧过眼去，挑剔地看了她一眼。我从郁郁的心绪里抽出身来，暗自诧异这是怎样一类人。她头发好看，眼睛好看，也自有几分动人之处。是的。此外还有一点别的——某种东西——某种并不附属于她美貌的东西。她脸庞的造型完美而纤巧，纤巧到近乎透明，却又丝毫不显纤弱；她的目光里有一种非凡的生命力。她的头发浅色而富有光泽，双颊像被不知何处投来的一片暖光照亮。你起初只觉得她面熟，直到忽然醒悟：她其实是陌生的。

===Original===
“Which way are you going?” she asked.
“I am going to walk to Dover,” I answered.
“And I may come with you?”
I looked at her﻿—intent on divining her in that one glance. It was of course impossible. “There will be time for analysis,” I thought.

===Chinese===
“你往哪边走？”她问。
“我打算步行去多佛（Dover）。”我答道。
“那我可以跟你同路吗？”
我看着她——想凭这一眼就把她看透。这当然不可能。“来日方长，有的是工夫分析。”我想。

===Original===
“The roads are free to all,” I said. “You are not an American?”
She shook her head. No. She was not an Australian either, she came from none of the British colonies.

===Chinese===
“条条道路向众人敞开，”我说。“你不是美国人吧？”
她摇了摇头。不是。她也不是澳大利亚人，英属殖民地里也没有哪一处是她来的地方。

===Original===
“You are not English,” I affirmed. “You speak too well.” I was piqued. She did not answer. She smiled again and I grew angry. In the cathedral she had smiled at the verger’s commendation of particularly abominable restorations, and that smile had drawn me toward her, had emboldened me to offer deferential and condemnatory remarks as to the plaster-of-Paris mouldings. You know how one addresses a young lady who is obviously capable of taking care of herself. That was how I had come across her. She had smiled at the gabble of the cathedral guide as he showed the obsessed troop, of which we had formed units, the place of martyrdom of Blessed Thomas, and her smile had had just that quality of superseder’s contempt. It had pleased me then; but, now that she smiled thus past me﻿—it was not quite at me﻿—in the crooked highways of the town, I was irritated. After all, I was somebody; I was not a cathedral verger. I had a fancy for myself in those days﻿—a fancy that solitude and brooding had crystallised into a habit of mind. I was a writer with high﻿—with the highest﻿—ideals. I had withdrawn myself from the world, lived isolated, hidden in the countryside, lived as hermits do, on the hope of one day doing something﻿—of putting greatness on paper. She suddenly fathomed my thoughts: “You write,” she affirmed. I asked how she knew, wondered what she had read of mine﻿—there was so little.

===Chinese===
“你不是英国人，”我断言道，“你的英文说得太好了。”我有点着恼。她不回答，又是一笑，我便生了气。在大教堂里，司事对几处特别不堪入目的修复工程大加赞颂，她当时冲那话微微一笑，正是那一笑把我引到她跟前，壮了我的胆，叫我就那些熟石膏线脚发了一通既谦恭又痛加贬斥的议论。你知道，对一位明摆着自己照管得了自己的年轻姑娘，人是怎么开口搭话的。我就是这样遇上她的。教堂导游领着一队听得入魔的游客——我和她也在队里各占一席——去看真福托马斯（Blessed Thomas）的殉难之地，一路上絮絮叨叨，她冲那絮叨微笑，那微笑里恰是那么一种取而代之者的轻蔑。那时我觉得有趣；可是此刻，在城里弯弯曲曲的大街上，她这样从我们身边笑过去——并不完全是冲我笑——我就恼了。毕竟我不是无名之辈；我可不是教堂司事。那些日子里我对自己颇有几分自许——孤寂与苦思又把这自许结晶成了一种心性上的积习。我是个有着高远的——有着至高——理想的作家。我退出了尘世，离群索居，遁迹乡野，像隐士那样活着，全指望有朝一日做成一件什么——把伟大落到纸上。

===Original===
“Are you a popular author?” she asked.
“Alas, no!” I answered. “You must know that.”
“You would like to be?”

===Chinese===
“你是位走红的作家吗？”她问。
“唉，不是！”我答道，“这你是知道的。”
“你希望走红？”

===Original===
“We should all of us like,” I answered; “though it is true some of us protest that we aim for higher things.”
“I see,” she said, musingly. As far as I could tell she was coming to some decision. With an instinctive dislike to any such proceeding as regarded myself, I tried to cut across her unknown thoughts.
“But, really﻿—” I said, “I am quite a commonplace topic. Let us talk about yourself. Where do you come from?”

===Chinese===
“我们谁不想呢，”我答道，“尽管的确有人一口咬定，自己瞄准的是更高尚的东西。”
“我明白了。”她若有所思地说。据我判断，她正在打定某个主意。凡有人在我身上动这类心思，我一向本能地反感，便想截断她那些未知的念头。
“不过，说真的，”我说，“我这人平淡无奇，没什么可谈的。谈谈你自己吧。你究竟是从哪儿来的？”

===Original===
It occurred to me again that I was intensely unacquainted with her type. Here was the same smile﻿—as far as I could see, exactly the same smile. There are fine shades in smiles as in laughs, as in tones of voice. I seemed unable to hold my tongue.
“Where do you come from?” I asked. “You must belong to one of the new nations. You are a foreigner, I’ll swear, because you have such a fine contempt for us. You irritate me so that you might almost be a Prussian. But it is obvious that you are of a new nation that is beginning to find itself.”

===Chinese===
我又一次意识到，对于她这一类型，我实在全然陌生。眼前还是那种微笑——据我看，分毫不差还是那种微笑。微笑之中自有微妙的色阶，一如笑声，一如说话的声气。可我偏偏管不住自己的舌头。
“你到底从哪儿来？”我问，“你必定属于某个新兴的民族。你是外国人，我敢起誓，因为你对我们怀着这么一种了不得的蔑视。你惹得我冒火，简直像个普鲁士人了。不过显而易见，你属于一个正开始认识自身的崭新民族。”

===Original===
“Oh, we are to inherit the earth, if that is what you mean,” she said.
“The phrase is comprehensive,” I said. I was determined not to give myself away. “Where in the world do you come from?” I repeated. The question, I was quite conscious, would have sufficed, but in the hope, I suppose, of establishing my intellectual superiority, I continued:

===Chinese===
“哦，我们注定要承受大地，如果你指的是这个的话。”她说。
“这说法可真包罗万象。”我说，打定主意不露自己的底。“你究竟是从世界上哪个角落来的？”我又问了一遍。我心里很清楚，这一问本已尽够，但多半是为了确立我智识上的优势，我又接着说：

===Original===
“You know, fair play’s a jewel. Now I’m quite willing to give you information as to myself. I have already told you the essentials﻿—you ought to tell me something. It would only be fair play.”
“Why should there be any fair play?” she asked.
“What have you to say against that?” I said. “Do you not number it among your national characteristics?”
“You really wish to know where I come from?”
I expressed lighthearted acquiescence.

===Chinese===
“要知道，公道贵如珍宝。我十分乐意把自己的一应情况奉告。要紧的我都已经告诉你了——你也该告诉我点什么才是。这不过是公道。”
“为什么要有公道？”她问。
“你对此有什么可指摘的？”我说，“你们不是也把公道算在民族特性之列吗？”
“你当真想知道我从哪儿来？”
我轻快地表示了同意。

===Original===
“Listen,” she said, and uttered some sounds. I felt a kind of unholy emotion. It had come like a sudden, suddenly hushed, intense gust of wind through a breathless day. “What﻿—what!” I cried.
“I said I inhabit the Fourth Dimension.”

===Chinese===
“听着。”她说，随即发了几个音。我感到一种近乎邪祟的情绪。那几个音来了，像凝滞无风的一天里，一阵陡然而至、又骤然噤声的烈风。“什么——什么！”我叫出声来。
“我说的是：我住在第四维。”

===Original===
I recovered my equanimity with the thought that I had been visited by some stroke of an obscure and unimportant physical kind.
“I think we must have been climbing the hill too fast for me,” I said, “I have not been very well. I missed what you said.” I was certainly out of breath.
“I said I inhabit the Fourth Dimension,” she repeated with admirable gravity.
“Oh, come,” I expostulated, “this is playing it rather low down. You walk a convalescent out of breath and then propound riddles to him.”

===Chinese===
我心想，这不过是某种无足轻重的小毛病突然发作罢了，如此一想，心境便平静下来。
“我想，方才这山坡，我们准是爬得太急了，”我说，“我近来身子不大好。你刚才的话我没听清。”我确实喘着气。
“我说的是：我住在第四维。”她又说了一遍，那种庄重令人叹服。
“哎，得了吧，”我劝道，“这手玩得可有点下作。把个养病的人走得直喘，然后又给他出谜语。”

===Original===
I was recovering my breath, and, with it, my inclination to expand. Instead, I looked at her. I was beginning to understand. It was obvious enough that she was a foreigner in a strange land, in a land that brought out her national characteristics. She must be of some race, perhaps Semitic, perhaps Sclav﻿—of some incomprehensible race. I had never seen a Circassian, and there used to be a tradition that Circassian women were beautiful, were fair-skinned, and so on. What was repelling in her was accounted for by this difference in national point of view. One is, after all, not so very remote from the horse. What one does not understand one shies at﻿—finds sinister, in fact. And she struck me as sinister.

===Chinese===
我的气息缓过来了，随之回来的，还有我打开话匣子的兴致。但我没有开口，只看着她。我开始明白了。事情够明显的了：她是个身处异邦的外国人，而这异邦把她的民族特性全都引了出来。她必定属于某个种族，兴许是闪米特种，兴许是斯拉夫种——某种无从索解的种族。我从没见过切尔克斯人（Circassian），不过旧相传，切尔克斯女人美貌，皮肤白皙，等等。她身上那股拒人之处，全可以用民族眼光的差异来解释。人呢，说到底离马也不算太远。凡不理解的东西，人见了就要惊跳着躲开——实际上就是觉得邪门。而她给我的印象，正是邪门。

===Original===
“You won’t tell me who you are?” I said.
“I have done so,” she answered.
“If you expect me to believe that you inhabit a mathematical monstrosity, you are mistaken. You are, really.”
She turned round and pointed at the city.
“Look!” she said.

===Chinese===
“你不肯告诉我你是谁？”我说。
“我已经告诉过了。”她答道。
“你要是指望我相信你住在一个数学上的怪物里，那你就想错了。真的想错了。”
她转过身去，朝那座城一指。
“看！”她说。

===Original===
We had climbed the western hill. Below our feet, beneath a sky that the wind had swept clean of clouds, was the valley; a broad bowl, shallow, filled with the purple of smoke-wreaths. And above the mass of red roofs there soared the golden stonework of the cathedral tower. It was a vision, the last word of a great art. I looked at her. I was moved, and I knew that the glory of it must have moved her.

===Chinese===
我们已经登上西面的山冈。脚底下，在一片被风扫拭得澄澈无云的天空下，是那道山谷；一只宽阔的浅盆，盛满烟霭的紫色。而在红瓦累累之上，大教堂钟塔的金色石身高高拔起。那是一场幻景，一门伟大艺术的极致。我看看她。我被感动了，我知道这份辉煌必定也感动了她。

===Original===
She was smiling. “Look!” she repeated. I looked.
There was the purple and the red, and the golden tower, the vision, the last word. She said something﻿—uttered some sound.

===Chinese===
她正微笑着。“看！”她又说。我看了。
还是那片紫、那片红，还有金色的塔，那幻景，那极致。她说了句什么——发了某个音。

===Original===
What had happened? I don’t know. It all looked contemptible. One seemed to see something beyond, something vaster﻿—vaster than cathedrals, vaster than the conception of the gods to whom cathedrals were raised. The tower reeled out of the perpendicular. One saw beyond it, not roofs, or smoke, or hills, but an unrealised, an unrealisable infinity of space.

===Chinese===
出了什么事？我不知道。那一切忽然显得可鄙起来。人仿佛望见了某个彼岸，某个更其浩大的东西——比大教堂更浩大，比为大教堂所供奉的诸神的观念更浩大。塔身晃出了铅垂线。人在塔之外望见的，不是屋顶，不是烟霭，不是丘峦，而是一片未及实现的、也无从实现的无限空间。

===Original===
It was merely momentary. The tower filled its place again and I looked at her.
“What the devil,” I said, hysterically﻿—“what the devil do you play these tricks upon me for?”
“You see,” she answered, “the rudiments of the sense are there.”
“You must excuse me if I fail to understand,” I said, grasping after fragments of dropped dignity. “I am subject to fits of giddiness.” I felt a need for covering a species of nakedness. “Pardon my swearing,” I added; a proof of recovered equanimity.

===Chinese===
那不过是一瞬。塔又归回原位，我看着她。
“搞什么鬼，”我歇斯底里地喊——“你搞什么鬼，要冲我玩这些把戏？”
“你看，”她答道，“那种感官的雏形，在你身上是有的。”
“你得原谅我不明白。”我说，一面去捞拾散落一地的尊严的碎片。“我这人常犯眩晕。”我觉得需要遮一遮某种赤裸。“原谅我说了粗话。”我又补一句；这证明我心境已经平复。

===Original===
We resumed the road in silence. I was physically and mentally shaken; and I tried to deceive myself as to the cause. After some time I said:
“You insist then in preserving your﻿—your incognito.”
“Oh, I make no mystery of myself,” she answered.
“You have told me that you come from the Fourth Dimension,” I remarked, ironically.

===Chinese===
我们默默无言地重新上路。我身心都在震颤；我竭力就其中的缘故自欺。过了一会儿，我说：
“那么说，你执意要保住你的——你的匿名了。”
“哦，我并不把自己弄成个谜。”她答道。
“你已经告诉过我，你来自第四维。”我讥诮地说。

===Original===
“I come from the Fourth Dimension,” she said, patiently. She had the air of one in a position of difficulty; of one aware of it and ready to brave it. She had the listlessness of an enlightened person who has to explain, over and over again, to stupid children some rudimentary point of the multiplication table.
She seemed to divine my thoughts, to be aware of their very wording. She even said “yes” at the opening of her next speech.

===Chinese===
“我来自第四维。”她说，耐着性子。她有一种身陷窘境者的神气；知道自己处境为难，也准备硬着头皮去闯。她带着先觉者的那种倦怠，仿佛一个开了慧的人，不得不向愚钝的孩子们一遍又一遍讲解乘法表上某个最初级的要点。
她仿佛能洞悉我的思想，连字眼都了如指掌。下一句话开口之前，她甚至先说了一声“是的”。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said. “It is as if I were to try to explain the new ideas of any age to a person of the age that has gone before.” She paused, seeking a concrete illustration that would touch me. “As if I were explaining to Dr. Johnson the methods and the ultimate vogue of the cockney school of poetry.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”她说，“这就好比，我要向属于既往时代的人，讲解任何一个时代的新观念。”她顿了顿，寻一个能打动我的具体比方。“又好比向约翰逊博士（Dr. Johnson）讲解伦敦佬诗派（cockney school of poetry）的手法，以及它最终的时兴。”

===Original===
“I understand,” I said, “that you wish me to consider myself as relatively a Choctaw. But what I do not understand is; what bearing that has upon﻿—upon the Fourth Dimension, I think you said?”
“I will explain,” she replied.
“But you must explain as if you were explaining to a Choctaw,” I said, pleasantly, “you must be concise and convincing.”
She answered: “I will.”

===Chinese===
“我明白了，”我说，“你是要我把自己看作一个相对而言的乔克托人（Choctaw）。可我不明白的是：这跟——跟第四维有什么相干，我记得你方才说的是？”
“我来解释。”她答道。
“不过你得像对乔克托人讲解那样来讲，”我客客气气地说，“务必简明，而且能服人。”
她答：“我会的。”

===Original===
She made a long speech of it; I condense. I can’t remember her exact words﻿—there were so many; but she spoke like a book. There was something exquisitely piquant in her choice of words, in her expressionless voice. I seemed to be listening to a phonograph reciting a technical work. There was a touch of the incongruous, of the mad, that appealed to me﻿—the commonplace rolling-down landscape, the straight, white, undulating road that, from the tops of rises, one saw running for miles and miles, straight, straight, and so white. Filtering down through the great blue of the sky came the thrilling of innumerable skylarks. And I was listening to a parody of a scientific work recited by a phonograph.

===Chinese===
她就此作了一篇长论；我加以删节。她的原话我记不全了——太多了；不过她说起话来像照着书念。她的遣词，她那毫无表情的嗓音，自有一种精微的辛辣。我仿佛在听一架留声机诵读一部学术专著。其间有一点不伦不类、近乎疯狂的意味，偏偏正投我的意——四下是平平无奇、缓缓倾伏的原野，一条笔直的白路起伏而去，从坡顶望去，只见它一英里又一英里地伸展出去，笔直，笔直，那样地白。无数云雀的颤鸣从浩浩蓝天里筛落下来。而我听着的，是一架留声机诵读的、一部科学著作的戏仿。

===Original===
I heard the nature of the Fourth Dimension﻿—heard that it was an inhabited plane﻿—invisible to our eyes, but omnipresent; heard that I had seen it when Bell Harry had reeled before my eyes. I heard the Dimensionists described: a race clear-sighted, eminently practical, incredible; with no ideals, prejudices, or remorse; with no feeling for art and no reverence for life; free from any ethical tradition; callous to pain, weakness, suffering and death, as if they had been invulnerable and immortal. She did not say that they were immortal, however. “You would﻿—you will﻿—hate us,” she concluded. And I seemed only then to come to myself. The power of her imagination was so great that I fancied myself face to face with the truth. I supposed she had been amusing herself; that she should have tried to frighten me was inadmissible. I don’t pretend that I was completely at my ease, but I said, amiably: “You certainly have succeeded in making these beings hateful.”

===Chinese===
我听说了第四维的性质——听说那是一个有生灵栖居的层面——我们的眼睛看不见它，它却无处不在；听说当贝尔哈里（Bell Harry）在我眼前打晃的那一瞬，我看见的正是它。我听人描述了第四维人（Dimensionists）：一个目光清明、极为务实、匪夷所思的种族；没有理想、偏见，也没有悔恨；对艺术无感，对生命无敬；不受任何伦理传统的约束；对痛苦、孱弱、苦难与死亡一概漠然，仿佛他们刀枪不入、永生不死。不过她并没有说他们不死。“你们会——你们终将——恨我们。”她这样作结。我仿佛直到此刻才回过神来。她的想象力如此强大，我竟恍然觉得自己直面着真理。我猜想她一直不过是在自娱；说她存心吓我，那是不可想象的。我不敢假充自己全然安然自在，但我还是和气地说：“你确实成功地把这些生灵变得可恨了。”

===Original===
“I have made nothing,” she said with a faint smile, and went on amusing herself. She would explain origins, now.
“Your”﻿—she used the word as signifying, I suppose, the inhabitants of the country, or the populations of the earth﻿—“your ancestors were mine, but long ago you were crowded out of the Dimension as we are today, you overran the earth as we shall do tomorrow. But you contracted diseases, as we shall contract them﻿—beliefs, traditions; fears; ideas of pity﻿ ﻿… of love. You grew luxurious in the worship of your ideals, and sorrowful; you solaced yourselves with creeds, with arts﻿—you have forgotten!”

===Chinese===
“我什么也没有制造。”她淡然一笑，继续她的自娱。这会儿她要讲解起源了。
“你们的”——她用这个词，我想，意指这地方的居民，或者泛称大地上的人口——“你们的祖先本就是我们的，只是在很久以前，你们被从那个维度里挤了出来，一如我们今日；你们漫溢大地，一如我们明日所为。只是你们染上了病，我们日后也会染上——信仰，传统；恐惧；怜悯的观念……爱的观念。你们在对自身理想的膜拜里养得骄奢，又生出悲愁；你们拿信条、拿各种艺术宽慰自己——你们已经忘啦！”

===Original===
She spoke with calm conviction; with an overwhelming and dispassionate assurance. She was stating facts; not professing a faith. We approached a little roadside inn. On a bench before the door a dun-clad country fellow was asleep, his head on the table.
“Put your fingers in your ears,” my companion commanded.
I humoured her.
I saw her lips move. The countryman started, shuddered, and by a clumsy, convulsive motion of his arms, upset his quart. He rubbed his eyes. Before he had voiced his emotions we had passed on.

===Chinese===
她说得平静而确凿；带着一种压倒的、不动声色的笃定。她是在陈述事实，不是在宣说信仰。我们走近一家路边小酒店。门前长凳上，一个穿茶褐色衣裳的乡下汉子睡着了，脑袋伏在桌上。
“把手指塞进耳朵。”我的同伴发令道。
我顺从了她。
我看见她的嘴唇动了动。那乡下汉子猛地惊起，打了个寒噤，两条胳膊一阵笨拙的痉挛，掀翻了他那一夸脱酒。他揉起眼睛来。还没等他把心里的激动嚷出声，我们已经走了过去。

===Original===
“I have seen a horse-coper do as much for a stallion,” I commented. “I know there are words that have certain effects. But you shouldn’t play pranks like the low-comedy devil in Faustus.”
“It isn’t good form, I suppose?” she sneered.
“It’s a matter of feeling,” I said, hotly, “the poor fellow has lost his beer.”

===Chinese===
“我见过马贩子对一匹种马也来过这么一手。”我议论道，“我知道有些字眼是有几分效力的。可你不该这样恶作剧，像《浮士德博士》（Faustus）里那个插科打诨的魔鬼。”
“这有失体统，我猜是吧？”她冷笑道。
“这是个感情的事，”我火气上来，“那可怜的家伙丢了啤酒。”

===Original===
“What’s that to me?” she commented, with the air of one affording a concrete illustration.
“It’s a good deal to him,” I answered.
“But what to me?”

===Chinese===
“那与我何干？”她说，一副正在提供具体例证的神气。
“对他可不是小事。”我答道。
“可与我何干？”

===Original===
I said nothing. She ceased her exposition immediately afterward, growing silent as suddenly as she had become discoursive. It was rather as if she had learnt a speech by heart and had come to the end of it. I was quite at a loss as to what she was driving at. There was a newness, a strangeness about her; sometimes she struck me as mad, sometimes as frightfully sane. We had a meal somewhere﻿—a meal that broke the current of her speech﻿—and then, in the late afternoon, took a byroad and wandered in secluded valleys. I had been ill; trouble of the nerves, brooding, the monotony of life in the shadow of unsuccess. I had an errand in this part of the world and had been approaching it deviously, seeking the normal in its quiet hollows, trying to get back to my old self. I did not wish to think of how I should get through the year﻿—of the thousand little things that matter. So I talked and she﻿—she listened very well.

===Chinese===
我没有作声。此后她的讲解便戛然而止，话头收得同打开时一样突然。倒像是她把一篇话背得烂熟，如今恰好背到了头。她到底想干什么，我全然摸不着头脑。她周身有一种新颖，一种陌生；有时我觉得她疯癫，有时又觉得她清醒得可怕。我们不知在哪儿吃了一顿饭——那顿饭打断了她的滔滔——随后，午后向晚，我们抄上一条小路，在僻静的山谷间信步游荡。我此前生过一场病；是神经的毛病，是郁结的苦思，是一事无成的阴影底下那种单调的生活。我到这一带来原有一桩事要办，却一直迂回着向它挨近，想在它安静的山坳里寻回常态，设法找回从前的自己。我不愿去想这一年该怎么捱过去——不愿去想那一千桩要紧的小事。于是我便说话，而她——她听得极好。

===Original===
But topics exhaust themselves and, at the last, I myself brought the talk round to the Fourth Dimension. We were sauntering along the forgotten valley that lies between Hardves and Stelling Minnis; we had been silent for several minutes. For me, at least, the silence was pregnant with the undefinable emotions that, at times, run in currents between man and woman. The sun was getting low and it was shadowy in those shrouded hollows. I laughed at some thought, I forget what, and then began to badger her with questions. I tried to exhaust the possibilities of the Dimensionist idea, made grotesque suggestions. I said: “And when a great many of you have been crowded out of the Dimension and invaded the earth you will do so-and-so﻿—” something preposterous and ironical. She coldly dissented, and at once the irony appeared as gross as the jocularity of a commercial traveller. Sometimes she signified: “Yes, that is what we shall do;” signified it without speaking﻿—by some gesture perhaps, I hardly know what. There was something impressive﻿—something almost regal﻿—in this manner of hers; it was rather frightening in those lonely places, which were so forgotten, so gray, so closed in. There was something of the past world about the hanging woods, the little veils of unmoving mist﻿—as if time did not exist in those furrows of the great world; and one was so absolutely alone; anything might have happened. I grew weary of the sound of my tongue. But when I wanted to cease, I found she had on me the effect of some incredible stimulant.

===Chinese===
可话头总有说尽的时候，到最后，是我自己又把话引回了第四维。我们正沿着那条被世人遗忘的山谷信步，山谷横在哈德夫斯（Hardves）与斯特林明尼斯（Stelling Minnis）之间；我们已经沉默了好几分钟。至少对我而言，这沉默里孕满了不可名状的情绪——那种时而像潜流一般在男人与女人之间往来的情绪。太阳低了下去，那些掩映的山坳里一片幽暗。我想到个什么念头，笑了——想不起是什么了——随后便开始拿问题缠磨她。我试图穷尽第四维人这个念头的种种可能，提出些荒诞不经的设想。我说：“等你们一大批人都从那个维度里挤出来、涌到大地上，你们就要如此这般——”云云，荒唐而且带刺。她冷冷地不以为然，于是那点讽刺顿时显得粗鄙不堪，像旅行推销员的逗趣。有时她示意：“是的，我们将要这么办；”示意而不出声——或许靠某个手势，我几乎说不上来是什么。她这种气度里有一种慑人的东西——一种近乎帝王气派的东西；在那些冷僻的去处，在那般被人遗忘、那般灰暗、那般封闭的地方，这气度实在叫人心里发毛。低垂的树林，一缕缕凝然不动的薄雾，都带着一点往世的气息——仿佛在巨大世界的这些褶皱里，时间并不存在；而人又是那样绝对的孤独；什么事都可能发生。我听腻了自己舌头的声音。可每当我想住口，却发现她对我有一种效力，像某种不可思议的兴奋剂。

===Original===
We came to the end of the valley where the road begins to climb the southern hill, out into the open air. I managed to maintain an uneasy silence. From her grimly dispassionate reiterations I had attained to a clear idea, even to a visualisation, of her fantastic conception﻿—allegory, madness, or whatever it was. She certainly forced it home. The Dimensionists were to come in swarms, to materialise, to devour like locusts, to be all the more irresistible because indistinguishable. They were to come like snow in the night: in the morning one would look out and find the world white; they were to come as the gray hairs come, to sap the strength of us as the years sap the strength of the muscles. As to methods, we should be treated as we ourselves treat the inferior races. There would be no fighting, no killing; we﻿—our whole social system﻿—would break as a beam snaps, because we were worm-eaten with altruism and ethics. We, at our worst, had a certain limit, a certain stage where we exclaimed: “No, this is playing it too low down,” because we had scruples that acted like handicapping weights. She uttered, I think, only two sentences of connected words: “We shall race with you and we shall not be weighted,” and, “We shall merely sink you lower by our weight.” All the rest went like this:

===Chinese===
我们走到山谷的尽头，路从这里开始爬向南面的山冈，爬向露天的开阔地。我勉强维持着一段惴惴的沉默。经过她冷酷而无动于衷的一再重申，我对她那个奇诡的构想——寓言也罢，疯话也罢，管它是什么——已经得到一个清晰的观念，甚至能把它想成一幅图像。她确实把它深深钉进了我的脑子。第四维人将成群结队而来，取得形体，像蝗虫一样吞噬一切，并且正因与人无从分辨而愈发不可抗拒。他们将像夜里的雪一样降临：早晨朝外一望，世界已经一片白；他们将像白发一样来临，消磨我们的气力，一如岁月消磨肌肉的气力。至于手段，我们受到的对待，将是我们自己加给劣等种族的那一种。不会有厮杀，不会有屠戮；我们——我们的整个社会体系——将像木梁喀喇一声折断那样垮掉，因为我们早被利他主义和伦理蛀得千疮百孔。我们哪怕在最不堪时，也还有某条底线，某一步一到便要喊出：“不行，这手玩得太下作”的地步，因为我们有顾忌，那顾忌压起手来就像负重。连贯成句的话，我想她统共只说了两句：“我们要同你们赛跑，而我们身上不带负重”，还有，“我们只需凭我们的分量，便把你们压得更低。”其余的一切，都是这个样子：

===Original===
“But then,” I would say﻿ ﻿… “we shall not be able to trust anyone. Anyone may be one of you.﻿ ﻿…” She would answer: “Anyone.” She prophesied a reign of terror for us. As one passed one’s neighbour in the street one would cast sudden, piercing glances at him.

===Chinese===
“可是这样一来，”我便说……“我们就谁也信不过了。谁都可能是你们中的一个。……”她便答：“谁都可能。”她为我们预言了一场恐怖统治。人在街上与邻人错身而过时，会朝他投去突然而刺人的一瞥。

===Original===
I was silent. The birds were singing the sun down. It was very dark among the branches, and from minute to minute the colours of the world deepened and grew sombre.
“But﻿—” I said. A feeling of unrest was creeping over me. “But why do you tell me all this?” I asked. “Do you think I will enlist with you?”

===Chinese===
我不作声了。群鸟唱着，把太阳唱落。枝柯之间已经很暗，天地间的颜色一分一分地深下去、沉下去。
“可是——”我说。一种不安悄悄爬上我的心头。“可你为什么把这一切都告诉我？”我问，“你以为我会入你们的伙吗？”

===Original===
“You will have to in the end,” she said, “and I do not wish to waste my strength. If you had to work unwittingly you would resist and resist and resist. I should have to waste my power on you. As it is, you will resist only at first, then you will begin to understand. You will see how we will bring a man down﻿—a man, you understand, with a great name, standing for probity and honour. You will see the nets drawing closer and closer, and you will begin to understand. Then you will cease resisting, that is all.”

===Chinese===
“到头来你不得不如此，”她说，“而我不愿浪费我的力气。倘若你在懵然不知中被驱使着做事，你就会抵抗、抵抗、再抵抗。我就只好把力量浪费在你身上。照现在这样，你只在一开始抵抗，随后你就会开始明白。你会看到我们如何把一个人拉下马——一个人，你懂吗，一个鼎鼎大名、身为廉洁与名誉之表率的人。你会看到罗网一步步收紧，你就会开始明白。到那时你便不再抵抗了，如此而已。”

===Original===
I was silent. A June nightingale began to sing, a trifle hoarsely. We seemed to be waiting for some signal. The things of the night came and went, rustled through the grass, rustled through the leafage. At last I could not even see the white gleam of her face.﻿ ﻿…
I stretched out my hand and it touched hers. I seized it without an instant of hesitation. “How could I resist you?” I said, and heard my own whisper with a kind of amazement at its emotion. I raised her hand. It was very cold and she seemed to have no thought of resistance; but before it touched my lips something like a panic of prudence had overcome me. I did not know what it would lead to﻿—and I remembered that I did not even know who she was. From the beginning she had struck me as sinister and now, in the obscurity, her silence and her coldness seemed to be a passive threatening of unknown entanglement. I let her hand fall.

===Chinese===
我沉默着。一只六月的夜莺开始啼唱，嗓音微有些哑。我们仿佛在等待什么信号。夜之物色来了又去，窸窣穿过草丛，窸窣穿过叶隙。到后来，我连她脸上一线白色的微光也看不见了。……
我伸出手去，碰到了她的手。我片刻也不迟疑地握住了它。“我怎么能抗拒你呢？”我说，听见自己的低语，为其中的激动暗暗吃惊。我托起她的手。那只手很冷，而她似乎全无抗拒之意；可不等它碰到我的嘴唇，一阵近乎恐慌的审慎压倒了我。我不知道这会把人引向何处——而且我想起，我连她是谁都还不知道。从一开始她就给我的感觉是不祥；而此刻，在晦暗里，她的沉默与她的冰冷像是一种消极的威胁，威胁着某种未知的纠缠。我任她的手落了下去。

===Original===
“We must be getting on,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我们得赶路了。”我说。

===Original===
The road was shrouded and overhung by branches. There was a kind of translucent light, enough to see her face, but I kept my eyes on the ground. I was vexed. Now that it was past the episode appeared to be a lost opportunity. We were to part in a moment, and her rare mental gifts and her unfamiliar, but very vivid, beauty made the idea of parting intensely disagreeable. She had filled me with a curiosity that she had done nothing whatever to satisfy, and with a fascination that was very nearly a fear. We mounted the hill and came out on a stretch of soft common sward. Then the sound of our footsteps ceased and the world grew more silent than ever. There were little enclosed fields all round us. The moon threw a wan light, and gleaming mist hung in the ragged hedges. Broad, soft roads ran away into space on every side.

===Chinese===
路被枝叶笼罩着，枝柯低垂。有一种半透的光，足够看清她的脸，我却只顾低头看路。我心里懊恼。事过之后，方才那段光景倒显得像一次坐失的机会。我们转眼就要分手，而她那罕见的心智天赋、她那陌生却又极鲜明的美，使分手这个念头变得说不出的难受。她使我心中充满了好奇，却丝毫不曾着手去满足它；还使我充满了一种魔力般的吸引，那吸引几乎就是恐惧。我们登上山冈，走上一片柔软的公地草皮。于是脚步声消失了，世界比先前更加寂静。四周尽是一方一方圈起的小田。月亮投下惨淡的光，发亮的雾挂满参差的树篱。宽阔而松软的道路，向四面八方遁入空茫。

===Original===
“And now﻿ ﻿…” I asked, at last, “shall we ever meet again?” My voice came huskily, as if I had not spoken for years and years.
“Oh, very often,” she answered.
“Very often?” I repeated. I hardly knew whether I was pleased or dismayed. Through the gate-gap in a hedge, I caught a glimmer of a white house front. It seemed to belong to another world; to another order of things.

===Chinese===
“那么现在……”我终于问，“我们还会再见面吗？”我的嗓音沙哑，仿佛已经许多许多年不曾开口说过话。
“哦，常见呢。”她答道。
“常见？”我重复道。我几乎说不清自己是欢喜还是心惊。透过树篱上一道栅门的豁口，我瞥见一方白色屋前的微光。它像是属于另一个世界；属于另一种事物的秩序。

===Original===
“Ah﻿ ﻿… here is Callan’s,” I said. “This is where I was going.﻿ ﻿…”
“I know,” she answered; “we part here.”
“To meet again?” I asked.
“Oh﻿ ﻿… to meet again; why, yes, to meet again.”

===Chinese===
“啊……这儿就是卡伦（Callan）家了。”我说，“我要去的就是这儿。……”
“我知道，”她答道，“我们在这儿分手。”
“还会再见？”我问。
“哦……再见；当然，是的，再见。”

## II / 第二章

===Original===
Her figure faded into the darkness, as pale things waver down into deep water, and as soon as she disappeared my sense of humour returned. The episode appeared more clearly, as a flirtation with an enigmatic, but decidedly charming, chance travelling companion. The girl was a riddle, and a riddle once guessed is a very trivial thing. She, too, would be a very trivial thing when I had found a solution. It occurred to me that she wished me to regard her as a symbol, perhaps, of the future﻿—as a type of those who are to inherit the earth, in fact. She had been playing the fool with me, in her insolent modernity. She had wished me to understand that I was old-fashioned; that the frame of mind of which I and my fellows were the inheritors was over and done with. We were to be compulsorily retired; to stand aside superannuated. It was obvious that she was better equipped for the swiftness of life. She had a something﻿—not only quickness of wit, not only ruthless determination, but a something quite different and quite indefinably more impressive. Perhaps it was only the confidence of the superseder, the essential quality that makes for the empire of the Occidental. But I was not a negro﻿—not even relatively a Hindu. I was somebody, confound it, I was somebody.

===Chinese===
她的身影隐入黑暗，像苍白之物摇曳着沉入深水；她一消失，我的幽默感便回来了。这件事显得更清楚了：不过是同一位神秘莫测、却又确实迷人的萍水相逢的旅伴调了一场情。那姑娘是个谜，而谜语一旦猜破，便实在无足轻重。等我找到谜底，她也会变成一件十分无足轻重的东西。我想到，她大概是想让我把她看成一个象征，未来的象征——实际上，看成那班将承受大地之人的典型。她一直拿我寻开心，带着她那副傲慢的现代气派。她是要我明白：我已属过时；我和我这一辈人作为继承人的那套心境，已经完结。我们将被强制退休，老迈无用，靠边站好。显然，她更有本领应付生活的疾速。她有一样什么东西——不只是机敏，不只是无情的决断，而是某种截然不同、无可名状地更慑人的东西。也许那不过是取而代之者的自信，是成就西方霸业的那份根本素质。可我又不是黑人——连个相对的印度教徒都算不上。我是个人物呢，见鬼，我是个人物。

===Original===
As an author, I had been so uniformly unsuccessful, so absolutely unrecognised, that I had got into the way of regarding myself as ahead of my time, as a worker for posterity. It was a habit of mind﻿—the only revenge that I could take upon despiteful Fate. This girl came to confound me with the common herd﻿—she declared herself to be that very posterity for which I worked.

===Chinese===
作为作家，我一向不得志，一向绝对地无人理睬，久而久之便惯于把自己看作超前于时代的人，一个为后世工作的劳动者。这是一种心境——是我对刻薄命运所能采取的唯一报复。这姑娘却来把我混同于芸芸众生——她自称正是我为之效力的那个后世。

===Original===
She was probably a member of some clique that called themselves Fourth Dimensionists﻿—just as there had been pre-Raphaelites. It was a matter of cant allegory. I began to wonder how it was that I had never heard of them. And how on earth had they come to hear of me!

===Chinese===
她多半是某个自称“第四维人”的小圈子的成员——就像从前有过前拉斐尔派一样。这不过是一套滥调寓言。我开始纳闷，我怎么从来没有听说过他们。而他们究竟又是怎么听说我的！

===Original===
“She must have read something of mine,” I found myself musing: “the Jenkins story perhaps. It must have been the Jenkins story; they gave it a good place in their rotten magazine. She must have seen that it was the real thing, and.﻿ ﻿…” When one is an author one looks at things in that way, you know.

===Chinese===
“她一定读过我的什么东西，”我发觉自己在暗自琢磨，“也许是那篇‘詹金斯故事’。一定是‘詹金斯故事’；他们在那本破杂志里给它登了个好位置。她一定看出了那是真货，于是……”你知道，人一当了作家，看事情就是那个样子。

===Original===
By that time I was ready to knock at the door of the great Callan. I seemed to be jerked into the commonplace medium of a great, great﻿—oh, an infinitely great﻿—novelist’s home life. I was led into a well-lit drawing-room, welcomed by the great man’s wife, gently propelled into a bedroom, made myself tidy, descended and was introduced into the sanctum, before my eyes had grown accustomed to the lamplight. Callan was seated upon his sofa surrounded by an admiring crowd of very local personages. I forget what they looked like. I think there was a man whose reddish beard did not become him and another whose face might have been improved by the addition of a reddish beard; there was also an extremely moody dark man and I vaguely recollect a person who lisped.

===Chinese===
这时我已经站在大人物卡伦门前，准备叩门了。我仿佛被猛地拽进一位伟大的、伟大的——哦，无限伟大的——小说家的家居生活那平庸的介质里。我被领进一间灯光明亮的客厅，受到伟人太太的欢迎，又被轻轻推进一间卧室，收拾停当，下楼，被引进内室——这一切都在我的眼睛尚未适应灯光之前便完成了。卡伦坐在他的沙发上，四周围着一圈满怀倾慕的地道本地名流。他们长什么模样我忘了。好像有一个红胡子衬得他不怎么样的男人，还有一个脸上若添一撮红胡子或可增色的男人；还有一位神情极端阴郁的深色男子，另外我恍惚记得还有个说话咬舌的人。

===Original===
They did not talk much; indeed there was very little conversation. What there was Callan supplied. He﻿—spoke﻿—very﻿—slowly﻿—and﻿—very﻿—authoritatively, like a great actor whose aim is to hold the stage as long as possible. The raising of his heavy eyelids at the opening door conveyed the impression of a dark, mental weariness; and seemed somehow to give additional length to his white nose. His short, brown beard was getting very grey, I thought. With his lofty forehead and with his superior, yet propitiatory smile, I was of course familiar. Indeed one saw them on posters in the street. The notables did not want to talk. They wanted to be spellbound﻿—and they were. Callan sat there in an appropriate attitude﻿—the one in which he was always photographed. One hand supported his head, the other toyed with his watch-chain. His face was uniformly solemn, but his eyes were disconcertingly furtive. He cross-questioned me as to my walk from Canterbury; remarked that the cathedral was a﻿—magnificent﻿—Gothic﻿—Monument and set me right as to the lie of the roads. He seemed pleased to find that I remembered very little of what I ought to have noticed on the way. It gave him an opportunity for the display of his local erudition.

===Chinese===
他们不大说话；实际上几乎没有什么交谈。仅有的那点话全由卡伦包办。他——说话——非常——慢——而且——非常——威严，像一位立志尽可能久地占据舞台的大演员。门开时他抬起沉重的眼皮，流露出一种幽暗的心智疲惫的印象；不知怎么，还似乎给他那只苍白的鼻子添了几分长度。我想，他那部棕色短须已经相当花白了。至于他那高耸的额头，和他那既居高临下又曲意讨好的微笑，我当然早已看熟。的确，街上的招贴上就看得见。名流们并不想交谈。他们只想被迷住——他们也被迷住了。卡伦以一个得体的姿势坐在那里——正是他照相时惯用的那个姿势。一只手支着头，另一只手摆弄着表链。他的脸一概庄重，眼睛却令人不安地躲闪。他盘问我从坎特伯雷（Canterbury）一路步行的情形；评说道那座大教堂是一件——宏伟的——哥特式——纪念碑，又就道路的走向纠正了我。发现我几乎不记得沿途本该留意的种种，他似乎颇为高兴。这给了他一个机会，展示他本地的掌故学问。

===Original===
“A﻿—remarkable woman﻿—used﻿—to﻿—live﻿—in﻿—the﻿—cottage﻿—next﻿—the﻿—mill﻿—at﻿—Stelling,” he said; “she was the original of Kate Wingfield.”

===Chinese===
“有——一位——非凡的——女人——曾经——住在——斯特林（Stelling）——磨坊——隔壁的——小屋里，”他说，“她就是凯特·温菲尔德（Kate Wingfield）的原型。”

===Original===
“In your *Boldero*?” the chorus chorused.

===Chinese===
“在您的《博尔德罗》（Boldero）里？”众人齐声合唱道。

===Original===
Remembrance of the common at Stelling﻿—of the glimmering white faces of the shadowy cottages﻿—was like a cold waft of mist to me. I forgot to say “Indeed!”

===Chinese===
想起斯特林的那片公地——想起那些朦胧村舍微微发亮的白墙面——对我就像一缕冷雾拂面而来。我忘了说一声“的确如此！”

===Original===
“She was﻿—a very﻿—remarkable﻿—woman﻿—She﻿—”

===Chinese===
“她是——一位非常——非凡的——女人——她——”

===Original===
I found myself wondering which was real; the common with its misty hedges and the blurred moon; or this room with its ranks of uniformly bound books and its bust of the great man that threw a portentous shadow upward from its pedestal behind the lamp.

===Chinese===
我发觉自己在想：哪一个才是真的？是那片公地，连同它雾蒙蒙的树篱和一轮模糊的月亮；还是这间屋子，连同那一排排装帧划一的书，和那尊伟人的半身像——它在灯后从座上朝上投出一道森然可怖的影子。

===Original===
Before I had entirely recovered myself, the notables were departing to catch the last train. I was left alone with Callan.

===Chinese===
我还没完全回过神来，名流们已在纷纷告辞，去赶末班火车。屋里只剩下我和卡伦。

===Original===
He did not trouble to resume his attitude for me, and when he did speak, spoke faster.

===Chinese===
他懒得为我重新摆出那个姿势，再开口时，话说得快了些。

===Original===
“Interesting man, Mr. Jinks?” he said; “you recognised him?”

===Chinese===
“有趣的人物吧，那位金克斯（Jinks）先生？”他说，“你认出他来了？”

===Original===
“No,” I said; “I don’t think I ever met him.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”我说，“我想我从来没见过他。”

===Original===
Callan looked annoyed.

===Chinese===
卡伦面露不悦。

===Original===
“I thought I’d got him pretty well. He’s Hector Steele. In my *Blanfield*,” he added.

===Chinese===
“我还以为我把他写得很传神了呢。他就是赫克托·斯蒂尔（Hector Steele）。在我的《布兰菲尔德》（Blanfield）里。”他补了一句。

===Original===
“Indeed!” I said. I had never been able to read *Blanfield*. “Indeed, ah, yes﻿—of course.”

===Chinese===
“的确如此！”我说。那部《布兰菲尔德》我一直没能读下去。“的确如此，啊，是啊——自然是。”

===Original===
There was an awkward pause.

===Chinese===
一阵尴尬的停顿。

===Original===
“The whiskey will be here in a minute,” he said, suddenly. “I don’t have it in when Whatnot’s here. He’s the Rector, you know; a great temperance man. When we’ve had a﻿—a modest quencher﻿—we’ll get to business.”

===Chinese===
“威士忌马上就到，”他忽然说，“沃特诺特（Whatnot）在这儿的时候，我是不让它进门的。他是教区长，你知道，戒酒运动的健将。等我们——呃——浅浅地润上一口——就谈正事。”

===Original===
“Oh,” I said, “your letters really meant﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哦，”我说，“您那些信原来是当真的——”

===Original===
“Of course,” he answered. “Oh, here’s the whiskey. Well now, Fox was down here the other night. You know Fox, of course?”

===Chinese===
“当然是当真的，”他答道，“啊，威士忌来了。这个嘛，前几天晚上福克斯下乡到我这儿来了。福克斯你当然是认识的？”

===Original===
“Didn’t he start the rag called﻿—?”

===Chinese===
“他不是创办过一份叫什么来着的小破报吗——？”

===Original===
“Yes, yes,” Callan answered, hastily, “he’s been very successful in launching papers. Now he’s trying his hand with a new one. He’s any amount of backers﻿—big names, you know. He’s to run my next as a feuilleton. This﻿—this venture is to be rather more serious in tone than any that he’s done hitherto. You understand?”

===Chinese===
“是的，是的，”卡伦急忙答道，“他办报一向很成功。如今他又要试手办一份新的。他背后有一大批出资人——都是响当当的名字，你知道。我的下一部就将在那上面连载（feuilleton）。这——这项事业呢，格调要比他以往办过的任何一种都严肃得多。你明白吗？”

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I said; “but I don’t see where I come in.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我说，“可我看不出这里面有我什么事。”

===Original===
Callan took a meditative sip of whiskey, added a little more water, a little more whiskey, and then found the mixture to his liking.

===Chinese===
卡伦沉思地呷了一口威士忌，加了一点水，又加了一点威士忌，然后觉得这配方正合口味。

===Original===
“You see,” he said, “Fox got a letter here to say that Wilkinson had died suddenly﻿—some affection of the heart. Wilkinson was to have written a series of personal articles on prominent people. Well, Fox was nonplussed and I put in a word for you.”

===Chinese===
“是这么回事，”他说，“福克斯收到一封信，说威尔金森（Wilkinson）突然死了——心脏上的什么毛病。本来，那组显要人物的人物侧写是要由威尔金森来写的。这下福克斯没了主意，我就替你进了一句言。”

===Original===
“I’m sure I’m much﻿—” I began.

===Chinese===
“我真是不胜——”我开口道。

===Original===
“Not at all, not at all,” Callan interrupted, blandly. “I’ve known you and you’ve known me for a number of years.”

===Chinese===
“哪里哪里，不必客气，”卡伦温和地打断我，“我认识你，你也认识我，都好些年了。”

===Original===
A sudden picture danced before my eyes﻿—the portrait of the Callan of the old days﻿—the fawning, shady individual, with the seedy clothes, the furtive eyes and the obliging manners.

===Chinese===
一幅图景忽然在我眼前舞动——旧日卡伦的写照——一个谄媚而阴私的人物，衣衫寒酸，目光躲闪，殷勤备至。

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I said; “but I don’t see that that gives me any claim.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我说，“可我看不出这就给了我什么权利。”

===Original===
Callan cleared his throat.

===Chinese===
卡伦清了清嗓子。

===Original===
“The lapse of time,” he said in his grand manner, “rivets what we may call the bands of association.”

===Chinese===
“时间的推移，”他以他那堂皇的气派说道，“把我们不妨称之为交谊纽带的东西铆得更牢了。”

===Original===
He paused to inscribe this sentence on the tablets of his memory. It would be dragged in﻿—to form a purple patch﻿—in his new serial.

===Chinese===
他停顿了一下，好把这句话铭刻到记忆的簿册上。往后它会被硬拽进去——充作一段华彩——用在他的新连载里。

===Original===
“You see,” he went on, “I’ve written a good deal of autobiographical matter and it would verge upon self-advertisement to do more. You know how much I dislike *that*. So I showed Fox your sketch in the *Kensington*.”

===Chinese===
“你要知道，”他接着说，“我写过不少自传性的东西，再写下去就近乎自我标榜了。你知道我多么厌恶那一套。所以我才把你在《肯辛顿》（the Kensington）上那篇特写拿给福克斯看了。”

===Original===
“The Jenkins story?” I said. “How did you come to see it?”

===Chinese===
“那篇‘詹金斯故事’？”我说，“您怎么会看到它的？”

===Original===
“They send me the *Kensington*,” he answered. There was a touch of sourness in his tone, and I remembered that the *Kensington* I had seen had been ballasted with seven goodly pages by Callan himself﻿—seven unreadable packed pages of a serial.

===Chinese===
“他们把《肯辛顿》寄给我，”他答道。他语气里带一丝酸味，我于是想起，我见过的那期《肯辛顿》，是拿卡伦本人的足足七页做的压舱——七页密密麻麻、没法读下去的连载。

===Original===
“As I was saying,” Callan began again, “you ought to know me very well, and I suppose you are acquainted with my books. As for the rest, I will give you what material you want.”

===Chinese===
“我方才说了，”卡伦重新开腔，“你应该非常了解我了；我想你也熟悉我的书。至于其余的，你要什么材料，我都会给你。”

===Original===
“But, my dear Callan,” I said, “I’ve never tried my hand at that sort of thing.”

===Chinese===
“可是，亲爱的卡伦，”我说，“这类东西我从来没试过手。”

===Original===
Callan silenced me with a wave of his hand.

===Chinese===
卡伦挥手止住了我。

===Original===
“It struck both Fox and myself that your﻿—your ‘Jenkins’ was just what was wanted,” he said; “of course, that was a study of a kind of broken-down painter. But it was well done.”

===Chinese===
“福克斯和我都觉得，你的——你的那篇‘詹金斯’正是我们要的东西，”他说，“当然啰，那写的是一种潦倒的画家。可是写得好。”

===Original===
I bowed my head. Praise from Callan was best acknowledged in silence.

===Chinese===
我低下头。卡伦的赞辞，以沉默领受为宜。

===Original===
“You see, what we want, or rather what Fox wants,” he explained, “is a kind of series of studies of celebrities *chez eux*. Of course, they are not broken down. But if you can treat them as you treated Jenkins﻿—get them in their studies, surrounded by what in their case stands for the broken lay figures and the faded serge curtains﻿—it will be exactly the thing. It will be a new line, or rather﻿—what is a great deal better, mind you﻿—an old line treated in a slightly, very slightly different way. That’s what the public wants.”

===Chinese===
“你要明白，我们想要的——不如说福克斯想要的，”他解释道，“是一组名人居家（chez eux）的写生。当然，他们并不潦倒。可是，你要是能照你写詹金斯的法子来写他们——让他们待在各自的书房里，身边围着些在他们那等人就相当于破损人体模型和褪色哔叽窗帘的东西——那就正合式了。那将是一条新路子，或者说——这可要好得多，你记住——一条旧路子，只用稍稍地、稍稍地改一点点来处理。公众要的就是这个。”

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” I said, “that’s what the public wants. But all the same, it’s been done time out of mind before. Why, I’ve seen photographs of you and your armchair and your pen-wiper and so on, half a score of times in the sixpenny magazines.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”我说，“公众要的就是这个。不过话说回来，这类玩意儿自古以来早被人做滥了。就拿您说吧，您和您的扶手椅、您的揩笔布之类的照片，我在六便士杂志上都见过不下十来次了。”

===Original===
Callan again indicated bland superiority with a wave of his hand.

===Chinese===
卡伦又挥手示意，温文地摆出居高临下的架势。

===Original===
“You undervalue yourself,” he said.

===Chinese===
“你把自己估得太低了。”他说。

===Original===
I murmured﻿—“Thanks.”

===Chinese===
我喃喃道——“多谢。”

===Original===
“This is to be﻿—not a mere pandering to curiosity﻿—but an attempt to get at the inside of things﻿—to get the atmosphere, so to speak; not merely to catalogue furniture.”

===Chinese===
“这将是——不是一味迎合猎奇——而是要探到事物的内里——可以说是要摄取那气氛；而不只是开列一份家具清单。”

===Original===
He was quoting from the prospectus of the new paper, and then cleared his throat for the utterance of a tremendous truth.

===Chinese===
他这是在引用那份新报招股书上的话；随后他清了清嗓子，要发表一条重大的真理。

===Original===
“Photography﻿—is not﻿—Art,” he remarked.

===Chinese===
“摄影——不是——艺术。”他说。

===Original===
The fantastic side of our colloquy began to strike me.

===Chinese===
我开始觉出我们这番谈话荒诞的一面。

===Original===
“After all,” I thought to myself, “why shouldn’t that girl have played at being a denizen of another sphere? She did it ever so much better than Callan. She did it too well, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
“说到底，”我心里想，“那姑娘为什么就不可以扮演一回别的界域的居民呢？她演得可比卡伦高明太多了。她演得太好了，我想。”

===Original===
“The price is very decent,” Callan chimed in. “I don’t know how much per thousand,﻿ ﻿… but.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“报酬很优厚，”卡伦插言道，“每千字多少钱，我说不准……不过……”

===Original===
I found myself reckoning, against my will as it were.

===Chinese===
我发觉自己在盘算起来，简直是身不由己。

===Original===
“You’ll do it, I suppose?” he said.

===Chinese===
“你会干的吧，我想？”他说。

===Original===
I thought of my debts﻿ ﻿… “Why, yes, I suppose so,” I answered. “But who are the others that I am to provide with atmospheres?”

===Chinese===
我想起了我的债务……“嗯，是啊，我想会吧，”我答道，“不过，其余那些要我为之提供气氛的，都是些什么人？”

===Original===
Callan shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
卡伦耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“Oh, all sorts of prominent people﻿—soldiers, statesmen, Mr. Churchill, the Foreign Minister, artists, preachers﻿—all sorts of people.”

===Chinese===
“哦，各式各样的显要人物——军人、政治家、外交大臣丘吉尔先生、艺术家、传道士——各式各样的人。”

===Original===
“All sorts of glory,” occurred to me.

===Chinese===
“各式各样的荣耀。”我心里冒出这么一句。

===Original===
“The paper will stand expenses up to a reasonable figure,” Callan reassured me.

===Chinese===
“报馆会承担开支，以一个合理的数目为限。”卡伦让我放心。

===Original===
“It’ll be a good joke for a time,” I said. “I’m infinitely obliged to you.”

===Chinese===
“有一阵子这会是个不错的笑谈，”我说，“我对您真是感激不尽。”

===Original===
He warded off my thanks with both hands.

===Chinese===
他双手一挡，挡开了我的道谢。

===Original===
“I’ll just send a wire to Fox to say that you accept,” he said, rising. He seated himself at his desk in the appropriate attitude. He had an appropriate attitude for every vicissitude of his life. These he had struck before so many people that even in the small hours of the morning he was ready for the kodak wielder. Beside him he had every form of labour-saver; every kind of literary knickknack. There were book-holders that swung into positions suitable to appropriate attitudes; there were piles of little green boxes with red capital letters of the alphabet upon them, and big red boxes with black small letters. There was a writing-lamp that cast an aesthetic glow upon another appropriate attitude﻿—and there was one typewriter with notepaper upon it, and another with MS. paper already in position.

===Chinese===
“我这就给福克斯拍封电报，说你应下了，”说着他站起身来。他以那个得体的姿势在书桌前坐下。他一生的每一种境遇，各有一个与之相配的得体姿势。这些姿势他在那么多人面前摆过，以致哪怕在凌晨时分，他也随时经得起端柯达（Kodak）相机的人一拍。他手边备有各式各样的省力器具，各种文学的零碎玩意儿：有能转到与得体姿势相合的位置上的书托；有一摞摞印着红色大写字母的小绿匣子，和印着黑色小写字母的大红匣子；有一盏写字灯，把一片审美的光辉投在另一个得体的姿势上——还有一台打字机，上面装着信纸，另一台则已装好稿纸。

===Original===
“My God!” I thought﻿—“to these heights the Muse soars.”

===Chinese===
“我的天哪！”我想——“缪斯竟翱翔到这样的高处来了。”

===Original===
As I looked at the gleaming pillars of the typewriters, the image of my own desk appeared to me; chipped, ink-stained, gloriously dusty. I thought that when again I lit my battered old tin lamp I should see ashes and match-ends; a tobacco-jar, an old gnawed penny penholder, bits of pink blotting-paper, matchboxes, old letters, and dust everywhere. And I knew that my attitude﻿—when I sat at it﻿—would be inappropriate.

===Chinese===
望着打字机闪亮的支柱，我自己那张书桌的影像浮现在我眼前：边角磕损，墨迹斑斑，灰尘积得蔚为壮观。我想，等我再点亮那盏破旧的铁皮灯，我看见的将是烟灰和火柴头；一只烟草罐，一支咬得斑驳的一便士笔杆，几角粉色吸墨纸，火柴盒，旧信，还有无处不在的灰尘。而我知道，我的姿势——当我坐到那张桌前的时候——将是不得体的。

===Original===
Callan was ticking off the telegram upon his machine. “It will go in the morning at eight,” he said.

===Chinese===
卡伦正在他的机器上嗒嗒地敲着电报。“明早八点发出。”他说。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
To encourage me, I suppose, Callan gave me the proof-sheets of his next to read in bed. The thing was so bad that it nearly sickened me of him and his jobs. I tried to read the stuff; to read it conscientiously, to read myself to sleep with it. I was under obligations to old Cal and I wanted to do him justice, but the thing was impossible. I fathomed a sort of a plot. It dealt in fratricide with a touch of adultery; a Great Moral Purpose loomed in the background. It would have been a dully readable novel but for that; as it was, it was intolerable. It was amazing that Cal himself could put out such stuff; that he should have the impudence. He was not a fool, not by any means a fool. It revolted me more than a little.

===Chinese===
大概是为了给我打气吧，卡伦把他下一部书的清样给了我，让我带去躺在床上读。那东西糟糕得几乎让我对他本人、对他那些差事都倒了胃口。我努力去读那玩意儿；凭着良心去读，想读着它把自己哄入睡。我欠着老卡尔的人情，也想公平地对待他，可这事实在办不到。我约略揣摩出一个大致的情节。写的是兄弟相残，外加一点通奸；一件伟大的道德目的隐现于背景之中。若没有那一层，它本会是一部读来乏味倒还读得下去的小说；如今这样子，实在叫人无法忍受。令人惊异的是卡尔自己居然拿得出这种货色；他竟有这样的厚脸皮。他不是个傻子，绝不是个傻子。这让我心里泛呕，不止一点点。

===Original===
I came to it out of a different plane of thought. I may not have been able to write then﻿—or I may; but I did know enough to recognise the flagrantly, the indecently bad, and, upon my soul, the idea that I, too, must cynically offer this sort of stuff if I was ever to sell my tens of thousands very nearly sent me back to my solitude. Callan had begun very much as I was beginning now; he had even, I believe, had ideals in his youth and had starved a little. It was rather trying to think that perhaps I was really no more than another Callan, that, when at last I came to review my life, I should have much such a record to look back upon. It disgusted me a little, and when I put out the light the horrors settled down upon me.

===Chinese===
我是从全然不同的思想层次来看待这件事的。我那时或许写不出东西来——或许也写得出；但好歹我还识得货，认得出那种彰明昭著的、有伤风化的坏；而我若想有朝一日卖出成千上万册，也就只好玩世不恭地端出这种货色——这个念头，我凭灵魂起誓，几乎把我赶回了我的孤独里去。卡伦当初起步的样子，同我现在开始的这一步如出一辙；我相信，他年轻时甚至也有过理想，也挨过一点饿。想到也许我实在不过是又一个卡伦；想到有朝一日我终于回顾生平，要面对的也差不多是这样一份记录——这念头真叫人不好受。这让我有点恶心；熄了灯，一阵阵恐怖便降落到我的身上。

===Original===
I woke in a shivering frame of mind, ashamed to meet Callan’s eye. It was as if he must be aware of my overnight thoughts, as if he must think me a fool who quarrelled with my victuals. He gave no signs of any such knowledge﻿—was dignified, cordial; discussed his breakfast with gusto, opened his letters, and so on. An anaemic amanuensis was taking notes for appropriate replies. How could I tell him that I would not do the work, that I was too proud and all the rest of it? He would have thought me a fool, would have stiffened into hostility, I should have lost my last chance. And, in the broad light of day, I was loath to do that.

===Chinese===
我醒来时心有余悸，羞于同卡伦对视。仿佛他必定察觉了我夜里的那些念头，仿佛他必定把我看成跟自己饭碗过不去的傻子。他丝毫没有露出知晓的神色——庄重，热诚；津津有味地评点他的早餐，拆阅他的信件，如此等等。一位面无血色的抄写员正记下要点，以备得体的回信。我怎么对他说我不干这桩差事，说我太清高，如此这般呢？他会把我当傻子，会板起脸来转为敌意，我就会丢掉最后的机会。而在大天白日之下，我不愿那么做。

===Original===
He began to talk about indifferent things; we glided out on to a current of mediocre conversation. The psychical moment, if there were any such, disappeared.

===Chinese===
他开始谈起无关紧要的事；我们滑进了一股平庸谈话的水流。那个心理时机——倘若真有那种东西的话——消失不见了。

===Original===
Someone bearing my name had written to express an intention of offering personal worship that afternoon. The prospect seemed to please the great Cal. He was used to such things; he found them pay, I suppose. We began desultorily to discuss the possibility of the writer’s being a relation of mine; I doubted. I had no relations that I knew of; there was a phenomenal old aunt who had inherited the acres and respectability of the Etchingham Grangers, but she was not the kind of person to worship a novelist. I, the poor last of the family, was without the pale, simply because I, too, was a novelist. I explained these things to Callan and he commented on them, found it strange how small or how large, I forget which, the world was. Since his own apotheosis shoals of Callans had claimed relationship.

===Chinese===
一个顶着我姓氏的人写来一封信，表示打算当天下午前来登门，献上个人的景仰。这个前景似乎让伟大的卡尔很受用。这种事他司空见惯；我想，他发现这有利可图。我们开始有一搭没一搭地讨论写信人是否我的亲戚；我表示怀疑。就我所知，我没有什么亲戚；只有一位不同凡响的老姑母，继承了埃钦厄姆·格兰杰家的田产和体面，可她不是那号会去景仰一个小说家的人物。而我，这家族可怜的末裔，被摒在体面圈外，恰恰因为我自己也是个小说家。我把这些向卡伦解释了一番，他发表了评论，只说这世界真是小——还是大来着，我忘了——真是奇怪。自打他自己飞升封神，成群结队的卡伦们便纷纷前来认亲。

===Original===
I ate my breakfast. Afterward, we set about the hatching of that article﻿—the thought of it sickens me even now. You will find it in the volume along with the others; you may see how I lugged in Callan’s surroundings, his writing-room, his dining-room, the romantic arbour in which he found it easy to write love-scenes, the clipped trees like peacocks and the trees clipped like bears, and all the rest of the background for appropriate attitudes. He was satisfied with any arrangements of words that suggested a gentle awe on the part of the writer.

===Chinese===
我吃了早饭。随后我们着手炮制那篇文章——如今想起它我还犯恶心。你会在那本集子里同其余几篇一起找到它；你可以看到我怎样生拉硬拽地搬进卡伦的环境：他的书房，他的餐室，那座他写起爱情场面来得心应手的浪漫凉棚，修剪成孔雀样的树和修剪成狗熊样的树，还有其余一切用作得体姿势背景的东西。只要文字的编排能暗示出执笔者一份浅浅的敬畏，他便心满意足。

===Original===
“Yes, yes,” he said once or twice, “that’s just the touch, just the touch﻿—very nice. But don’t you think.﻿ ﻿…” We lunched after some time.

===Chinese===
“好，好，”他有一两回说道，“正是这一笔，正是这一笔——很好。不过你不觉得……”过了一阵，我们进了午餐。

===Original===
I was so happy. Quite pathetically happy. It had come so easy to me. I had doubted my ability to do the sort of thing; but it had written itself, as money spends itself, and I was going to earn money like that. The whole of my past seemed a mistake﻿—a childishness. I had kept out of this sort of thing because I had thought it below me; I had kept out of it and had starved my body and warped my mind. Perhaps I had even damaged my work by this isolation. To understand life one must live﻿—and I had only brooded. But, by Jove, I would try to live now.

===Chinese===
我真快活。快活得相当可怜。这东西到我手里竟这么容易。我原怀疑自己有没有本事干这一路；可它自己就写成了，就像钱自己就花掉一样，而我往后就要这样挣钱了。我的整个过去仿佛是一场错误——一场孩子气。我一直躲着这类营生，因为我觉得它配不上我；我躲开了它，结果是饿坏了身体，扭曲了心智。说不定这种自我隔绝连我的作品都给毁了。要懂得生活，人就得去生活——而我只是枯坐着冥想。可是，老天在上，从今往后我要试着活一活了。

===Original===
Callan had retired for his accustomed siesta and I was smoking pipe after pipe over a confoundedly bad French novel that I had found in the bookshelves. I must have been dozing. A voice from behind my back announced:

===Chinese===
卡伦已照例去午憩了，我在书架上寻出一本糟糕透顶的法国小说，一斗接一斗地抽着烟读。我一定是打起了瞌睡。背后一个声音通报道：

===Original===
“Miss Etchingham Granger!” and added﻿—“Mr. Callan will be down directly.” I laid down my pipe, wondered whether I ought to have been smoking when Cal expected visitors, and rose to my feet.

===Chinese===
“埃钦厄姆·格兰杰小姐！”随后又添了一句——“卡伦先生即刻就下楼来。”我放下烟斗，暗自忖度卡尔既在等客，我方才抽烟是不是失了分寸，随即站起身来。

===Original===
“You!” I said, sharply. She answered, “You see.” She was smiling. She had been so much in my thoughts that I was hardly surprised﻿—the thing had even an air of pleasant inevitability about it.

===Chinese===
“是你！”我厉声说道。她答道：“你瞧。”她含着微笑。她在我心头盘踞得如此之深，我竟几乎不觉得惊讶——这件事甚至带着一种令人愉快的必然意味。

===Original===
“You must be a cousin of mine,” I said, “the name﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你准是我的哪位表亲，”我说，“这个姓氏——”

===Original===
“Oh, call it sister,” she answered.

===Chinese===
“哦，就叫姐妹吧。”她答道。

===Original===
I was feeling inclined for farce, if blessed chance would throw it in my way. You see, I was going to live at last, and life for me meant irresponsibility.

===Chinese===
我正有兴致来一场滑稽戏，只看天赐的机缘肯不肯把它送到我面前。你要知道，我终于要活一活了，而生活对我便意味着无拘无束。

===Original===
“Ah!” I said, ironically, “you are going to be a sister to me, as they say.” She might have come the bogey over me last night in the moonlight, but now﻿ ﻿… There was a spice of danger about it, too, just a touch lurking somewhere. Besides, she was good-looking and well set up, and I couldn’t see what could touch me. Even if it did, even if I got into a mess, I had no relatives, not even a friend, to be worried about me. I stood quite alone, and I half relished the idea of getting into a mess﻿—it would be part of life, too. I was going to have a little money, and she excited my curiosity. I was tingling to know what she was really at.

===Chinese===
“啊！”我讥诮地说，“像人家说的那样，你要来给我当姐妹了。”昨夜月光底下，她尽可以对我装神弄鬼，可现在……这里头还掺着一撮危险的作料，正有一星半点潜伏在什么角落。再说，她人长得好看，身段也挺拔，我看不出有什么能奈何得了我。就算奈何得了，就算我闹出一团乱子，我也没有亲戚，连一个朋友都没有，会替我担忧。我孑然一身，而闹出一团乱子的念头我竟有几分受用——那也算生活的一部分。我眼看就要有一点钱了，而她勾起了我的好奇心。我心里发痒，想知道她葫芦里究竟卖的什么药。

===Original===
“And one might ask,” I said, “what you are doing in this﻿—in this.﻿ ﻿…” I was at a loss for a word to describe the room﻿—the smugness parading as professional Bohemianism.

===Chinese===
“那么可否请教，”我说，“你在这——在这……”我一时找不出一个词来形容这间屋子——那副冒充职业波希米亚作风、骨子里却自满体面的气派。

===Original===
“Oh, I am about my own business,” she said, “I told you last night﻿—have you forgotten?”

===Chinese===
“哦，我在办我自己的事，”她说，“我昨晚告诉过你——你忘了？”

===Original===
“Last night you were to inherit the earth,” I reminded her, “and one doesn’t start in a place like this. Now I should have gone﻿—well﻿—I should have gone to some politician’s house﻿—a cabinet minister’s﻿—say to Gurnard’s. He’s the coming man, isn’t he?”

===Chinese===
“昨晚你可是要承受大地的，”我提醒她，“可没有人从这样一个地方起家。换了是我，就会去——嗯——去某位政客的府上——一位内阁大臣的——比方说格纳德家。他是未来的人物，不是吗？”

===Original===
“Why, yes,” she answered, “he’s the coming man.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”她答道，“他是未来的人物。”

===Original===
You will remember that, in those days, Gurnard was only the dark horse of the ministry. I knew little enough of these things, despised politics generally; they simply didn’t interest me. Gurnard I disliked platonically; perhaps because his face was a little enigmatic﻿—a little repulsive. The country, then, was in the position of having no Opposition and a Cabinet with two distinct strains in it﻿—the Churchill and the Gurnard﻿—and Gurnard was the dark horse.

===Chinese===
你会记得，在那些日子里，格纳德还只是内阁里的一匹黑马。这些事我知道得极少，又一向把政治整个儿看不上眼；它们根本引不起我的兴趣。对格纳德，我的反感纯属隔空的理论；也许因为他的脸有点让人捉摸不透——有点叫人反感。当时国家正处于这样一种境地：没有反对党，内阁里却有两派泾渭分明的脉络——丘吉尔系与格纳德系——而格纳德便是那匹黑马。

===Original===
“Oh, you should join your flats,” I said, pleasantly. “If he’s the coming man, where do you come in?﻿ ﻿… Unless he, too, is a Dimensionist.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你们该把各自的布景板拼拢来，”我愉快地说，“既然他是未来的人物，那你自己又排在哪儿呢？……除非他也是个第四维人。”

===Original===
“Oh, both﻿—both,” she answered. I admired the tranquillity with which she converted my points into her own. And I was very happy﻿—it struck me as a pleasant sort of fooling.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
“哦，都是——都是。”她答道。她把我抛过去的论点一一收作己有，那份安详真令我叹服。我很快活——我只当这是一种愉快的胡闹……

===Original===
“I suppose you will let me know some day who you are?” I said.

===Chinese===
“我想，总有一天你会让我知道你究竟是谁吧？”我说。

===Original===
“I have told you several times,” she answered.

===Chinese===
“我告诉过你好几回了。”她答道。

===Original===
“Oh, you won’t frighten me today,” I asserted, “not here, you know, and anyhow, why should you want to?”

===Chinese===
“哦，今天你可吓不倒我，”我断然说道，“至少在这儿不行，你知道；而且说到底，你又何必要吓我呢？”

===Original===
“I have told you,” she said again.

===Chinese===
“我告诉过你了。”她又说了一遍。

===Original===
“You’ve told me you were my sister,” I said; “but my sister died years and years ago. Still, if it suits you, if you want to be somebody’s sister﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你告诉过我你是我的姐妹，”我说，“可我的姐妹许多许多年前就死了。不过，要是这合你的意，要是你想做某个人的姐妹……”

===Original===
“It suits me,” she answered﻿—“I want to be placed, you see.”

===Chinese===
“正合我的意，”她答道——“我想给自己寻个名分，你明白。”

===Original===
I knew that my name was good enough to place anyone. We had been the Grangers of Etchingham since﻿—oh, since the flood. And if the girl wanted to be my sister and a Granger, why the devil shouldn’t she, so long as she would let me continue on this footing? I hadn’t talked to a woman﻿—not to a well setup one﻿—for ages and ages. It was as if I had come back from one of the places to which younger sons exile themselves, and for all I knew it might be the correct thing for girls to elect brothers nowadays in one set or another.

===Chinese===
我知道，我的姓氏给任何人安名分都足够分量。我们是埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家，从——哦，从大洪水时代起就是了。既然这姑娘想做我的姐妹、做一个格兰杰，那她又有什么见鬼的理由不可以呢，只要她肯让我照现在这样的关系继续下去？我已经许多许多年没同女人说过话了——没同一个身段出众的女人说过话。这情形就像我刚从次子们自我流放的那些地方回来，说不定如今姑娘们在这样那样的圈子里认个兄弟，正是合乎时宜的礼数呢。

===Original===
“Oh, tell me some more,” I said, “one likes to know about one’s sister. You and the Right Honourable Charles Gurnard are Dimensionists, and who are the others of your set?”

===Chinese===
“哦，再多讲一点，”我说，“谁都乐意多了解了解自己的姐妹。你和尊敬的查尔斯·格纳德（Charles Gurnard）是第四维人，那你们那一伙里其余的都有些谁？”

===Original===
“There is only one,” she answered. And would you believe it!﻿—it seems he was Fox, the editor of my new paper.

===Chinese===
“只有一个。”她答道。可你信不信！——那人竟然就是福克斯，我那份新报纸的主编。

===Original===
“You select your characters with charming indiscriminateness,” I said. “Fox is only a sort of toad, you know﻿—he won’t get far.”

===Chinese===
“你挑人挑得真有雅兴，不分青红皂白，”我说，“福克斯不过是只癞蛤蟆，你知道——他走不远。”

===Original===
“Oh, he’ll go far,” she answered, “but he won’t get there. Fox is fighting against us.”

===Chinese===
“哦，他走得很远，”她答道，“但他到不了那里。福克斯在同我们作对。”

===Original===
“Oh, so you don’t dwell in amity?” I said. “You fight for your own hands.”

===Chinese===
“哦，这么说，你们相处得可不算和睦？”我说，“你们各自为己。”

===Original===
“We fight for our own hands,” she answered, “I shall throw Gurnard over when he’s pulled the chestnuts out of the fire.”

===Chinese===
“我们各自为己，”她答道，“等格纳德火中取栗完毕，我就把他甩了。”

===Original===
I was beginning to get a little tired of this. You see, for me, the scene was a veiled flirtation and I wanted to get on. But I had to listen to her fantastic scheme of things. It was really a duel between Fox, the Journal-founder, and Gurnard, the Chancellor of the Exchequer. Fox, with Churchill, the Foreign Minister, and his supporters, for pieces, played what he called “the Old Morality business” against Gurnard, who passed for a cynically immoral politician.

===Chinese===
我开始对这一套有点厌烦了。你要知道，在我眼里，这场戏是一场蒙着薄纱的调情，我想往前推进。可我不得不听她讲那套关于世事的奇想。那其实是办报人福克斯与财政大臣格纳德之间的一场决斗。福克斯以外交大臣丘吉尔及其拥护者为棋子，拿他所谓的“旧道德买卖”（the Old Morality business）来对付格纳德——而格纳德呢，在外人看来是个玩世不恭、不讲道德的政客。

===Original===
I grew more impatient. I wanted to get out of this stage into something more personal. I thought she invented this sort of stuff to keep me from getting at her errand at Callan’s. But I didn’t want to know her errand; I wanted to make love to her. As for Fox and Gurnard and Churchill, the Foreign Minister, who really was a sympathetic character and did stand for political probity, she might be uttering allegorical truths, but I was not interested in them. I wanted to start some topic that would lead away from this Dimensionist farce.

===Chinese===
我更加不耐烦了。我想跳出这一幕，进到更贴近个人的什么戏码里去。我想，她编造这类东西，为的是不让我探到她来卡伦家的使命。可我并不想知道她的使命；我想向她求爱。至于福克斯、格纳德，还有那位外交大臣丘吉尔——他倒真是个讨人喜欢的人物，也确实代表着政治上的清正——她说的兴许是些寓言式的真理，可我对这些毫无兴趣。我想挑起一个话头，好从这场第四维人的闹剧里引开去。

===Original===
“My dear sister,” I began.﻿ ﻿… Callan always moved about like a confounded eavesdropper, wore carpet slippers, and stepped round the corners of screens. I expect he got copy like that.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的姐妹，”我开口道。……卡伦走路永远像个可恶的偷听者，趿着地毯拖鞋，绕屏风的拐角挪步。我想他就是靠这个弄到素材的。

===Original===
“So, she’s your sister?” he said suddenly, from behind me. “Strange that you shouldn’t recognise the handwriting.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“这么说，她是你的姐妹？”他忽然在我背后开腔，“奇怪，你竟然没认出那笔迹……”

===Original===
“Oh, we don’t correspond,” I said light-heartedly, “we are *so* different.” I wanted to take a rise out of the creeping animal that he was. He confronted her blandly.

===Chinese===
“哦，我们不通书信，”我轻快地说，“我们俩啊，实在是太不一样了。”我很想撩拨一下他这种鬼鬼祟祟、贴地爬行的东西。他温文地面对着她。

===Original===
“You must be the little girl that I remember,” he said. He had known my parents ages ago. That, indeed, was how I came to know him; I wouldn’t have chosen him for a friend. “I thought Granger said you were dead﻿ ﻿… but one gets confused.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“您准是我记得的那个小姑娘了，”他说。他多年前就认识我的父母。说实在的，我也就是这么才认识他的；若由我自己挑，我是不会挑他做朋友的。“我记得格兰杰说过您已经死了……可是人会记岔……”

===Original===
“Oh, we see very little of each other,” she answered. “Arthur might have said I was dead﻿—he’s capable of anything, you know.” She spoke with an assumption of sisterly indifference that was absolutely striking. I began to think she must be an actress of genius, she did it so well. She *was* the sister who had remained within the pale; I, the rapscallion of a brother whose vagaries were trying to his relations. That was the note she struck, and she maintained it. I didn’t know what the deuce she was driving at, and I didn’t care. These scenes with a touch of madness appealed to me. I was going to live, and here, apparently, was a woman ready to my hand. Besides, she was making a fool of Callan, and that pleased me. His patronising manners had irritated me.

===Chinese===
“哦，我们彼此很少见面，”她答道，“阿瑟（Arthur）说不定就说过我死了——他什么事都干得出来，你知道。”她带着一副俨然做姐妹的淡漠说这话，那份淡漠简直惊人。我开始想，她必定是个天才女演员，她演得实在太到家了。她就是那位留在体面圈里的姐妹；我呢，是个荒唐行径让亲族头疼的浪荡兄长。她定下的就是这个调子，而且自始至终拿捏得住。我不知道她究竟要搞什么名堂，我也满不在乎。这种带一点疯狂的场面正对我的胃口。我是要活一活的人了，而眼前，分明就有一个现成的女人。再说，她正在把卡伦当傻子耍，这让我高兴。他那种居高临下的做派早就惹恼了我。

===Original===
I assisted rather silently. They began to talk of mutual acquaintances﻿—as one talks. They both seemed to know everyone in this world. She gave herself the airs of being quite in the inner ring; alleged familiarity with quite impossible persons, with my portentous aunt, with Cabinet Ministers﻿—that sort of people. They talked about them﻿—she, as if she lived among them; he, as if he tried very hard to live up to them.

===Chinese===
我在旁边相当沉默地作陪。他们开始谈起共同的熟人——人们寒暄时就是那么谈的。他们两位似乎认识这个世界上的每一个人。她摆出一副十足核心圈内人的气派；自称与一些绝无可能高攀的人物过从甚密——与我那位威严赫赫的老姑母，与各位内阁大臣——诸如此类的人物。他们谈论这些人——她谈起来，仿佛就生活在这班人中间；他谈起来，仿佛在苦苦努力，要让自己配得上这班人。

===Original===
She affected reverence for his person, plied him with compliments that he swallowed raw﻿—horribly raw. It made me shudder a little; it was tragic to see the little great man confronted with that woman. It shocked me to think that, really, I must appear much like him﻿—must have looked like that yesterday. He was a little uneasy, I thought, made little confidences as if in spite of himself; little confidences about the *Hour*, the new paper for which I was engaged. It seemed to be run by a small gang with quite a number of assorted axes to grind. There was some foreign financier﻿—a person of position whom she knew (a noble man in the *best* sense, Callan said); there was some politician (she knew him too, and he was equally excellent, so Callan said), Mr. Churchill himself, an artist or so, an actor or so﻿—and Callan. They all wanted a little backing, so it seemed. Callan, of course, put it in another way. The Great﻿—Moral﻿—Purpose turned up, I don’t know why. He could not think he was taking me in and she obviously knew more about the people concerned than he did. But there it was, looming large, and quite as farcical as all the rest of it. The foreign financier﻿—they called him the Duc de Mersch﻿—was by way of being a philanthropist on megalomaniac lines. For some international reason he had been allowed to possess himself of the pleasant land of Greenland. There was gold in it and train-oil in it and other things that paid﻿—but the Duc de Mersch was not thinking of that. He was first and foremost a State Founder, or at least he was that after being titular ruler of some little spot of a Teutonic grand-duchy. No one of the great powers would let any other of the great powers possess the country, so it had been handed over to the Duc de Mersch, who had at heart, said Cal, the glorious vision of founding a model state﻿—*the* model state, in which washed and broadclothed Eskimo would live, side by side, regenerated lives, enfranchised equals of choicely selected younger sons of whatever occidental race. It was that sort of thing. I was even a little overpowered, in spite of the fact that Callan was its trumpeter; there was something fine about the conception and Churchill’s acquiescence seemed to guarantee an honesty in its execution.

===Chinese===
她对他本人佯出一副虔敬，高帽子一顶接一顶地递过去，他都囫囵生吞——生吞得吓人。我看得有点儿发毛；小个子伟人面对那个女人，那景象近乎悲剧。想到我自己——说实在的——必定也同他相差无几，昨天必定也是那副模样，我不禁骇然。我想，他有一点局促，身不由己似的吐露些小小的知心话；都是关于《时辰报》的小小知心话，就是我受聘的那份新报纸。看样子，它由一个小集团经营，其中各色人等，各有各的算盘要打。有一位外国金融家——一位有身份的人物，她认识（卡伦说，是一位最充分意义上的高贵者）；有一位政客（她也认识他，此人同样卓越，卡伦这么说），还有丘吉尔先生本人，一两位艺术家，一两位演员——以及卡伦。看起来，他们个个都需要有人给撑一点腰。当然，卡伦换了个说法。那位伟大的——道德——目的又冒出来了，我不知道为什么。他不会觉得自己是在蒙我，而她显然比他更清楚这班当事人的底细。可它就在那儿，影子投得老大，同其余一切一样荒诞。那位外国金融家——他们管他叫德·默施公爵——走的正是妄自尊大一路的慈善家路子。出于某个国际间的缘故，他被容许把格陵兰那片宜人的土地据为己有。那里面有黄金，有鲸油，还有别的生利之物——可德·默施公爵想的不在这个上头。他首先是一位建国者，或者说，在做过某个条顿弹丸大公国的名义统治者之后，他才至少算得上这个。列强中谁也不肯让另外任何一强占得那片国土，于是它便被交到了德·默施公爵手里；据卡尔说，公爵心底怀着一项辉煌的憧憬：创建一个模范国家——独一无二的模范国家。在那里，洗得干干净净、穿上了呢料衣裳的爱斯基摩人，将与从西方各色种族中精选出来的次子们比邻而居，过上焕然一新的生活，成为享有同等权利的平等一员。就是这么一类的名堂。尽管卡伦是它的吹鼓手，我竟还有些被镇住了；这构想自有其宏大之处，而丘吉尔的默许似乎保证了执行上的诚实。

===Original===
The Duc de Mersch wanted money, and he wanted to run a railway across Greenland. His idea was that the British public should supply the money and the British Government back the railway, as they did in the case of a less philanthropic Suez Canal. In return he offered an eligible harbour and a strip of coast at one end of the line; the British public was to be repaid in casks of train-oil and gold and with the consciousness of having aided in letting the light in upon a dark spot of the earth. So the Duc de Mersch started the *Hour*. The *Hour* was to extol the Duc de Mersch’s moral purpose; to pat the Government’s back; influence public opinion; and generally advance the cause of the System for the Regeneration of the Arctic Regions.

===Chinese===
德·默施公爵需要钱，也需要修一条横贯格陵兰的铁路。他的主意是：钱由英国公众来出，铁路由英国政府担保，就像他们对那条慈善色彩稍逊的苏伊士运河所做的那样。作为回报，他愿意让出线路一端的一处合宜港址和一段海岸；英国公众得到的偿付，将是一桶桶的鲸油和黄金，外加一份意识——意识到自己曾协力把光明引进地球上的一处黑暗角落。于是德·默施公爵创办了《时辰报》。《时辰报》的使命是：颂扬德·默施公爵的道德目的；恭维政府；影响舆论；总之，推进北极地区复兴体系的大业。

===Original===
I tell the story rather flippantly, because I heard it from Callan, and because it was impossible to take him seriously. Besides, I was not very much interested in the thing itself. But it did interest me to see how deftly she pumped him﻿—squeezed him dry.

===Chinese===
我把这故事讲得相当轻浮，因为我是从卡伦嘴里听来的，也因为压根儿没法拿他当真。再说，我对事情本身也不大感兴趣。可她套他话时那股利落劲儿——把他榨得涓滴不剩——倒真让我看得有趣。

===Original===
I was even a little alarmed for poor old Cal. After all, the man had done me a service; had got me a job. As for her, she struck me as a potentially dangerous person. One couldn’t tell, she might be some adventuress, or if not that, a speculator who would damage Cal’s little schemes. I put it to her plainly afterward; and quarrelled with her as well as I could. I drove her down to the station. Callan must have been distinctly impressed or he would never have had out his trap for her.

===Chinese===
我甚至替可怜的老卡尔捏了一点心。毕竟，这人帮过我一个忙，替我谋过一个差事。至于她，她给我的印象是个潜在的危险人物。说不准，她兴许是个女冒险家；退一步说，也是个会坑坏卡尔那些小算盘的投机客。事后我把话对她挑明了；也尽我所能地同她拌了一场嘴。我驾车送她去车站。卡伦必定是印象极深，否则他绝不会为她把自己那辆轻便马车都套出来。

===Original===
“You know,” I said to her, “I won’t have you play tricks with Callan﻿—not while you’re using my name. It’s very much at your service as far as I’m concerned﻿—but, confound it, if you’re going to injure him I shall have to show you up﻿—to tell him.”

===Chinese===
“你要知道，”我对她说，“我不能容你在卡伦身上玩花招——尤其是在你还用着我姓氏的时候。就我而言，这姓氏尽可听凭你取用——可是，见鬼，你要是打算坑害他，我就只好揭你的底——去告诉他。”

===Original===
“You couldn’t, you know,” she said, perfectly calmly, “you’ve let yourself in for it. He wouldn’t feel pleased with you for letting it go as far as it has. You’d lose your job, and you’re going to live, you know﻿—you’re going to live.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你做不到的，你知道，”她说，平静至极，“是你自己把这摊事揽到身上的。放任它走到这一步，他不会对你存什么好感。你会丢掉差事，而你是要活一场的，你知道——你是要活一场的……”

===Original===
I was taken aback by this veiled threat in the midst of the pleasantry. It wasn’t fair play﻿—not at all fair play. I recovered some of my old alarm, remembered that she really was a dangerous person; that﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这句藏在玩笑当中的隐晦威胁让我吃了一惊。这不算光明磊落——全然不算光明磊落。我重新拾起了几分先前的戒惧，想起她确实是个危险人物；想起……

===Original===
“But I shan’t hurt Callan,” she said, suddenly, “you may make your mind easy.”

===Chinese===
“不过我不会伤害卡伦的，”她突然说，“你尽管放心。”

===Original===
“You really won’t?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“真的不会？”我问。

===Original===
“Really not,” she answered. It relieved me to believe her. I did not want to quarrel with her. You see, she fascinated me, she seemed to act as a stimulant, to set me tingling somehow﻿—and to baffle me.﻿ ﻿… And there was truth in what she said. I had let myself in for it, and I didn’t want to lose Callan’s job by telling him I had made a fool of him.

===Chinese===
“真的不会。”她答道。相信了她，我松了口气。我不想同她闹翻。你要知道，她让我着迷，她仿佛起着兴奋剂的作用，不知怎么叫我浑身发麻——又叫我摸不着头脑……况且她的话也有道理。是我自己揽下了这摊事，我不想因为告诉卡伦说他被我耍了，反倒丢掉他给我谋的这份差事。

===Original===
“I don’t care about anything else,” I said. She smiled.

===Chinese===
“别的事我都不管。”我说。她微微一笑。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
I went up to town bearing the Callan article, and a letter of warm commendation from Callan to Fox. I had been very docile; had accepted emendations; had lavished praise, had been unctuous and yet had contrived to retain the dignified savour of the editorial “we.” Callan himself asked no more.

===Chinese===
我带着那篇写卡伦的稿子上伦敦去，还带着卡伦写给福克斯的一封热忱的举荐信。我一直十分驯顺；接受了删改；不吝赞美之辞，表现得殷勤而油腻，却又设法保住了社论式“我们”的那份庄严余味。连卡伦本人也别无所求了。

===Original===
I was directed to seek Fox out﻿—to find him immediately. The matter was growing urgent. Fox was not at the office﻿—the brand new office that I afterward saw pass through the succeeding stages of businesslike comfort and dusty neglect. I was directed to ask for him at the stage door of the Buckingham.

===Chinese===
人家吩咐我去寻福克斯——而且要立刻找到他。事情渐渐紧迫起来。福克斯不在报馆——那间簇新的报馆，后来我眼看它接连经历各个阶段：先是生意人似的舒适，后是蒙尘的荒怠。人家让我到白金汉剧院（the Buckingham）的后台门去打听他。

===Original===
I waited in the doorkeeper’s glass box at the Buckingham. I was eyed by the suspicious commissionaire with the contempt reserved for resting actors. Resting actors are hungry suppliants as a rule. Call-boys sought Mr. Fox. “Anybody seen Mr. Fox? He’s gone to lunch.”

===Chinese===
我在白金汉剧院守门人的玻璃岗亭里干等。那个满脸狐疑的门警上下打量我，目光里带着专门留给歇业演员的那种轻蔑。歇业的演员照例是饥饿的求告者。催场员们四处找福克斯先生。“谁见着福克斯先生了？他吃午饭去了。”

===Original===
“Mr. Fox is out,” said the commissionaire.

===Chinese===
“福克斯先生出去了。”门警说。

===Original===
I explained that the matter was urgent. More call-boys disappeared through the folding doors. Unenticing personages passed the glass box, casting hostile glances askance at me on my high stool. A message came back.

===Chinese===
我说明事情紧急。又有几个催场员从折叠门里消失了。几个面目不讨喜的人物打玻璃岗亭前经过，朝高凳上的我斜投来含敌意的一瞥。有回话传了回来。

===Original===
“If it’s Mr. Etchingham Granger, he’s to follow Mr. Fox to Mrs. Hartly’s at once.”

===Chinese===
“要是来的是埃钦厄姆·格兰杰先生，就让他即刻去哈特利太太（Mrs. Hartly）府上找福克斯先生。”

===Original===
I followed Mr. Fox to Mrs. Hartly’s﻿—to a little flat in a neighbourhood that I need not specify. The eminent journalist was lunching with the eminent actress. A husband was in attendance﻿—a nonentity with a heavy yellow moustache, who hummed and hawed over his watch.

===Chinese===
我便追到哈特利太太府上去找福克斯先生——一套小公寓，坐落在哪个地界，我无须细表。显赫的新闻记者正与显赫的女演员共进午餐。一位丈夫在旁侍坐——一个蓄着浓重的黄胡子、对着怀表哼哼唧唧的无名之辈。

===Original===
Mr. Fox was full-faced, with a persuasive, peremptory manner. Mrs. Hartly was﻿—well, she was just Mrs. Hartly. You remember how we all fell in love with her figure and her manner, and her voice, and the way she used her hands. She broke her bread with those very hands; spoke to her husband with that very voice, and rose from table with that same graceful management of her limp skirts. She made eyes at me; at her husband; at little Fox, at the man who handed the asparagus﻿—great round grey eyes. She was just the same. The curtain never fell on that eternal dress rehearsal. I don’t wonder the husband was forever looking at his watch.

===Chinese===
福克斯先生脸盘宽阔，气派既能叫人信服，又不由分说。哈特利太太嘛——唉，她就是哈特利太太，仅此而已。你总记得，我们当年是怎样一齐倾倒于她的身段、她的风度、她的嗓音，还有她那双手的姿式的。她恰恰就是用那双手掰的面包；恰恰用那种嗓音对丈夫说话；起身离席时，也正是那样优雅地打点她那柔软下垂的裙裾。她朝我使眼色；朝她丈夫；朝小个子福克斯，朝端芦笋上来的那人——一双又大又圆的灰眼睛。她还是老样子。那场永无止境的服装排演，幕永远不会落下。难怪那位丈夫一分钟也离不开他的表。

===Original===
Mr. Fox was a friend of the house. He dispensed with ceremony, read my manuscript over his Roquefort, and seemed to find it add to the savour.

===Chinese===
福克斯先生是这一家子的熟不拘礼的朋友。他免了客套，就着他的罗克福尔奶酪（Roquefort）读我的稿子，似乎觉得稿子给奶酪添了滋味。

===Original===
“You are going to do me for Mr. Fox,” Mrs. Hartly said, turning her large grey eyes upon me. They were very soft. They seemed to send out waves of intense sympatheticism. I thought of those others that had shot out a razor-edged ray.

===Chinese===
“您要替福克斯先生写我了，”哈特利太太说着，把她那双灰色的大眼睛转向我。那双眼睛十分温柔，仿佛送出一阵阵浓烈的同情之波。我想起了另外那双眼睛——它们射出的是一道剃刀刃似的光。

===Original===
“Why,” I answered, “there was some talk of my doing somebody for the *Hour*.”

===Chinese===
“哦，”我答道，“是有人提过，要我替《时辰报》写一写什么人来着。”

===Original===
Fox put my manuscript under his empty tumbler.

===Chinese===
福克斯把我的稿子压在他的空酒杯底下。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said, sharply. “He will do, I think. H’m, yes. Why, yes.”

===Chinese===
“行，”他干脆地说，“我看他行。唔，行。啊，行。”

===Original===
“You’re a friend of Mr. Callan’s, aren’t you?” Mrs. Hartly asked, “What a dear, nice man he is! You should see him at rehearsals. You know I’m doing his *Boldero*; he’s given me a perfectly lovely part﻿—perfectly lovely. And the trouble he takes. He tries every chair on the stage.”

===Chinese===
“您是卡伦先生的朋友，对不对？”哈特利太太问，“他是个多么亲爱、多么好的人啊！您真该看看他排戏的样子。您知道，我正演着他的《博尔德罗》；他给了我一个妙不可言的角色——妙不可言。他费的那些心思哟。台上的每一把椅子他都要亲自坐过。”

===Original===
“H’m; yes,” Fox interjected, “he likes to have his own way.”

===Chinese===
“唔；是啊，”福克斯插嘴道，“他喜欢凡事自作主张。”

===Original===
“We *all* like that,” the great actress said. She was quoting from her first great part. I thought﻿—but, perhaps, I was mistaken﻿—that all her utterances were quotations from her first great part. Her husband looked at his watch.

===Chinese===
“我们‘人人’都喜欢那样，”这位大女演员说。她这是在引用她第一个伟大角色里的台词。我于是想——不过，也许是我弄错了——她的一切言语，无不是从她那第一个伟大角色里引用来的。她丈夫看了看表。

===Original===
“Are you coming to this confounded flower show?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你到底去不去那个该死的花展？”他问。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, turning her mysterious eyes upon him, “I’ll go and get ready.”

===Chinese===
“去，”她说着，把那双神秘的眼睛转向他，“我去更衣。”

===Original===
She disappeared through an inner door. I expected to hear the pistol-shot and the heavy fall from the next room. I forgot that it was not the end of the fifth act.

===Chinese===
她从一扇内门里消失了。我只等着隔壁房间传来一声枪响、一具身体沉重倒地的声响。我忘了此刻还不是第五幕的结尾。

===Original===
Fox put my manuscript into his breast pocket.

===Chinese===
福克斯把我的稿子放进胸前口袋。

===Original===
“Come along, Granger,” he said to me, “I want to speak to you. You’ll have plenty of opportunity for seeing Mrs. Hartly, I expect. She’s tenth on your list. Good day, Hartly.”

===Chinese===
“走吧，格兰杰，”他对我说，“我有话要同你讲。见哈特利太太的机会，往后多的是，我想。她在你的名单上排第十。再会，哈特利。”

===Original===
Hartly’s hand was wavering between his moustache and his watch pocket.

===Chinese===
哈特利的那只手，在胡子和表袋之间摇摆不定。

===Original===
“Good day,” he said sulkily.

===Chinese===
“再会。”他悻悻地说。

===Original===
“You must come and see me again, Mr. Granger,” Mrs. Hartly said from the door. “Come to the Buckingham and see how we’re getting on with your friend’s play. We must have a good long talk if you’re to get my local colour, as Mr. Fox calls it.”

===Chinese===
“您一定还要来看我，格兰杰先生，”哈特利太太在门口说，“到白金汉剧院来，看看您朋友那出戏我们排得怎么样了。您要抓我那个‘地方色彩’——福克斯先生是这么叫它的——咱们就得好好长谈一回。”

===Original===
> “To gild refined gold; to paint the lily,
 

 To throw a perfume on the violet﻿—”

===Chinese===
> “给炼精的黄金镀金，给百合花敷彩，

 往紫罗兰上抛洒一番香气——”

===Original===
I quoted banally.

===Chinese===
我陈腔滥调地引了这么一句。

===Original===
“That’s it,” she said, with a tender smile. She was fastening a button in her glove. I doubt her recognition of the quotation.

===Chinese===
“正是这话，”她说，带着一个温柔的微笑。她正在扣手套上的扣子。我疑心她压根儿没有听出这句引文来。

===Original===
When we were in our hansom, Fox began:

===Chinese===
等我们坐进双轮马车，福克斯开了腔：

===Original===
“I’m relieved by what I’ve seen of your copy. One didn’t expect this sort of thing from you. You think it a bit below you, don’t you? Oh, I know, I know. You literary people are usually so impracticable; you know what I mean. Callan said you were the man. Callan has his uses; but one has something else to do with one’s paper. I’ve got interests of my own. But you’ll do; it’s all *right*. You don’t mind my being candid, do you, now?” I muttered that I rather liked it.

===Chinese===
“看了你的稿子，我算放心了。原先可没料到你能写出这种东西。你觉得这有点辱没你的身份，是不是？哦，我知道，我知道。你们这班文人通常多么不切实际；你懂我的意思。卡伦说你就是那块料。卡伦自有他的用处；可自己的报纸，总还有别的用场要派。我自己有我自己的利益。不过你行；这就‘对’了。你不介意我有话直说吧，嗯？”我咕哝说，我倒蛮喜欢这样。

===Original===
“Well then,” he went on, “now I see my way.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”他接着说，“现在我心里有数了。”

===Original===
“I’m glad you do,” I murmured. “I wish I did.”

===Chinese===
“您有数我就高兴了，”我喃喃道，“但愿我自己也有个数。”

===Original===
“Oh, that will be all right,” Fox comforted. “I dare say Callan has rather sickened you of the job; particularly if you ain’t used to it. But you won’t find the others as trying. There’s Churchill now, he’s your next. You’ll have to mind him. You’ll find him a decent chap. Not a bit of side on him.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那个不成问题，”福克斯宽慰道，“我猜想是卡伦叫你对这差事倒了胃口；尤其是你还不惯的缘故。不过其余几位你不会觉得那么难缠。就说丘吉尔吧，下一个就轮到他。你得留神对付他。你会发现他是个正派的家伙。一点儿架子也没有。”

===Original===
“What Churchill?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“哪个丘吉尔？”我问。

===Original===
“The Foreign Minister.”
