===Chinese===
“外交大臣。”

===Original===
“The devil,” I said.

===Chinese===
“见鬼。”我说。

===Original===
“Oh, you’ll find him all right,” Fox reassured; “you’re to go down to his place tomorrow. It’s all arranged. Here we are. Hop out.” He suited his own action to his words and ran nimbly up the new terra-cotta steps of the *Hour*’s home. He left me to pay the cabman.

===Chinese===
“哦，你会发现他好相处的，”福克斯给我吃定心丸；“明天你就下乡到他府上去。一切都安排好了。我们到了。下来吧。”他说到做到，一溜轻快地跑上《时辰报》新宅那赤陶色的台阶。他把我撂在那里付车钱。

===Original===
When I rejoined him he was giving directions to an invisible somebody through folding doors.

===Chinese===
等我再赶上他，他正隔着折叠门对一个看不见的什么人发号施令。

===Original===
“Come along,” he said, breathlessly. “Can’t see him,” he added to a little boy, who held a card in his hands. “Tell him to go to Mr. Evans. One’s life isn’t one’s own here,” he went on, when he had reached his own room.

===Chinese===
“来吧，”他上气不接下气地说。“不能见他，”他又对一个小男孩补了一句——那孩子手里捏着一张名片。“叫他去找埃文斯（Evans）先生。在这地方，人连自己的生活都由不得自己，”等进了他自己的房间，他接着说道。

===Original===
It was a palatial apartment furnished in white and gold﻿—Louis Quinze, or something of the sort﻿—with very new decorations after Watteau covering the walls. The process of disfiguration, however, had already begun. A roll desk of the least possible Louis Quinze order stood in one of the tall windows; the carpet was marked by muddy footprints, and a matchboard screen had been run across one end of the room.

===Chinese===
那是一间宫殿式的房间，白底描金的陈设——路易十五式（Louis Quinze），或是诸如此类的路数——墙上覆满崭新的仿华托（Watteau）装饰。然而毁容的工序已经开工。一张怎么也够不上路易十五的卷盖写字台立在一扇高窗前；地毯上印满泥泞的脚印，屋子一头还横拦起一道薄板屏风。

===Original===
“Hullo, Evans,” Fox shouted across it, “just see that man from Grant’s, will you? Heard from the Central News yet?”

===Chinese===
“喂，埃文斯，”福克斯隔着屏风喊道，“去照应一下格兰特商号（Grant’s）来的那位，行不行？中央新闻社（Central News）那边有信儿没有？”

===Original===
He was looking through the papers on the desk.

===Chinese===
他正翻检桌上的报纸。

===Original===
“Not yet, I’ve just rung them up for the fifth time,” the answer came.

===Chinese===
“还没有，我刚给他们挂了第五回电话，”那边回话道。

===Original===
“Keep on at it,” Fox exhorted.

===Chinese===
“接着催。”福克斯叮嘱道。

===Original===
“Here’s Churchill’s letter,” he said to me. “Have an armchair; those blasted things are too uncomfortable for anything. Make yourself comfortable. I’ll be back in a minute.”

===Chinese===
“这是丘吉尔的信，”他对我说。“坐把扶手椅吧；那些遭瘟的玩意儿实在不舒服透了。随意点儿。我去去就回。”

===Original===
I took an armchair and addressed myself to the Foreign Minister’s letter. It expressed bored tolerance of a potential interviewer, but it seemed to please Fox. He ran into the room, snatched up a paper from his desk, and ran out again.

===Chinese===
我拣了把扶手椅，埋头去读外交大臣的信。信对一个可能上门的采访者表示了不胜其烦的容忍，可它似乎很讨福克斯喜欢。他冲进屋来，从桌上抓起一份报纸，又冲了出去。

===Original===
“Read Churchill’s letter?” he asked, in passing. “I’ll tell you all about it in a minute.” I don’t know what he expected me to do with it﻿—kiss the postage stamp, perhaps.

===Chinese===
“丘吉尔的信看了？”他打旁边过时问。“过一分钟我把底细全告诉你。”我不知道他指望我拿它怎么办——也许，是去亲那枚邮票。

===Original===
At the same time, it was pleasant to sit there idle in the midst of the hurry, the breathlessness. I seemed to be at last in contact with real life, with the life that matters. I was somebody, too. Fox treated me with a kind of deference﻿—as if I were a great unknown. His “you literary men” was pleasing. It was the homage that the pretender pays to the legitimate prince; the recognition due to the real thing from the machine-made imitation; the homage of the builder to the architect.

===Chinese===
不过与此同时，在这一片忙乱、这阵上气不接下气的奔忙当中闲坐着，倒也惬意。我仿佛终于沾着了真实的生活——那要紧的生活。我也算个人物了。福克斯待我带着几分敬意——仿佛我是一位无名的伟人。他那声“你们文人”听着很受用。那是僭位者对正统君王的敬意；是机器仿品对真品应致的承认；是营造匠对建筑师的敬意。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” it seemed to say, “we jobbing men run up our rows and rows of houses; build whole towns and fill the papers for years. But when we want something special﻿—something monumental﻿—we have to come to you.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”那话音仿佛在说，“我们这些打零工的，盖起一排又一排的房子；建起整座整座的城，把报纸填满好些年。可一旦要点特别的货色——点纪念碑式的东西——就只好来求您了。”

===Original===
Fox came in again.

===Chinese===
福克斯又进来了。

===Original===
“Very sorry, my dear fellow, find I can’t possibly get a moment for a chat with you. Look here, come and dine with me at the Paragraph round the corner﻿—tonight at six sharp. You’ll go to Churchill’s tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“真对不起，亲爱的伙计，我实在抽不出一刻工夫同你谈谈。这么着，到拐角上的帕拉格拉夫（the Paragraph）来同我共进晚餐——今晚六点整。明天你就去丘吉尔那儿。”

===Original===
The Paragraph Club, where I was to meet Fox, was one of those sporadic establishments that spring up in the neighbourhood of the Strand. It is one of their qualities that they are always just round the corner; another, that their stewards are too familiar; another, that they﻿—in the opinion of the other members﻿—are run too much for the convenience of one in particular.

===Chinese===
我要同福克斯会面的帕拉格拉夫俱乐部（the Paragraph Club），属于那种在斯特兰德（the Strand）一带东一处西一处冒将出来的零星会所。它们的品性之一，是永远“就在拐角上”；其二，是管事们过分亲昵；其三，是照其余会员的意见，它们为着某一个人的便利，经营得过了头。

===Original===
In this case it was Fox who kept the dinner waiting. I sat in the little smoking-room and, from behind a belated morning paper, listened to the conversation of the three or four journalists who represented the members. I felt as a new boy in a new school feels on his first introduction to his fellows.

===Chinese===
这一回，让晚饭久等的正是福克斯。我坐在小吸烟室里，躲在一份过时的晨报后面，听那三四个代表着全体会员的记者交谈。我那感觉，活像新学校里的新生初次被引见给同窗们时的感觉。

===Original===
There was a fossil dramatic critic sleeping in an armchair before the fire. At dinnertime he woke up, remarked:

===Chinese===
壁炉前的扶手椅里，睡着一位化石级的戏剧评论家。到了开饭的时辰他醒过来，评了一句：

===Original===
“You should have seen Fanny Ellsler,” and went to sleep again.

===Chinese===
“你们真该看看范妮·埃尔斯勒（Fanny Ellsler），”说罢又睡了过去。

===Original===
Sprawling on a red velvet couch was a *beau jeune homme*, with the necktie of a Parisian-American student. On a chair beside him sat a personage whom, perhaps because of his plentiful lack of *h*’s, I took for a distinguished foreigner.

===Chinese===
红丝绒长榻上四仰八叉躺着一个翩翩美少年（beau jeune homme），系一条巴黎的美国留学生式的领带。他身旁的椅子上坐着一位人物，我之所以把他认作某位显赫的外国人，多半是因为他满口的 h 音丢得精光。

===Original===
They were talking about a splendid subject for a music-hall dramatic sketch of some sort﻿—afforded by a bus driver, I fancy.

===Chinese===
他们正谈论着一个绝妙的题材，可以编成某种杂耍剧场的小戏——我猜想，素材是公共马车夫提供的。

===Original===
I heard afterward that my Frenchman had been a costermonger and was now half journalist, half financier, and that my art student was an employee of one of the older magazines.

===Chinese===
后来我才听说，我那位法国人原是个街头菜贩，如今半是记者半是金融家；而我那位学艺术的，是一家老牌杂志的雇员。

===Original===
“Dinner’s on the table, gents,” the steward said from the door. He went toward the sleeper by the fire. “I expect Mr. Cunningham will wear that armchair out before he’s done,” he said over his shoulder.

===Chinese===
“晚饭上桌了，诸位先生，”管事的在门口说。他朝壁炉边那位睡着的人走去。“我看坎宁安（Cunningham）先生早晚要把那把扶手椅坐穿了。”他扭头说道。

===Original===
“Poor old chap; he’s got nowhere else to go to,” the magazine employee said.

===Chinese===
“可怜的老家伙；他没别处可去，”那位杂志雇员说。

===Original===
“Why doesn’t he go to the work’ouse,” the journalist financier retorted. “Make a good sketch that, eh?” he continued, reverting to his bus-driver.

===Chinese===
“他干吗不进济贫院去呢，”那位记者兼金融家反唇相讥。“拿这个编出戏来准不错，嗯？”他又接着说，回到他那公共马车夫身上去了。

===Original===
“Jolly!” the magazine employee said, indifferently.

===Chinese===
“妙！”杂志雇员淡淡地说。

===Original===
“Now, then, Mr. Cunningham,” the steward said, touching the sleeper on the shoulder, “dinner’s on the table.”

===Chinese===
“好了，坎宁安先生，”管事的碰了碰睡着的人的肩膀，“晚饭上桌了。”

===Original===
“God bless my soul,” the dramatic critic said, with a start. The steward left the room. The dramatic critic furtively took a set of false teeth out of his waistcoat pocket; wiped them with a bandana handkerchief, and inserted them in his mouth.

===Chinese===
“老天爷保佑。”戏剧评论家一个激灵，说道。管事的出去了。戏剧评论家鬼鬼祟祟地从马甲口袋里掏出一副假牙；用一条印花大手帕擦了擦，安进嘴里。

===Original===
He tottered out of the room.

===Chinese===
他颤颤巍巍地出了屋。

===Original===
I got up and began to inspect the pen-and-ink sketches on the walls.

===Chinese===
我站起身，开始细看墙上那些钢笔素描。

===Original===
The faded paltry caricatures of faded paltry lesser lights that confronted me from flyblown frames on the purple walls almost made me shiver.

===Chinese===
紫红色的墙上，蝇垢斑斑的画框里，一幅幅褪了色的小名家的小漫画迎面对着我，几乎叫我打起寒噤。

===Original===
“There you are, Granger,” said a cheerful voice behind me. “Come and have some dinner.”

===Chinese===
“你在这儿哪，格兰杰，”我背后一个欢快的声音说。“来吃点儿晚饭吧。”

===Original===
I went and had some dinner. It was seasoned by small jokes and little personalities. A Teutonic journalist, a musical critic, I suppose, inquired as to the origin of the meagre pheasant. Fox replied that it had been preserved in the backyard. The dramatic critic mumbled unheard that some piece or other was off the bills of the Adelphi. I grinned vacantly. Afterward, under his breath, Fox put me up to a thing or two regarding the inner meaning of the new daily. Put by him, without any glamour of a moral purpose, the case seemed rather mean. The dingy smoking-room depressed me and the whole thing was, what I had, for so many years, striven to keep out of. Fox hung over my ear, whispering. There were shades of intonation in his sibillating. Some of those “in it,” the voice implied, were not aboveboard; others were, and the tone became deferential, implied that I was to take my tone from itself.

===Chinese===
我便去吃了点儿晚饭。饭桌上调味的是些小小的笑话和零碎的人身小议。一位条顿记者——我想是位音乐评论家——问起那只瘦山鸡的来历。福克斯答说，它是一直在后院里“保护”着的。戏剧评论家咕哝了一句谁的什么戏从阿德尔菲（the Adelphi）的戏单上撤了下去，谁也没有听清。我咧开嘴空洞地笑了笑。饭后，福克斯压低嗓音，给我点破了一两处有关这份新日报内在含义的事。经他这样道来，没了任何道德目的的光晕，这桩事情显得颇为寒碜。昏暗的吸烟室叫我心中抑郁，而这整个儿的东西，正是我多年来极力避而远之的东西。福克斯俯在我耳边窃窃私语。他那咝咝的语声里有着音调的种种层次。话音暗示：那些“局内”诸君，有的行事不上台面；有的则不然——语调随即变得恭敬起来，暗示我该拿这语调本身，作我的语调。

===Original===
“Of course, a man like the Right Honourable C. does it on the straight,﻿ ﻿… quite on the straight,﻿ ﻿… has to have some sort of semiofficial backer.﻿ ﻿… In this case, it’s me,﻿ ﻿… the *Hour*. They’re a bit splitty, the Ministry, I mean.﻿ ﻿… They say Gurnard isn’t playing square﻿ ﻿… they *say* so.” His broad, red face glowed as he bent down to my ear, his little sea-blue eyes twinkled with moisture. He enlightened me cautiously, circumspectly. There was something unpleasant in the business﻿—not exactly in Fox himself, but the kind of thing. I wish he would cease his explanations﻿—I didn’t want to hear them. I have never wanted to know how things are worked; preferring to take the world at its face value. Callan’s revelations had been bearable, because of the farcical pompousness of his manner. But this was different, it had the stamp of truth, perhaps because it was a little dirty. I didn’t want to hear that the Foreign Minister was ever so remotely mixed up in this business. He was only a symbol to me, but he stood for the stability of statesmanship and for the decencies that it is troublesome to have touched.

===Chinese===
“当然啰，像 C 阁下这样的人物，办这事是光明正大的……相当光明正大的……只是总得有个半官方的后台。……在这件事上，那便是我……《时辰报》。内阁嘛，我是说，有点儿闹分裂。……他们说格纳德办事不规矩……他们是‘那么’说的。”他俯下身来凑近我的耳朵，宽阔的红脸放着光，小小的海蓝眼睛湿润地闪烁。他谨慎地、圆通地开导我。这桩生意里有一种叫人不痛快的东西——倒不确切是在福克斯本人身上，而是在这类事情本身。我但愿他别再解释了——我不想听。我从来就不想知道事情是怎样运转的；我宁愿照世界的表面价值来接受它。卡伦的那些内幕还受得了，是因为他那副滑稽的堂皇做派。可这就不同了，它带着真理的印记——也许正因为它有一点儿脏。我不愿听见外交大臣的名字同这桩生意有哪怕最间接的牵扯。他于我不过是一个象征，可他代表着政治家风范的稳固，代表着种种一旦遭人染指便后患无穷的体统。

===Original===
“Of course,” he was proceeding, “the Churchill gang would like to go on playing the standoff to us. But it won’t do, they’ve got to come in or see themselves left. Gurnard has pretty well nobbled their old party press, so they’ve got to begin all over again.”

===Chinese===
“当然，”他继续说道，“丘吉尔那一伙倒想继续对我们端着架子。可是不成，他们要么入伙，要么眼看自己被甩下。格纳德差不多把他们旧党的报界都收买过去了，他们只好从头再来。”

===Original===
That was it﻿—that was precisely it. Churchill ought to have played the standoff to people like us﻿—to have gone on playing it at whatever cost. That was what I demanded of the world as I conceived it. It was so much less troublesome in that way. On the other hand, this was life﻿—I was living now and the cost of living is disillusionment; it was the price I had to pay. Obviously, a Foreign Minister had to have a semiofficial organ, or I supposed so.﻿ ﻿… “Mind you,” Fox whispered on, “I think myself, that it’s a pity he is supporting the Greenland business. The thing’s not *altogether* straight. But it’s going to be made to pay like hell, and there’s the national interest to be considered. If this Government didn’t take it up, some other would﻿—and that would give Gurnard and a lot of others a peg against Churchill and his. We can’t afford to lose any more coaling stations in Greenland or anywhere else. And, mind you, Mr. C. can look after the interests of the niggers a good deal better if he’s a hand in the pie. You see the position, eh?”

===Chinese===
正是这么回事——一点儿不错，正是这么回事。丘吉尔本该对我们这号人端着架子——无论付出什么代价，都本该一直端下去。在我所设想的那个世界里，这就是我对世界的要求。那样可要省却多少麻烦。可话又说回来，这就是生活——如今我是在生活里了，而生活的代价便是幻灭；这是我不得不付的价钱。显然，一位外交大臣总得有一份半官方的机关报——或者说，我是这么设想的。……“你听着，”福克斯继续窃窃私语，“我自己就认为，他去支持格陵兰那桩买卖，实在可惜。那件事办得并不‘完全’正派。可它会给办得大发利市，何况还有国家利益要考虑。这届政府若不接手，别的政府也会接——那就会给格纳德和许多旁的人留下一个攻击丘吉尔一伙的口实。在格陵兰也好，在旁的地方也好，加煤站我们再也丢不起了。还有，你听着，C 先生若能在里头插上一手，倒能把黑鬼们的利益照管得好得多。你明白这局面了吧，嗯？”

===Original===
I wasn’t actually listening to him, but I nodded at proper intervals. I knew that he wanted me to take that line in confidential conversations with fellows seeking copy. I was quite resigned to that. Incidentally, I was overcome by the conviction﻿—perhaps it was no more than a sensation﻿—that that girl was mixed up in this thing, that her shadow was somewhere among the others flickering upon the sheet. I wanted to ask Fox if he knew her. But, then, in that absurd business, I did not even know her name, and the whole story would have sounded a little mad. Just now, it suited me that Fox should have a moderate idea of my sanity. Besides, the thing was out of tone, I idealised her then. One wouldn’t talk about her in a smoking-room full of men telling stories, and one wouldn’t talk about her at all to Fox.

===Chinese===
其实我并没有真在听他，只是按合宜的间隔点头。我知道他要我在同那些觅新闻的家伙密谈时采取这个口径。这我全认了。顺带一说，我当时被一种确信攫住了——也许那不过是一种感觉——那位姑娘就掺和在这桩事情里，她的影子就在幕布上闪动的众影之间的某处。我想问问福克斯认不认识她。可是，在那桩荒唐事里，我连她的名字都不知道，整个故事讲出来会显得有几分疯癫。眼下，让福克斯对我的神智保持一个适度的评价，于我更为相宜。况且，这话头格调不合——我那时把她理想化了。在一间满屋子讲着荤段子的男人的吸烟室里，谁也不会谈起她；而对福克斯，则根本谈不上谈她。

===Original===
The musical critic had been prowling about the room with Fox’s eyes upon him. He edged suddenly nearer, pushed a chair aside, and came toward us.

===Chinese===
那位音乐评论家一直在屋里踱来踱去，福克斯的眼睛盯着他。他忽然挨近过来，推开一把椅子，朝我们走来。

===Original===
“Hullo,” he said, in an ostentatiously genial, after-dinner voice, “what are you two chaps a-talking about?”

===Chinese===
“喂，”他用一种刻意显得亲热的饭后嗓音说，“你们两位家伙在聊什么呀？”

===Original===
“Private matters,” Fox answered, without moving a hair.

===Chinese===
“私事。”福克斯答道，一根眉毛也没动。

===Original===
“Then I suppose I’m in the way?” the other muttered. Fox did not answer.

===Chinese===
“那我想我是碍事了？”那人咕哝道。福克斯没有答话。

===Original===
“Wants a job,” he said, watching the discomfited Teuton’s retreat, “but, as I was saying﻿—oh, it pays both ways.” He paused and fixed his eyes on me. He had been explaining the financial details of the matter, in which the Duc de Mersch and Callan and Mrs. Hartly and all these people clubbed together and started a paper which they hired Fox to run, which was to bring their money back again, which was to scratch their backs, which.﻿ ﻿… It was like the house that Jack built; I wondered who Jack was. That was it, who was Jack? It all hinged upon that.

===Chinese===
“想谋个差事，”他说，眼看着那位讪讪而退的条顿人，“不过，我方才说到哪儿了——哦，这里头两头都来钱。”他顿了顿，两眼盯住我。他方才一直在给我解释这桩事情的银钱细目：德·默施公爵、卡伦、哈特利太太以及这班人物凑起份子，办起一份报纸，雇福克斯来经营；这报纸要替他们把钱再赚回来，要替他们搔背，要……这活脱是童谣里那座《杰克盖的房子》；我纳闷杰克是谁。症结就在这儿——杰克是谁？全盘都系在这上头。

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I said. “It seems rather neat.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”我说，“这安排看着挺妥帖。”

===Original===
“Of course,” Fox wandered on, “you are wondering why the deuce I tell you all this. Fact is, you’d hear it all if I didn’t, and a good deal more that isn’t true besides. But I believe you’re the sort of chap to respect a confidence.”

===Chinese===
“当然，”福克斯信马由缰地说下去，“你在纳闷，我究竟干吗把这些全告诉你。事实是，我不说你也全会听到，还要多听到一大堆不实之词。不过我相信，你是那种懂得尊重别人私托的人。”

===Original===
I didn’t rise to the sentiment. I knew as well as he did that he was bamboozling me, that he was, as he said, only telling me﻿—not the truth, but just what I should hear everywhere. I did not bear him any ill-will; it was part of the game, that. But the question was, who was Jack? It might be Fox himself.﻿ ﻿… There might, after all, be some meaning in the farrago of nonsense that that fantastic girl had let off upon me. Fox really and in a figure of speech such as she allowed herself, might be running a team consisting of the Duc de Mersch and Mr. Churchill. He might really be backing a foreign, philanthropic ruler and State-founder, and a British Foreign Minister, against the rather sinister Chancellor of the Exchequer that Mr. Gurnard undoubtedly was. It might suit him; perhaps he had shares in something or other that depended on the success of the Duc de Mersch’s Greenland Protectorate. I knew well enough, you must remember, that Fox was a big man﻿—one of those big men that remain permanently behind the curtain, perhaps because they have a certain lack of comeliness of one sort or another and don’t look well on the stage itself. And I understood now that if he had abandoned﻿—as he had done﻿—half a dozen enterprises of his own for the sake of the *Hour*, it must be because it was very well worth his while. It was not merely a question of the editorship of a paper; there was something very much bigger in the background. My Dimensionist young lady, again, might have other shares that depended on the Chancellor of the Exchequer’s blocking the way. In that way she might very well talk allegorically of herself as in alliance with Gurnard against Fox and Churchill. I was at sea in that sort of thing﻿—but I understood vaguely that something of the sort was remotely possible.

===Chinese===
我没有接这个话头。我同他一样清楚，他是在哄我；他如他自己所说，告诉我的并非实情，只是我在任何地方都会听到的那些话。我并不因此记恨他；那是这场游戏的一部分。可问题仍旧是：杰克是谁？说不定就是福克斯本人。……那位奇诡的姑娘朝我发过的那一大套胡言乱语，说不定到底还有些意思。福克斯说不定真的——用她容许自己的那种譬喻来说——驾驭着一支由德·默施公爵和丘吉尔先生组成的队伍。他可能真的在给一位外国的、慈善家式的统治者和建国者，以及一位英国外交大臣撑腰，去对抗那位无疑相当阴森的财政大臣格纳德先生。这或许正合他的算盘；也许他在某样或另样东西里持有股份，而那东西的成败，系于德·默施的格陵兰保护领能否成事。你必得记住，我当时就很清楚，福克斯是个大人物——那种永远留在幕后的大人物之一，也许因为他们总缺几分这一样或那一样的体面，自己上不得台面。而我现在明白了：他既然——事实如此——为了《时辰报》抛下了自己手头的半打事业，那必定是因为这于他大有利可图。这绝不仅仅是一份报纸主编位子的问题；背后另有一件大得多的事。再说，我那位第四维人小姐也可能持有别的股份，其成败指望的正是财政大臣的从中拦阻。这么看来，她满可以打比方说，自己与格纳德结盟，同福克斯和丘吉尔作对。这类事情里，我是全然如堕云雾——但我模糊地明白，诸如此类的什么，未尝没有一线遥远的可能。

===Original===
I didn’t feel called upon to back out of it on that account, yet I very decidedly wished that the thing could have been otherwise. For myself, I came into the matter with clean hands﻿—and I was going to keep my hands clean; otherwise, I was at Fox’s disposal.

===Chinese===
我并不因此觉得有退出的必要，然而我又万分希望这件事本可以是另一副样子。至于我自己，我进这件事时两手干干净净——也打算让这双手保持干净；除此之外，我悉听福克斯差遣。

===Original===
“I understand,” I said, the speech marking my decision, “I shall have dealings with a good many of the proprietors﻿—I am the scratcher, in fact, and you don’t want me to make a fool of myself.”

===Chinese===
“我明白，”我说——这句话标出了我的决定，“我要同好些位东家打交道——说到底，我就是那个替人搔背的，而您总不想叫我出乖露丑吧。”

===Original===
“Well,” he answered, gauging me with his blue, gimlet eyes, “it’s just as well to know.”

===Chinese===
“唔，”他答道，用他那双蓝色的锥子眼掂量着我，“心里有数总是好的。”

===Original===
“It’s just as well to know,” I echoed. It *was* just as well to know.

===Chinese===
“心里有数总是好的。”我应和道。心里有数的确总是好的。

## V / 第五章

===Original===
I had gone out into the blackness of the night with a firmer step, with a new assurance. I had had my interview, the thing was definitely settled; the first thing in my life that had ever been definitely settled; and I felt I must tell Lea before I slept. Lea had helped me a good deal in the old days﻿—he had helped everybody, for that matter. You would probably find traces of Lea’s influence in the beginnings of every writer of about my decade; of everybody who ever did anything decent, and of some who never got beyond the stage of burgeoning decently. He had given me the material help that a publisher’s reader could give, until his professional reputation was endangered, and he had given me the more valuable help that so few can give. I had grown ashamed of this one-sided friendship. It was, indeed, partly because of that that I had taken to the wilds﻿—to a hut near a wood, and all the rest of what now seemed youthful foolishness. I had desired to live alone, not to be helped any more, until I could make *some* return. As a natural result I had lost nearly all my friends and found myself standing there as naked as on the day I was born.

===Chinese===
我迈着更坚实的步子走进夜的黑暗里，带着一种新的笃定。我谈过了那场见面，事情确凿地定了下来；这是我一辈子里第一件确凿定下来的事；我觉得非得赶在睡觉前告诉利（Lea）不可。从前利帮过我许多忙——说起来，他谁都帮。与我年纪相仿的那一代每一位作家的发端里，你多半都找得到利的影响的痕迹；凡是做成过什么像样事情的人，还有那些始终没超出像样萌芽阶段的人，身上都有。他给过我一个出版社审稿人所能给的实实惠惠的帮助，直到他自己的职业声誉都搭上了风险；他还给过我那种极少数人才给得出的、更珍贵的帮助。这种单方面的友谊渐渐让我羞愧。说实在的，我当年之所以遁入蛮荒——住进林边一座小屋，连同其余那些如今看来纯属少年意气的蠢事——一部分正是为了这个。我那时渴望独居，不再受人接济，直到我能作出某种回报。自然而然的结果，我几乎失去了所有的朋友，发现自己站在那里，赤条条一如出生那天。

===Original===
All around me stretched an immense town﻿—an immense blackness. People﻿—thousands of people hurried past me, had errands, had aims, had others to talk to, to trifle with. But I had nobody. This immense city, this immense blackness, had no interiors for me. There were house fronts, staring windows, closed doors, but nothing within; no rooms, no hollow places. The houses meant nothing to me, nothing more than the solid earth. Lea remained the only one the thought of whom was not like the reconsideration of an ancient, a musty pair of gloves.

===Chinese===
我的四周铺展着一座浩大的城——一片浩大的黑暗。人们——成千上万的人从我身边匆匆而过，各有事务，各有目标，各有可以交谈、可以调笑的人。而我谁也没有。这座浩大的城，这片浩大的黑暗，于我没有内里。有的是临街的门面、瞪视的窗、紧闭的门，里面却空无一物；没有房间，没有空穴。这些房子对我毫无意义，同坚实的土地没有两样。如今想起谁来，都像重新拾起一副年代久远、霉味扑鼻的手套；唯独利是例外。

===Original===
He lived just anywhere. Being a publisher’s reader, he had to report upon the probable commercial value of the manuscripts that unknown authors sent to his employer, and I suppose he had a settled plan of life, of the sort that brought him within the radius of a given spot at apparently irregular, but probably ordered, intervals. It seemed to be no more than a piece of good luck that let me find him that night in a little room in one of the byways of Bloomsbury. He was sprawling angularly on a cane lounge, surrounded by whole rubbish heaps of manuscript, a grey scrawl in a foam of soiled paper. He peered up at me as I stood in the doorway.

===Chinese===
他住在哪儿从来没个准儿。身为出版社审稿人，他得估量无名作者寄给他雇主的手稿可能有什么商业价值，写成报告；我想他自有其一套固定的生活方案，属于那一类让他以表面毫无规律、实则多半有序的间隔，回到某个地点半径之内的方案。那天夜里我竟在布卢姆斯伯里（Bloomsbury）某条僻巷的一间小屋里找着了他，这看来不过是一桩好运罢了。他有棱有角地摊在一张藤躺椅上，四周是一整堆一整堆的手稿垃圾，整个人像污损纸页的浪沫里一团灰色的潦草涂鸦。我站在门口，他眯着眼朝上打量我。

===Original===
“Hullo!” he said, “what’s brought you here? Have a manuscript?” He waved an abstracted hand round him. “You’ll find a chair somewhere.” A claret bottle stood on the floor beside him. He took it by the neck and passed it to me.

===Chinese===
“喂！”他说，“什么风把你吹来了？带了稿子？”他心不在焉地朝四下摆了摆手。“哪儿有椅子，你自己找一把。”他身旁的地板上立着一只红葡萄酒瓶。他攥住瓶颈，递给了我。

===Original===
He bent his head again and continued his reading. I displaced three bulky folio sheaves of typewritten matter from a chair and seated myself behind him. He continued to read.

===Chinese===
他又低下头去，继续读他的稿子。我从一把椅子上挪开三大摞厚厚的对开本打字稿，在他身后坐了下来。他继续读。

===Original===
“I hadn’t seen these rooms before,” I said, for want of something to say.

===Chinese===
“这几间屋子我以前没见过，”我说，纯粹为了找话说。

===Original===
The room was not so much scantily as arbitrarily furnished. It contained a big mahogany sideboard; a common deal table, an extraordinary kind of folding wash-hand-stand; a deal bookshelf, the cane lounge, and three unrelated chairs. There were three framed Dutch prints on the marble mantelshelf; striped curtains before the windows. A square, cheap looking-glass, with a razor above it, hung between them. And on the floor, on the chairs, on the sideboard, on the unmade bed, the profusion of manuscripts.

===Chinese===
这间屋子的陈设与其说是简省，不如说是随心所欲。屋里有一口红木大餐具柜；一张寻常的松木桌，一种古怪得出奇的折叠盥洗架；一个松木书架，那张藤躺椅，还有三把彼此全不相干的椅子。大理石壁炉台上立着三幅装框的荷兰版画；窗前挂着条纹窗帘。两面窗子之间挂着一方廉价的方镜子，镜子上方是一柄剃刀。而地板上、椅子上、餐具柜上、没铺叠的床上，是漫溢成灾的手稿。

===Original===
He scribbled something on a blue paper and began to roll a cigarette. He took off his glasses, rubbed them, and closed his eyes tightly.

===Chinese===
他在一张蓝纸上潦草涂了几笔，开始卷一支烟。他摘下眼镜，擦了擦，紧紧闭上了眼睛。

===Original===
“Well, and how’s Sussex?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“哎，苏塞克斯（Sussex）那边怎么样？”他问。

===Original===
I felt a sudden attack of what, essentially, was nostalgia. The fact that I was really leaving an old course of life, was actually and finally breaking with it, became vividly apparent. Lea, you see, stood for what was best in the mode of thought that I was casting aside. He stood for the aspiration. The brooding, the moodiness; all the childish qualities, were my own importations. I was a little ashamed to tell him, that﻿—that I was going to live, in fact. Some of the glory of it had gone, as if one of two candles I had been reading by had flickered out. But I told him, after a fashion, that I had got a job at last.

===Chinese===
我感到一阵突如其来的发作，究其实质，是乡愁。我真的要离开一种旧的生活轨道了，真的、最终地要同它决裂了——这个事实蓦地变得历历分明。你瞧，利所代表的，正是我正抛开的那种思想方式之中最好的东西。他代表那份抱负。郁结的沉思、多变的忧郁；那些孩子气的品质，全是我自己附加上去的。要告诉他我——我实际上要开始“生活”了，我有点说不出口。这件事的光彩已经黯淡了几分，仿佛我照着读书的两支蜡烛，熄了一支。但我还是含含糊糊告诉了他，说我终于找到了一份差事。

===Original===
“Oh, I congratulate you,” he said.

===Chinese===
“哦，恭喜恭喜，”他说。

===Original===
“You see,” I began to combat the objections he had not had time to utter, “even for my work it will be a good thing﻿—I wasn’t seeing enough of life to be able to.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你要知道，”我开始迎战他还来不及说出口的那些异议，“就算为了我的写作，这也是件好事——我见到的生活不够多，没法……”

===Original===
“Oh, of course not,” he answered﻿—“it’ll be a good thing. You must have been having a pretty bad time.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那当然不够，”他答道——“这会是一件好事。你这一向的日子一定很不好过。”

===Original===
It struck me as abominably unfair. I hadn’t taken up with the *Hour* because I was tired of having a bad time, but for other reasons: because I had felt my soul being crushed within me.

===Chinese===
我只觉得这不公平得可恶。我投靠《时辰报》，并不是因为苦日子过腻了，而是另有缘故：因为我感到自己的灵魂正在我体内受着碾轧。

===Original===
“You’re mistaken,” I said. And I explained. He answered, “Yes, yes,” but I fancied that he was adding to himself﻿—“They all say that.” I grew more angry. Lea’s opinion formed, to some extent, the background of my life. For many years I had been writing quite as much to satisfy him as to satisfy myself, and his coldness chilled me. He thought that my heart was not in my work, and I did not want Lea to think that of me. I tried to explain as much to him﻿—but it was difficult, and he gave me no help.

===Chinese===
“你弄错了，”我说。我便解释。他答着“是，是”，我却疑心他心里在补一句——“他们都这么说。”我火气更大了。利的见解，在某种程度上，构成了我人生的底色。这么多年里，我的写作既是为满足自己，也同样是为满足他；他的冷淡便叫我心寒。他以为我的心不在写作上，而我不愿意让利这么看我。我试图把这一层向他解释清楚——可是很难，他也一点不帮我。

===Original===
I knew there had been others that he had fostered, only to see them, in the end, drift into the backwash. And now he thought I was going too.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我知道从前也有过他栽培过的别的人，到头来，却只落得眼看他们漂进死水回澜里去。如今他以为我也要往那条路上去了。……

===Original===
“Here,” he said, suddenly breaking away from the subject, “look at that.”

===Chinese===
“喏，”他忽然撇开话头，“看看这个。”

===Original===
He threw a heavy, ribbon-bound mass of matter into my lap, and recommenced writing his report upon its saleability as a book. He was of opinion that it was too delicately good to attract his employer’s class of readers. I began to read it to get rid of my thoughts. The heavy black handwriting of the manuscript sticks in my mind’s eye. It must have been good, but probably not so good as I then thought it﻿—I have entirely forgotten all about it; otherwise, I remember that we argued afterward: I for its publication; he against. I was thinking of the wretched author whose fate hung in the balance. He became a pathetic possibility, hidden in the heart of the white paper that bore pen-markings of a kind too good to be marketable. There was something appalling in Lea’s careless﻿—“Oh, it’s too good!” He was used to it, but as for me, in arguing that man’s case I suddenly became aware that I was pleading my own﻿—pleading the case of my better work. Everything that Lea said of this work, of this man, applied to my work; and to myself. “There’s no market for that sort of thing, no public; this book’s been all round the trade. I’ve had it before. The man will never come to the front. He’ll take to inn-keeping, and that will finish him off.” That’s what he said, and he seemed to be speaking of me. Someone was knocking at the door of the room﻿—tentative knocks of rather flabby knuckles. It was one of those sounds that one does not notice immediately. The man might have been knocking for ten minutes. It happened to be Lea’s employer, the publisher of my first book. He opened the door at last, and came in rather peremptorily. He had the air of having worked himself into a temper﻿—of being intellectually rather afraid of Lea, but of being, for this occasion, determined to assert himself.

===Chinese===
他把沉甸甸一捆用带子束着的稿子扔到我腿上，重新动笔写他关于此书销路的报告。他的意见是，它太精美太好了，吸引不了他雇主那一类读者。我开始读它，好摆脱自己的念头。那部手稿浓黑的笔迹至今黏在我心灵的眼睛上。它必定是好的，但多半没有我当时想的那么好——我已经把它忘得一干二净；不过我还记得，后来我们争执过：我主张出版，他反对。我想着那位命运悬而未决的可怜作者。他成了白纸内里一个惹人怜的可能性，纸上的笔痕好得过了头，好得卖不出去。利那句轻描淡写的“哦，太好了！”里，有一种叫人心惊的东西。他对此惯了；至于我，在为那人的案子辩护时，猛然察觉自己辩护的正是我自己的案子——为我那些更好的作品辩护。利评这部作品、这个人所说的一切，都适用于我的作品；也适用于我自己。“这种东西没有市场，没有公众；这本书在行里已经兜了一圈。以前我经手过这样的。这个人永远出不了头。他会改行去开客栈，那就把他了结了。”他说的就是这些，而他仿佛正是在说我。有人正在敲房门——软塌塌的指节，试探地敲着。那种声音，人不会立刻留意到。那人也许已经敲了十分钟。碰巧是利的雇主，我第一本书的出版商。他终于自己开了门，相当不容分说地走了进来。他一脸把自己鼓捣出了一腔火气的样子——理智上对利颇有几分忌惮，但这一回，打定了主意要拿出威风来。

===Original===
The introduction to myself﻿—I had never met him﻿—which took place after he had hastily brought out half a sentence or so, had the effect of putting him out of his stride, but, after having remotely acknowledged the possibility of my existence, he began again.

===Chinese===
给我引见——我从未见过他——是在他急急巴巴先吐出半句话之后进行的；这一下打乱了他的步点，不过在极敷衍地承认了我存在的可能性之后，他又重新开了腔。

===Original===
The matter was one of some delicacy. I myself should have hesitated to broach it before a third party, even one so negligible as myself. But Mr. Polehampton apparently did not. He had to catch the last post.

===Chinese===
这件事有几分微妙。换了是我，当着第三者在场会踌躇不好开口——哪怕那第三者微末得如同我。可波尔汉普顿（Polehampton）先生显然并不。他还得赶最后一班收邮。

===Original===
Lea, it appeared, had advised him to publish a manuscript by a man called Howden﻿—a moderately known writer.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
原来，利曾劝他出版一部一个叫豪登（Howden）的人写的手稿——一位小有名气的作家……

===Original===
“But I am disturbed to find, Mr. Lea, that is, my daughter tells me that the manuscript is not﻿ ﻿… is not at all the thing.﻿ ﻿… In fact, it’s quite﻿—and﻿—eh﻿ ﻿… I suppose it’s too late to draw back?”

===Chinese===
“可是我心里不安地发现，利先生，就是说，我女儿告诉我，这部稿子不……实在有失体统。……事实上，它非常——而且——呃……我想，如今要抽身已经太晚了吧？”

===Original===
“Oh, it’s altogether too late for *that*,” Lea said, nonchalantly. “Besides, Howden’s theories always sell.”

===Chinese===
“哦，要抽身，那可是彻底太晚了，”利漫不经心地说，“再说，豪登的理论向来卖得动。”

===Original===
“Oh, yes, of course, of course,” Mr. Polehampton interjected, hastily, “but don’t you think now﻿ ﻿… I mean, taking into consideration the damage it may do our reputation﻿ ﻿… that we ought to ask Mr. Howden to accept, say fifty pounds less than.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哦，是，当然，当然，”波尔汉普顿先生急忙插话，“可是您现在不觉得……我是说，考虑到它可能给我们名誉造成的损害……我们是不是应该请豪登先生接受，比方说，少拿五十镑，比起……”

===Original===
“I should think it’s an excellent idea,” Lea said. Mr. Polehampton glanced at him suspiciously, then turned to me.

===Chinese===
“我看这是个绝妙的主意，”利说。波尔汉普顿先生狐疑地瞥了他一眼，然后转向我。

===Original===
“You see,” he began to explain, “one has to be *so* careful about these things.”

===Chinese===
“您要知道，”他开始解释，“这种事情，人可得十二万分地小心。”

===Original===
“Oh, I can quite understand,” I answered. There was something so naive in the man’s point of view that I had felt my heart go out to him. And he had taught me at last how it is that the godly grow fat at the expense of the unrighteous. Mr. Polehampton, however, was not fat. He was even rather thin, and his peaked grey hair, though it was actually well brushed, looked as if it ought not to have been. He had even an anxious expression. People said he speculated in some stock or other, and I should say they were right.

===Chinese===
“哦，我完全理解，”我答道。此人的观点里有一种天真的东西，令我不觉对他心生好感。他还到底教会了我，敬神的人是如何靠着不义之徒养肥自己的。不过波尔汉普顿先生并不肥，甚至相当瘦；他那头灰白的头发尖耸着，虽然其实梳得整整齐齐，看上去却像是本不该梳过似的。他甚至带着一脸忧容。人们说他投机着这样那样的股票，我看他们说对了。

===Original===
“I﻿ ﻿… eh﻿ ﻿… believe I published your first book﻿ ﻿… I lost money by it, but I can assure you that I bear no grudge﻿—almost a hundred pounds. I bear no grudge.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我……呃……想是我出过您的第一本书……我在上面赔了钱，不过我可以向您保证，我毫无怨怼——差不多一百镑。我毫无怨怼。……”

===Original===
The man was an original. He had no idea that I might feel insulted; indeed, he really wanted to be pleasant, and condescending, and forgiving. I didn’t feel insulted. He was too big for his clothes, gave that impression at least, and he wore black kid gloves. Moreover, his eyes never left the cornice of the room. I saw him rather often after that night, but never without his gloves and never with his eyes lowered.

===Chinese===
此人是个怪杰。他压根想不到我可能会觉得受了侮辱；的确，他是真心想显得和气、屈尊又宽厚。我并不觉得受辱。他的身量大得衣服盛不下——至少给人那个印象——我还戴着一双黑色小山羊皮手套。再者，他的眼睛从不离开屋子的檐口线。那晚以后我相当常见他，但从不曾见他脱下手套，也从不曾见他垂下眼睛。

===Original===
“And﻿ ﻿… eh﻿ ﻿…” he asked, “what are you doing now, Mr. Granger?”

===Chinese===
“那么……呃……”他问，“您如今在做什么呢，格兰杰先生？”

===Original===
Lea told him Fox had taken me up; that I was going to go. I suddenly remembered it was said of Fox that everyone he took up did “go.” The fact was obviously patent to Mr. Polehampton. He unbent with remarkable suddenness; it reminded me of the abrupt closing of a stiff umbrella. He became distinctly and crudely cordial﻿—hoped that we should work together again; once more reminded me that he had published my first book (the words had a different savour now), and was enchanted to discover that we were neighbours in Sussex. My cottage was within four miles of his villa, and we were members of the same golf club.

===Chinese===
利告诉他，福克斯把我提携起来了；说我就要“发”了。我猛然想起，人们说起福克斯，都说凡他提携的人个个都“发”。这个事实对波尔汉普顿先生显然一目了然。他陡然放下了架子——快得非同小可；让我想起一把僵硬的伞猛地收拢。他变得明白而露骨地热络起来——希望我们日后再度合作；又一次提醒我他出过我的第一本书（这话如今滋味不同了），还欣喜地发现我们在苏塞克斯是邻居。我的小屋离他的别墅不出四英里，我们还是同一家高尔夫俱乐部的会员。

===Original===
“We must have a game﻿—several games,” he said. He struck me as the sort of man to find a difficulty in getting anyone to play with him.

===Chinese===
“我们得打一局——打上几局，”他说。他给我的印象，是那种很难找到人肯陪他打球的人。

===Original===
After that he went away. As I had said, I did not dislike him﻿—he was pathetic; but his tone of mind, his sudden change of front, unnerved me. It proved so absolutely that I was “going to go,” and I did not want to go﻿—in that sense. The thing is a little difficult to explain, I wanted to take the job because I wanted to have money﻿—for a little time, for a year or so, but if I once began to go, the temptation would be strong to keep on going, and I was by no means sure that I should be able to resist the temptation. So many others had failed. What if I wrote to Fox, and resigned?﻿ ﻿… Lea was deep in a manuscript once more.

===Chinese===
随后他就走了。正如我说过的，我并不讨厌他——他怪可怜的；可他那种心境，那种突然的变脸，让我心头发虚。它再确凿不过地证明，我确实要“发”了，而我并不想发——在那个意义上。这层道理有点难解释：我想接这份差事，是因为我想要钱——只要一小阵子，一年上下——可我一朝开始发，一路发下去的诱惑就会极强，而我全无把握自己抵得住这份诱惑。栽在前头的已经太多了。我要是写信给福克斯，辞掉不干呢？……利又埋首到一份手稿里去了。

===Original===
“Shall I throw it up?” I asked suddenly. I wanted the thing settled.

===Chinese===
“我把它撂下不干，好不好？”我突然问。我要这事有个定局。

===Original===
“Oh, go on with it, by all means go on with it,” Lea answered.

===Chinese===
“哦，干下去，千万千万干下去，”利答道。

===Original===
“And﻿ ﻿… ?” I postulated.

===Chinese===
“再往后……？”我推问道。

===Original===
“Take your chance of the rest,” he supplied; “you’ve had a pretty bad time.”

===Chinese===
“其余的，碰你的运气吧，”他替我接了下去；“你这一向日子够苦的了。”

===Original===
“I suppose,” I reflected, “if I haven’t got the strength of mind to get out of it in time, I’m not up to much.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”我思忖道，“如果我连及时脱身的意志力都没有，那我也就值不得什么。”

===Original===
“There’s that, too,” he commented, “the game may not be worth the candle.” I was silent. “You must take your chance when you get it,” he added.

===Chinese===
“这话也在理，”他评说道，“这局牌也许值不回蜡烛钱。”我沉默了。“机会来了，就得抓住，”他补了一句。

===Original===
He had resumed his reading, but he looked up again when I gave way, as I did after a moment’s thought.

===Chinese===
他已重新读起稿子来，但等我松口时——我想了片刻，果然松了口——他又抬起了眼。

===Original===
“Of course,” he said, “it will probably be all right. You do your best. It’s a good thing﻿ ﻿… might even do you good.”

===Chinese===
“当然，”他说，“多半会顺顺当当的。你尽力去干。这是件好事……说不定还对你有好处。”

===Original===
In that way the thing went through. As I was leaving the room, the idea occurred to me, “By the way, you don’t know anything of a clique: the Dimensionists﻿—*Fourth* Dimensionists?”

===Chinese===
事情就这么定了。临出屋的时候，我忽然起了一个念头：“对了，你可听说过一个小团体：第四维人——地道的‘第四维’人？”

===Original===
“Never heard of them,” he negatived. “What’s their specialty?”

===Chinese===
“从没听说过，”他否定道，“他们是干哪一门的？”

===Original===
“They’re going to inherit the earth,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“他们要承受大地，”我答道。

===Original===
“Oh, I wish them joy,” he closed.

===Chinese===
“哦，我祝他们愉快，”他收梢道。

===Original===
“You don’t happen to be one yourself? I believe it’s a sort of secret society.” He wasn’t listening. I went out quietly.

===Chinese===
“你自己该不会就是其中一个吧？我相信那是个什么秘密团体。”他没在听。我悄悄走了出去。

===Original===
The night effects of that particular neighbourhood have always affected me dismally. That night they upset me, upset me in much the same way, acting on much the same nerves as the valley in which I had walked with that puzzling girl. I remembered that she had said she stood for the future, that she was a symbol of my own decay﻿—the whole silly farrago, in fact. I reasoned with myself﻿—that I was tired, out of trim, and so on, that I was in a fit state to be at the mercy of any nightmare. I plunged into Southampton Row. There was safety in the contact with the crowd, in jostling, in being jostled.

===Chinese===
那一带的夜景，向来叫我丧气。那一晚它们让我心绪大乱——乱的方式、牵动的神经，都同我陪那位难解的姑娘走过的那条山谷几乎一样。我记得她说过，她代表未来，她是我自身衰朽的一个象征——整套愚蠢的大杂烩，实际上。我给自己讲道理——说我累了，状态不佳，等等，说我正处在任凭任何噩梦摆布的恰当状态。我一头扎进南安普敦街（Southampton Row）。同人群接触，推搡着人、挨着人推搡，其中自有一种安全。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
It was Saturday and, as was his custom during the session, the Foreign Secretary had gone for privacy and rest till Monday to a small country house he had within easy reach of town. I went down with a letter from Fox in my pocket, and early in the afternoon found myself talking without any kind of inward disturbance to the Minister’s aunt, a lean, elderly lady, with a keen eye, and credited with a profound knowledge of European politics. She had a rather abrupt manner and a businesslike, brown scheme of coloration. She looked people very straight in the face, bringing to bear all the penetration which, as rumour said, enabled her to take a hidden, but very real part in the shaping of our foreign policy. She seemed to catalogue me, label me, and lay me on the shelf, before I had given my first answer to her first question.

===Chinese===
那是个星期六。议会开会期间，外交大臣照例躲到城外不远的一所小乡宅去，图个清静休养，歇到星期一。我口袋里揣着福克斯的信前去，午后不久，已经毫无心虚地同大臣的姑母攀谈上了——那是位清瘦的老太太，目光锐利，据说对欧洲政治素有极深的了解。她举止相当生硬，一身利落的棕色装束。她看人总是直盯住对方的脸，把传闻中那份洞察力全数使将出来——据说正是凭这份洞察力，她才得以在我国外交政策的形成中扮演一个不露面、却十分切实的角色。我还没来得及回答她的第一个问题，她仿佛已经给我编了目、贴了标签、放上架子了。

===Original===
“You ought to know this part of the country well,” she said. I think she was considering me as a possible canvasser﻿—an infinitesimal thing, but of a kind possibly worth remembrance at the next General Election.

===Chinese===
“这一带地方您该很熟悉吧。”她说。我想她是在把我当作一个可能的竞选拉票人来掂量——微末得很，但没准是下一届大选时值得记上一笔的那类人。

===Original===
“No,” I said, “I’ve never been here before.”

===Chinese===
“不，”我说，“我从来没来过这里。”

===Original===
“Etchingham is only three miles away.”

===Chinese===
“埃钦厄姆（Etchingham）离这儿只有三英里。”

===Original===
It was new to me to be looked upon as worth consideration for my place-name. I realised that Miss Churchill accorded me toleration on its account, that I was regarded as one of the Grangers of Etchingham, who had taken to literature.

===Chinese===
因地名姓氏而被人另眼相看，这于我是桩新鲜事。我明白了，丘吉尔小姐（Miss Churchill）给我的那份容让，全是看在这个份上；我被当成了埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家中投身文学的一个。

===Original===
“I met your aunt yesterday,” Miss Churchill continued. She had met everybody yesterday.

===Chinese===
“我昨天见到了你的姑母。”丘吉尔小姐接着说。她昨天把谁都见过了。

===Original===
“Yes,” I said, non-committally. I wondered what had happened at that meeting. My aunt and I had never been upon terms. She was a great personage in her part of the world, a great dowager landowner, as poor as a mouse, and as respectable as a hen. She was, moreover, a keen politician on the side of Miss Churchill. I, who am neither landowner, nor respectable, nor politician, had never been acknowledged﻿—but I knew that, for the sake of the race, she would have refrained from enlarging on my shortcomings.

===Chinese===
“是啊。”我不置可否地说。我不知道那场会面情形如何。我同老姑母素来不睦。在她那一方天地里，她是个了不起的人物，一位堂堂的孀居大地主，穷得像只老鼠，体面得像只母鸡。而且她还是丘吉尔小姐一党热心的政客。而我呢，既非地主，也谈不上体面，更非政客，从来没有被承认过——可我知道，为了家族的体面，她定会忍住不去细数我的种种短处。

===Original===
“Has she found a companion to suit her yet?” I said, absentmindedly. I was thinking of an old legend of my mother’s. Miss Churchill looked me in between the eyes again. She was preparing to relabel me, I think. I had become a spiteful humourist. Possibly I might be useful for platform malice.

===Chinese===
“她找到合意的女伴了吗？”我心不在焉地问。我正想着我母亲传下的一桩旧传闻。丘吉尔小姐又直直地看进我的双眼之间。我想她是在准备给我重贴标签。我变成了一个刻薄的幽默家。说不定在讲坛上的恶语方面，还能派上用场。

===Original===
“Why, yes,” she said, the faintest of twinkles in her eyes, “she has adopted a niece.”

===Chinese===
“哦，找到了，”她说，眼里闪过一丝极淡的亮光，“她认了一个侄女。”

===Original===
The legend went that, at a hotly contested election in which my aunt had played a prominent part, a rainbow poster had beset the walls. “Who starved her governess?” it had inquired.

===Chinese===
那传闻说的是：在一次角逐激烈的选举里，我姑母出过很大的风头，一面五颜六色的招贴却爬满了墙头。它问的是：“是谁饿死了她的家庭女教师？”

===Original===
My accidental reference to such electioneering details placed me upon an excellent footing with Miss Churchill. I seemed quite unawares to have asserted myself a social equal, a person not to be treated as a casual journalist. I became, in fact, not the representative of the *Hour*﻿—but an Etchingham Granger that competitive forces had compelled to accept a journalistic plum. I began to see the line I was to take throughout my interviewing campaign. On the one hand, I was “one of us,” who had temporarily strayed beyond the pale; on the other, I was to be a sort of great author’s bottle-holder.

===Chinese===
我无意间提起这等竞选掌故，顿然使我在丘吉尔小姐那里有了极好的立足之地。我仿佛在浑然不觉间表明了自己是社交上的平等人，一个不能当随随便便的记者来对待的人。事实上，我不再是《时辰报》的代表——而是一个迫于竞争大势、才不得不领受报界肥差的埃钦厄姆·格兰杰。我开始看清了自己在整个采访战役中要走的路线：一方面，我是“自己人”，不过暂时流落到了圈子之外；另一方面，我又要充当大作家的持瓶人——拳台角落里替拳手拿水瓶的那种角色。

===Original===
A side door, behind Miss Churchill, opened gently. There was something very characteristic in the tentative manner of its coming ajar. It seemed to say: “Why any noisy vigour?” It seemed to be propelled by a contemplative person with many things on his mind. A tall, grey man in the doorway leaned the greater part of his weight on the arm that was stretched down to the handle. He was looking thoughtfully at a letter that he held in his other hand. A face familiar enough in caricatures suddenly grew real to me﻿—more real than the face of one’s nearest friends, yet older than one had any wish to expect. It was as if I had gazed more intently than usual at the face of a man I saw daily, and had found him older and greyer than he had ever seemed before﻿—as if I had begun to realise that the world had moved on.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔小姐身后的一扇侧门轻轻开了。它试探着虚掩出一条缝来，那样子颇见性格。它仿佛在说：“何必闹出什么响动气力？”它仿佛正被一位心事重重的沉思者推着。门口立着一个高大的灰发男人，大半个身子的重量都压在那条直伸到门把的手臂上，正若有所思地看着另一只手里的一封信。一张在漫画上早已看熟的脸，突然对我变得真实起来——比至交好友的脸更真实，却又比人们情愿料想的更见苍老。仿佛我比平日更专注地端详了一张天天见面的人的脸，发现他比以往任何时候看上去都更老、更灰败——仿佛我才开始意识到，世界已经往前走了。

===Original===
He said, languidly﻿—almost protestingly, “What am I to do about the Duc de Mersch?”

===Chinese===
他懒洋洋地——几乎是抗议似的——说：“德·默施公爵的事，叫我怎么办呢？”

===Original===
Miss Churchill turned swiftly, almost apprehensively, toward him. She uttered my name and he gave the slightest of starts of annoyance﻿—a start that meant, “Why wasn’t I warned before?” This irritated me; I knew well enough what were his relations with de Mersch, and the man took me for a little eavesdropper, I suppose. His attitudes were rather grotesque, of the sort that would pass in a person of his eminence. He stuck his eyeglasses on the end of his nose, looked at me shortsightedly, took them off and looked again. He had the air of looking down from an immense height﻿—of needing a telescope.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔小姐霍地转向他，几乎是带着惊惶。她说出了我的名字，他嫌烦地微微一怔——那一怔的意思是：“怎么事先没人提醒我？”这惹恼了我；他同德·默施的种种关系我清楚得很，而这个人，我猜想，竟把我当成了一个小小的偷听者。他的姿态颇为怪诞，是那种在他这样显赫的人物身上便能通行无阻的怪诞。他把单片眼镜架到鼻尖上，近视地看我，摘下来，又看一遍。他一副从极高处往下看的神气——仿佛非得有一架望远镜不可。

===Original===
“Oh, ah﻿ ﻿… Mrs. Granger’s son, I presume.﻿ ﻿… I wasn’t aware.﻿ ﻿…” The hesitation of his manner made me feel as if we never should get anywhere﻿—not for years and years.

===Chinese===
“哦，啊……想必是格兰杰太太（Mrs. Granger）的公子了。……失敬，我先前不晓得。……”

===Original===
“No,” I said, rather brusquely, “I’m only from the *Hour*.”

===Chinese===
“不，”我相当生硬地说，“我不过是《时辰报》来的。”

===Original===
He thought me one of Fox’s messengers then, said that Fox might have written: “Have saved you the trouble, I mean﻿ ﻿… or.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他这下把我当成了福克斯差来的送信人，说福克斯本可以写信来的：“我是说，本可省去您这趟麻烦……或者。……”

===Original===
He had the air of wishing to be amiable, of wishing, even, to please me by proving that he was aware of my identity.

===Chinese===
他一副想表示和气的神气，甚至想通过证明他知道我是谁来讨我的好。

===Original===
“Oh,” I said, a little loftily, “I haven’t any message, I’ve only come to interview you.” An expression of dismay sharpened the lines of his face.

===Chinese===
“哦，”我略带高傲地说，“我没带什么口信，我只是来采访您的。”一阵惊惶的表情使他脸上的线条绷紧了。

===Original===
“To.﻿ ﻿…” he began, “but I’ve never allowed﻿—” He recovered himself sharply, and set the glasses vigorously on his nose; at last he had found the right track. “Oh, I remember now,” he said, “I hadn’t looked at it in that way.”

===Chinese===
“采访……”他开了腔，“可我从来不曾允许——”他猛地定住神，利落地把眼镜架上鼻梁；他总算找对了思路。“哦，我想起来了，”他说，“我先前没从这个角度想过。”

===Original===
The whole thing grated on my self-love and I became, in a contained way, furiously angry. I was impressed with the idea that the man was only a puppet in the hands of Fox and de Mersch, and that lot. And he gave himself these airs of enormous distance. I, at any rate, was clean-handed in the matter; I hadn’t any axe to grind.

===Chinese===
这一切磨伤了我的自尊，我按捺着，怒不可遏。我深深觉得，此人不过是福克斯和德·默施那一伙人手里的傀儡。可他还给自己摆出这副高不可攀的架子。我呢，在这件事上至少两手干净；我没有什么私利可图。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” he said, hastily, “you are to draw my portrait﻿—as Fox put it. He sent me your Jenkins sketch. I read it﻿—it struck a very nice note. And so﻿—.” He sat himself down on a preposterously low chair, his knees on a level with his chin. I muttered that I feared he would find the process a bore.

===Chinese===
“啊，对了，”他连忙说，“你是要给我画一幅肖像——福克斯是这么说的。他把你那篇詹金斯速写寄给了我。我读了——调子拿捏得很不错。那么——。”他在一把低得离谱的椅子上坐下来，膝盖翘得同下巴一般齐。我咕哝说，恐怕他会对这套流程感到腻烦。

===Original===
“Not more for me than for you,” he answered, seriously﻿—“one has to do these things.”

===Chinese===
“我腻烦，也不见得比你更甚，”他一本正经地答道——“人总得做这些事。”

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I echoed, “one has to do these things.” It struck me that he regretted it﻿—regretted it intensely; that he attached a bitter meaning to the words.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我应声道，“人总得做这些事。”我忽然觉得，他是懊悔的——懊悔得很深；他给这句话附着一种辛酸的意思。

===Original===
“And﻿ ﻿… what is the procedure?” he asked, after a pause. “I am new to the sort of thing.” He had the air, I thought, of talking to some respectable tradesman that one calls in only when one is *in extremis*﻿—to a distinguished pawnbroker, a man quite at the top of a tree of inferior timber.

===Chinese===
“那么……程序是怎样的？”停了停，他问道。“这种事我是头一回。”我觉得他那副神气，像在同某个体面的买卖人说话——那种人只有到了弥留之际才请得来——像同一位显赫的当铺老板说话，材质低劣的树上偏偏长得最高的那一棵。

===Original===
“Oh, for the matter of that, so am I,” I answered. “I’m supposed to get your atmosphere, as Callan put it.”

===Chinese===
“哦，说起来，我也是头一回，”我答道，“人家要我来摄取您的气氛——用卡伦的话说。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” he answered, absently, and then, after a pause, “You know Callan?” I was afraid I should fall in his estimation.

===Chinese===
“是吗，”他心不在焉地应道，随后停了停，“你认识卡伦？”我怕自己会在他眼里掉价。

===Original===
“One has to do these things,” I said; “I’ve just been getting his atmosphere.”

===Chinese===
“人总得做这些事，”我说，“我刚去摄取过他的气氛。”

===Original===
He looked again at the letter in his hand, smoothed his necktie and was silent. I realised that I was in the way, but I was still so disturbed that I forgot how to phrase an excuse for a momentary absence.

===Chinese===
他又看了看手里的信，抚了抚领带，沉默了。我意识到自己碍事，可我心绪仍乱得很，一时想不起该怎样措辞，告退片刻。

===Original===
“Perhaps,﻿ ﻿…” I began.

===Chinese===
“也许，……”我开口道。

===Original===
He looked at me attentively.

===Chinese===
他留意地看了看我。

===Original===
“I mean, I think I’m in the way,” I blurted out.

===Chinese===
“我是说，我怕我在这儿碍事。”我脱口而出。

===Original===
“Well,” he answered, “it’s quite a small matter. But, if you are to get my atmosphere, we may as well begin out of doors.” He hesitated, pleased with his witticism; “Unless you’re tired,” he added.

===Chinese===
“呃，”他答道，“这是桩顶小的事。不过，你既要摄取我的气氛，我们不妨到户外去开始。”他顿了顿，为自己的俏皮话感到得意；“除非你累了。”他补了一句。

===Original===
“I will go and get ready,” I said, as if I were a lady with bonnet-strings to tie. I was conducted to my room, where I kicked my heels for a decent interval. When I descended, Mr. Churchill was lounging about the room with his hands in his trouser-pockets and his head hanging limply over his chest. He said, “Ah!” on seeing me, as if he had forgotten my existence. He paused for a long moment, looked meditatively at himself in the glass over the fireplace, and then grew brisk. “Come along,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我去收拾一下。”我说，好像我是位要系帽带的太太。我被领到自己的房间里，像模像样地干等了一阵。我下楼时，丘吉尔先生正在屋里踱来踱去，两手插在裤袋里，脑袋无力地垂在胸前。一见我，他说了声“啊！”仿佛忘了我的存在。他停了好一会儿，对着壁炉上方的镜子若有所思地照了照自己，随即振作起来。“走吧。”他说。

===Original===
We took a longish walk through a lush home-country meadow land. We talked about a number of things, he opening the ball with that infernal Jenkins sketch. I was in the stage at which one is sick of the thing, tired of the bare idea of it﻿—and Mr. Churchill’s laboriously kind phrases made the matter no better.

===Chinese===
我们走了长长的一段路，穿过家乡郡一片肥沃湿润的草场。我们谈了不少事情，他拿那篇可恶的詹金斯速写开了场。我正处在对那东西深恶痛绝的阶段——连那念头本身都腻烦透了——而丘吉尔先生费劲凑出的那些殷勤话，也无补于事。

===Original===
“You know who Jenkins stands for?” I asked. I wanted to get away on the side issues.

===Chinese===
“您知道詹金斯影射的是谁吗？”我问。我想岔到旁枝问题上去。
===Original===
“Oh, I guessed it was —﻿—﻿—” he answered. They said that Mr. Churchill was an enthusiast for the school of painting of which Jenkins was the last exponent. He began to ask questions about him. Did he still paint? Was he even alive?

===Chinese===
“哦，我猜是———”他答道。人们说，丘吉尔先生是那个绘画流派的热心推崇者，而詹金斯正是该派最后的传人。他开始问起他的近况。他还在画吗？他还活着吗？

===Original===
“I once saw several of his pictures,” he reflected. “His work certainly appealed to me﻿ ﻿… yes, it appealed to me. I meant at the time﻿ ﻿… but one forgets; there are so many things.” It seemed to me that the man wished by these detached sentences to convey that he had the weight of a kingdom﻿—of several kingdoms﻿—on his mind; that he could spare no more than a fragment of his thoughts for everyday use.

===Chinese===
“我从前见过他的好几幅画。”他若有所思地说。“他的作品确实合过我的意……是的，合过我的意。我当时还想过……可是人会忘事；事情太多了。”我觉得，此人想用这些断断续续的句子表明：他心头压着一个王国——好几个王国——的分量；他的思虑，只能匀出零星一角来应付日常。

===Original===
“You must take me to see him,” he said, suddenly. “I ought to have something.” I thought of poor white-haired Jenkins, and of his long struggle with adversity. It seemed a little cruel that Churchill should talk in that way without meaning a word of it﻿—as if the words were a polite formality.

===Chinese===
“你务必带我去看他，”他忽然说，“我总该收一件他的东西才是。”我想起可怜的白发詹金斯，想起他同厄运的长年苦斗。丘吉尔说这种话，一个字也不曾当真——仿佛这些话只是一种客套——这未免有点残忍。

===Original===
“Nothing would delight me more,” I answered, and added, “nothing in the world.”

===Chinese===
“那是再叫我高兴不过的了，”我答道，又补了一句，“天底下再没有比这更叫我高兴的了。”

===Original===
He asked me if I had seen such and such a picture, talked of artists, and praised this and that man very fittingly, but with a certain timidity﻿—a timidity that lured me back to my normally overbearing frame of mind. In such matters I was used to hearing my own voice. I could talk a man down, and, with a feeling of the unfitness of things, I talked Churchill down. The position, even then, struck me as gently humorous. It was as if some infinitely small animal were bullying some colossus among the beasts. I was of no account in the world, he had his say among the Olympians. And I talked recklessly, like any little schoolmaster, and he swallowed it.

===Chinese===
他问我见过某幅某幅画没有，谈起艺术家，很得体地夸了这位那位——却带着几分怯意——那怯意诱使我恢复了我平日那副居高临下的心绪。这类事情上，我听惯了自己的声音。我能把人说得哑口无言，于是，怀着一种事事失序之感，我把丘吉尔说得哑口无言。这局面即便在当时也让我觉出一种淡淡的滑稽。仿佛一只无比渺小的动物，正在欺凌兽类中的一个巨人。我在世上微不足道，他却在奥林匹斯诸神间有一席发言权。而我任意妄言，活像个小小的教书先生，他竟一一咽了下去。

===Original===
We reached the broad marketplace of a little, red and grey, home county town; a place of but one street dominated by a great inn-signboard atop of an enormous white post. The effigy of So-and-So of gracious memory swung lazily, creaking, overhead.

===Chinese===
我们走到了一座红灰两色的家乡郡小镇的宽阔集市广场；那地方只有一条街，主宰它的是一根巨大的白柱，柱顶悬着一方大旅店的招牌。顶上，已故贤君某某的画像懒洋洋地摇晃着，吱呀作响。

===Original===
“This is Etchingham,” Churchill said.

===Chinese===
“这就是埃钦厄姆。”丘吉尔说。

===Original===
It was a pleasant commentary on the course of time, this entry into the home of my ancestors. I had been without the pale for so long, that I had never seen the haunt of ancient peace. They had done very little, the Grangers of Etchingham﻿—never anything but live at Etchingham and quarrel at Etchingham and die at Etchingham and be the monstrous important Grangers of Etchingham. My father had had the undesirable touch, not of the genius, but of the Bohemian. The Grangers of Etchingham had cut him adrift and he had swum to sink in other seas. Now I was the last of the Grangers and, as things went, was quite the best known of all of them. They had grown poor in their generation; they bade fair to sink, even as, it seemed, I bade fair to rise, and I had come back to the old places on the arm of one of the great ones of the earth. I wondered what the portentous old woman who ruled alone in Etchingham thought of these times﻿—the portentous old woman who ruled, so they said, the place with a rod of iron; who made herself unbearable to her companions and had to fall back upon an unfortunate niece. I wondered idly who the niece could be; certainly not a Granger of Etchingham, for I was the only one of the breed. One of her own nieces, most probably. Churchill had gone into the post-office, leaving me standing at the foot of the signpost. It was a pleasant summer day, the air very clear, the place very slumbrous. I looked up the street at a pair of great stone gateposts, august, in their way, standing distinctly aloof from the common houses, a little weather-stained, staidly lichened. At the top of each column sat a sculptured wolf﻿—as far as I knew, my own crest. It struck me pleasantly that this must be the entrance of the Manor house.

===Chinese===
就这样走进我祖先的家园，对时光的流转是一番愉快的评注。我被摒在圈子外面太久，从未见过这古老安宁的渊薮。埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家成的事很少——从来只会在埃钦厄姆活着、在埃钦厄姆吵嘴、在埃钦厄姆死去，做埃钦厄姆了不得的、顶顶了不得的格兰杰。我父亲身上有一点要不得的气质——不是天才的，而是波希米亚式的。埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家割断了他的缆，任他游开，沉进别的海里。如今我成了格兰杰家最后一人，而且就眼前情形论，还是他们中间最有名的一个。他们那一辈已经败落；眼看就要沉下去了，恰如——看起来——我眼看要浮上来；而我是挽着世上一位大人物的手臂，重回故地的。我不知道那位独自君临埃钦厄姆的了不得的老太太，对这些时世作何感想——那位据说以铁杖统治此地的了不得的老太太；她把自己的女伴个个逼得无法忍受，只好退而求诸一个倒霉的侄女。我漫不经心地想，那侄女能是谁呢；肯定不是埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家的人，因为这一族只剩我一个了。多半是她自家的侄女罢。丘吉尔进了邮局，把我撂在招牌柱下站着。那是个宜人的夏日，空气非常明净，小镇昏沉欲睡。我朝街那头望去，看见一对巨大的石门柱，自成一种庄严气派，同寻常房舍判然有别，略带风雨剥蚀之痕，稳稳覆着一层地衣。每根柱顶蹲着一头石雕的狼——据我所知，那是我自家的族徽。我愉快地想到：这必定是庄园宅第的大门了。

===Original===
The tall iron gates swung inward, and I saw a girl on a bicycle curve out, at the top of the sunny street. She glided, very clear, small, and defined, against the glowing wall, leaned aslant for the turn, and came shining down toward me. My heart leapt; she brought the whole thing into composition﻿—the whole of that slumbrous, sunny street. The bright sky fell back into place, the red roofs, the blue shadows, the red and blue of the signboard, the blue of the pigeons walking round my feet, the bright red of a postman’s cart. She was gliding toward me, growing and growing into the central figure. She descended and stood close to me.

===Chinese===
高大的铁门向里荡开，我看见一个骑自行车的姑娘，在洒满阳光的街顶上拐了出来。她映着灼灼发亮的墙滑行而来，清晰、渺小、轮廓分明，侧身倾着过弯，闪闪地朝我驶下。我的心一跳；她把整个画面收进了构图——那整条昏沉欲睡、阳光满街的街。明亮的天空各归其位，红色的屋顶，蓝色的阴影，招牌的红与蓝，绕着我脚下踱步的鸽子的蓝，邮车的那一亮红。她朝我滑来，越长越大，长成了画面的中心人物。她下了车，紧挨着我站定。

===Original===
“You?” I said. “What blessed chance brought you here?”

===Chinese===
“是你？”我说。“是哪路天赐的机缘，把你送到了这里？”

===Original===
“Oh, I am your aunt’s companion,” she answered, “her niece, you know.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我是你姑母的女伴，”她答道，“她的侄女，你知道。”

===Original===
“Then you *must* be a cousin,” I said.

===Chinese===
“那你必定是我的堂妹了。”我说。

===Original===
“No; sister,” she corrected, “I assure you it’s sister. Ask anyone﻿—ask your aunt.” I was braced into a state of puzzled buoyancy.

===Chinese===
“不；是姐妹，”她纠正道，“我向你保证，是姐妹。问问谁去——问问你姑母。”我被托进了一种困惑而轻快的心境。

===Original===
“But really, you know,” I said. She was smiling, standing up squarely to me, leaning a little back, swaying her machine with the motion of her body.

===Chinese===
“可是说真的，”我说。她微笑着，端端正正地立在我面前，微微后仰，身子一动，车子也随着晃。

===Original===
“It’s a little ridiculous, isn’t it?” she said.

===Chinese===
“这有点荒唐，是不是？”她说。

===Original===
“Very,” I answered, “but even at that, I don’t see﻿—. And I’m not phenomenally dense.”

===Chinese===
“很有点，”我答道，“可即便这样，我还是看不懂——。再说我也并不蠢得出奇。”

===Original===
“Not phenomenally,” she answered.

===Chinese===
“并不出奇。”她答道。

===Original===
“Considering that I’m not a﻿—not a Dimensionist,” I bantered. “But you have really palmed yourself off on my aunt?”

===Chinese===
“想想我又不是个——不是个第四维人，”我打趣道。“可你真的连我姑母都蒙混过去了？”

===Original===
“Really,” she answered, “she doesn’t know any better. She believes in me immensely. I am such a real Granger, there never was a more typical one. And we shake our heads together over you.” My bewilderment was infinite, but it stopped short of being unpleasant.

===Chinese===
“真的，”她答道，“她压根不疑心。她对我信得不得了。我是这么地道的一个格兰杰，再没有比这更典型的了。我们还一块儿为你摇头叹气呢。”我的惶惑无穷无尽，但还不至于叫人不快。

===Original===
“Might I call on my aunt?” I asked. “It wouldn’t interfere﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我可以去拜见我姑母吗？”我问。“这总不至于妨碍——”

===Original===
“Oh, it wouldn’t *interfere*,” she said, “but we leave for Paris tomorrow. We are very busy. We﻿—that is, my aunt; I am too young and too, too discreet﻿—have a little salon where we hatch plots against half the regimes in Europe. You have no idea how Legitimate we are.”

===Chinese===
“哦，妨碍是不会妨碍的，”她说，“可我们明天就动身去巴黎。我们忙得很。我们——也就是我姑母；我呢，太年轻，又太、太谨慎——有个小沙龙，在那儿孵化反对欧洲半数政权的阴谋。你想象不到我们何等‘正统’（Legitimate）。”

===Original===
“I don’t understand in the least,” I said; “not in the least.”

===Chinese===
“我一点儿也不明白，”我说；“一点儿也不。”

===Original===
“Oh, you must take me literally if you want to understand,” she answered, “and you won’t do that. I tell you plainly that I find my account in unsettled states, and that I am unsettling them. Everywhere. You will see.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你要想明白，就得拿我的话字字当真，”她答道，“可你偏不肯。我明明白白告诉你：我从不安稳的邦国里取利，而我正在搅乱它们。到处都在搅。你往后自会看见。”

===Original===
She spoke with her monstrous dispassionateness, and I felt a shiver pass down my spine, very distinctly. I was thinking what she might do if ever she became in earnest, and if ever I chanced to stand in her way﻿—as her husband, for example.

===Chinese===
她带着那副骇人的无动于衷说这番话，一缕寒意清清楚楚地顺着我的脊梁滑了下去。我在想，她若有朝一日认真起来，而我碰巧挡了她的道——比如说，做了她的丈夫——她会干出什么来。

===Original===
“I wish you would talk sense﻿—for one blessed minute,” I said; “I want to get things a little settled in my mind.”

===Chinese===
“我但愿你能讲一分钟的明白话——就一分钟的工夫，”我说；“我想把事情在心里稍稍理出个头绪。”

===Original===
“Oh, I’ll talk sense,” she said, “by the hour, but you won’t listen. Take your friend, Churchill, now. He’s the man that we’re going to bring down. I mentioned it to you, and so.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哦，明白话我自会讲，”她说，“讲几个钟头都行，可你不肯听。就拿你那位朋友丘吉尔来说吧。他就是我们要扳倒的人。我早对你提过，所以。……”

===Original===
“But this is sheer madness,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“可这纯粹是疯话。”我答道。

===Original===
“Oh, no, it’s a bald statement of fact,” she went on.

===Chinese===
“哦，不，这是不加虚饰的事实陈述。”她继续道。

===Original===
“I don’t see how,” I said, involuntarily.

===Chinese===
“我看不出怎么个扳倒法。”我不由自主地说。

===Original===
“Your article in the *Hour* will help. Every trifle will help,” she said. “Things that you understand and others that you cannot.﻿ ﻿… He is identifying himself with the Duc de Mersch. That looks nothing, but it’s fatal. There will be friendships﻿ ﻿… and desertions.”

===Chinese===
“你那篇登在《时辰报》上的文章就会帮上忙。每件小事都会帮上忙，”她说。“有些事你明白，还有些你不明白。……他正在把自己同德·默施公爵捆在一起。这看上去没什么，却是致命的。将来自会有亲善……也自会有背弃。”

===Original===
“Ah!” I said. I had had an inkling of this, and it made me respect her insight into home politics. She must have been alluding to Gurnard, whom everybody﻿—perhaps from fear﻿—pretended to trust. She looked at me and smiled again. It was still the same smile; she was not radiant today and pensive tomorrow. “Do you know I don’t like to hear that?” I began.

===Chinese===
“啊！”我说。这一点我早有过一丝预感，这使我不禁佩服她对国内政治的洞察。她指的必定是格纳德——那个人人——也许是出于畏惧——都装作信任的人。她看着我，又笑了。还是那个微笑；她不是那种今天容光焕发、明天便愁思脉脉的人。“你可知道，我不爱听这种话。”我开口道。

===Original===
“Oh, there’s irony in it, and pathos, and that sort of thing,” she said, with the remotest chill of mockery in her intonation. “He goes into it clean-handed enough and he only half likes it. But he sees that it’s his last chance. It’s not that he’s worn out﻿—but he feels that his time has come﻿—unless he does something. And so he’s going to do something. You understand?”

===Chinese===
“哦，这里面有反讽，有悲情，还有诸如此类的东西，”她说，语调里透着一缕最淡远的讥诮寒意。“他入局的时候两手够干净，也只是一半情愿。可他看得出，这是他最后的机会。倒不是他已灯枯油尽——而是他觉得自己的时运到了——不干点什么不行。所以他就打算干点什么。你明白吗？”

===Original===
“Not in the least,” I said, light-heartedly.

===Chinese===
“一点儿也不明白。”我轻快地说。

===Original===
“Oh, it’s the System for the Regeneration of the Arctic Regions﻿—the Greenland affair of my friend de Mersch. Churchill is going to make a grand coup with that﻿—to keep himself from slipping down hill, and, of course, it would add immensely to your national prestige. And he only half sees what de Mersch is or *isn’t*.”

===Chinese===
“哦，就是北极地区复兴体系——我朋友德·默施的格陵兰买卖。丘吉尔要拿它干一票漂亮的大手笔——好叫自己不再往下滑，当然喽，这还会给你们的国家声威大大增光。至于德·默施是什么、不是什么，他也只看明白了一半。”

===Original===
“This is all Greek to me,” I muttered rebelliously.

===Chinese===
“这对我简直是天书。”我忿忿地咕哝。

===Original===
“Oh, I know, I know,” she said. “But one has to do these things, and I want you to understand. So Churchill doesn’t like the whole business. But he’s under the shadow. He’s been thinking a good deal lately that his day is over﻿—I’ll prove it to you in a minute﻿—and so﻿—oh, he’s going to make a desperate effort to get in touch with the spirit of the times that he doesn’t like and doesn’t understand. So he lets you get his atmosphere. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道，我知道，”她说。“可人总得做这些事，而我要你明白。是这样：丘吉尔不喜欢这桩买卖。可他处在那片阴影的笼罩之下。他近来常常觉得，他的时代已经完了——我马上就证明给你看——于是——哦，他要不惜一切地去够上那种他不喜欢也不理解的时代精神。所以他才由你去摄取他的气氛。就这么回事。”
===Original===
“Oh, that’s *all*,” I said, ironically.

===Chinese===
“哦，就这么回事。”我讥诮道。

===Original===
“Of course he’d have liked to go on playing the standoff to chaps like you and me,” she mimicked the tone and words of Fox himself.

===Chinese===
“当然喽，他原本满想对像你我这样的家伙继续端着架子。”她把福克斯本人的腔调和措辞都学了个十足。

===Original===
“This is witchcraft,” I said. “How in the world do you know what Fox said to me?”

===Chinese===
“这是妖术，”我说。“你究竟是怎么知道福克斯对我说过什么的？”

===Original===
“Oh, I know,” she said. It seemed to me that she was playing me with all this nonsense﻿—as if she must have known that I had a tenderness for her and were fooling me to the top of her bent. I tried to get my hook in.

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道。”她说。我觉得她是在拿这些胡话耍弄我——仿佛她必定知道我对她存着一份情愫，于是把我作弄了个十足。我想把钩子下下去。

===Original===
“Now look here,” I said, “we must get things settled. You﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我说，”我开了腔，“我们得把事情定一定。你……”

===Original===
She carried the speech off from under my nose.

===Chinese===
她从我鼻子底下把话头截了过去。

===Original===
“Oh, you won’t denounce me,” she said, “not any more than you did before; there are so many reasons. There would be a scene, and you’re afraid of scenes﻿—and our aunt would back *me* up. She’d have to. My money has been reviving the glories of the Grangers. You can see, they’ve been regilding the gate.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你不会揭发我的，”她说，“不会比上回更起劲；理由多得是。一揭发就要闹一场，而你是怕闹场面的——再说，咱们姑母会替我撑腰。她非撑不可。我的钱一直在重振格兰杰家的荣光。你看得见，他们正给大门重新镀金呢。”

===Original===
I looked almost involuntarily at the tall iron gates through which she had passed into my view. It was true enough﻿—some of the scroll work was radiant with new gold.

===Chinese===
我几乎是身不由己地朝那对高大的铁门望去——她正是从那里进入我的视线的。一点不假——卷叶花纹的铁饰上有几处，正焕然闪着新金。

===Original===
“Well,” I said, “I will give you credit for not wishing to﻿—to prey upon my aunt. But still﻿ ﻿…” I was trying to make the thing out. It struck me that she was an American of the kind that subsidizes households like that of Etchingham Manor. Perhaps my aunt had even forced her to take the family name, to save appearances. The old woman was capable of anything, even of providing an obscure nephew with a brilliant sister. And I should not be thanked if I interfered. This skeleton of swift reasoning passed between word and word﻿ ﻿… “You are no sister of mine!” I was continuing my sentence quite amiably.

===Chinese===
“好吧，”我说，“我不妨承认，你并不想——不想盘剥我姑母，这点我信你。可是……”我还在努力把这事想明白。我觉得，她是那种出钱供养埃钦厄姆庄园这类人家的美国人。说不定还是我姑母逼她改用了家里的姓氏，好保全体面。那老太太什么事都干得出来，甚至给默默无闻的侄子平添一位光彩照人的姐妹。而我若去干预，是落不到一声谢的。这副急速推理的骨架，在一字一句的缝隙间掠过……“你算不得我的姐妹！”我把我的句子相当和气地说完了。

===Original===
Her face brightened to greet someone approaching behind me.

===Chinese===
她的脸亮了起来，迎着我身后走来的人。

===Original===
“Did you hear him?” she said. “*Did* you hear him, Mr. Churchill. He casts off﻿—he disowns me. Isn’t he a stern brother? And the quarrel is about nothing.” The impudence﻿—or the presence of mind of it﻿—overwhelmed me.

===Chinese===
“您听见了吗？”她说。“您听见了吗，丘吉尔先生。他要抛弃我——不认我了。他这个做兄长的，好严厉啊。可吵架的缘故呢，什么也不为。”那份厚颜——或者说那份临危不乱——压得我透不过气来。

===Original===
Churchill smiled pleasantly.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔愉快地微笑。

===Original===
“Oh﻿—one always quarrels about nothing,” Churchill answered. He spoke a few words to her; about my aunt; about the way her machine ran﻿—that sort of thing. He behaved toward her as if she were an indulged child, impertinent with licence and welcome enough. He himself looked rather like the shortsighted, but indulgent and very meagre lion that peers at the unicorn across a plum-cake.

===Chinese===
“哦——吵架从来都是为了些无所谓的事。”丘吉尔答道。他同她寒暄了几句；谈我姑母；谈她那辆车骑着怎么样——诸如此类。他待她，就像待一个惯坏了的孩子，放肆无礼，却纵容得十足、欢迎得十足。他自己那样子，倒颇像童谣里那头近视而宽厚、瘦削得很的狮子，隔着一块李子蛋糕窥望独角兽。

===Original===
“So you are going back to Paris,” he said. “Miss Churchill will be sorry. And you are going to continue to﻿—to break up the universe?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你们要回巴黎去了，”他说。“丘吉尔小姐会怅然的。你们还要继续——继续拆散宇宙？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” she answered, “we are going on with that, my aunt would never give it up. She couldn’t, you know.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是的，”她答道，“我们要接着干下去，我姑母决不会放弃的。她放弃不了，你知道。”

===Original===
“You’ll get into trouble,” Churchill said, as if he were talking to a child intent on stealing apples. “And when is our turn coming? You’re going to restore the Stuarts, aren’t you?” It was his idea of badinage, amiable without consequence.

===Chinese===
“你们会惹祸的。”丘吉尔说，像在同一个一门心思要偷苹果的孩子说话。“那几时轮到我们呢？你们打算复辟斯图亚特王朝（the Stuarts），对吧？”这是他心目中的打趣，和善而无后果。

===Original===
“Oh, not quite that,” she answered, “not *quite* that.” It was curious to watch her talking to another man﻿—to a man, not a bagman like Callan. She put aside the face she always showed me and became at once what Churchill took her for﻿—a spoiled child. At times she suggested a certain kind of American, and had that indefinable air of glib acquaintance with the names, and none of the spirit of tradition. One half expected her to utter rhapsodies about donjon-keeps.

===Chinese===
“哦，还不至于那个地步，”她答道，“还不至于。”看她同另一个男人说话是桩奇事——同一个真正的男人，不是卡伦那种挎包行商。她收起了总给我看的那副面孔，顿时变成了丘吉尔眼中的她——一个惯坏的孩子。她时时让人想到某一类美国人，带着那种说不清的神气：对种种名目烂熟于口，对传统的精神却一无所知。谁都会料到，她能对着城堡主楼大发狂想。

===Original===
“Oh, you know,” she said, with a fine affectation of aloofness, “we shall have to be rather hard upon you; we shall crumple you up like﻿—” Churchill had been moving his stick absentmindedly in the dust of the road, he had produced a big *C* *H* *U*. She had erased it with the point of her foot﻿—“like that,” she concluded.

===Chinese===
“哦，您知道的，”她说，精致地装出一副超然物外，“我们将不得不对你们相当不容气；我们会把您揉成一团，像——”丘吉尔一直在路边尘土里心不在焉地划着手杖，已经划出了一个大大的 C、H、U。她用鞋尖把它抹掉了——“就像这样。”她收束道。

===Original===
He laid his head back and laughed almost heartily.

===Chinese===
他把头往后一仰，笑得几乎由衷。

===Original===
“Dear me,” he said, “I had no idea that I was so much in the way of﻿—of yourself and Mrs. Granger.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，”他说，“我全然不知，我竟这样碍了——碍了您本人同格兰杰太太的事。”

===Original===
“Oh, it’s not only that,” she said, with a little smile and a cast of the eye to me. “But you’ve got to make way for the future.”

===Chinese===
“哦，还不止这个呢，”她说，带一点微笑，眼睛朝我这边瞟了一瞟。“可您总得给未来让路。”

===Original===
Churchill’s face changed suddenly. He looked rather old, and grey, and wintry, even a little frail. I understood what she was proving to me, and I rather disliked her for it. It seemed wantonly cruel to remind a man of what he was trying to forget.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔的脸陡然变了。他看上去相当苍老、灰败、萧索，甚至有几分孱弱。我明白她这是在向我证明什么，为此我颇有些不喜欢她。提醒一个人记起他正竭力忘却的事情，未免残忍得无谓。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” he said, with the gentle sadness of quite an old man, “I dare say there is more in that than you think. Even you will have to learn.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”他说，带着十足老年人的温和哀伤，“我想这话里的道理，比你以为的要多。连你，也终归要领教的。”

===Original===
“But not for a long time,” she interrupted audaciously.

===Chinese===
“但那还早得很呢。”她大胆地打断道。

===Original===
“I hope not,” he answered, “I hope not.” She nodded and glided away.

===Chinese===
“我希望不至于，”他答道，“我希望不至于。”她点点头，滑行而去。

===Original===
We resumed the road in silence. Mr. Churchill smiled at his own thoughts once or twice.

===Chinese===
我们默然继续赶路。丘吉尔先生对着自己的心事，独自微笑了一两回。

===Original===
“A most amusing﻿ ﻿…” he said at last. “She does me a great deal of good, a great deal.”

===Chinese===
“非常有趣的一位……”他终于说。“她对我大有好处，大有好处。”

===Original===
I think he meant that she distracted his thoughts.

===Chinese===
我想他的意思是，她让他的心思有了个排遣。

===Original===
“Does she always talk like that?” I asked. He had hardly spoken to me, and I felt as if I were interrupting a reverie﻿—but I wanted to know.

===Chinese===
“她说话一向是这样吗？”我问。他几乎没同我讲过话，我觉得自己像在打断一段遐思——可我想知道。

===Original===
“I should say she did,” he answered; “I should *say* so. But Miss Churchill says that she has a real genius for organization. She used to see a good deal of them, before they went to Paris, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我看是这样，”他答道，“我看是这样。不过丘吉尔小姐说，她有一种组织方面的真正天才。她们去巴黎以前，丘吉尔小姐常同她们来往，你知道。”

===Original===
“What are they doing there?” It was as if I were extracting secrets from a sleepwalker.

===Chinese===
“她们在那儿做些什么呢？”这情形，就像我在从梦游者嘴里套取秘密。

===Original===
“Oh, they have a kind of a meeting place, for all kinds of Legitimist pretenders﻿—French and Spanish, and that sort of thing. I believe Mrs. Granger takes it very seriously.” He looked at me suddenly. “But you ought to know more about it than I do,” he said.

===Chinese===
“哦，她们有个聚会的场所，招待各色正统派（Legitimist）的王位觊觎者——法国的、西班牙的，诸如此类。我相信，格兰杰太太对这事是十分认真的。”他忽然看了看我。“不过，这些你总该比我知道得多。”他说。

===Original===
“Oh, we see very little of each other,” I answered, “you could hardly call us brother and sister.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我们彼此很少见面，”我答道，“简直称不上是兄妹。”

===Original===
“Oh, I see,” he answered. I don’t know what he saw. For myself, I saw nothing.

===Chinese===
“哦，我明白了。”他答道。他明白了什么，我不知道。至于我自己，我什么也没看出来。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
I succeeded in giving Fox what his journal wanted; I got the atmosphere of Churchill and his house, in a way that satisfied the people for whom it was meant. His house was a pleasant enough place, of the sort where they do you well, but not nauseously well. It stood in a tranquil countryside, and stood there modestly. Architecturally speaking, it was gently commonplace; one got used to it and liked it. And Churchill himself, when one had become accustomed to his manner, one liked very well﻿—very well indeed. He had a dainty, dilettante mind, delicately balanced, with strong limitations, a fantastic temperament for a person in his walk of life﻿—but sane, mind you, persistent. After a time, I amused myself with a theory that his heart was not in his work, that circumstance had driven him into the career of politics and ironical fate set him at its head. For myself, I had an intense contempt for the political mind, and it struck me that he had some of the same feeling. He had little personal quaintnesses, too, a deference, a modesty, an open-mindedness.

===Chinese===
我成功地给了福克斯他的报纸想要的东西；我把丘吉尔和他宅邸的气氛抓了出来，写得让这文章为之而写的那班人都感满意。他的宅子是个相当宜人的地方，属于待客周到、却不至周到得叫人发腻的那一类。它坐落在一派宁静的乡野之间，又低调地立在那里。就建筑而论，它温温地平庸；处得久了，人便会喜欢上它。至于丘吉尔本人，一旦习惯了他的做派，也是叫人非常喜欢的——实在非常喜欢。他有一种精致的、业余鉴赏家式的头脑，权衡精微，界限制人，在他那一行的人物里算得一种奇想的气质——然而神志清明，你记住，而且坚忍。过了一阵，我便拿一个理论自娱：说他的心并不在他的事业上，是境遇把他驱进了政治这一行，而讥诮的命运偏又把他放在了这一行的头上。至于我，我对政治这号头脑怀着极度的轻蔑，而且我看他自己也有几分同感。他还有些许个人的古雅小趣致：一分谦敬，一点自谦，还有一种肯纳言的开明。

===Original===
I was with him for the greater part of his weekend holiday; hung, perforce, about him whenever he had any leisure. I suppose he found me tiresome﻿—but one has to do these things. He talked, and I talked; heavens, how we talked! He was almost always deferential, I almost always dogmatic; perhaps because the conversation kept on my own ground. Politics we never touched. I seemed to feel that if I broached them, I should be checked﻿—politely, but very definitely. Perhaps he actually contrived to convey as much to me; perhaps I evolved the idea that if I were to say:

===Chinese===
他的周末假日，有大半我都是同他一起过的；每逢他有一刻闲暇，我便不得不守在他身边。我想他是觉得我烦人——可这类事情总归非做不可。他谈，我也谈；天哪，我们谈得可真凶！他几乎总是谦抑客气，我几乎总是武断独尊；也许因为话头总落在我的本行上。政治我们从不沾边。我仿佛觉得，我一提这个，就会遭到制止——客客气气地，然而斩钉截铁。也许他确实设法把这一层意思传给了我；也许是我自己琢磨出了这么个念头：倘若我开口问——

===Original===
“What do you think about the ‘Greenland System’ ”﻿—he would answer:

===Chinese===
“你对‘格陵兰体系’怎么看？”——他就会答道：

===Original===
“I try not to think about it,” or whatever gently closuring phrase his mind conceived. But I never did so; there were so many other topics.

===Chinese===
“我尽量不去想它。”——或是他心里想出的随便哪句温和地终止辩论的话。可我从来没有问过；旁的话题多得很。

===Original===
He was then writing his *Life of Cromwell* and his mind was very full of his subject. Once he opened his heart, after delicately sounding me for signs of boredom. It happened, by the merest chance﻿—one of those blind chances that inevitably lead in the future﻿—that I, too, was obsessed at that moment by the Lord Oliver. A great many years before, when I was a yearling of tremendous plans, I had set about one of those glorious novels that one plans﻿—a splendid thing with Old Noll as the hero or the heavy father. I had haunted the bookstalls in search of local colour and had wonderfully well invested my half-crowns. Thus a company of seventeenth century tracts, dog-eared, coverless, but very glorious under their dust, accompany me through life. One parts last with those relics of a golden age, and during my late convalescence I had reread many of them, the arbitrary half-remembered phrases suggesting all sorts of scenes﻿—lamplight in squalid streets, trays full of weather-beaten books. So, even then, my mind was full of *Mercurius Rusticus*. Mr. Churchill on Cromwell amused me immensely and even excited me. It was life, this attending at a self-revelation of an impossible temperament. It did me good, as he had said of my pseudo-sister. It was fantastic﻿—as fantastic as herself﻿—and it came out more in his conversation than in the book itself. I had something to do with that, of course. But imagine the treatment accorded to Cromwell by this delicate, negative, obstinately judicial personality. It was the sort of thing one wants to get into a novel. It was a lesson to me﻿—in temperament, in point of view; I went with his mood, tried even to outdo him, in the hope of spurring him to outdo himself. I only mention it because I did it so well that it led to extraordinary consequences.

===Chinese===
他那时正在写他的《克伦威尔传》（Life of Cromwell），满脑子都是他的题目。有一回，他先细细探了探我有没有厌倦的迹象，然后向我敞开了心扉。事情纯属偶然——那种日后注定要引出下文的瞎碰的偶然——我那时恰好也正让奥利弗勋爵（the Lord Oliver）缠住在心头。许多年前，当我还是个满脑子宏大计划的初生之犊时，也曾动手写过一部人人都会计划的那种辉煌小说——一部以老内尔（Old Noll）为主角、或者请他充任严父角色（heavy father）的煌煌之作。我常在书摊间转悠，搜罗地方色彩，把我的半克朗花得妙极。就这样，一批十七世纪的小册子——卷了角，没了封面，尘垢之下却自有堂堂气象——陪我走完此生。人对黄金时代的这类遗物总是最后才舍得撒手；前番养病期间，我又重读了其中许多，那些记得零零星星的断语随意浮现，勾出种种景象——陋巷里的灯光，一盘一盘风霜尽历的旧书。所以，就连那时，我脑子里也满是《乡村墨丘利》（Mercurius Rusticus）。丘吉尔先生谈克伦威尔，让我大感兴趣，甚至为之兴奋。亲历一种绝无可能的气质在那里自我剖白——这就是生活。这于我有益——正如他谈起我那位假姐妹（pseudo-sister）时说过的那样。这情形奇幻——同她本人一样奇幻——而且在他的谈话里比在那本书本身里流露得更多。这里头当然有我的一份。可是你不妨想想，克伦威尔落到这位精致的、消极的、固执地摆着法官面孔的人物手里，会受到怎样的对待。这正是那种人人都想写进小说里去的东西。于我这是一课——气质上的一课，视点上的一课；我顺着他的情绪走，甚至力求盖过他，指望以此激他盖过他自己。我提这件事，只因为我做得太好，好到引出了非同小可的后果。

===Original===
We were walking up and down his lawn, in the twilight, after his Sunday supper. The pale light shone along the gleaming laurels and dwelt upon the soft clouds of orchard blossoms that shimmered above them. It dwelt, too, upon the silver streaks in his dark hair and made his face seem more pallid, and more old. It affected me like some intense piece of irony. It was like hearing a dying man talk of the year after next. I had the sense of the unreality of things strong upon me. Why should nightingale upon nightingale pour out volley upon volley of song for the delight of a politician whose heart was not in his task of keeping back the waters of the deluge, but who grew animated at the idea of damning one of the titans who had let loose the deluge?

===Chinese===
那是主日晚餐之后，我们在暮色里沿着他的草坪踱步。淡白的光照亮一排排莹润的月桂，又停驻在果园繁花的软云上——花云就在月桂上方微微闪烁。那光也停驻在他黑发间的银丝上，使他的脸显得更苍白，也更见老。这情景打动我，像一段入骨的反讽。这就像听一个垂死的人谈后年的事。万事皆不真实的感觉重重压在我心头。夜莺一只接一只，一阵接一阵地倾泻出歌声，去娱悦一位政客——此人对于拦挡洪水的本分并不上心，一想到要去痛斥那放开洪水的提坦（titan）之一，倒来了精神——这却是为了什么？

===Original===
About a week after﻿—or it may have been a fortnight﻿—Churchill wrote to me and asked me to take him to see the Jenkins of my Jenkins story. It was one of those ordeals that one goes through when one has tried to advance one’s friends. Jenkins took the matter amiss, thought it was a display of insulting patronage on the part of officialism. He was reluctant to show his best work, the forgotten masterpieces, the things that had never sold, that hung about on the faded walls and rotted in cellars. He would not be his genial self; he would not talk. Churchill behaved very well﻿—I think he understood.

===Chinese===
大约一个星期之后——或者两个星期也未可知——丘吉尔写信给我，请我带他去见我那“詹金斯故事”里的詹金斯本人。这是一个人想提携朋友之后免不了要经受的一场磨难。詹金斯把这事见怪了，认定这是官府方面摆出来的一种侮辱人的恩赐。他不情愿亮出他最好的作品，那些被遗忘的杰作，那些从来没卖出去过、在褪色的墙上挂着、在地窖里烂着的东西。他不肯拿出他和蔼的本色；也不肯说话。丘吉尔表现得非常好——我想他是懂的。

===Original===
Jenkins thawed before his gentle appreciations. I could see the change operating within him. He began to realise that this incredible visit from a man who ought to be hand and glove with Academicians was something other than a spy’s encroachment. He was old, you must remember, and entirely unsuccessful. He had fought a hard fight and had been worsted. He took his revenge in these suspicions.

===Chinese===
詹金斯在他温文的赏识面前渐渐解冻。我看得出他内里正在起变化。他开始明白，这样一位本该同皇家院士（Academician）们过从甚密的人物居然来访，此中另有缘故，并非奸细的越界窥探。你要记住，他老了，而且彻底地不得志。他苦斗过一场，败下阵来。他便在这些猜疑里讨他的报复。

===Original===
We younger men adored him. He had the ruddy face and the archaic silver hair of the King of Hearts; and a wonderful elaborate politeness that he had inherited from his youth﻿—from the days of Brummell. And, whilst all his belongings were rotting into dust, he retained an extraordinarily youthful and ingenuous habit of mind. It was that, or a little of it, that gave the charm to my Jenkins story.

===Chinese===
我们这些年岁轻些的人都崇拜他。他有一张红润的脸、一头古意的银发，活脱是张红桃K（the King of Hearts）；还有一种精妙而繁缛的礼数，是从他青年时代——从布鲁梅尔（Brummell）的年代——承袭下来的。而且，尽管他的全部家当都在霉烂成尘，他却保有一种出奇年轻、全无城府的心性。我那篇“詹金斯故事”的魅力，正是打这儿来的，或者有一点点是打这儿来的。

===Original===
It was a disagreeable experience. I wished so much that the perennial hopefulness of the man should at last escape deferring and I was afraid that Churchill would chill before Jenkins had time to thaw. But, as I have said, I think Churchill understood. He smiled his kindly, shortsighted smile over canvas after canvas, praised the right thing in each, remembered having seen this and that in such and such a year, and Jenkins thawed.

===Chinese===
