那是一段不痛快的经历。我多么盼望这人那经年不谢的希望这一回终于免于再被延宕，又生怕詹金斯还没来得及解冻，丘吉尔倒先冷了下去。可是，正如我说过的，我想丘吉尔是懂的。他对着一幅又一幅画布露出他和蔼的、近视的微笑，每一幅都夸到了点子上，还记得某年某月见过这张那张，于是詹金斯解冻了。

===Original===
He happened to leave the room﻿—to fetch some studies, to hurry up the tea or for some such reason. Bereft of his presence the place suddenly grew ghostly. It was as if the sun had died in the sky and left us in that nether world where dead, buried pasts live in a grey, shadowless light. Jenkins’ palette glowed from above a medley of stained rags on his open colour table. The rush-bottom of his chair resembled a wind-torn thatch.

===Chinese===
他碰巧离开了屋子——去取几张习作，去催一催茶点，或是为着这一类缘由。他一不在场，这地方顿时鬼气森森起来。仿佛太阳已在天上死去，把我们撇在那个幽冥之境——死了的、葬了的诸般过去，就在一种灰色的、无影的光里活着。詹金斯的调色板从他敞开的颜料台上、从一堆斑驳的烂布上方透出光来。他那把椅子的灯芯草坐面，像一张被风撕烂的茅草顶。

===Original===
“One can draw morals from a life like that,” I said suddenly. I was thinking rather of Jenkins than of the man I was talking to.

===Chinese===
“这样的一辈子，是能引出教训来的，”我忽然说道。我心里想着的倒是詹金斯，不是同我说话的这一位。

===Original===
“Why, yes,” he said, absently, “I suppose there are men who haven’t the knack of getting on.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”他心不在焉地说，“我想总有那么一些人，天生没有发迹的窍门。”

===Original===
“It’s more than a knack,” I said, with unnecessary bitterness. “It’s a temperament.”

===Chinese===
“那不止是窍门，”我说，带着几分不必有的辛酸，“那是气质。”

===Original===
“I think it’s a habit, too. It may be acquired, mayn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“我想这也是种习惯。习惯是养得成的，不是吗？”

===Original===
“No, no,” I fulminated, “it’s precisely because it can’t be acquired that the best men﻿—the men like﻿ ﻿…” I stopped suddenly, impressed by the idea that the thing was out of tone. I had to assert myself more than I liked in talking to Churchill. Otherwise I should have disappeared. A word from him had the weight of three kingdoms and several colonies behind it, and I was forced to get that out of my head by making conversation a mere matter of temperament. In that I was the stronger. If I wanted to say a thing, I said it; but he was hampered by a judicial mind. It seemed, too, that he liked a dictatorial interlocutor, else he would hardly have brought himself into contact with me again. Perhaps it was new to him. My eye fell upon a couple of masks, hanging one on each side of the fireplace. The room was full of a profusion of little casts, thick with dust upon the shoulders, the hair, the eyelids, on every part that projected outward.

===Chinese===
“不，不，”我厉声道，“恰恰因为它养不成，那些最好的人——像……的那班人——”我猛地收住，深感这话不合时宜。同丘吉尔说话，我不得不比自己所愿的更使劲地坚持自己。不然我就要消失了。他的一句话背后，压着三个王国和好几处殖民地的分量；我只好把这一层从脑子里排出去，办法就是把谈话变成纯粹气质上的事。在这一项上我是强者。我想说什么，就说了；他却受着一副法官头脑的牵累。况且他似乎也喜欢一个专断的对手，不然他断不至于再来同我接触。这在他兴许还是桩新鲜事。我的目光落在一对面模上——一面一个，分挂在壁炉两侧。满屋子散置着许多小型石膏像，肩头、头发、眼皮上，凡是向外凸出的部位，都积着厚厚的尘。

===Original===
“By the by,” I said, “that’s a death-mask of Cromwell.”

===Chinese===
“对了，顺便说一句，”我开口道，“那是一具克伦威尔的死后面模。”

===Original===
“Ah!” he answered, “I knew there *was*.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“啊！”他应道，“我本来就知道那儿是有一件的……”

===Original===
He moved very slowly toward it, rather as if he did not wish to bring it within his field of view. He stopped before reaching it and pivotted slowly to face me.

===Chinese===
他朝它走去，走得极慢，倒像并不愿让它进入自己的视野。还没走到，他便停住，缓缓地旋过身来，正对着我。

===Original===
“About my book,” he opened suddenly, “I have so little time.” His briskness dropped into a half complaint, like a faintly suggested avowal of impotence. “I have been at it four years now. It struck me﻿—you seemed to coincide so singularly with my ideas.”

===Chinese===
“关于我的书，”他突然开腔，“我的时间太少了。”他那利落劲儿塌成半声抱怨，像一声影影绰绰的自认无力。“到现在，我在它上头已经四年了。我忽然想到——你同我的想法竟这样奇异地吻合。”

===Original===
His speech came wavering to a close, but he recommenced it apologetically﻿—as if he wished me to help him out.

===Chinese===
他的话音虚虚地收了尾，却又带着歉意重新开头——仿佛要我帮他接下去。

===Original===
“I went to see Smithson the publisher about it, and he said he had no objection.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“为这个，我去见过出版商史密森（Smithson），他说他不反对……”

===Original===
He looked appealingly at me. I kept silence.

===Chinese===
他恳求似地望着我。我保持着沉默。

===Original===
“Of course, it’s not your sort of work. But you might try.﻿ ﻿… You see.﻿ ﻿…” He came to a sustained halt.

===Chinese===
“当然啰，这不是你那一类的工作。可你不妨试一试……你明白……”他陷入一个绵长的停顿。

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” I said, rather coldly, when the silence became embarrassing. “You want me to ‘ghost’ for you?”

===Chinese===
“我不明白，”等到沉默变得难堪了，我相当冷淡地说，“您是要我替您‘捉刀’？”

===Original===
“ ‘Ghost,’ good gracious no,” he said, energetically; “dear me, no!”

===Chinese===
“‘捉刀’？老天爷，哪儿的话，”他起劲地说，“哎呀，不是！”

===Original===
“Then I really don’t understand,” I said.

===Chinese===
“那我就真的不明白了，”我说。

===Original===
“I thought you might see your﻿ ﻿… I wanted you to collaborate with me. Quite publicly, of course, as far as the epithet applies.”

===Chinese===
“我想你也许看得出你的……我是想请你同我合作。当然是完全公开的，就‘合作’这个字眼所能适用的程度而言。”

===Original===
“To collaborate,” I said slowly. “You.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“合作，”我缓缓地说，“您……”

===Original===
I was looking at a miniature of the Farnese Hercules﻿—I wondered what it meant, what club had struck the wheel of my fortune and whirled it into this astounding attitude.

===Chinese===
我正望着一具法尔内塞的赫拉克勒斯（Farnese Hercules）的小型像——我不知道这是什么征兆，是什么巨棒击中了我命运之轮，把它抡成了这么一个骇人的姿势。

===Original===
“Of course you must think about it,” he said.

===Chinese===
“当然，你得考虑考虑，”他说。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” I muttered; “the idea is so new. It’s so little in my line. I don’t know what I should make of it.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，”我喃喃道，“这主意太新了。这同我的路数相去太远。我不知道我会把它做成个什么样子。”

===Original===
I talked at random. There were so many thoughts jostling in my head. It seemed to carry me so much farther from the kind of work I wanted to do. I did not really doubt my ability﻿—one does not. I rather regarded it as work upon a lower plane. And it was a tremendous﻿—an incredibly tremendous﻿—opportunity.

===Chinese===
我信口说着。脑子里挤着太多念头。这件事似乎要把我带得离我想做的那种工作更远。我倒并不真怀疑自己的能力——谁也不怀疑自己。我只是把它看作低一档的工作。然而这是一个了不起的——一个大得难以置信的——机会。

===Original===
“You know pretty well how much I’ve done,” he continued. “I’ve got a good deal of material together and a good deal of the actual writing is done. But there is ever so much still to do. It’s getting beyond me, as I said just now.”

===Chinese===
“我已经做了多少，你是大致清楚的，”他接着说，“材料我搜集起了一大批，实际的写作也做好了许多。可是还有极多的活儿。正像我方才说的，它正在超出我的力气。”

===Original===
I looked at him again, rather incredulously. He stood before me, a thin parallelogram of black with a mosaic of white about the throat. The slight grotesqueness of the man made him almost impossibly real in his abstracted earnestness. He so much meant what he said that he ignored what his hands were doing, or his body or his head. He had taken a very small, very dusty book out of a little shelf beside him, and was absently turning over the rusty leaves, while he talked with his head bent over it. What was I to him, or he to me?

===Chinese===
我又看了他一眼，颇有些不敢相信。他站在我面前，一条瘦削的黑色平行四边形，喉头镶着一方马赛克似的白。这人身上那点轻微的怪诞，使他在心不在焉的恳切里真实得几乎不可能。他的话如此出于本心，以致全不理会自己的双手在做什么，身子、脑袋在做什么。他从身旁的小书架上抽出一本极小、积尘极厚的书，一边低头对着它讲话，一边漫不经心地翻那些锈色的书页。我于他算得了什么，他又于我算得了什么？

===Original===
“I could give my Saturday afternoons to it,” he was saying, “whenever you could come down.”

===Chinese===
“我的星期六下午都可以给它，”他正说着，“只要你哪天下得乡来。”

===Original===
“It’s immensely kind of you,” I began.

===Chinese===
“您真是太好心了，”我开口道。

===Original===
“Not at all, not at all,” he waived. “I’ve set my heart on doing it and, unless you help me, I don’t suppose I ever shall get it done.”

===Chinese===
“哪里哪里，谈不上，”他挥手谢道，“我是铁了心要做成它，你要是不帮我，我看我永远也做不成。”

===Original===
“But there are hundreds of others,” I said.

===Chinese===
“可是旁人有成百上千呢，”我说。

===Original===
“There may be,” he said, “there may be. But I have not come across them.”

===Chinese===
“也许有，”他说，“也许有。可我还没有遇到过。”

===Original===
I was beset by a sudden emotion of blind candour.

===Chinese===
一阵盲目坦率的情绪突然攫住了我。

===Original===
“Oh, nonsense, nonsense,” I said. “Don’t you see that you are offering me the chance of a lifetime?”

===Chinese===
“哦，胡说，胡说，”我说，“您还不明白吗？您这是把一生难逢的机会捧到我面前呀。”

===Original===
Churchill laughed.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔笑了。

===Original===
“After all, one cannot refuse to take what offers,” he said. “Besides, your right man to do the work might not suit me as a collaborator.”

===Chinese===
“说到底，送到眼前的东西，谁也没法拒绝，”他说，“再说，你那位干这活儿的正牌人选，真要合起来做搭档，倒未必合我的意。”

===Original===
“It’s very tempting,” I said.

===Chinese===
“这实在诱人，”我说。

===Original===
“Why, then, succumb,” he smiled.

===Chinese===
“那还等什么，就范吧，”他微笑道。

===Original===
I could not find arguments against him, and I succumbed as Jenkins reentered the room.

===Chinese===
我找不出话来驳他，于是我就范了——恰在此时，詹金斯重新走进屋来。

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===
After that I began to live, as one lives; and for forty-nine weeks. I know it was forty-nine, because I got fifty-two atmospheres in all; Callan’s and Churchill’s, and those forty-nine and the last one that finished the job and the year of it. It was amusing work in its way; people mostly preferred to have their atmospheres taken at their country houses﻿—it showed that they had them, I suppose. Thus I spent a couple of days out of every week in agreeable resorts, and people were very nice to me﻿—it was part of the game.

===Chinese===
此后我便开始生活了——像常人那样生活；就这样生活了四十九个星期。我知道是四十九个，因为我一共写了五十二篇“气氛”：卡伦的一篇，丘吉尔的一篇，那四十九篇，外加把这件差事连同那一年一并了结的最后一篇。这活计自有它的趣味；人们多半宁愿让人到自家乡间宅邸去摄取“气氛”——我想，这说明他们有气氛可摄。于是每星期我总有两天消磨在叫人心旷神怡的去处，人们待我又非常好——这是这场游戏的一部分。

===Original===
So I had a pretty good time for a year and enjoyed it, probably because I had had a pretty bad one for several years. I filled in the rest of my weeks by helping Fox and collaborating with Mr. Churchill and adoring Mrs. Hartly at odd moments. I used to hang about the office of the *Hour* on the chance of snapping up a blank three lines fit for a subtle puff of her. Sometimes they were too hurried to be subtle, and then Mrs. Hartly was really pleased.

===Chinese===
就这样，我过了一年相当好的日子，而且过得津津有味——多半因为此前一连几年，我过得实在太坏。一星期里剩下的日子，我便拿去帮福克斯的忙，同丘吉尔先生合作，再趁零星片刻崇拜崇拜哈特利太太。我惯常在《时辰报》馆里泡着，指望逮住三行空白，好给她来上一段不着痕迹的捧场。有时那三行来得太仓促，来不及不着痕迹——那种时候，哈特利太太才真叫欢喜。

===Original===
I never understood her in the least, and I very much doubt whether she ever understood a word I said. I imagine that I must have talked to her about her art or her mission﻿—things obviously as strange to her as to the excellent Hartly himself. I suppose she hadn’t any art; I am certain she hadn’t any mission, except to be adored. She walked about the stage and one adored her, just as she sat about her flat and was adored, and there the matter ended.

===Chinese===
我从来一点儿也不了解她，而且我很怀疑，她可曾听懂过我说的一个字。我想，我对她谈的必定是她的艺术、她的使命——这些玩意儿于她之陌生，显然不亚于于那位出色的哈特利本人。我想她并没有什么艺术；我敢肯定她并没有什么使命——除了受人崇拜。她在台上走来走去，人便崇拜她；正如她在自家公寓里东坐坐、西坐坐，受人崇拜；事情也就到此为止。

===Original===
As for Fox, I seemed to suit him﻿—I don’t in the least know why. No doubt he knew me better than I knew myself. He used to get hold of me whilst I was hanging about the office on the chance of engaging space for Mrs. Hartly, and he used to utilise me for the ignoblest things. I saw men for him, scribbled notes for him, abused people through the telephone, and wrote articles. Of course, there were the pickings.

===Chinese===
至于福克斯，我似乎很中他的意——究竟为什么，我丝毫不知道。毫无疑问，他比我更了解我自己。趁我在报馆里转悠、指望为哈特利太太谋几行版面的当儿，他常一把抓住我，把我派上最不体面的用场。我替他会客，替他涂便条，在电话里替他骂人，还写文章。当然，外快是有一些的。

===Original===
I never understood Fox﻿—not in the least, not more than I understood Mrs. Hartly. He had the mannerisms of the most incredible vulgarian and had, apparently, the point of view of a pig. But there was something else that obscured all that, that forced one to call him a *wonderful* man. Everyone called him that. He used to say that he knew what he wanted and that he got it, and that was true, too. I didn’t in the least want to do his odd jobs, even for the ensuing pickings, and I didn’t want to be hail-fellow with him. But I did them and I was, without even realising that it was distasteful to me. It was probably the same with everybody else.

===Chinese===
我从来不懂福克斯——一点儿也不懂，不比我多懂哈特利太太多少。他一身习气，是最不可思议的俗物的习气；观点呢，看样子同猪的一模一样。可是另有一种东西把这一切都遮掩了过去，逼得人只好称他一声“奇才”。人人都这么称他。他常说，他知道自己要什么，而且都弄到了手——这也是实话。我丝毫不想替他办那些杂差，哪怕有随之而来的外快，也不想同他称兄道弟。可我照办了，也照交了，甚至没有意识到这于我是件苦事。别的人多半也是如此。

===Original===
I used to have an idea that I was going to reform him; that one day I should make him convert the *Hour* into an asylum for writers of merit. He used to let me have my own way sometimes﻿—just often enough to keep my conscience from inconveniencing me. He let me present Lea with an occasional column and a half; and once he promised me that one day he would allow me to get the atmosphere of Arthur Edwards, the novelist.

===Chinese===
我从前老抱着一个主意，说要改造他；说有朝一日，我要劝他把《时辰报》改造成有真才的作家们的收容所。他有时也由我自行其是——次数恰恰够让我的良心不来找我的麻烦。他容我偶尔给利捧上一栏半的版面；有一回他还答应我，说总有一天，会让我去摄取小说家阿瑟·爱德华兹（Arthur Edwards）的气氛。

===Original===
Then there was Churchill and the *Life of Cromwell* that progressed slowly. The experiment succeeded well enough, as I grew less domineering and he less embarrassed. Toward the end I seemed to have become a familiar inmate of his house. I used to go down with him on Saturday afternoons and we talked things over in the train. It was, to an idler like myself, wonderful the way that essential idler’s days were cut out and fitted in like the squares of a child’s puzzle; little passages of work of one kind fitting into quite unrelated passages of something else. He did it well, too, without the remotest semblance of hurry.

===Chinese===
再有便是丘吉尔，和他那部进展缓慢的《克伦威尔传》。这项试验成效颇佳：我渐渐少了些专断，他渐渐少了些窘迫。到后来，我俨然成了他家中一名熟客。星期六下午我常随他下乡，我们在火车上把各样事情商量停当。在我这样一个闲人看来，那位地地道道的闲人，一天又一天竟裁成一小块一小块，像儿童拼图似的拼合无间，真是妙极了：这一种工作的小小段落，嵌进同它毫不相干的另一种事情的段落里去。而且他办得漂亮，连最远的一点匆忙的影子都没有。

===Original===
I suppose that actually the motive power was his aunt. People used to say so, but it did not appear on the surface to anyone in close contact with the man; or it appeared only in very small things. We used to work in a tall, dark, pleasant room, book-lined, and giving on to a lawn that was always an asylum for furtive thrushes. Miss Churchill, as a rule, sat half forgotten near the window, with the light falling over her shoulder. She was always very absorbed in papers; seemed to be spending laborious days in answering letters, in evolving reports. Occasionally she addressed a question to her nephew, occasionally received guests that came informally but could not be refused admittance. Once it was a semi-royal personage, once the Duc de Mersch, my reputed employer.

===Chinese===
我想，实际的动力其实是他的姑母。人们一向这么说；可是凡是同这人接近的人，从表面上看不出这一点——或者说，只在极小的事情上露出来。我们惯常在一间高爽、幽暗、舒适的屋子里工作，四壁都是书，窗子开向一片草坪——那草坪永远是那些鬼鬼祟祟的画眉的庇护所。丘吉尔小姐照例坐在窗畔，半被人遗忘，光线从她肩头泻落。她总是深深埋头于纸堆；仿佛把一个个辛劳的日子，都花在回复信函、炮制报告上。偶尔她向侄子问一句话；偶尔接待几位不拘形迹而来、却又无法拒之门外的客人。有一回来的是位半属皇族的人物，有一回是德·默施公爵——我那位名义上的雇主。

===Original===
The latter, I remember, was announced when Churchill and I were finally finishing our account of the tremendous passing of the Protector. In that silent room I had a vivid sense of the vast noise of the storm in that twilight of the crowning mercy. I seemed to see the candles a-flicker in the eddies of air forced into the gloomy room; the great bed and the portentous uncouth form that struggled in the shadows of the hangings. Miss Churchill looked up from the card that had been placed in her hands.

===Chinese===
后者来的那一次我记得清楚：丘吉尔和我正在给护国公惊心动魄的弃世写下最后的几笔。在那间静室里，我竟鲜明地感到，那至高恩典的暮色里，风暴正发出漫天的喧嚣。我仿佛看见烛火在灌进幽暗屋子的气流涡旋里摇曳；看见那张大床，和床帐阴影里挣扎着的那具森然笨拙的身形。丘吉尔小姐从递到她手里的名帖上抬起眼来。

===Original===
“Edward,” she said, “the Duc de Mersch.”

===Chinese===
“爱德华，”她说，“德·默施公爵。”

===Original===
Churchill rose irritably from his low seat. “Confound him,” he said, “I won’t see him.”

===Chinese===
丘吉尔从他那矮座上烦躁地站了起来。“真见鬼，”他说，“我不见他。”

===Original===
“You can’t help it, I think,” his aunt said, reflectively; “you will have to settle it sooner or later.”

===Chinese===
“我看你由不得自己，”他的姑母若有所思地说，“这事你迟早总要了结。”

===Original===
I know pretty well what it was they had to settle﻿—the Greenland affair that had hung in the air so long. I knew it from hearsay, from Fox, vaguely enough. Mr. Gurnard was said to recommend it for financial reasons, the Duc to be eager, Churchill to hang back unaccountably. I never had much head for details of this sort, but people used to explain them to me﻿—to explain the reasons for de Mersch’s eagerness. They were rather shabby, rather incredible reasons, that sounded too reasonable to be true. He wanted the money for his railways﻿—wanted it very badly. He was vastly in want of money, he was this, that, and the other in certain international-philanthropic concerns, and had a finger in this, that, and the other pie. There was an “All Round the World Cable Company” that united hearts and hands, and a “Pan-European Railway, Exploration, and Civilisation Company” that let in light in dark places, and an “International Housing of the Poor Company,” as well as a number of others. Somewhere at the bottom of these seemingly bottomless concerns, the Duc de Mersch was said to be moving, and the *Hour* certainly contained periodically complimentary allusions to their higher philanthropy and dividend-earning prospects. But that was as much as I knew. The same people﻿—people one met in smoking-rooms﻿—said that the Trans-Greenland Railway was the last card of de Mersch. British investors wouldn’t trust the Duc without some sort of guarantee from the British Government, and no other investor would trust him on any terms. England was to guarantee something or other﻿—the interest for a number of years, I suppose. I didn’t believe them, of course﻿—one makes it a practice to believe nothing of the sort. But I recognised that the evening was momentous to somebody﻿—that Mr. Gurnard and the Duc de Mersch and Churchill were to discuss something and that I was remotely interested because the *Hour* employed me.

===Chinese===
他们得解决的究竟是什么，我知道得相当清楚——就是那桩悬了许久的格陵兰事体。我是从传闻里、从福克斯那里知道的，模模糊糊。据说格纳德先生出于财政上的理由而赞成它；公爵急切；丘吉尔却说不清缘由地迟迟不前。这类细节我向来不大开窍，可是人们总爱向我解释——解释德·默施为什么急切。那都是些相当寒碜、相当难信的理由，听着又太合情理，反而不像真的。他需要钱来修他的铁路——要得非常之急。他极其缺钱；他在这样那样的国际慈善事业里挂着这个、那个的头衔，在这块饼、那块饼里都插着一根指头。有一个“环球电报公司”（All Round the World Cable Company），沟通着四海的人心；有一个“泛欧铁路、探险与文明公司”（Pan-European Railway, Exploration, and Civilisation Company），往黑暗之地放进光明；有一个“国际贫民住房公司”（International Housing of the Poor Company），此外还有另外若干家。在这些看似无底的事业底下的什么地方，据说正是德·默施公爵在活动；《时辰报》也确实定期登载恭维话，称道它们高尚的慈善与生利的前景。可是我知道的也就到此为止。还是那班人——吸烟室里遇到的那班人——说，横贯格陵兰铁路（Trans-Greenland Railway）是德·默施的最后一张牌。没有英国政府的某种担保，英国投资人信不过公爵；至于别的投资人，无论什么条件都信不过他。英国要担保个什么名目——若干年的利息吧，我想。他们的话我当然不信——对这一类话一概不信，向来是人的操守。可是我承认，这个晚上对某些人是重大的——格纳德先生、德·默施公爵和丘吉尔要商议一件事，而我因为《时辰报》雇着我，也远远地沾着一点边。

===Original===
Churchill continued to pace up and down.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔继续踱来踱去。

===Original===
“Gurnard dines here tonight,” his aunt said.

===Chinese===
“格纳德今晚在这儿用饭。”他的姑母说。

===Original===
“Oh, I see.” His hands played with some coins in his trouser-pockets. “I see,” he said again, “they’ve﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哦，我明白了。”他的双手在裤袋里摆弄着几枚硬币。“我明白了，”他又说了一遍，“他们已经……”

===Original===
The occasion impressed me. I remember very well the manner of both nephew and aunt. They seemed to be suddenly called to come to a decision that was no easy one, that they had wished to relegate to an indefinite future.

===Chinese===
这个场合给了我印象。姑侄两人的神态，我记得清清楚楚。他们像是突然被召来作一个决定——一个并不轻松的决定，一个他们本来巴望推给无限期将来的决定。

===Original===
She left Churchill pacing nervously up and down.

===Chinese===
她听任丘吉尔焦躁地踱来踱去。

===Original===
“I could go on with something else, if you like,” I said.

===Chinese===
“要是您愿意，我可以先接着做点别的。”我说。

===Original===
“But I don’t like,” he said, energetically; “I’d much rather not see the man. You know the sort of person he is.”

===Chinese===
“我才不愿意呢，”他斩截地说，“我宁可不见这个人。你知道他是哪一路人。”

===Original===
“Why, no,” I answered, “I never studied the *Almanac de Gotha*.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，还真不知道，”我答道，“我从来不研究《哥达年鉴》（Almanac de Gotha）。”

===Original===
“Oh, I forgot,” he said. He seemed vexed with himself.

===Chinese===
“哦，我倒忘了。”他说。他像是对自己有些着恼。

===Original===
Churchill’s dinners were frequently rather trying to me. Personages of enormous importance used to drop in﻿—and reveal themselves as rather asinine. At the best of times they sat dimly opposite to me, discomposed me, and disappeared. Sometimes they stared me down. That night there were two of them.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔的晚宴，于我常常是相当难堪的。身系天下之重的大人物们常会不期而至——随即现出原形：蠢得可以。运气最好的时候，他们也只是影影绰绰坐在我对面，弄得我心神不宁，然后退场。有时候，他们用眼睛把我瞪得败下阵来。那天晚上，这样的角色来了两位。

===Original===
Gurnard I had heard of. One can’t help hearing of a Chancellor of the Exchequer. The books of reference said that he was the son of one William Gurnard, Esq., of Grimsby; but I remember that once in my club a man who professed to know everything, assured me that W. Gurnard, Esq. (whom he had described as a fish salesman), was only an adoptive father. His rapid rise seemed to me inexplicable till the same man accounted for it with a shrug: “When a man of such ability believes in nothing, and sticks at nothing, there’s no saying how far he may go. He has kicked away every ladder. He doesn’t mean to come down.”

===Chinese===
格纳德其人，我是听说过的。一个人做了财政大臣，想不听人说也难。各种名人录上说，他是格里姆斯比（Grimsby）一位威廉·格纳德绅士的儿子；可是我记得，有一回在我那个俱乐部里，一个自称无所不知的人向我打包票，说那位威廉·格纳德绅士——照他的形容是个贩鱼的——不过是养父罢了。他的扶摇直上，我原本觉得无从解释，直到还是这个人耸了耸肩，替我点破：“这样才干的人，什么都不信，又什么都不顾，天晓得他会走多远。他把梯子一级一级全踢开了。他不打算再下来。”

===Original===
This, no doubt, explained much; but not everything in his fabulous career. His adherents called him an inspired statesman; his enemies set him down a mere politician. He was a man of forty-five, thin, slightly bald, and with an icy assurance of manner. He was indifferent to attacks upon his character, but crushed mercilessly everyone who menaced his position. He stood alone, and a little mysterious; his own party was afraid of him.

===Chinese===
这番话无疑解释了许多；可他那传奇式的经历里，还有它解释不了的一切。他的拥趸称他是受神启的政治家；他的政敌把他断作区区一个政客。他四十五岁，清瘦，微微秃顶，举止间带着冰冷的笃定。人家攻击他的人品，他无动于衷；可是谁威胁到他的地位，他就毫不留情地把谁碾碎。他孑然独立，带几分神秘；连他自己的党都怕他。

===Original===
Gurnard was quite hidden from me by table ornaments; the Duc de Mersch glowed with light and talked voluminously, as if he had for years and years been starved of human society. He glowed all over, it seemed to me. He had a glorious beard, that let one see very little of his florid face and took the edge away from an almost nonexistent forehead and depressingly wrinkled eyelids. He spoke excellent English, rather slowly, as if he were forever replying to toasts to his health. It struck me that he seemed to treat Churchill in nuances as an inferior, whilst for the invisible Gurnard, he reserved an attitude of nervous self-assertion. He had apparently come to dilate on the *Système Groënlandais*, and he dilated. Some mistaken persons had insinuated that the *Système* was neither more nor less than a corporate exploitation of unhappy Eskimo. De Mersch emphatically declared that those *mistaken* people were *mistaken*, declared it with official finality. The Eskimo were not unhappy. I paid attention to my dinner, and let the discourse on the affairs of the Hyperborean Protectorate lapse into an unheeded murmur. I tried to be the simple amanuensis at the feast.

===Chinese===
格纳德让我完全看不见——叫餐桌上的摆设挡了个严实；德·默施公爵满面红光，谈锋甚健，仿佛多少年来一直饿着没沾过人间往来的滋味。我看他浑身上下都在发光。他有一部堂皇的大胡子，把他那张红润的脸遮得所剩无几，也叫那几乎不存在的额头、那皱得叫人丧气的眼皮，都不那么扎眼。他英语说得极好，只是说得慢，仿佛永远在答谢别人为他的健康举杯。我察觉，他似乎在种种只可意会的细微处，拿丘吉尔当低一等的人待；而对那位看不见的格纳德，他倒端着一副惴惴然自我标榜的姿态。他显然是专程来把“格陵兰体系”（Système Groënlandais）大加发挥的，于是他便大加发挥。有些搞错了的人曾暗示，说这套“体系”无非是对不幸爱斯基摩人的一场公司化剥削。德·默施着重声明：那些搞错了的人搞错了——声明的口气，带着官方的一锤定音。爱斯基摩人并不不幸。我专心对付我的晚饭，听任那篇关于极北保护领（Hyperborean Protectorate）事务的高论，滑成一片无人理会的嗡嗡声。我竭力做宴席上一个头脑简单的笔录者。

===Original===
Suddenly, however, it struck me that de Mersch was talking at me; that he had by the merest shade raised his intonation. He was dilating upon the immense international value of the proposed Trans-Greenland Railway. Its importance to British trade was indisputable; even the opposition had no serious arguments to offer. It was the obvious duty of the British Government to give the financial guarantee. He would not insist upon the moral aspect of the work﻿—it was unnecessary. Progress, improvement, civilisation, a little less evil in the world﻿—more light! It was our duty not to count the cost of humanising a lower race. Besides, the thing would pay like another Suez Canal. Its terminus and the British coaling station would be on the west coast of the island.﻿ ﻿… I knew the man was talking at me﻿—I wondered why.

===Chinese===
然而，突然之间，我察觉出德·默施这话是冲着我说的；他把声调抬高了那么一丝丝。他正在发挥拟议中的横贯格陵兰铁路的巨大国际价值。它对英国贸易的重要性无可争辩；连反对的一方也提不出像样的论据。由英国政府给予财务担保，是明摆着的责任。这项事业的道德一面，他就不多说了——不必多说。进步、改良、文明，让世上的恶少一点——光多一点！使一个低等种族人性化，是我们的义务，不该计较代价。再说，这件事会像再一条苏伊士运河一样生利。它的终点站，连同英国的加煤站，将设在这座岛的西海岸。……我知道这人是在冲着我说话——我不明白为什么。

===Original===
Suddenly he turned his glowing countenance full upon me.

===Chinese===
忽然，他把那张红光满面的脸整个转向了我。

===Original===
“I think I must have met a member of your family,” he said. The solution occurred to me. I was a journalist, he a person interested in a railway that he wished the Government to back in some way or another. His attempts to capture my suffrage no longer astonished me. I murmured:

===Chinese===
“我想，我必定见过您府上的一位亲戚。”他说。谜底立刻有了。我是个记者；他呢，是个对一条铁路利害攸关的人物，指望政府以这样那样的方式替它撑腰。他要笼络我这一票的种种努力，便不再叫我惊异了。我含糊地道：

===Original===
“Indeed!”

===Chinese===
“是嘛！”

===Original===
“In Paris﻿—Mrs. Etchingham Granger,” he said.

===Chinese===
“在巴黎——埃钦厄姆·格兰杰太太。”他说。

===Original===
I said, “Oh, yes.”

===Chinese===
我说：“哦，是的。”

===Original===
Miss Churchill came to the rescue.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔小姐出来解了围。

===Original===
“The Duc de Mersch means our friend, your aunt,” she explained. I had an unpleasant sensation. Through fronds of asparagus fern I caught the eyes of Gurnard fixed upon me as though something had drawn his attention. I returned his glance, tried to make his face out. It had nothing distinctive in its half-hidden pallid oval; nothing that one could seize upon. But it gave the impression of never having seen the light of day, of never having had the sun upon it. But the conviction that I had aroused his attention disturbed me. What could the man know about me? I seemed to feel his glance bore through the irises of my eyes into the back of my skull. The feeling was almost physical; it was as if some incredibly concentrant reflector had been turned upon me. Then the eyelids dropped over the metallic rings beneath them. Miss Churchill continued to explain.

===Chinese===
“公爵说的是我们的朋友，你的老姑母。”她解释道。我心里泛起一阵不快。隔着餐桌上芦笋蕨的羽叶，我捕捉到格纳德的目光正定在我身上，仿佛有什么东西勾起了他的注意。我迎着那目光看回去，想辨出他这张脸。那半掩着的苍白鹅蛋脸上没有任何特征；没有任何抓得住的东西。可它给人一种从未见过天日、从未让太阳晒过的印象。而我已唤起他注意——这个确信让我不安。这个人能知道我些什么呢？我仿佛觉得他的目光穿透我眼睛的虹膜，直钻到我颅骨的背面。那感觉几乎是肉体上的；仿佛一面聚光之力不可思议的反射镜，正对准了我。随后，他的眼皮垂了下来，盖住底下那两圈金属似的圆环。丘吉尔小姐继续解释。

===Original===
“She has started a sort of *Salon des Causes Perdues* in the Faubourg Saint Germain.” She was recording the vagaries of my aunt. The Duc laughed.

===Chinese===
“她办起了一个‘失败事业沙龙’（Salon des Causes Perdues），就在圣日耳曼区（Faubourg Saint Germain）。”她这是在记叙我老姑母的种种奇行。公爵笑了。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” he said, “what a menagerie﻿—Carlists, and Orleanists, and Papal Blacks. I wonder she has not held a bazaar in favour of your White Rose League.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是呀，”他说，“好一座动物园——卡洛斯派、奥尔良派，还有教皇黑党。她竟没有替你们的白玫瑰同盟（White Rose League）办一场义卖，倒叫我觉得奇怪。”

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” I echoed, “I have heard that she was mad about the divine right of kings.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是呀，”我应和道，“我听说她对君权神授迷得要命。”

===Original===
Miss Churchill rose, as ladies rise at the end of a dinner. I followed her out of the room, in obedience to some minute signal.

===Chinese===
丘吉尔小姐站起身来，像女士们在宴席终了时那样起身。我应着某一个细微的信号，随她走出了房间。

===Original===
We were on the best of terms﻿—we two. She mothered me, as she mothered everybody not beneath contempt or above a certain age. I liked her immensely﻿—the masterful, absorbed, brown lady. As she walked up the stairs, she said, in half apology for withdrawing me.

===Chinese===
我们俩——我们相处得再好不过。她慈母似的照管我，一如她慈母似的照管一切还不至于沦落到不值一顾、年纪又不超过某个限度的人。我极其喜欢她——那位精明强悍、专心一志、棕色面庞的女主人。上楼时，她半带歉意地说了一句，算是为把我从席间调开作个交代：

===Original===
“They’ve got things to talk about.”

===Chinese===
“他们有事要谈。”

===Original===
“Why, yes,” I answered; “I suppose the railway matter has to be settled.” She looked at me fixedly.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我答道，“我想，铁路的事总要有个解决。”她直直地看着我。

===Original===
“You﻿—you mustn’t talk,” she warned.

===Chinese===
“你——你可不许说出去。”她提醒道。

===Original===
“Oh,” I answered, “I’m not indiscreet﻿—not essentially.”

===Chinese===
“哦，”我答道，“我并非口风不严——本质上不严。”

===Original===
The other three were somewhat tardy in making their drawing-room appearance. I had a sense of them, leaning their heads together over the edges of the table. In the interim a rather fierce political dowager convoyed two well-controlled, blond daughters into the room. There was a continual coming and going of such people in the house; they did with Miss Churchill social business of some kind, arranged electoral rarée-shows, and whatnot; troubled me very little. On this occasion the blond daughters were types of the sixties’ survivals﻿—the type that unemotionally inspected albums. I was convoying them through a volume of views of Switzerland, the dowager was saying to Miss Churchill:

===Chinese===
另外三位到客厅露面，未免迟了些。我有个印象，他们还在餐桌边，隔着桌沿彼此凑着头。这段空当里，一位相当凶悍的政界老夫人，押着两个调教得规规矩矩的金发女儿进了屋。这类人在那宅子里川流不息；她们同丘吉尔小姐办着某种社交上的事务，张罗选举拉洋片之类的名堂；很少来搅扰我。这一回，那两位金发女儿是六十年代残存下来的典型——面无表情翻看相册的那一型。我正领着她们浏览一册瑞士风景照，那位老夫人正对丘吉尔小姐说：

===Original===
“You think, then, it will be enough if we have.﻿ ﻿…” When the door opened behind my back. I looked round negligently and hastily returned to the consideration of a shining photograph of the Dent du Midi. A very gracious figure of a girl was embracing the grim Miss Churchill, as a gracious girl should virginally salute a grim veteran.

===Chinese===
“那么您以为，只要我们有了……”这时，我背后的门开了。我漫不经心地回头一望，随即赶紧回去端详米迪峰（Dent du Midi）一张亮闪闪的照片。一个仪态极为优雅的姑娘身影，正拥抱着神情冷峻的丘吉尔小姐——正如一位优雅的姑娘，向一位冷峻的宿将行圣洁的初见之礼。

===Original===
“Ah, my dear Miss Churchill!” a fluting voice filled the large room, “we were very nearly going back to Paris without once coming to see you. We are only over for two days﻿—for the Tenants’ Ball, and so my aunt﻿ ﻿… but surely that is Arthur.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“啊，亲爱的丘吉尔小姐！”一个长笛似的嗓音灌满了大房间，“我们差一点儿就回巴黎去了，一次也没来看您。我们只过来两天——为着佃户舞会（Tenants' Ball），所以我姑母……咦，这不是阿瑟嘛……”

===Original===
I turned eagerly. It was the Dimensionist girl. She continued talking to Miss Churchill. “We meet so seldom, and we are never upon terms,” she said lightly. “I assure you we are like cat and dog.” She came toward me and the blond maidens disappeared, everybody, everything disappeared. I had not seen her for nearly a year. I had vaguely gathered from Miss Churchill that she was regarded as a sister of mine, that she had, with wealth inherited from a semi-fabulous Australian uncle, revived the glories of my aunt’s house. I had never denied it, because I did not want to interfere with my aunt’s attempts to regain some of the family’s prosperity. It even had my sympathy to a small extent, for, after all, the family was my family too.

===Chinese===
我热切地转过身去。正是那位第四维姑娘。她继续同丘吉尔小姐说话。“我们难得见上一面，而且从来处不来，”她轻描淡写地说，“我向您担保，我们就像猫和狗。”她朝我走来，那两位金发少女不见了——人人、事事，一齐不见了。我快一年没见到她了。我从丘吉尔小姐那里零零碎碎地听说：她被当作了我的一个姐妹；她拿从一个半属传奇的澳大利亚叔叔那里继承来的财产，重振了我老姑母之家的荣光。我从未否认过，因为我不想妨碍老姑母挽回家族几分兴旺的种种努力。这努力甚至在小小程度上还得着我的同情——毕竟，那个家族也是我的家族。

===Original===
As a memory my pseudo-sister had been something bright and clear-cut and rather small; seen now, she was something that one could not look at for glow. She moved toward me, smiling and radiant, as a ship moves beneath towers of shining canvas. I was simply overwhelmed. I don’t know what she said, what I said, what she did or I. I have an idea that we conversed for some minutes. I remember that she said, at some point,

===Chinese===
在记忆里，我的假姐妹是某种明亮的、轮廓分明、而且相当小的东西；如今一见，她却是某种光艳得叫人无法正视的东西。她朝我走来，微笑着，光彩照人，像一艘船张着高塔般的闪亮船帆前行。我简直被压倒了。她说了什么、我说了什么、她做了什么、我又做了什么，我一概不知。我只依稀记得我们交谈了几分钟。记得她在某个当口说过：

===Original===
“Go away now; I want to talk to Mr. Gurnard.”

===Chinese===
“现在走开吧；我要同格纳德先生说话。”

===Original===
As a matter of fact, Gurnard was making toward her﻿—a deliberate, slow progress. She greeted him with nonchalance, as, beneath eyes, a woman greets a man she knows intimately. I found myself hating him, thinking that he was not the sort of man she ought to know.

===Chinese===
其实，格纳德正朝她走去——一路从容，一路迟缓。她神色淡漠地招呼他，像女人当着众人的眼，招呼一个她知之甚深的男人。我发觉自己在恨他，心想：他可不是她该结识的那一号男人。

===Original===
“It’s settled?” she asked him, as he came within range. He looked at me inquiringly﻿—insolently. She said, “My brother,” and he answered:

===Chinese===
“定下来了？”他走到近前时，她问。他打量了我一眼——探询地、无礼地。她说：“我哥哥。”他答：

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” as I moved away. I hated the man and I could not keep my eyes off him and her. I went and stood against the mantelpiece. The Duc de Mersch bore down upon them, and I welcomed his interruption until I saw that he, too, was intimate with her, intimate with a pomposity of flourishes as irritating as Gurnard’s nonchalance.

===Chinese===
“哦，定了。”他说这话的当儿，我正走开去。我恨这个人，却怎么也没法把眼睛从他俩身上挪开。我走过去，靠在壁炉架上。德·默施公爵朝他们压了过去；他的打岔本来让我称快，直到我看出，他同她也亲密——那亲密带着一套夸炫的繁文缛节，同格纳德的淡漠一样惹人恼。

===Original===
I stood there and glowered at them. I noted her excessive beauty; her almost perilous self-possession while she stood talking to those two men. Of me there was nothing left but the eyes. I had no mind, no thoughts. I saw the three figures go through the attitudes of conversation﻿—she very animated, de Mersch grotesquely *empressé*, Gurnard undisguisedly saturnine. He repelled me exactly as grossly vulgar men had the power of doing, but he, himself, was not that﻿—there was something﻿ ﻿… something. I could not quite make out his face, I never could. I never did, any more than I could ever quite visualise hers. I wondered vaguely how Churchill could work in harness with such a man, how he could bring himself to be closeted, as he had just been, with him and with a fool like de Mersch﻿—I should have been afraid.

===Chinese===
我站在那里，对他们怒目而视。我留意到她那过盛的美貌；留意到她站在那里同那两个男人交谈时，那份镇定自若几乎到了危险的地步。我整个人只剩下一双眼睛。没有心智，没有思绪。我看着三个身影做出一幕幕交谈的姿态——她神采飞扬，德·默施殷勤得怪模怪样，格纳德毫不掩饰地阴沉。他令我生厌，那份厌烦，恰如粗俗不堪的男人们素有本领唤起的那种；可他本人又绝非那种人——总有一样什么……一样什么。我辨不清他的脸，从来辨不清。始终没能辨清，一如我也从来没能把她那张脸明晰地想出来。我恍惚地诧异：丘吉尔怎么能同这样一个人套在一副挽具里干活；怎么能让自己像方才那样，同他、同一个德·默施那样的蠢货关起门来密谈——换了是我，是会害怕的。

===Original===
As for de Mersch, standing between those two, he seemed like a country lout between confederate sharpers. It struck me that she let me see, made me see, that she and Gurnard had an understanding, made manifest to me by glances that passed when the Duc had his unobservant eyes turned elsewhere.

===Chinese===
至于德·默施，站在那两人中间，他活像个被夹在一伙串通好的骗子当中的乡下笨伯。我察觉到，她这是让我看见、是有心让我看见：她同格纳德之间有一层默契——趁公爵那双视而不见的眼睛转向别处时彼此交换的眼色，把这层默契向我明明白白地摆了出来。

===Original===
I saw Churchill, in turn, move desultorily toward them, drawn in, like a straw toward a little whirlpool. I turned my back in a fury of jealousy.

===Chinese===
我又看见丘吉尔也散漫地朝他们挪过去，被吸了过去，像一根稻草被吸向一个小小的漩涡。我满怀妒火的狂怒，掉转身去。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
I had a pretty bad night after that, and was not much in the mood for Fox on the morrow. The sight of her had dwarfed everything; the thought of her disgusted me with everything, made me out of conceit with the world﻿—with that part of the world that had become my world. I wanted to get up into hers﻿—and I could not see any way. The room in which Fox sat seemed to be hopelessly off the road﻿—to be hopelessly off any road to any place; to be the end of a blind alley. One day I might hope to occupy such a room﻿—in my shirtsleeves, like Fox. But that was not the end of my career﻿—not the end that I desired. She had upset me.

===Chinese===
那以后我熬过了相当难受的一夜，第二天见福克斯，也没了多少兴致。见到她一面，一切便都显得渺小了；一想到她，我便对一切都生厌，对整个世界意兴索然——对已成为我的世界的那一部分世界。我想爬进她的世界里去——却看不出任何路径。福克斯坐的那间屋子，似乎绝望地偏离了大路——绝望地不沾任何一条通往任何地方的路；简直是死胡同的尽头。有朝一日，我或许也能指望坐上这样一间屋子——也只穿衬衫袖子，像福克斯那样。可那并不是我事业的尽头——不是我想望的尽头。她把我搅乱了。

===Original===
“You’ve just missed Polehampton,” Fox said; “wanted to get hold of your ‘Atmospheres.’ ”

===Chinese===
“你刚错过了波尔汉普顿，”福克斯说，“他想弄你那部《气氛》。”

===Original===
“Oh, damn Polehampton,” I said, “and particularly damn the ‘Atmospheres.’ ”

===Chinese===
“哦，让波尔汉普顿见鬼去吧，”我说，“尤其让《气氛》见鬼去。”

===Original===
“Willingly,” Fox said, “but I told Mr. P. that you were willing if.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“乐意之至，”福克斯说，“不过我跟波先生（Mr. P.）说了，你是愿意的，只要……”

===Original===
“I don’t want to know,” I repeated. “I tell you I’m sick of the things.”

===Chinese===
“我不想知道，”我又说了一遍，“我告诉你，这种东西我烦透了。”

===Original===
“What a change,” he asserted, sympathetically, “I *thought* you would.”

===Chinese===
“真是大变样了，”他满含同情地说，“我早料到，你会的。”

===Original===
It struck me as disgusting that a person like Fox should think about me at all. “Oh, I’ll see it through,” I said. “Who’s the next?”

===Chinese===
像福克斯这样一个人，居然会替我费心思，这让我觉得恶心。“哦，我会干到底的，”我说，“下一位是谁？”

===Original===
“We’ve got to have the Duc de Mersch now,” he answered, “De Mersch as State Founder﻿—written as large as you can﻿—all across the page. The moment’s come and we’ve got to rope it in, that’s all. I’ve been middling good to you.﻿ ﻿… You understand.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“现在我们得做德·默施公爵了，”他答道，“德·默施作为建国者——字号放得多大就多大——横贯整版。时机到了，我们得把它圈住，就是这么回事。我待你还算不赖……你懂的……”

===Original===
He began to explain in his dark sentences. The time had come for an energetically engineered boom in de Mersch﻿—a boom all along the line. And I was to commence the campaign. Fox had been good to me and I was to repay him. I listened in a sort of apathetic indifference.

===Chinese===
他开始用他那含浑的句子解释起来。时机到了，该给德·默施鼓起一股劲头十足的人造行情——全线的大行情。而这场战役由我来打响。福克斯待我不薄，我该报答他。我听着，带着一种麻木的淡然。

===Original===
“Oh, very well,” I said. I was subconsciously aware that, as far as I was concerned, the determining factor of the situation was the announcement that de Mersch was to be in Paris. If he had been in his own particular grand duchy I wouldn’t have gone after him. For a moment I thought of the interview as taking place in London. But Fox﻿—ostensibly, at least﻿—wasn’t even aware of de Mersch’s visit; spoke of him as being in Paris﻿—in a flat in which he was accustomed to interview the continental financiers who took up so much of his time.

===Chinese===
“哦，很好，”我说。我潜意识里明白，就我而论，局面的决定性因素，是德·默施将身在巴黎这一消息。他若是待在他自己那个特有的大公国里，我是不会去追他的。有一刻，我曾设想这访谈在伦敦进行。可福克斯——至少表面看来——对德·默施此行甚至一无所知；只说他在巴黎——在一套公寓里，他惯于在那儿接见那些占去他大量时间的欧陆金融家。

===Original===
I realised that I wanted to go to Paris because she was there. She had said that she was going to Paris on the morrow of yesterday. The name was pleasant to me, and it turned the scale.

===Chinese===
我明白过来，我想去巴黎，是因为她在那里。她说过，她要在昨天之后的明天去巴黎。这个名字让我听来愉快，天平便倒向了一边。

===Original===
Fox’s eyes remained upon my face.

===Chinese===
福克斯的眼睛一直停在我脸上。

===Original===
“Do you good, eh?” he dimly interpreted my thoughts. “A run over. I thought you’d like it and, look here, Polehampton’s taken over the *Bi-Monthly*; wants to get new blood into it, see? He’d take something. I’ve been talking to him﻿—a short series.﻿ ﻿… ‘Aspects.’ That sort of thing.” I tried to work myself into some sort of enthusiasm of gratitude. I knew that Fox had spoken well of me to Polehampton﻿—as a sort of set off.

===Chinese===
“对你有好处，嗯？”他隐约地解出了我的心思。“出去跑一趟。我想你会乐意的；还有，听着，波尔汉普顿把《双月刊》（the Bi-Monthly）盘下来了；想往里面注点新血，明白吗？他会要你一点东西的。我一直在跟他谈——一个短系列……‘方方面面’（Aspects）。那一类的东西。”我努力唤起某种感激的热忱。我知道福克斯已在波尔汉普顿跟前替我说了好话——算是一种抵偿。

===Original===
“You go and see Mr. P.,” he confirmed; “it’s really all arranged. And then get off to Paris as fast as you can and have a good time.”

===Chinese===
“你去见一见波先生吧，”他把话敲实，“其实全都安排妥了。然后就尽快动身去巴黎，痛痛快快玩一趟。”

===Original===
“Have I been unusually cranky lately?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“我这阵子脾气特别坏吗？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, you’ve been a little off the hooks, I thought, for the last week or so.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我寻思，这一个来星期你是有那么点儿不对劲。”

===Original===
He took up a large bottle of white mucilage, and I accepted it as a sign of dismissal. I was touched by his solicitude for my health. It always did touch me, and I found myself unusually broad-minded in thought as I went down the terra-cotta front steps into the streets. For all his frank vulgarity, for all his shirtsleeves﻿—I somehow regarded that habit of his as the final mark of the Beast﻿—and the Louis Quinze accessories, I felt a warm good-feeling for the little man.

===Chinese===
他拿起一大瓶白胶水，我便把这当作送客的表示。他对我健康的挂念让我感动。这总是让我感动；走下那赤陶色的门前台阶、来到街上，我发觉自己的心思异乎寻常地宽厚。尽管他粗俗得坦率，尽管他总挽着衬衫袖子——我不知怎么总把他这个习惯看作那兽的终极印记——还有那路易十五式的零碎陈设，我对这个小个子男人仍怀着一股温暖的好感。

===Original===
I made haste to see Polehampton, to beard him in a sort of den that contained a number of shelves of books selected for their glittering back decoration. They gave the impression that Mr. Polehampton wished to suggest to his visitors the fitness and propriety of clothing their walls with the same gilt cloth. They gave that idea, but I think that, actually, Mr. Polehampton took an aesthetic delight in the gilding. He was not a publisher by nature. He had drifted into the trade and success, but beneath a polish of acquaintance retained a fine awe for a book as such. In early life he had had such shining things on a shiny table in a parlour. He had a similar awe for his daughter, who had been born after his entry into the trade, and who had the literary flavour﻿—a flavour so pronounced that he dragged her by the heels into any conversation with us who hewed his raw material, expecting, I suppose, to cow us. For the greater good of this young lady he had bought the *Bi-Monthly*﻿—one of the portentous political organs. He had, they said, ideas of forcing a seat out of the party as a recompense.

===Chinese===
我赶紧去见波尔汉普顿，到他一处洞穴似的私室里当面堵他。那屋里立着几架书，全是照书脊上闪亮的装饰挑选来的。它们给人一种印象：波尔汉普顿先生有意暗示来客，用同样的烫金布面给自家的墙壁也披上衣裳，是何等合宜、何等得体。它们确能给人这个念头；但我想，实际上，波尔汉普顿先生是拿那烫金自娱，得着一种审美的愉悦。他天性本不是出版家。他是漂流进了这一行，也漂流进了成功；但在一层熟行的光漆底下，他对书这件东西本身仍存着一种精致的敬畏。早年，他曾在客厅里一张亮闪闪的桌上摆过这类亮闪闪的物件。他对他的女儿也有类似的敬畏——她生在他入行之后，带着一股文学的气味；那气味浓重得出奇，以致他动不动便倒拽着她的脚跟，把她拖进同我们这些替他砍伐原材料的人的任何谈话里，我想，是指望借此慑服我们。为了这位年轻小姐更大的好处，他买下了《双月刊》——那班煞有介事的政治刊物之一。据说，他还盘算着从党内硬逼出一个议席，作为报偿。

===Original===
It didn’t matter much what was the nature of my series of articles. I was to get the atmosphere of cities as I had got those of the various individuals. I seemed to pay on those lines, and Miss Polehampton commended me.

===Chinese===
我这组文章性质如何，倒无关紧要。我要去摄取各座城市的气氛，如同先前摄取各色人物的气氛一样。我走这种路子似乎颇见收益，波尔汉普顿小姐也赏识我。

===Original===
“My daughter likes﻿ ﻿… eh﻿ ﻿… your touch, you know, and.﻿ ﻿…” His terms were decent﻿—for the man, and were offered with a flourish that indicated special benevolence and a reference to the hundred pounds. I was at a loss to account for his manner until he began to stammer out an indication. Its lines were that I knew Fox, and I knew Churchill and the Duc de Mersch, and the *Hour*. “And those financial articles﻿ ﻿… in the *Hour*﻿ ﻿… were they now?﻿ ﻿… *Were* they﻿ ﻿… was the Trans-Greenland railway actually﻿ ﻿… did I think it would be worth one’s while﻿ ﻿… in fact.﻿ ﻿…” and so on.

===Chinese===
“我女儿喜欢……呃……你的笔调，你知道，还有……”他的条件不算亏待——就他这号人而论——而且是带着一番神气开出来的，显示着特别的恩顾，并点到了那一百镑。我一时弄不清他这副做派，直到他开始期期艾艾地透出口风。口风的大意是：我认识福克斯，认识丘吉尔和德·默施公爵，还有《时辰报》。“还有那些金融文章……登在《时辰报》上的……是真的么？……究竟是不是……那条横贯格陵兰铁路当真……我看值不值得下本钱……事实上……”如此等等。

===Original===
I never was any good in a situation of that sort, never any good at all. I ought to have assumed blank ignorance, but the man’s eyes pleaded; it seemed a tremendous matter to him. I tried to be noncommittal, and said: “Of course I haven’t any right.” But I had a vague, stupid sense that loyalty to Churchill demanded that I should back up a man he was backing. As a matter of fact, nothing so direct was a-gate, it couldn’t have been. It was something about shares in one of de Mersch’s other enterprises. Polehampton was going to pick them up for nothing, and they were going to rise when the boom in de Mersch’s began﻿—something of the sort. And the boom would begin as soon as the news of the agreement about the railway got abroad.

===Chinese===
这种场面我从来就不中用，从来一点都不中用。我本该装出一概不知，可那人的眼睛在哀告；这事在他看来似乎天大。我试着不置可否，说道：“我当然没有任何权利。”但我又有一种模糊而愚蠢的感觉：对丘吉尔的忠心，要求我去撑一个他正在撑的人。事实上，这么直截了当的事根本不会在运作，也不可能。是德·默施另外某家企业里股票的事。波尔汉普顿打算近乎白捡地把它们吃进来，等德·默施各项事业的大行情启动，它们就会跟着涨上去——诸如此类。而铁路协定的消息一传开，大行情便会启动。

===Original===
I let him get it out of me in a way that makes the thought of that bare place with its gilt book-backs and its three uncomfortable office-chairs and the ground-glass windows through which one read the inversion of the legend “Polehampton,” all its gloom and its rigid lines and its pallid light, a memory of confusion. And Polehampton was properly grateful, and invited me to dine with him and his phantasmal daughter﻿—who wanted to make my acquaintance. It was like a command to a state banquet given by a palace official, and Lea would be invited to meet me. Miss Polehampton did not like Lea, but he had to be asked once a year﻿—to encourage good feeling, I suppose. The interview dribbled out on those lines. I asked if it was one of Lea’s days at the office. It was not. I tried to put in a good word for Lea, but it was not very effective. Polehampton was too subject to his assistant’s thorns to be responsive to praise of him.

===Chinese===
我竟让他从我嘴里把话套了出去，弄得如今想起那间光秃秃的屋子——连同它烫金的书脊、三把硌人的办公椅、那隔着便能读出反写的“波尔汉普顿”（Polehampton）字样的磨砂玻璃窗，连同它全部的阴郁、僵直的线条和惨白的光线——都成了一段昏乱的回忆。波尔汉普顿倒是正经感激得很，请我去同他和他的幽灵般的女儿共进晚餐——那位小姐想要结识我。这像是一位宫廷官员发出的国宴敕请，而利将被请来作陪。波尔汉普顿小姐不喜欢利，可他一年总得被请上一回——我猜，是为了敦睦情谊。会面便顺着这些话头零落收场。我问今天是不是利来办公的日子。不是。我想替利进几句好话，却不太见效。波尔汉普顿平日受足了这位助手的针刺，对他的称许无从感应。

===Original===
So I hurried out of the place. I wanted to be out of this medium in which my ineffectiveness threatened to proclaim itself to me. It was not a very difficult matter. I had, in those days, rooms in one of the political journalists’ clubs﻿—a vast mausoleum of white tiles. But a man used to pack my portmanteau very efficiently and at short notice. At the station one of those coincidences that are not coincidences made me run against the great Callan. He was rather unhappy﻿—found it impossible to make an already distracted porter listen to the end of one of his sentences with two-second waits between each word. For that reason he brightened to see me﻿—was delighted to find a through-journey companion who would take him on terms of greatness. In the railway carriage, divested of troublesome bags that imparted anxiety to his small face and a stagger to his walk, he swelled to his normal dimensions.

===Chinese===
于是我匆匆逃出那地方。我要脱出那种介质——在它里面，我的无能眼看就要向我本人揭自己的底。这倒不算什么难事。我那时在一家政治记者俱乐部里赁着房间——一座白瓷砖砌的巨大陵墓。好在有个当差的，惯会一声招呼便把我的旅行箱收拾得又快又妥帖。在车站，一桩那种算不得巧合的巧合，让我一头撞上了了不起的卡伦。他正颇为苦恼——他发觉，要叫一个早已忙得七荤八素的搬运工，听完他一句每个词之间都停两秒钟的话，根本办不到。为这个缘故，他一见我便精神一振——高兴地发现一位全程同路的旅伴，肯按伟人的规格接待他。进了车厢，卸下那几只叫他小脸犯愁、走路打晃的累赘提包，他便重新膨胀回了他正常的尺寸。

===Original===
“So you’re﻿—going to﻿—Paris,” he meditated, “for the *Hour*.”

===Chinese===
“这么说——你——要——去——巴黎，”他沉吟道，“为的是《时辰报》。”

===Original===
“I’m going to Paris for the *Hour*,” I agreed.

===Chinese===
“我是要为《时辰报》去巴黎。”我表示同意。

===Original===
“Ah!” he went on, “you’re going to interview the Elective Grand Duke.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“啊！”他接着说，“你要去采访那位选任大公（the Elective Grand Duke）……”

===Original===
“We call him the Duc de Mersch,” I interrupted, flippantly. It was a matter of nuances. The Elective Grand Duke was a philanthropist and a State Founder, the Duc de Mersch was the hero as financier.

===Chinese===
“我们管他叫德·默施公爵，”我轻率地打断道。这中间有个微妙的分寸。选任大公是慈善家、建国者；德·默施公爵则是金融家一路的英雄。

===Original===
“Of Holstein-Launewitz,” Callan ignored. The titles slipped over his tongue like the last drops of some inestimable oily vintage.

===Chinese===
“霍尔斯坦-劳内维茨（Holstein-Launewitz）的。”卡伦不加理会。那些名号从他舌面上滑过，像某种无价而油润的陈年佳酿的最后几滴。

===Original===
“I might have saved you the trouble. I’m going to see him myself.”

===Chinese===
“我本可以省掉你这趟麻烦。我自己就要去见他。”

===Original===
“*You*,” I italicised. It struck me as phenomenal and rather absurd that everybody that I came across should, in some way or other, be mixed up with this portentous philanthropist. It was as if a fisherman were drawing in a ground line baited with hundreds of hooks. He had a little offended air.

===Chinese===
“你，”我加重语气说。我一路碰到的每一个人，竟都以这种或那种方式，同这位煞有介事的慈善家牵扯在一起；这让我觉得既属奇观，又颇为荒唐。就像一个渔夫在收一根缀着几百枚钓钩的延绳。他微微露出一点见怪的神气。

===Original===
“He, or, I should say, a number of people interested in a philanthropic society, have asked me to go to Greenland.”

===Chinese===
“他——或者说，我应该说，一群对一家慈善协会怀有利益的人——请我到格陵兰去。”

===Original===
“Do they want to get rid of you?” I asked, flippantly. I was made to know my place.

===Chinese===
“他们是想打发掉你吗？”我轻率地问。我随即被提点，认清了自己的本分。

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” Callan said, in his most deliberate, most Olympian tone. “I believe you’re entirely mistaken, I believe﻿ ﻿… I’ve been informed that the *Système Groënlandais* is one of the healthiest places in the Polar regions. There are interested persons who.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我亲爱的伙计，”卡伦以他最从容不迫、最奥林匹克的腔调说，“我相信你完全想岔了，我相信……人家告诉我，那格陵兰体系（Système Groënlandais）是极地各处最有益于健康的地方之一。有些利害相关的人士他们……”

===Original===
“So I’ve heard,” I interrupted, “but I can assure you I’ve heard nothing but good of the *Système* and the﻿ ﻿… and its philanthropists. I meant nothing against them. I was only astonished that you should go to such a place.”

===Chinese===
“这个我也听说了，”我打断他，“不过我可以向您担保，关于那套体系，还有那……还有它的慈善家们，我听到的净是好话。我对他们绝无半点冒犯之意。我只是惊讶，您居然要到那样一个地方去。”

===Original===
“I have been asked to go upon a mission,” he explained, seriously, “to ascertain what the truth about the *Système* really is. It is a new country with, I am assured, a great future in store. A great deal of English money has been invested in its securities, and naturally great interest is taken in its affairs.”

===Chinese===
“人家请我去负一项使命，”他郑重地解释，“查明那套体系的真相究竟如何。那是一个新的国度，有人向我保证，储藏着远大的前程。英国已有大笔资金投在它的证券上，人们自然对它的事务深为关切。”

===Original===
“So it seems,” I said, “I seem to run upon it at every hour of the day and night.”

===Chinese===
“看来是这样，”我说，“我似乎日日夜夜、时时刻刻都要撞上它。”

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” Callan rhapsodised, “it has a great future in store, a great future. The Duke is a true philanthropist. He has taken infinite pains﻿—infinite pains. He wished to build up a model state, *the* model protectorate of the world, a place where perfect equality shall obtain for all races, all creeds, and all colours. You would scarcely believe how he has worked to ensure the happiness of the native races. He founded the great society to protect the Eskimo, the Society for the Regeneration of the Arctic Regions﻿—the S.R.A.R.﻿—as you called it, and now he is only waiting to accomplish his greatest project﻿—the Trans-Greenland railway. When that is done, he will hand over the *Système* to his own people. That is the act of a great man.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”卡伦咏叹起来，“它储藏着远大的前程，远大的前程。公爵是位真正的慈善家。他费了无穷的心血——无穷的心血。他想建立起一个模范国家，举世无双的那座模范保护领，一个对一切种族、一切信仰、一切肤色都行完全平等的地方。他为确保土著种族的幸福操了多少劳，你简直难以置信。他创办了保护爱斯基摩人的伟大协会——北极地区复兴会（the Society for the Regeneration of the Arctic Regions）——照你们的叫法，就是 S.R.A.R.——如今他只等着完成他最伟大的工程——横贯格陵兰铁路。等铁路建成，他就把那套体系交给他自己的人民。这是一个伟大人物的作为。”

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” I said.

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊。”我说。

===Original===
“Well,” Callan began again, but suddenly paused. “By the by, this must go no farther,” he said, anxiously, “I will let you have full particulars when the time is ripe.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”卡伦重新开口，却又突然顿住。“对了，这话可不能再往下传，”他不安地说，“等时机成熟，我会把详情全给你。”

===Original===
“My dear Callan,” I said, touchily, “I can hold my tongue.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的卡伦，”我愠恼地说，“我是能管住自己舌头的。”

===Original===
He went off at tangent.

===Chinese===
他话锋一岔，扯到别处去了。

===Original===
“I don’t want you to take my word﻿—I haven’t seen it yet. But I feel assured about it myself. The most distinguished people have spoken to me in its favour. The celebrated traveller, Aston, spoke of it with tears in his eyes. He was the first governor-general, you know. Of course I should not take any interest in it, if I were not satisfied as to that. It is percisely because I feel that the thing is one of the finest monuments of a grand century that I am going to lend it the weight of my pen.”

===Chinese===
“我并不要你单凭我一句话——我自己还没见过它呢。可我自己对此是放心的。最有身份的人物们都曾当着我替它说话。那位著名的旅行家阿斯顿（Aston），说起它来眼里含着泪。他是首任总督，你知道的。当然，倘若在这一点上我不满意，我是不会对它有半分兴趣的。恰恰是因为我感到这桩事业是一个宏伟世纪里最精美的丰碑之一，我才要把我这支笔的分量借给它。”

===Original===
“I quite understand,” I assured him; then, solicitously, “I hope they don’t expect you to do it for nothing.”

===Chinese===
“我完全明白，”我向他保证；接着又关切地说，“但愿他们不至于指望你白干吧。”

===Original===
“Oh, dear, no,” Callan answered.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，那倒不会。”卡伦答道。

===Original===
“Ah, well, I wish you luck,” I said. “They couldn’t have got a better man to win over the National conscience. I suppose it comes to that.”

===Chinese===
“啊，好吧，祝你好运，”我说，“要赢取国民的良心，他们再也寻不出比你更合适的人了。我想，归结起来就是这么回事。”

===Original===
Callan nodded.

===Chinese===
卡伦点了点头。

===Original===
“I fancy I have the ear of the public,” he said. He seemed to get satisfaction from the thought.

===Chinese===
“我想，公众的耳朵是向着我的。”他说。他似乎从这个念头里得到了满足。

===Original===
The train entered Folkestone Harbour. The smell of the sea and the easy send of the boat put a little heart into me, but my spirits were on the down grade. Callan was a trying companion. The sight of him stirred uneasy emotions, the sound of his voice jarred.

===Chinese===
火车驶入福克斯通（Folkestone）港。海水的气味和渡船从容的起伏，给我添了一点心气，可我的心绪仍在走下坡。卡伦是个磨人的旅伴。一看见他，不安的情绪就被搅动；一听见他的声音，耳朵就被刺得生疼。

===Original===
“Are you coming to the Grand?” he said, as we passed St. Denis.

===Chinese===
“你要住到大饭店（the Grand）去吗？”驶过圣但尼（St. Denis）时，他问。

===Original===
“My God, no,” I answered, hotly, “I’m going across the river.”

===Chinese===
“老天，不，”我急躁地答道，“我到河对岸去。”

===Original===
“Ah,” he murmured, “the Quartier Latin. I wish I could come with you. But I’ve my reputation to think of. You’d be surprised how people get to hear of my movements. Besides, I’m a family man.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”他喃喃道，“拉丁区（the Quartier Latin）。我真想能跟你一道去。可我还得顾念自己的名声。人们多么快就能打听到我的行踪，说出来准叫你吃惊。再说，我是个有家室的人。”

===Original===
I was agitatedly silent. The train steamed into the glare of the electric lights, and, getting into a fiacre, I breathed again. I seemed to be at the entrance of a new life, a better sort of paradise, during that drive across the night city. In London one is always a passenger, in Paris one has reached a goal. The crowds on the pavements, under the plane-trees, in the black shadows, in the white glare of the open spaces, are at leisure﻿—they go nowhere, seek nothing beyond.

===Chinese===
我心潮翻涌，一言不发。火车喷着汽驶入电灯的强光里；坐进一辆出租马车（fiacre），我才重新透出一口气。乘车穿过夜色中的城市那一程，我仿佛站在一种新生活的入口，一座更胜一筹的乐园的门前。在伦敦，人永远是个过客；在巴黎，人已经抵达了目标。人行道上的、梧桐树下的、黑色阴影里的、空旷处白亮灯光下的人群，都从容闲在——他们哪儿也不去，此外也别无所求。

===Original===
We crossed the river, the unwinking towers of Notre Dame towering pallidly against the dark sky behind us; rattled into the new light of the resuming boulevard; turned up a dark street, and came to a halt before a half-familiar shut door. You know how one wakes the sleepy concierge, how one takes one’s candle, climbs up hundreds and hundreds of smooth stairs, following the slipshod footfalls of a half-awakened guide upward through Rembrandt’s own shadows, and how one’s final sleep is sweetened by the little inconveniences of a strange bare room and of a strange hard bed.

===Chinese===
我们过了河，身后，巴黎圣母院（Notre Dame）那不眨眼的塔楼衬着黑沉沉的夜幕，苍白地巍然耸立；马车嗒嗒地驶进林荫大道重又亮起的灯光里；拐上一条黑黢黢的街，在一扇半熟不熟、紧紧关着的门前停了下来。你知道人是怎样唤醒睡意正浓的门房，怎样接过自己的蜡烛，跟着一位半睡半醒的向导那趿拉趿拉的脚步，攀上几百几百级光滑的楼梯，一路穿行在伦勃朗（Rembrandt）本人的阴影里；也知道一间陌生的空屋子、一张陌生的硬床那小小的不便，如何把人最后的一场睡眠酿得更甜。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
Before noon of the next day I was ascending the stairs of the new house in which the Duc had his hermitage. There was an air of secrecy in the broad publicity of the carpeted stairs that led to his flat; a hush in the atmosphere; in the street itself, a glorified cul de sac that ran into the bustling life of the Italiens. It had the sudden sluggishness of a backwater. One seemed to have grown suddenly deaf in the midst of the rattle.

===Chinese===
第二天晌午之前，我已经在登那幢新楼的楼梯——公爵把他的隐居之所设在了里面。通往他那套公寓的楼梯铺着地毯，宽宽绰绰地敞在明处，却透着一股隐秘气；空气里有一种屏息的静；那条街本身是一条装点得体面的死胡同，一头扎进意大利人剧院街区（the Italiens）喧腾的生活。那地方有一种回水死湾般突如其来的滞缓。人在那片喧响当中，仿佛骤然间聋了。

===Original===
There was an incredible suggestion of silence﻿—the silence of a private detective﻿—in the mien of the servant who ushered me into a room. He was the English servant of the theatre﻿—the English servant that foreigners affect. The room had a splendour of its own, not a cheaply vulgar splendour, but the vulgarity of the most lavish plush and purple kind. The air was heavy, killed by the scent of exotic flowers, darkened by curtains that suggested the voluminous velvet backgrounds of certain old portraits. The Duc de Mersch had carried with him into this place of retirement the taste of the New Palace, that showplace of his that was the stupefaction of swarms of honest tourists.

===Chinese===
那个引我进房间的仆人，气度间有一种令人难以置信的静默——私家侦探式的静默。他是戏台上的那种英国仆人——外国人装模作样学来的那种英国仆人。这房间自有一种豪华，不是廉价俗气的豪华，而是最挥霍的长毛绒与紫绛那一类的俗。空气滞重，被异国花香熏得死气沉沉，又被窗帘压得昏暗——那些窗帘令人想起某些旧肖像画里厚重层叠的天鹅绒背景。德·默施公爵把新宫（the New Palace）的趣味也随身带进了这处退隐之地——新宫是他那座炫耀之所，一向令成群结队的老实游客看得目瞪口呆。

===Original===
I remembered soon enough that the man was a philanthropist, that he might be an excellent man of heart and indifferent of taste. He must be. But I was prone to be influenced by things of this sort, and felt depressed at the thought that so much of royal excellence should weigh so heavily in the wrong scale of the balance of the applied arts. I turned my back on the room and gazed at the blazing white decorations of the opposite house-fronts.

===Chinese===
我很快就想起，此人是个慈善家，他兴许心地极好，只是趣味低劣。想必如此。可我素来容易被这类事物左右，一想到这么多帝王家的卓异品质，竟沉甸甸地压在实用艺术这杆秤错了的一侧秤盘上，便觉得抑郁。我转过身去，背对房间，凝望对面一排房屋立面那白得晃眼的装饰。

===Original===
A door behind me must have opened, for I heard the sounds of a concluding tirade in a high-pitched voice.

===Chinese===
我背后准是有一扇门开了，因为我听见一个尖利的嗓音，正嚷到一段长篇斥骂的收梢。

===Original===
“*Et quant à un duc de farce, je ne m’en fiche pas mal, moi*,” it said in an accent curiously compounded of the foreign and the *coulisse*. A muttered male remonstrance ensued, and then, with disconcerting clearness:

===Chinese===
“Et quant à un duc de farce, je ne m’en fiche pas mal, moi（至于一个滑稽戏里的公爵，我嘛，可一点儿也不放在心上），”那声音说，口音古怪地掺和着外国腔与后台（coulisse）腔。接着是一阵男人含混的低声劝阻，随后，字字清晰得叫人难堪：

===Original===
“*Gr-r-rangeur﻿—Eschingan﻿—eh bien﻿—il entend. Et moi, j’entends, moi aussi. Tu veux me jouer contre elle. La Grangeur﻿—pah! Consoles-toi avec elle, mon vieux. Je ne veux plus de toi. Tu m’as donné de tes sales rentes Groenlandoises, et je n’ai pas pu les vendre. Ah, vieux farceur, tu vas voir ce que j’en vais faire.*”

===Chinese===
“Gr-r-rangeur—Eschingan—eh bien—il entend. Et moi, j’entends, moi aussi. Tu veux me jouer contre elle. La Grangeur—pah! Consoles-toi avec elle, mon vieux. Je ne veux plus de toi. Tu m’as donné de tes sales rentes Groenlandoises, et je n’ai pas pu les vendre. Ah, vieux farceur, tu vas voir ce que j’en vais faire.（格兰——若——埃辛冈——好嘛——他听得见。我呢，我也听得见。你想拿我当筹码去对付她。勒·格兰若——呸！老东西，你找她去彼此安慰吧。我不要再要你了。你把你那些臭格陵兰债券塞给我，我一张都脱不了手。啊，老滑头，你等着瞧我拿它们怎么办。）”

===Original===
A glorious creature﻿—a really glorious creature﻿—came out of an adjoining room. She was as frail, as swaying as a garden lily. Her great blue eyes turned irefully upon me, her bowed lips parted, her nostrils quivered.

===Chinese===
隔壁房间里走出来一位光彩照人的造物——一位真正光彩照人的造物。她纤弱而摇曳，像园中一株百合。她那双蓝色的大眼睛怒冲冲地转向我，噘起的双唇分开了，鼻翼翕动。

===Original===
“*Et quant à vous, M. Grangeur Eschingan*,” she began, “*je vais vous donner mon idée à moi﻿ ﻿…*”

===Chinese===
“Et quant à vous, M. Grangeur Eschingan（至于您，格兰若·埃辛冈先生），”她开口道，“je vais vous donner mon idée à moi …（我这就把我的主意告诉您……）”

===Original===
I did not understand the situation in the least, but I appreciated the awkwardness of it. The world seemed to be standing on its head. I was overcome; but I felt for the person in the next room. I did not know what to do. Suddenly I found myself saying:

===Chinese===
我全然不明白这是怎样一种局面，却掂得出其中的尴尬。世界像是倒了个个儿。我狼狈不堪；可心里又同情隔壁房里的那位。我不知道该怎样才好。忽然间，我发觉自己开口说道：

===Original===
“I am extremely sorry, madam, but I don’t understand French.” An expression of more intense vexation passed into her face﻿—her beautiful face. I fancy she wished﻿—wished intensely﻿—to give me the benefit of her “*idée à elle*.” She made a quick, violent gesture of disgusted contempt, and turned toward the half-open door from which she had come. She began again to dilate upon the little weaknesses of the person behind, when silently and swiftly it closed. We heard the lock click. With extraordinary quickness she had her mouth at the keyhole: “*Peeg, peeg*,” she enunciated. Then she stood to her full height, her face became calm, her manner stately. She glided halfway across the room, paused, looked at me, and pointed toward the unmoving door.

===Chinese===
“非常抱歉，夫人，我不懂法语。”一种更强烈的恼怒掠过她的脸——她那张美丽的脸。我想她本是极想——极其想——让我领教她的“idée à elle（她自己的主意）”的。她迅疾而暴烈地做了个嫌恶轻蔑的手势，转身朝她出来的那扇半开的门走去。她又开始铺陈门后那人的种种小毛病，那门却无声而疾速地关上了。我们听见锁里咔哒一响。她以惊人的敏捷把嘴贴上锁眼：“Peeg, peeg（猪猡，猪猡）”，她一字一顿地念。随后她直起身子站定，脸色平静下来，仪态庄严。她翩然滑行到房间中央，停住，望着我，朝那扇纹丝不动的门指了一指。

===Original===
“*Peeg, peeg*,” she explained, mysteriously. I think she was warning me against the wiles of the person behind the door. I gazed into her great eyes. “I understand,” I said, gravely. She glided from the room. For me the incident supplied a welcome touch of comedy. I had leisure for thought. The door remained closed. It made the Duc a more real person for me. I had regarded him as a rather tiresome person in whom a pompous philanthropism took the place of human feelings. It amused me to be called *Le Grangeur*. It amused me, and I stood in need of amusement. Without it I might never have written the article on the Duc. I had started out that morning in a state of nervous irritation. I had wanted more than ever to have done with the thing, with the *Hour*, with journalism, with everything. But this little new experience buoyed me up, set my mind working in less morbid lines. I began to wonder whether de Mersch would funk, or whether he would take my non-comprehension of the woman’s tirades as a thing assured.

===Chinese===
“Peeg, peeg，”她神秘地解释道。我想她是在提醒我提防门后那人的诡诈。我凝视进她那双大眼睛。“我明白了。”我郑重地说。她翩然滑出了房间。这一幕于我恰好添了一笔受欢迎的喜剧色彩。我得了余暇可以思想。那扇门始终关着。它使公爵在我这里成了一个更真实的人物。此前我只把他看作一个相当讨厌的人，一身浮夸的慈善心肠顶替了人的感情。被人称作“勒·格兰若（Le Grangeur）”让我觉得好笑。这让我觉得好笑，而我正需要消遣。若没有这一笔，我或许根本写不出那篇关于公爵的文章。那天早晨出门时，我正处于一种神经质的烦躁之中。我前所未有地想同这件事一刀两断——同《时辰报》，同新闻行当，同一切。可这段小小的新经历让我振作起来，思路也转到不那么阴郁的轨道上去了。我开始琢磨，德·默施会不会临阵怯场，或者，他会不会把“我听不懂那女人的长篇斥骂”当作一件确凿无疑的事。

===Original===
The door at which I had entered, by which she had left, opened.

===Chinese===
我进来时走的那扇门——也是她出去的那扇——开了。

===Original===
He must have impressed me in some way or other that evening at the Churchills. He seemed a very stereotyped image in my memory. He spoke just as he had spoken, moved his hands just as I expected him to move them. He called for no modification of my views of his person. As a rule one classes a man so-and-so at first meeting, modifies the classification at each subsequent one, and so on. He seemed to be all affability, of an adipose turn. He had the air of the man of the world among men of the world; but none of the unconscious reserve of manner that one expects to find in the temporarily great. He had in its place a kind of sub-sulkiness, as if he regretted the pedestal from which he had descended.

===Chinese===
那天晚上在丘吉尔府上，他想必多少给过我一点印象。他在我的记忆里似乎是一幅非常刻板的图像。他说话正是先前那种说法，双手的动作也正是我料想中的那几动。他丝毫不需要我修正对自己这个人的看法。通常，人初次见面便把对方归入某类，此后每见一次修正一次分类，如此等等。他似乎满身和气，带着发福的势头。他有那种见惯世面的人置身于见惯世面者们中间的气派；却没有人们预期能在一时显贵身上看到的那种不自觉的矜持。取而代之的是一种隐隐的愠色，仿佛在惋惜他已经走下来的那座台座。

===Original===
In his slow commercial English he apologised for having kept me waiting; he had been taking the air of this fine morning, he said. He mumbled the words with his eyes on my waistcoat, with an air that accorded rather ill with the semblance of portentous probity that his beard conferred on him. But he set an eyeglass in his left eye immediately afterward, and looked straight at me as if in challenge. With a smiling “Don’t mention,” I tried to demonstrate that I met him halfway.

===Chinese===
他用他那口缓慢的商务英语道歉，说让我久等了；他说，方才他正趁着这晴好的早晨透透空气。他两眼盯着我的马甲咕哝出这几句话，那副神气，同他的胡须赋予他的俨然诚信的表象颇不相称。但他随即往左眼里架上一副单片眼镜，直勾勾地看着我，像是在下战书。我微笑着应了声“哪里的话”，想表明我是半路相迎的。

===Original===
“You want to interview me,” he said, blandly. “I am only too pleased. I suppose it is about my Arctic schemes that you wish to know. I will do what I can to inform you. You perhaps remember what I said when I had the pleasure of meeting you at the house of the Right Honourable Mr. Churchill. It has been the dream of my life to leave behind me a happy and contented State﻿—as much as laws and organisation can make one. This is what I should most like the English to know of me.” He was a dull talker. I supposed that philanthropists and state founders kept their best faculties for their higher pursuits. I imagined the low, receding forehead and the pink-nailed, fleshy hands to belong to a new Solon, a latter-day Aeneas. I tried to work myself into the properly enthusiastic frame of mind. After all, it was a great work that he had undertaken. I was too much given to dwell upon intellectual gifts. These the Duc seemed to lack. I credited him with having let them be merged in his one noble idea.

===Chinese===
“你是要来采访我，”他温和地说，“我高兴还来不及呢。我想，你要了解的是我的北极各项计划。我愿尽力让你知情。你也许还记得，我有幸在尊敬的丘吉尔先生阁下府上见到你时说过的话。在我身后留下一个幸福而知足的国家——尽法律与组织所能造就的那样一个——是我毕生的梦想。这是我最想让英国人了解的关于我的一切。”他是个乏味的谈客。我想，慈善家和建国者们总把最好的才具留给他们更崇高的追求。我想象着那低平后缩的额头和粉红指甲的厚实的手，属于一位新的梭伦（Solon）、一位当代的埃涅阿斯（Aeneas）。我努力使自己进入恰如其分的热情心境。毕竟，他着手的是一项伟大的事业。我太惯于计较智识上的禀赋了。这些，公爵似乎欠缺。我宁可相信，他已经让它们溶进了自己那唯一的高贵理想里。

===Original===
He furnished me with statistics. They had laid down so many miles of railways, used so many engines of British construction. They had taught the natives to use and to value sewing-machines and European costumes. So many hundred of English younger sons had gone to make their fortunes and, incidentally, to enlighten the Eskimo﻿—so many hundreds of French, of Germans, Greeks, Russians. All these lived and moved in harmony, employed, happy, free labourers, protected by the most rigid laws. Man-eating, fetish-worship, slavery had been abolished, stamped out. The great international society for the preservation of Polar freedom watched over all, suggested new laws, modified the old. The country was unhealthy, but not to men of clean lives﻿—*hominibus bonae voluntatis*. It asked for no others.

===Chinese===
他向我提供统计数字。他们铺了多少英里的铁路，用了多少台英国造的机车。他们教会土著使用并珍视缝纫机和欧式服装。多少百名英国人家的次子们到那里去发家，顺带教化爱斯基摩人——又有多少百名法国人、德国人、希腊人、俄国人。所有人都和睦地生活、活动，是受雇的、快乐的、自由的劳工，受着最严苛的法律保护。食人、拜物、奴隶制都已废除，被扑灭干净。那个保护极地自由的伟大国际社团监护着一切，提议新的法律，修改旧的。那地方有损健康，但只对不清白度日的人如此——对善意的人们（hominibus bonae voluntatis）则不然。它也不要别样的人。

===Original===
“I have had to endure much misrepresentation. I have been called names,” the Duc said.

===Chinese===
“我不得不忍受许许多多的曲解。我被人辱骂过。”公爵说。

===Original===
The figure of the lady danced before my eyes, lithe, supple﻿—a statue endued with the motion of a serpent. I seemed to see her sculptured white hand pointing to the closed door.

===Chinese===
那位女士的身影在我眼前舞动，轻盈、柔韧——一尊被赋予了蛇行动作的雕像。我仿佛看见她那只雕塑般的白手，正指着那扇关着的门。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” I said, “but one knows the people that call you names.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”我说，“不过是什么样的人在骂您，大家心里都有数。”

===Original===
“Well, then,” he answered, “it is your task to make them know the truth. Your nation has so much power. If it will only realise.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”他答道，“让他们知道真相，就是你的任务了。贵国有的是力量。只要它肯明白过来。”

===Original===
“I will do my best,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我一定尽力。”我说。

===Original===
I saw the apotheosis of the Press﻿—a Press that makes a State Founder suppliant to a man like myself. For he had the tone of a deprecating petitioner. I stood between himself and a people, the arbiter of the peoples, of the kings of the future. I was nothing, nobody; yet here I stood in communion with one of those who change the face of continents. He had need of me, of the power that was behind me. It was strange to be alone in that room with that man﻿—to be there just as I might be in my own little room alone with any other man.

===Chinese===
我看见了报刊的神化——一种竟能让一位建国者向我这号人屈身相求的报刊。因为他的口气，正是一个低声下气的哀求者的口气。我立在他与一个民族之间，是万民的仲裁者，未来诸王的仲裁者。我什么也不是，是个无名之辈；可我竟站在这里，同一位改换大陆面貌的人物心意相通。他有求于我，有求于我背后的力量。同那个人单独待在那间屋子里——就像我可能在自己那间小屋里同随便哪个人单独待着一样——真是奇怪。

===Original===
I was not unduly elated, you must understand. It was nothing to me. I was just a person elected by some suffrage of accidents. Even in my own eyes I was merely a symbol﻿—the sign visible of incomprehensible power.

===Chinese===
你得明白，我并没有得意忘形。这在我算不得什么。我不过是被某种偶然凑成的普选推举出来的人物。哪怕在我自己眼里，我也只是一个象征——一种莫测的力量那看得见的符号。

===Original===
“I will do my best,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我一定尽力。”我说。

===Original===
“Ah, yes, do,” he said, “Mr. Churchill told me how nicely you can do such things.”

===Chinese===
“啊，是的，务必，”他说，“丘吉尔先生告诉过我，这类事情你能办得多漂亮。”

===Original===
I said that it was very kind of Mr. Churchill. The tension of the conversation was relaxed. The Duc asked if I had yet seen my aunt.

===Chinese===
我说丘吉尔先生真是费心了。谈话的紧张松弛了下来。公爵问我，可曾去见过我的姑母。

===Original===
“I had forgotten her,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我把她给忘了。”我说。

===Original===
“Oh, you must see her,” he said; “she is a most remarkable lady. She is one of my relaxations. All Paris talks about her, I can assure you.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你一定要去看看她，”他说，“她是位非常了不起的女士。她是我的一种消遣。全巴黎都在谈论她，我可以向你担保。”

===Original===
“I had no idea,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我一点儿也没想到。”我说。

===Original===
“Oh, cultivate her,” he said; “you will be amused.”

===Chinese===
“哦，你要多多亲近她，”他说，“你会觉得有趣的。”

===Original===
“I will,” I said, as I took my leave.

===Chinese===
“我会的。”我告辞时说。

===Original===
I went straight home to my little room above the roofs. I began at once to write my article, working at high pressure, almost hysterically. I remember that place and that time so well. In moments of emotion one gazes fixedly at things, hardly conscious of them. Afterward one remembers.

===Chinese===
我径直回到我那间高踞屋顶之上的小屋，立刻动笔写那篇文章，高负荷地赶写，近乎歇斯底里。那个地方、那段时光，我记得那么清楚。人在情绪激动的时刻会死死盯着事物看，几乎意识不到它们。事后才记得。

===Original===
I can still see the narrow room, the bare, brown, discoloured walls, the incongruous marble clock on the mantelpiece, the single rickety chair that swayed beneath me. I could almost draw the tortuous pattern of the faded cloth that hid the round table at which I sat. The ink was thick, pale, and sticky; the pen spluttered. I wrote furiously, anxious to be done with it. Once I went and leaned over the balcony, trying to hit on a word that would not come. Miles down below, little people crawled over the cobbled street, little carts rattled, little workmen let down casks into a cellar. It was all very grey, small, and clear.

===Chinese===
我至今还看得见那间狭窄的屋子，光秃秃的、褐色的、变了色的墙壁，壁炉架上那座不伦不类的大理石钟，我身子底下那把摇摇晃晃的独一破椅。我几乎能把罩着我伏案那张圆桌的褪色桌布上曲折的纹样画出来。墨水稠得发白、黏黏糊糊；笔尖直溅墨。我狂乱地写着，只求赶紧写完。有一回我走到阳台边俯身张望，想逮住一个偏不肯来的词。远远的下界，小小的人影在鹅卵石街上爬动，小小的车子嗒嗒作响，小小的工人把酒桶下放进地窖。一切都那么灰色、微小、清晰。

===Original===
Through the open window of an opposite garret I could see a sculptor working at a colossal clay model. In his white blouse he seemed big, out of all proportion to the rest of the world. Level with my eyes there were flat lead roofs and chimneys. On one of these was scrawled, in big, irregular, blue-painted letters: “*A bas Coignet.*”

===Chinese===
透过对面一间阁楼敞开的窗子，我看得见一位雕塑家正在制作一座巨大的黏土模型。穿着白色罩衫的他显得硕大无朋，同世界上其余的一切全然不成比例。与我视线齐平的地方，是一片片铅皮平屋顶和烟囱。其中一面屋顶上，用蓝漆歪歪扭扭地涂着几个大字：“A bas Coignet（打倒科涅）。”

===Original===
Great clouds began to loom into view over the housetops, rounded, toppling masses of grey, lit up with sullen orange against the pale limpid blue of the sky. I stood and looked at all these objects. I had come out here to think﻿—thoughts had deserted me. I could only look.

===Chinese===
大团大团的云开始从屋脊后面隐隐浮现，一团团浑圆的、摇摇欲坠的灰色，透着阴沉的橘色光，衬在天空苍白的澄蓝上。我站着，望着这一切景物。我到外头来是为了思考——思想却弃我而去。我只能看。

===Original===
The clouds moved imperceptibly, fatefully onward, a streak of lightning tore them apart. They whirled like tortured smoke and grew suddenly black. Large spots of rain with jagged edges began to fall on the lead floor of my balcony.

===Chinese===
云几乎不可觉察地、宿命般地向前推移，一道闪电把它们撕开。它们像受刑的浓烟般翻卷，骤然变黑。大颗大颗边缘参差的雨点，开始落在我阳台的铅皮地面上。

===Original===
I turned into the twilight of my room and began to write. I can still feel the tearing of my pen-point on the coarse paper. It was a hindrance to thought, but my flow of words ignored it, gained impetus from it, as a stream does at the breaking of a dam.

===Chinese===
我转身回到我屋内的昏暗里，开始写作。我至今还能感到笔尖在粗糙纸面上撕刮的感觉。它妨碍思想，可我的语流不理会它，反而从它那里获得冲力，正像溪流在大坝溃决时那样。

===Original===
I was writing a paean to a great coloniser. That sort of thing was in the air then. I was drawn into it, carried away by my subject. Perhaps I let it do so because it was so little familiar to my lines of thought. It was fresh ground and I revelled in it. I committed myself to that kind of emotional, lyrical outburst that one dislikes so much on rereading. I was half conscious of the fact, but I ignored it.

===Chinese===
我在给一位伟大的殖民者写颂歌。那一类东西当年正弥漫在空气里。我被它裹挟进去，被我的题材卷走了。也许我任其如此，是因为它同我的思路太不熟。那是一片新鲜的土地，我在里面纵情徜徉。我纵身投入了那类感情的、抒情的迸发——就是人们重读时最厌恶的那一类。这一点我半有所觉，却置之不理。

===Original===
The thunderstorm was over, and there was a moist sparkling freshness in the air when I hurried with my copy to the *Hour* office in the Avenue de l’Opéra. I wished to be rid of it, to render impossible all chance of revision on the morrow.

===Chinese===
雷雨过去了，空气里带着湿润的、晶亮的新鲜，我带上稿子，匆匆赶往歌剧院大道（Avenue de l'Opéra）上《时辰报》的报馆。我要把它脱手，叫明天再没有任何修改的机会。

===Original===
I wanted, too, to feel elated; I expected it. It was a right. At the office I found the foreign correspondent, a little cosmopolitan Jew whose eyebrows began their growth on the bridge of his nose. He was effusive and familiar, as the rest of his kind.

===Chinese===
我也想要感到意气飞扬；我期待着它。那是一种权利。在报馆里，我遇见了那位驻外记者——一个世界主义的小个子犹太人，他的两道眉毛打鼻梁上就开始生长。他热情而自来熟，同他那一类人一个样。

===Original===
“Hullo, Granger,” was his greeting. I was used to regarding myself as fallen from a high estate, but I was not yet so humble in spirit as to relish being called Granger by a stranger of his stamp. I tried to freeze him politely.

===Chinese===
“喂，格兰杰，”这是他的招呼。我惯于把自己看作从高位跌落的人，可精神上还没有卑贱到乐于被他这号陌生人直呼“格兰杰”。我试图客客气气地把他冷下去。

===Original===
“Read your stuff in the *Hour*,” was his rejoinder; “jolly good I call it. Been doing old Red-Beard? Let’s have a look. Yes, yes. That’s the way﻿—that’s the real thing﻿—I call it. Must have bored you to death﻿ ﻿… old de Mersch I mean. I ought to have had the job, you know. My business, interviewing people in Paris. But *I* don’t mind. Much rather you did it than I. You do it a heap better.”

===Chinese===
“在《时辰报》上读了你写的东西，”他应道，“要我说，好得很。是去写老红胡子（old Red-Beard）了吧？让我瞧瞧。是的，是的。就是这个路数——这才是真货——要我说。准把你烦得要死……我是说老德·默施。这差事本来该归我的，你知道。在巴黎采访人物，是我的本行。不过我可是满不在乎。你去做比我去做好多了。你干这个比我强一大截。”

===Original===
I murmured thanks. There was a pathos about the sleek little man﻿—a pathos that is always present in the type. He seemed to be trying to assume a deprecating equality.

===Chinese===
我含含糊糊地道了谢。这个油光水滑的小个子男人身上有一种哀婉——这种类型的人身上总有的一种哀婉。他似乎在竭力摆出一副贬抑自己的平等姿态。

===Original===
“Where are you going tonight?” he asked, with sudden effusiveness. I was taken aback. One is not used to being asked these questions after five minutes’ acquaintance. I said that I had no plans.

===Chinese===
“你今晚去哪儿？”他忽然热络起来，问道。我吃了一惊。相识不过五分钟就被人问这类问题，谁也惯不了。我说我没有什么安排。

===Original===
“Look here,” he said, brightening up, “come and have dinner with me at Breguet’s, and look in at the Opera afterward. We’ll have a real nice chat.”

===Chinese===
“我说，”他喜形于色地说，“跟我到布雷盖（Breguet's）去吃顿晚饭，回头再顺道去歌剧院看看。咱们好好聊一聊。”

===Original===
I was too tired to frame an adequate excuse. Besides, the little man was as eager as a child for a new toy. We went to Breguet’s and had a really excellent dinner.

===Chinese===
我累得编不出一个像样的借口。再说，这小人儿正像盼新玩具的孩子一样热切。我们去了布雷盖，吃了一顿着实出色的晚饭。

===Original===
“Always come here,” he said; “one meets a lot of swells. It runs away with a deal of money﻿—but I don’t care to do things on the cheap, not for the *Hour*, you know. You can always be certain when I say that I have a thing from a senator that he is a senator, and not an old woman in a paper kiosk. Most of them do that sort of thing, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我总来这儿，”他说，“能碰上好些头面人物。钱花起来像流水——可为《时辰报》办事，你知道，我不乐意小家子气。我说哪件事是从一位参议员那里得来的，你就尽管放心那准是个参议员，不是报刊亭里的一个老太太。他们那班人多半都干这种事，你知道。”

===Original===
“I always wondered,” I said, mildly.

===Chinese===
“我向来就纳闷来着。”我温和地说。

===Original===
“That’s de Sourdam I nodded to as we came in, and that old chap there is Pluyvis﻿—the Affaire man, you know. I must have a word with him in a minute, if you’ll excuse me.”

===Chinese===
“我们进来的时候我点头招呼的那位，是德苏尔当（de Sourdam）；那边那个老家伙是普吕伊维斯（Pluyvis）——德雷福斯案（the Affaire）的那位，你知道。恕我失陪片刻，回头我得跟他说句话。”

===Original===
He began to ask affectionately after the health of the excellent Fox, asked if I saw him often, and so on and so on. I divined with amusement that was pleasurable that the little man had his own little axe to grind, and thought I might take a turn at the grindstone if he managed me well. So he nodded to de Sourdam of the Austrian embassy and had his word with Pluyvis, and rejoiced to have impressed me﻿—I could see him bubble with happiness and purr. He proposed that we should stroll as far as the paper kiosk that he patronised habitually﻿—it was kept by a fellow-Israelite﻿—a snuffy little old woman.

===Chinese===
他开始亲亲热热地问候那位出色的福克斯的贵体康健，问我是否常常见到他，如此等等，不一而足。我带着愉快的兴味猜出，这小人儿自有他的小算盘要打，还想着只要把我调理得服帖，我兴许也能替他推几圈磨。于是他朝奥地利使馆的德苏尔当点了点头，同普吕伊维斯说上了话，并为让我开了眼界而欢喜——我看得出他乐得直冒泡，像猫一样咕噜咕噜直念满足经。他提议，我们溜达着走到他素来光顾的那个报刊亭去——那是一个犹太同胞开的——一个沾着鼻烟的干瘦小老太太。

===Original===
I understood that in the joy of his heart he was for expanding, for wasting a few minutes on a stroll.

===Chinese===
我明白，他是心里高兴，想要舒展舒展，乐意在散步上消磨几分钟。

===Original===
“Haven’t stretched my legs for months,” he explained.

===Chinese===
“好几个月没伸伸腿了。”他解释道。

===Original===
We strolled there through the summer twilight. It was so pleasant to saunter through the young summer night. There were so many little things to catch the eyes, so many of the little things down near the earth; expressions on faces of the passers, the set of a collar, the quaint foreign tightness of waist of a good bourgeoise who walked arm in arm with her perspiring spouse. The gilding on the statue of Joan of Arc had a pleasant littleness of Philistinism, the arcades of the Rue de Rivoli broke up the grey light pleasantly too. I remembered a little shop﻿—a little Greek affair with a windowful of pinchbeck﻿—where I had been given a false five-franc piece years and years ago. The same villainous old Levantine stood in the doorway, perhaps the fez that he wore was the same fez. The little old woman that we strolled to was bent nearly double. Her nose touched her wares as often as not, her mittened hands sought quiveringly the papers that the correspondent asked for. I liked him the better for his solicitude for this forlorn piece of flotsam of his own race.

===Chinese===
我们穿过夏日的暮色，一路信步走去。在初夏的夜里闲逛，是这样惬意。有那么多小东西扑进眼里，那么多贴近地面的小东西：过路人脸上的表情，一只衣领的样子，一位体面的市民太太腰身那种古怪的外国式紧束——她正挽着她那汗涔涔的丈夫散步。圣女贞德（Joan of Arc）像上的鎏金，有一种讨人喜欢的市侩式小巧；里沃利街（Rue de Rivoli）的拱廊把灰蒙蒙的光线切碎，也切得颇为宜人。我想起一家小铺子——一家希腊人的小店，一橱窗的假金饰物——许多许多年以前，我在那里被人找给过一枚假的五法郎。同一个恶棍相的老黎凡特人（Levantine）站在门口，也许他头上那顶土耳其毡帽，还是当年那一顶。我们信步走去的那个干瘦小老太太，腰弯得几乎对折。她的鼻子动不动就碰到她的货品，她那双戴露指手套的手哆哆嗦嗦地摸索着记者要的报纸。他对自己种族里这件孤苦无依的漂流遗物这般挂心，我因此更喜欢他了些。

===Original===
“Always come here,” he exclaimed; “one gets into habits. Very honest woman, too, you can be certain of getting your change. If you’re a stranger you can’t be sure that they won’t give you Italian silver, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我总上这儿来，”他扬声道，“人都有个习惯。这老太太又非常诚实，找你的零钱错不了。你要是个生人，可就保不齐他们不找给你意大利银币，你知道。”

===Original===
“Oh, I know,” I answered. I knew, too, that he wished me to purchase something. I followed the course of her groping hands, caught sight of the *Revue Rouge*, and remembered that it contained something about Greenland. I helped myself to it, paid for it, and received my just change. I felt that I had satisfied the little man, and felt satisfied with myself.

===Chinese===
“哦，这我知道。”我答道。我也知道，他是想让我买点什么。我顺着她那双摸索的手的去路，瞥见了一本《红色评论》（Revue Rouge），记起那上面有点关于格陵兰的东西。我自己动手取了一本，付了钱，如数收回了我该找的零头。我觉得我让这小人儿称了心，自己也颇为满意。

===Original===
“I want to see Radet’s article on Greenland,” I said.

===Chinese===
“我想看看拉代（Radet）那篇论格陵兰的文章。”我说。

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” he explained, once more exhibiting himself in the capacity of the man who knows, “Radet gives it to them. Rather a lark, I call it, though you mustn’t let old de Mersch know you read him. Radet got sick of Cochin, and tried Greenland. He’s getting touched by the Whites you know. They say that the priests don’t like the way the *Système*’s playing into the hands of the Protestants and the English Government. So they set Radet on to write it down.
