He’s going in for mysticism and all that sort of thing﻿—just like all these French jokers are doing. Got deuced thick with that lot in the F. St. Germain﻿—some relation of yours, ain’t they? Rather a lark that lot, quite the thing just now, everyone goes there; old de Mersch too. Have frightful rows sometimes, such a mixed lot, you see.” The good little man rattled amiably along beside me.

===Chinese===
“哦，是啊，”他解释道，又一次把自己摆出万事通的架势，“拉代笔下可真给他们厉害瞧。要我说，相当逗乐，不过你可别让老德·默施知道你读他的东西。拉代写交趾支那（Cochin）写腻了，就改试格陵兰。他呀，正叫那班白衣神父（the Whites）熏染得有点神魂颠倒，你知道。据说神父们不喜欢那套体系把好处白白送进新教徒和英国政府手里的做法。所以他们便支使拉代去把它写垮。他如今搞起神秘主义和那一类名堂来了——正跟所有那些法国活宝一个样。跟圣日耳曼区（the F. St. Germain，即 Faubourg Saint Germain）那一伙打得火热——跟你算沾亲带故吧，嗯？那伙人相当逗乐，眼下正时兴，谁都知道往那儿跑；老德·默施也去。有时候吵得天翻地覆，那一伙杂得很，你知道。”这位好心的小个子在我身边嗒嗒嗒地一路说个不停，和和气气。

===Original===
“Seems quite funny to be buying books,” he said. “I haven’t read a thing I’ve bought, not for years.”

===Chinese===
“买起书来，显得怪有意思的，”他说，“我买来的东西，一样也没读过，好些年了。”

===Original===
We reached the Opera in time for the end of the first act﻿—it was Aïda, I think. My little friend had a free pass all over the house. I had not been in it for years. In the old days I had always seen the stage from a great height, craning over people’s heads in a sultry twilight; now I saw it on a level, seated at my ease. I had only the power of the Press to thank for the change.

===Chinese===
我们赶到歌剧院，正赶上第一幕的收尾——记得演的是《阿依达》（Aïda）。我的小朋友有一张全场通行的免票。我已经好几年没进过那里了。从前我总是从高得多的地方看舞台，在闷热的幽暗里越过众人的头顶伸长脖子看；如今我平视着舞台，安安逸逸地坐着。这变化，我只得感谢报刊的力量。

===Original===
“Come here as often as I can,” my companion said; “can’t do without music when it’s to be had.” Indeed he had the love of his race for it. It seemed to soften him, to change his nature, as he sat silent by my side.

===Chinese===
“能来就来，”我的同伴说，“有音乐听的时候，缺了它可不行。”的确，他有着他那个种族对音乐的热爱。他默默坐在我身边的时候，音乐似乎把他软化了下来，改变了他的天性。

===Original===
But the closing notes of each scene found him out in the cool of the corridors, talking, and being talked to by anyone that would vouchsafe him a word.

===Chinese===
可每一幕落幕的乐音一起，他人准已到了走廊的凉快地方，正同人说这话；不论谁肯赏他一句话，他也听人家同他说。

===Original===
“Pick up a lot here,” he explained.

===Chinese===
“在这儿能捡着不少东西。”他解释道。

===Original===
After the finale we leaned over one of the side balconies to watch the crowd streaming down the marble staircases. It is a scene that I never tire of. There is something so fantastically tawdry in the coloured marble of the architecture. It is for all the world like a triumph of ornamental soap work; one expects to smell the odours. And the torrent of humanity pouring liquidly aslant through the mirror-like light, and the spaciousness.﻿ ﻿… Yes, it is fantastic, somehow; ironical, too.

===Chinese===
终场之后，我们俯在侧楼厅的一处栏杆上，看人流沿着大理石楼梯倾泻而下。这景象我百看不厌。那建筑的彩色大理石里，有一种何等荒诞的俗艳。它活脱脱像装饰皂雕的登峰造极之作；人简直要以为闻得见那股香气。还有那人的洪流，液体一般斜斜地淌过镜面似的光，还有那份空阔……是的，这景象总有点荒诞；也带点讽刺。

===Original===
I was watching the devious passage of a rather drunken, gigantic, florid Englishman, wondering, I think, how he would reach his bed.

===Chinese===
我正望着一个相当醉醺醺、高大、红脸膛的英国人曲里拐弯地穿行，心里想着——大概是想着——他怎么才摸得回自己的床。

===Original===
“That must be a relation of yours,” the correspondent said, pointing. My glance followed the line indicated by his pale finger. I made out the glorious beard of the Duc de Mersch; on his arm was an old lady to whom he seemed to pay deferential attention. His head was bent on one side; he was smiling frankly. A little behind them, on the stairway, there was a space. Perhaps I was mistaken; perhaps there was no space﻿—I don’t know. I was only conscious of a figure, an indescribably clear-cut woman’s figure, gliding down the way. It had a coldness, a self-possession, a motion of its own. In that clear, transparent, shimmering light, every little fold of the dress, every little shadow of the white arms, the white shoulders, came up to me. The face turned up to meet mine. I remember so well the light shining down on the face, not a shadow anywhere, not a shadow beneath the eyebrows, the nostrils, the waves of hair. It was a vision of light, theatening, sinister.

===Chinese===
“那准是您的一位亲戚。”记者指着说。我的目光顺着他那苍白的手指所点的方向望去。我认出了德·默施公爵那部辉煌的大胡子；他手臂上挽着一位老夫人，对她似乎敬奉殷勤。他的头偏向一侧，笑得坦率。在他们身后不远的楼梯上，有一小片空当。也许我看错了；也许根本没有空当——我不知道。我只意识到一个身影，一个轮廓清晰得难以描述的女人身影，正顺着那道楼梯滑下来。它有一种冷淡，一种自持，一种属于它自己的动法。在那清澈、透明、微微闪烁的光里，衣裙的每一道细褶，白手臂、白肩膀上的每一小片阴影，都朝我涌上来。那张脸仰起来，迎住我的目光。我记得那么清楚：光洒在那张脸上，哪儿都没有阴影，眉下没有，鼻翼没有，发浪间也没有。那是一个光的幻象，咄咄逼人，凶险不祥。

===Original===
She smiled, her lips parted.

===Chinese===
她微笑了，双唇分开了。

===Original===
“You come to me tomorrow,” she said. Did I hear the words, did her lips merely form them? She was far, far down below me; the air was alive with the rustling of feet, of garments, of laughter, full of sounds that made themselves heard, full of sounds that would not be caught.

===Chinese===
“你明天到我这儿来。”她说。这话我究竟是听见的，还是只看见她的唇形拼出来的？她在我下面很远很远的地方；空气里满是脚步的窸窣、衣裳的窸窣、笑声，满是争先恐后让人听见的声音，也满是怎么也捕捉不住的声音。

===Original===
“You come to me﻿ ﻿… tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“你到我这儿来……明天。”

===Original===
The old lady on the Duc de Mersch’s arm was obviously my aunt. I did not see why I should not go to them tomorrow. It struck me suddenly and rather pleasantly that this was, after all, my family. This old lady actually was a connection more close than anyone else in the world. As for the girl, to all intents and, in everyone else’s eyes, she was my sister. I cannot say I disliked having her for my sister, either. I stood looking down upon them and felt less alone than I had done for many years.

===Chinese===
德·默施公爵手臂上挽着的那位老夫人，明摆着是我的老姑母。我看不出为什么明天不该到他们那儿去。我忽然——而且相当愉快地——意识到：这毕竟是我在世的亲人。这位老夫人，实实在在是比世上任何别人都更亲的一门血亲。至于那位姑娘，无论从哪方面说，在别人眼里，她都是我的姐妹。我也不能说，我不乐意有她做我的姐妹。我站在那里俯望着他们，多少年来第一次觉得不那么孤单了。

===Original===
A minute scuffle of the shortest duration was taking place beside me. There were a couple of men at my elbow. I don’t in the least know what they were﻿—perhaps marquises, perhaps railway employees﻿—one never can tell over there. One of them was tall and blond, with a heavy, bow-shaped red moustache﻿—Irish in type; the other of no particular height, excellently groomed, dark, and exemplary. I knew he was exemplary from some detail of costume that I can’t remember﻿—his gloves or a strip of silk down the sides of his trousers﻿—something of the sort. The blond was saying something that I did not catch. I heard the words “de Mersch” and “*Anglaise*,” and saw the dark man turn his attention to the little group below. Then I caught my own name mispronounced and somewhat of a stumbling-block to a high-pitched contemptuous intonation. The little correspondent, who was on my other arm, started visibly and moved swiftly behind my back.

===Chinese===
我身旁正发生着一场时间极短的轻微争执。我的肘边站着两个男人。他们究竟是干什么的，我全然无从知道——也许是两位侯爵，也许是铁路上的职员——在那边，谁也说不准。一个是高个金发，蓄着两撇浓重的弓形红胡子——爱尔兰型的；另一个个子平平，修饰得无可挑剔，深色头发，一派楷模样。我所以知道他是楷模，凭的是衣着上某个如今记不起来的细节——他的手套，或是裤腿外侧的丝条——诸如此类。金发那位正说着什么，我没有听清。我听见了“德·默施”和“Anglaise（英国女人）”两个词，又见黑发那位把注意力转向楼下那一小群人。接着，我听见自己的名字被人念得走了音，还磕磕绊绊，嵌在一种尖利的、轻蔑的语调里。那位小个子记者——他正挽着我的另一条手臂——明显地一惊，迅速闪到了我背后。

===Original===
“*Messieurs*,” he said in an urgent whisper, and drew them to a little distance. I saw him say something, saw them pivot to look at me, shrug their shoulders and walk away. I didn’t in the least grasp the significance of the scene﻿—not then.

===Chinese===
“Messieurs（二位先生），”他急促地悄声说，把他们引到稍远一点的地方。我看见他说了句什么，看见他们转过身来朝我看，耸耸肩，走开了。这场面的含义，我那时丝毫不曾领会——当时没有。

===Original===
“What’s the matter?” I asked my returning friend; “were they talking about me?” He answered nervously.

===Chinese===
“怎么回事？”我问回到我身边的朋友；“他们方才是在谈论我吗？”他答得慌慌张张。

===Original===
“Oh, it was about your aunt’s Salon, you know. They might have been going to say something awkward﻿ ﻿… one never knows.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是关于你姑母的沙龙，你知道。他们差点说出什么难听的话来……这种事谁也保不齐。”

===Original===
“They really *do* talk about it then?” I said. “I’ve a good mind to attend one of their exhibitions.”

===Chinese===
“那么他们当真是在谈论它了？”我说。“我倒真想去参观一回他们那种展览。”

===Original===
“Why, of course,” he said, “you ought. I really think you *ought*.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，当然啦，”他说，“你该去的。我真的觉得你该去。”

===Original===
“I’ll go tomorrow,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“我明天就去。”我答道。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
I couldn’t get to sleep that night, but lay and tossed, lit my candle and read, and so on, forever and ever﻿—for an eternity. I was confoundedly excited; there were a hundred things to be thought about; clamouring to be thought about; out-clamouring the recurrent chimes of some near clock. I began to read the article by Radet in the *Revue Rouge*﻿—the one I had bought of the old woman in the kiosk. It upset me a good deal﻿—that article. It gave away the whole Greenland show so completely that the ecstatic bosh I had just despatched to the *Hour* seemed impossible. I suppose the good Radet had his axe to grind﻿—just as I had had to grind the State Founder’s, but Radet’s axe didn’t show. I was reading about an inland valley, a broad, shadowy, grey thing; immensely broad, immensely shadowy, winding away between immense, half-invisible mountains into the silence of an unknown country. A little band of men, microscopic figures in that immensity, in those mists, crept slowly up it. A man among them was speaking; I seemed to hear his voice, low, monotonous, overpowered by the wan light and the silence and the vastness.

===Chinese===
那天夜里我怎么也睡不着，只是躺着辗转反侧，点了蜡烛看看书，如此等等，没完没了——像是熬着一个永恒。我兴奋得不可开交；有一百件事要想，吵嚷着要想，嚷得盖过了近处某座钟一遍又一遍的报时。我开始读拉代（Radet）在《红色评论》（Revue Rouge）上的那篇文章——就是我从报亭老妇人手里买来的那一本。那篇文章搅得我心绪大乱。它把整个格陵兰把戏拆穿得那样彻底，我方才发往《时辰报》的那篇欣喜若狂的昏话，竟显得全然站不住脚。我想，好心的拉代也有他自己要磨的斧子——正像我此前不得不替建国者磨斧一样，只是拉代的斧子看不出来。我读到一处内陆山谷：一片开阔、幽暗、灰蒙蒙的所在；无限地开阔，无限地幽暗，在无限而半隐半现的群山之间蜿蜒远去，没入一个未知国度的沉寂。一小队人马，在那浩瀚里、那雾霭里，只是几粒显微镜下的身影，正沿着山谷缓缓向上爬。队伍中有一个人在说话；我仿佛听得见他的声音，低沉，单调，被惨淡的光、被沉寂与浩瀚压住了。

===Original===
And how well it was done﻿—how the man could write; how skilfully he made his points. There was no slosh about it, no sentiment. The touch was light, in places even gay. He saw so well the romance of that dun band that had cast remorse behind; that had no return, no future, that spread desolation desolately. This was merely a review article﻿—a thing that in England would have been unreadable; the narrative of a nomad of some genius. I could never have written like that﻿—I should have spoilt it somehow. It set me tingling with desire, with the desire that transcends the sexual; the desire for the fine phrase, for the right word﻿—for all the other intangibles. And I had been wasting all this time; had been writing my inanities. I must go away; must get back, right back to the old road, must work. There was so little time. It was unpleasant, too, to have been mixed up in this affair, to have been trepanned into doing my best to help it on its foul way. God knows I had little of the humanitarian in me. If people must murder in the byways of an immense world they must do murder and pay the price. But that I should have been mixed up in such was not what I had wanted. I must have done with it all; with all this sort of thing, must get back to my old self, must get back. I seemed to hear the slow words of the Duc de Mersch.

===Chinese===
写得又是多么好啊——这个人多么会写；下手多么巧妙。里头没有一点儿水分，没有一点儿感伤。笔触轻灵，有的地方甚至近乎快活。他把那一小队褐色人马身上的浪漫体察得那么透——那伙人已把悔恨抛在身后；没有回头路，没有前程，一路散播着荒凉，散播得一片荒凉。这不过是一篇评论文章——在英国，这种东西会叫人读不下去；是一个颇有天才的浪游者的记叙。我永远写不出那样的文字——我总会不知怎么把它糟蹋了。它叫我浑身为欲望震颤，为那种超越情欲的欲望震颤：对佳句的欲望，对那个恰切的词的欲望——对其余一切无形之物的欲望。而我竟一直把工夫都荒废了，一直在写我的那些无聊玩意儿。我必须走开；必须回头，一径退回旧日的路上，必须用功。时间已经那么少了。再有，缠进这桩事里，被人诱着竭尽全力送它在它那条肮脏路上往前走，也实在叫人不痛快。天知道我心里本没有多少人道主义的成分。要是人们非得在一个浩瀚世界的偏僻小径上杀人，那就让他们杀，让他们付代价好了。可我竟被缠进了这样的事里，这原不是我所想要的。我必须同这一切做个了断；同这一类的一切事，必须找回旧日的我，必须回头。我仿佛听得见德·默施公爵缓慢的话语。

===Original===
“We have increased exports by so much; the imports by so much. We have protected the natives, have kept their higher interests ever present in our minds. And through it all we have never forgotten the mission entrusted to us by Europe﻿—to remove the evil of darkness from the earth﻿—to root out barbarism with its nameless horrors, whose existence has been a blot on our consciences. Men of goodwill and self-sacrifice are doing it now﻿—are laying down their priceless lives to root out﻿ ﻿… to root out.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我们让出口增长了这么多；进口增长了这么多。我们保护了土著，时时刻刻把他们的更高利益放在心上。而贯穿这一切，我们从未忘记欧洲托付给我们的使命——把黑暗之恶从大地上除去——铲除那连同无数无名恐怖的野蛮，它的存在素来是我们良心上的一块污渍。怀着善意与自我牺牲的人们此刻正在做这件事——正抛洒着他们无价的性命，去铲除……去铲除……”

===Original===
Of course they *were* rooting them out.

===Chinese===
当然，他们铲除的正是人。

===Original===
It didn’t matter to me. One supposes that that sort of native exists for that sort of thing﻿—to be rooted out by men of goodwill, with careers to make. The point was that that was what they were really doing out there﻿—rooting out the barbarians as well as the barbarism, and proving themselves worthy of their hire. And I had been writing them up and was no better than the farcical governor of a department who would write on the morrow to protest that that was what they did not do. You see I had a sort of personal pride in those days; and preferred to think of myself as a decent person. I knew that people would say the same sort of thing about me that they said about all the rest of them. I couldn’t very well protest. I *had* been scratching the backs of all sorts of creatures; out of friendship, out of love﻿—for all sorts of reasons. This was only a sort of last straw﻿—or perhaps it was the sight of her that had been the last straw. It seemed naively futile to have been wasting my time over Mrs. Hartly and those she stood for, when there was something so different in the world﻿—something so like a current of east wind.

===Chinese===
这与我没什么相干。人们不妨这样想：那种土著生来就是为那种事的——等着被胸怀善意、又还要建功立业的好心人铲除。要紧的是，他们在那边真正干着的正是这个——把野蛮人连同野蛮一起铲除掉，并证明自己受酬无愧。而我一直在替他们写颂扬文章，同外省那位滑稽的省长没什么两样——那人明天就会写信来抗议，说他们绝没有干这种事。你要知道，我那些日子里还有几分个人的自尊，宁愿把自己看作一个正派人。我知道，人家说起我，会同说起其余所有那些人一样，说同一套话。我没什么好抗议的。我确确实实替各式各样的货色搔过背——出于友谊，出于爱情——出于各式各样的缘故。这不过是又一根压下来的稻草——或者说，压下来的也许竟是见到她那一眼。我竟一直把工夫浪费在哈特利太太和她所代表的那一类人物身上，而世上分明还有那样不同的东西——某种如此像一股东风气流的东西——现在想来，这真是天真得徒劳。

===Original===
That vein of thought kept me awake, and a worse came to keep it company. The men from the next room came home﻿—students, I suppose. They talked gaily enough, their remarks interspersed by the thuds of falling boots and the other incomprehensible noises of the night. Through the flimsy partition I caught half sentences in that sort of French intonation that is so impossible to attain. It reminded me of the voices of the two men at the Opera. I began to wonder what they had been saying﻿—what they could have been saying that concerned me and affected the little correspondent to interfere. Suddenly the thing dawned upon me with the startling clearness of a figure in a complicated pattern﻿—a clearness from which one cannot take one’s eyes.

===Chinese===
这一线思绪让我清醒无眠，又来了一条更糟的与它作伴。隔壁房里的男人们回来了——大概是几个学生。他们谈得相当快活，话音间夹着靴子落地的钝响和夜里其他莫名其妙的声响。透过薄薄的隔板，我捕捉到一些半截句子，是那种法国腔调——一种怎么也学不来的腔调。它让我想起歌剧院里那两个人的声音。我开始琢磨他们当时说了些什么——他们能说出什么与我有关、又惹得那位小个子记者要出面拦阻的话。忽然，整件事像繁复花纹里蓦地显出的一个图形，带着触目的清晰向我亮了出来——一种让人无法移开目光的清晰。

===Original===
It threw everything﻿—the whole world﻿—into more unpleasant relations with me than even the Greenland affair. They had not been talking about my aunt and her Salon, but about my﻿ ﻿… my sister. She was De Mersch’s “*Anglaise*.” I did not believe it, but probably all Paris﻿—the whole world﻿—said she was. And to the whole world I was her brother! Those two men who had looked at me over their shoulders had shrugged and said, “Oh, *he’s*﻿ ﻿…” And the whole world wherever I went would whisper in asides, “Don’t you know Granger? He’s the brother. De Mersch employs him.”

===Chinese===
它把一切——把整个世界——抛进了比格陵兰那桩事更叫我难堪的、与我相干的关系里。他们谈论的根本不是我老姑母和她的沙龙，而是我的……我的姐妹。她是德·默施的“英国女人”（Anglaise）。我不信，可多半整个巴黎——整个世界——都咬定她是。而在整个世界眼里，我是她的兄弟！那两个回头打量过我的人，想必耸了耸肩，说：“哦，他呀……”而今后无论我走到哪里，整个世界都会附耳低语：“你认识格兰杰吧？就是那位兄弟。德·默施雇着他呢。”

===Original===
I began to understand everything; the woman in de Mersch’s room with her “Eschingan-Grangeur-r-r”; the deference of the little Jew﻿—the man who knew. *He* knew that I﻿—that I, who patronised him, was a person to stand well with because of my﻿—my sister’s hold over de Mersch. I wasn’t, of course, but you can’t understand how the whole thing maddened me all the same. I hated the world﻿—this world of people who whispered and were whispered to, of men who knew and men who wanted to know﻿—the shadowy world of people who didn’t matter, but whose eyes and voices were all round one and did somehow matter. I knew well enough how it had come about. It was de Mersch﻿—the State Founder, with his shamed face and his pallid hands. She had been attracted by his air of greatness, by his elective grand-dukedom, by his protestations. Women are like that. She had been attracted and didn’t know what she was doing, didn’t know what the world was over here﻿—how people talked. She had been excited by the whirl and flutter of it, and perhaps she didn’t care. The thing must come to an end, however. She had said that I should go to her on the morrow. Well, I would go, and I would put a stop to this. I had suddenly discovered how very much I was a Granger of Etchingham, after all I *had* family traditions and graves behind me. And for the sake of all these people whose one achievement had been the making of a good name I *had* to intervene now. After all﻿—“*Bon sang ne*”﻿—does not get itself talked about in *that* way.

===Chinese===
我开始明白了一切：德·默施房里那个女人，和她那一声“埃辛冈-格兰若-r-r”；那个矮小犹太人的殷勤——那是个知情的人。他知道我——知道我这个平日居高临下照拂他的人，是个值得结好的人物，因为我——因为我的姐妹笼得住德·默施。当然并非如此，可你没法体会这件事仍旧把我气得发了狂。我恨这个世界——这个人人低语、又人人被低语的世界，这个有人知情、有人想知情的世界——这些无足轻重的幽暗人影，可他们的眼睛和嗓音团团围住你，不知怎么又偏能作数。这一切是怎么来的，我清楚得很。是德·默施——那位建国者，带着他那张羞惭的脸和他那双苍白的手。她被他那副伟人的气派迷住了，被他那顶选任大公的头衔迷住了，被他那一套信誓旦旦迷住了。女人就是如此。她被迷住了，却不知道自己在做什么，不知道这边的世界是个什么世界——不知道人家会怎么讲。她被这里的旋流与纷扰撩得兴奋，也许她根本不在乎。然而，这件事必须有个了结。她说过，明天我该到她那儿去。好，我就去，我要叫这一切停下来。我忽然发现，自己终究是何等彻头彻尾埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家的一个人——我背后到底是有家世传统、有一座座坟茔的。为了这许多人——他们毕生的功业无非挣下一个好名声——我如今不能不出头了。毕竟——好血统（Bon sang ne）——是不能让人那样去讲闲话的。

===Original===
The early afternoon of the morrow found me in a great room﻿—a faded, sombre salon of the house my aunt had taken in the Faubourg Saint Germain. Numbers of strong-featured people were talking in groups among the tables and chairs of a time before the Revolution. I rather forget how I had got there, and what had gone before. I must have arisen late and passed the intervening hours in a state of trepidation. I was going to see her, and I was like a cub in love, with a man’s place to fill. It was a preposterous state of things that set the solid world in a whirl. Once there, my eyes suddenly took in things.

===Chinese===
第二天午后不久，我已置身于一间大厅——老姑母在圣日耳曼区租下的宅子里一间褪色的、幽暗的客厅。好些面目浓重的人正三五成群，在革命前那个时代的桌椅之间谈话。我是怎么到的那里、先前又经过些什么，我差不多忘了。想必我起身很迟，中间那几个钟头是在惴惴不安里打发的。我要去见她了，而我像一头初坠情网的幼兽，却要去顶一个男人的位置。这是一种荒唐透顶的局面，把结结实实的世道搅得团团转。可一到那里，我的眼睛便忽然把四下种种收了进去。

===Original===
I had a sense of her standing by my side. She had just introduced me to my aunt﻿—a heavy-featured, tired-eyed village tyrant. She was so obviously worn out, so obviously “not what she had been,” that her face would have been pitiful but for its immovable expression of class pride. The Grangers of Etchingham, you see, were so absolutely at the top of their own particular kind of tree that it was impossible for them to meet anyone who was not an inferior. A man might be a cabinet minister, might even be a prince, but he couldn’t be a Granger of Etchingham, couldn’t have such an assortment of graves, each containing a Granger, behind his back. The expression didn’t even lift for me who had. It couldn’t, it was fixed there. One wondered what she was doing in this *galère*. It seemed impossible that she should interest herself in the restoration of the Bourbons﻿—they were all very well, but they weren’t even English, let alone a county family. I figured it out that she must have set her own village so much in order that there remained nothing but the setting in order of the rest of the world. Her bored eyes wandered sleepily over the assemblage. They seemed to have no preferences for any of them. They rested on the vacuously Bonaparte prince, on the moribund German Jesuit to whom he was listening, on the darkly supple young Spanish priest, on the rosy-gilled English Passionist, on Radet, the writer of that article in the *Revue Rouge*, who was talking to a compatriot in one of the tall windows. She seemed to accept the saturnine-looking men, the political women, who all spoke a language not their own, with an accent and a fluency, and a dangerous faraway smile and a display of questionable teeth all their own. She seemed to class the political with the pious, the obvious adventurer with the seeming fanatic. It was amazing to me to see her there, standing with her county family self-possession in the midst of so much that was questionable. She offered me no explanation; I had to find one for myself.

===Chinese===
我觉出她正站在我身旁。她刚把我引见给老姑母——一位五官粗重、倦眼惺忪的乡村暴君。她显然操劳坏了，显然已“不复当年”，若不是脸上那副纹丝不动的阶级傲慢，这张脸本会惹人怜悯。你要知道，埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家在他们自己那一种门第的树顶上站得太绝了，以致他们不可能遇上一个不是等而下之的人。一个人也许当得上内阁大臣，甚至当得上亲王，可他当不成埃钦厄姆的格兰杰家的人，背后没有那样齐全的一列坟茔，每座里躺着一位格兰杰。这副神情对我也未曾稍霁，虽然我恰恰有这样一列坟。它霁不了，它是焊死在那里的。人们纳罕，她到底跑到这条贼船（galère）上来做什么。她竟会关心波旁王室的复辟，这似乎不可能——那一家子倒也罢了，可他们连英国人都不是，更别说郡望世家（county family）了。我替她琢磨出一个道理：她必定是把自己的村子整治得太井井有条，以致只剩下整治世上其余地方这一桩事可做了。她那双厌倦的眼睛恹恹地扫过满堂的人，对哪一个似乎都无所偏爱。目光落在那位神情茫然的波拿巴亲王身上，落在他正倾听着的那位行将就木的德国耶稣会士身上，落在肤色黝黑、身段柔韧的年轻西班牙神父身上，落在腮帮红润的英国苦难会（Passionist）神父身上，落在《红色评论》那篇文章的作者拉代身上——他正倚着一扇高窗同一位同胞说话。那些神情阴鸷的男人、那些政治里的女人，她似乎一概照单收下——那些女人个个说着一种非母语的语言，带着非母语的口音和流利，而那种危险的、恍惚辽远的微笑，和那一口来路可疑的牙，倒是她们自己的。她似乎把弄政治的与虔信的归作一类，把一眼即穿的冒险家与貌似狂热的信徒归作一类。看她站在那里，以郡望世家的从容自若立在这么多可疑人物中间，我真觉得惊奇。她不给我任何解释；我只得自己找一个。

===Original===
We stood and talked in the centre of the room. It did not seem a place in which one *could* sit.

===Chinese===
我们站在房间中央说话。这地方看上去根本不是一个能坐的地方。

===Original===
“Why have you never been to see me?” she asked languidly. “I might never have known of your existence if it had not been for your sister.” My sister was standing at my side, you must remember. I don’t suppose that I started, but I made my aunt no answer.

===Chinese===
“你怎么从来也不来看看我？”她懒懒地问。“要不是有你那位姐妹，我兴许永远不知道世上还有你这么个人。”你得记得，我那位“姐妹”当时就站在我身边。我想我并没有惊跳起来，可我也没有回答姑母的话。

===Original===
“Indeed,” she went on, “I should never have known that you had a sister. Your father was so *very* peculiar. From the day he married, my husband never heard a word from him.”

===Chinese===
“真是的，”她接着说，“我本来怎么也不会想到你还有个姐妹。你父亲那个人太——太古怪了。从他娶亲那天起，我丈夫就再没收到过他一个字。”

===Original===
“They were so very different,” I said, listlessly.

===Chinese===
“他们兄弟俩性子差得太远了。”我恹恹地说。

===Original===
“Ah, yes,” she answered, “brothers so often are.” She sighed, apropos of nothing. She continued to utter disjointed sentences from which I gathered a skeleton history of my *soi distant* sister’s introduction of herself and of her pretensions. She had, it seemed, casually introduced herself at some garden-party or function of the sort, had represented herself as a sister of my own to whom a maternal uncle had left a fabulous fortune. She herself had suggested her being sheltered under my aunt’s roof as a singularly welcome “paying guest.” She herself, too, had suggested the visit to Paris and had hired the house from a degenerate Duc de Luynes who preferred the delights of an *appartement* in the less lugubrious Avenue Marceau.

===Chinese===
“啊，是啊，”她答道，“兄弟俩往往就是这样。”她没来由地叹了口气。她接着零零碎碎说了些话，我从中拼出一段梗概，关于我那位所谓姐妹（soi distant）如何自我引荐、又如何自命身价。事情似乎是这样：她是在某次游园会或那一类的名目上，随随便便自我引荐了一番，自称是我的一个姐妹，一位母舅给她留下了一笔神话般的遗产。是她自己提议栖到我老姑母的屋檐底下，做一位再受欢迎不过的“寄膳宿房客”（paying guest）。也是她自己提议来巴黎走走，并且从一位堕落的德·吕伊纳公爵（Duc de Luynes）手里赁下了这座宅子——那位公爵更偏爱马尔索大街（Avenue Marceau）上不那么阴郁的一套公寓（appartement）里的种种乐事。

===Original===
“We have tastes so much in common,” my aunt explained, as she moved away to welcome a new arrival. I was left alone with the woman who called herself my sister.

===Chinese===
“我们的趣味太投合了。”老姑母解释着，一边挪开脚步去迎接新到的客人。剩下我一个人，同那个自称是我姐妹的女人相对。

===Original===
We stood a little apart. Each little group of talkers in the vast room seemed to stand just without earshot of the next. I had my back to the door, my face to her.

===Chinese===
我们站得略微离开些。宽敞的屋子里每一小圈谈话的人，似乎都恰好站在邻圈听不见的地方。我背对着门，脸朝着她。

===Original===
“And so you have come,” she said, maliciously it seemed to me.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你到底是来了。”她说——我觉得那话音里带着恶意。

===Original===
It was impossible to speak in *such* a position; in such a place; impossible to hold a discussion on family affairs when a diminutive Irishwoman with too mobile eyebrows, and a couple of gigantic, rawboned, lugubrious Spaniards, were in a position to hear anything that one uttered above a whisper. One might want to raise one’s voice. Besides, she was so﻿—so terrible; there was no knowing what she might not say. She so obviously did not care what the Irish or the Spaniards or the Jesuits heard or thought, that I was forced to the mortifying conclusion that I did.

===Chinese===
在这样的处境里，没法说话；在这样的地方，没法说话；当一位眉毛动个不停的小个子爱尔兰女人，和两位高大、瘦骨嶙峋、愁眉苦脸的西班牙人，都处在一句话只要高过耳语就会被听去的位置上，就没法商谈家事。人说不定正想提高嗓门。再说，她是那样——那样可怕；谁也保不准她会说出什么来。她对爱尔兰人也好、西班牙人也好、耶稣会士也好，听见什么、想些什么，显然一概不在乎，这就逼我得出一个丢人的结论：偏偏我在乎。

===Original===
“Oh, I’ve come,” I answered. I felt as outrageously out of it as one does at a suburban hop where one does not know one animal of the menagerie. I did not know what to do or what to say, or what to do with my hands. I was pervaded by the unpleasant idea that all those furtive eyes were upon me; gauging me because I was the brother of a personality. I was concerned about the fit of my coat and my boots, and all the while I was in a furious temper; my errand was important.

===Chinese===
“哦，我来了。”我答道。我觉出自己出格地格格不入，就像在一场城郊舞会上，满场兽栏里的牲口一头也不认得。我不知道该做什么、该说什么，也不知道这双手往哪里搁。一个不痛快的念头渗透了我：那些躲躲闪闪的眼睛全都在看我，都在掂量我，因为我是位名人的兄弟。我一会儿惦记上衣合不合身、靴子像不像样，可与此同时我正憋着一腔怒火；我的使命是要紧的。

===Original===
She stood looking at me, a sinuous, brilliant thing, with a light in the eyes half challenging, half openly victorious.

===Chinese===
她站在那里望着我，一个蜿蜒生辉的东西，眼睛里一片光，一半是挑衅，一半是公开亮出的胜利。

===Original===
“You have come,” she said, “and﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你来了，”她说，“而且……”

===Original===
I became singularly afraid of her; and wanted to stop her mouth. She might be going to say anything. She overpowered me so that I actually dwindled﻿—into the gawkiness of extreme youth. I became a goggle-eyed, splayfooted boy again and made a boy’s desperate effort after a recovery at one stroke of an ideal standard of dignity.

===Chinese===
我变得异样地怕她，想堵住她的嘴。她保不齐会说出什么来。她把我压得那样厉害，以致我当真缩小了——缩成一个极端年少的愣小子。我又变回一个凸眼外八的男孩，作着男孩式的孤注一掷的努力，想一举恢复到体面的理想标准。

===Original===
“I must have a word with you,” I said, remembering. She made a little gesture with her hands, signifying “I am here.” “But in private,” I added.

===Chinese===
“我必须同你说句话。”我说，想起了自己的来意。她双手微微一摊，做了个小手势，意思是“我就在这里”。“可是要私下里。”我补了一句。

===Original===
“Oh, everything’s in private here,” she said. I was silent.

===Chinese===
“哦，这儿样样都是私下的。”她说。我不作声了。

===Original===
“I must,” I added after a time.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，我又说：“我必须。”

===Original===
“I can’t retire with you,” she said; “ ‘it would look odd,’ you’d say, wouldn’t you?” I shrugged my shoulders in intense irritation. I didn’t want to be burlesqued. A flood of fresh people came into the room. I heard a throaty “ahem” behind me. The Duc de Mersch was introducing himself to notice. It was as I had thought﻿—the man was an habitué, with his well-cut clothes, his air of protestation, and his tremendous golden poll. He was the only sunlight that the gloomy place rejoiced in. He bowed low over my oppressor’s hand, smiled upon me, and began to utter platitudes in English.

===Chinese===
“我可不能同你一道退出去，”她说，“‘那看着会惹眼’——你会这么说的，不是吗？”我极度恼火地耸了耸肩。我不愿让人拿我打诨。一大拨新到的客人涌进屋来。我听见背后一声喉音沉重的“啊哼”。德·默施公爵正在把自己引荐给众人的注意。这同我料想的一样——此人是这里的熟客，衣着剪裁考究，一副信誓旦旦的气派，一颗气派堂堂的金黄脑袋。他是这阴郁场所所享有的唯一阳光。他在我那位征服者的手上深深一躬，朝我微微一笑，开始用英语说些四平八稳的陈言。

===Original===
“Oh, you may speak French,” she said carelessly.

===Chinese===
“哦，您只管说法语。”她随口道。

===Original===
“But your brother.﻿ ﻿…” he answered.

===Chinese===
“可是令兄……”他答道。

===Original===
“I understand French very well,” I said. I was in no mood to spare him embarrassments; wanted to show him that I had a hold over him, and knew he wasn’t the proper person to talk to a young lady. He glared at me haughtily.

===Chinese===
“我法语听得很好。”我说。我无心替他遮掩难堪；想让他知道我手里有他的把柄，也知道他不是配同年轻姑娘攀谈的人。他傲慢地瞪了我一眼。

===Original===
“But yesterday﻿ ﻿…” he began in a tone that burlesqued august displeasure. I was wondering what he had looked like on the other side of the door﻿—whilst that lady had been explaining his nature to me.

===Chinese===
“可是昨天……”他开口了，用的腔调把天威震怒演成了滑稽。我在想那天门那一边的他是个什么模样——那会儿，那位太太正在向我解说他的品性。

===Original===
“Yesterday I wished to avoid embarrassments,” I said; “I was to represent your views about Greenland. I might have misunderstood you in some important matter.”

===Chinese===
“昨天我是想避免难堪，”我说，“我本要代述您对格陵兰的看法。说不定在什么要紧问题上，我会误解了您。”

===Original===
“I see, I see,” he said conciliatorily. “Yesterday we spoke English for the benefit of the British public. When we speak French we are not in public, I hope.” He had a semi-supplicating manner.

===Chinese===
“我明白，我明白。”他息事宁人地说。“昨天我们说英语，是照顾英国公众。我希望，我们说法语的时候，就不算在公众面前了。”他带着半是求告的姿态。

===Original===
“Everything’s rather too much in public here,” I answered. My part as I imagined it was that of a British brother defending his sister from questionable attentions﻿—the person who “tries to show the man he isn’t wanted.” But de Mersch didn’t see the matter in that light at all. He could not, of course. He was as much used to being purred to as my aunt to looking down on non-county persons. He seemed to think I was making an incomprehensible insular joke, and laughed non-committally. It wouldn’t have been possible to let him know he wasn’t wanted.

===Chinese===
“这儿的一切未免太都在大庭广众之下了。”我答道。在我自己的想象里，我充当的是一个英国兄弟的角色，护着自己的姐妹不受来路不正的殷勤——就是那种“设法让那男人明白他不受欢迎”的人。可德·默施全然不从这个角度看这件事。他当然不能。他听惯了别人猫儿似的呜呜讨好，正如我老姑母惯于俯视非郡望人家的人。他似乎以为我在讲一个莫名其妙的岛国笑话，便不置可否地笑了笑。想让他明白他不受欢迎，那是办不到的。

===Original===
“Oh, you needn’t be afraid of my brother,” she said suddenly. “He is quite harmless. He is even going to give up writing for the papers except when we want him.”

===Chinese===
“哦，您不用怕我这位兄弟，”她忽然说，“他全然无害。他甚至打算从此不给报纸写稿了——除非我们用得着他。”

===Original===
The Duc turned from me to her, smiled and bowed. His smile was inane, but he bowed very well; he had been groomed into that sort of thing or had it in the blood.

===Chinese===
公爵从我转向她，微笑着躬身行礼。他的微笑蠢然，躬却行得极好；这一套要么是调教出来的，要么是血里带来的。

===Original===
“We work together still?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“我们还在一道共事？”他问。

===Original===
“Why not?” she answered.

===Chinese===
“为什么不呢？”她答道。

===Original===
A hubbub of angry voices raised itself behind my back. It was one of the contretemps that made the Salon Grangeur famous throughout the city.

===Chinese===
一阵怒气冲冲的人声在我背后掀起。这正是那一类意外风波，叫“格兰热沙龙”（Salon Grangeur）名闻全城。

===Original===
“You forced yourself upon me. Did I say anywhere that you were responsible? If it resembles your particular hell upon earth, what is that to me? You do worse things; you, yourself, monsieur. Haven’t I seen﻿ ﻿… haven’t I seen it?”

===Chinese===
“是您自己硬凑上来的。我哪儿有一句话说过这事归您负责？就算这地方像您那独一份的人间地狱，与我什么相干？您干的坏事更凶；您自己，先生。我难道没见过……难道没见过吗？”

===Original===
The Duc de Mersch looked swiftly over his shoulder toward the window.

===Chinese===
德·默施公爵霍然回头，朝窗子那边望去。

===Original===
“They seem to be angry there,” he said nervously. “Had not something better be done, Miss Granger?”

===Chinese===
“那边像是动起火来了，”他不安地说，“是不是想个法子才好，格兰杰小姐？”

===Original===
Miss Granger followed the direction of his eyes.

===Chinese===
格兰杰小姐顺着他的目光望去。

===Original===
“Why,” she said, “we’re used to these differences of opinion. Besides, it’s only Monsieur Radet; he’s forever at war with someone or other.”

===Chinese===
“嗨，”她说，“这种意见相左的事我们见惯了。再说，那不过是拉代先生；他永远在跟这个那个人交战。”

===Original===
“He ought to be shown the door,” the Duc grumbled.

===Chinese===
“该让人把他送出门去。”公爵咕哝道。

===Original===
“Oh, as for that,” she answered, “we couldn’t. My aunt would be desolated by such a necessity. He is very influential in certain quarters. My aunt wants to catch him for the﻿—He’s going to write an article.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那个嘛，”她答道，“我们可办不到。真有那个必要，我姑母会伤透心的。他在某些圈子里极有势力。我姑母想把他网罗来，为那个——他就要写一篇文章了。”

===Original===
“He writes too many articles,” the Duc said, with heavy displeasure.

===Chinese===
“他的文章写得太多了。”公爵说，郁郁不快。

===Original===
“Oh, he has written *one* too many,” she answered, “but that can be traversed.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“哦，他正是多写了一篇，”她答道，“不过那一篇也还可以驳一驳……”

===Original===
“But no one believes,” the Duc objected﻿ ﻿… Radet’s voice intermittently broke in upon his sotto-voce, coming to our ears in gusts.

===Chinese===
“可是没有谁会信哪。”公爵反驳道……拉代的声音时断时续地打断他压低的语声，一阵一阵送进我们耳朵。

===Original===
“Haven’t I seen you﻿ ﻿… and then﻿ ﻿… and you offer me the cross﻿ ﻿… to bribe me to silence﻿ ﻿… me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我难道没有见过您……而且后来……您却要授我十字勋章……贿买我沉默……给我……”

===Original===
In the general turning of faces toward the window in which stood Radet and the other, mine turned too. Radet was a cadaverous, weatherworn, passion-worn individual, badger-grey, and worked up into a grotesquely attitudinised fury of injured self-esteem. The other was a denationalised, shifty-eyed, sallow, grey-bearded governor of one of the provinces of the *Système Groënlandais*; had a closely barbered head, a bull neck, and a great belly. He cast furtive glances round him, uncertain whether to escape or to wait for his say. He looked at the ring that encircled the window at a little distance, and his face, which had betrayed a half-apparent shame, hardened at sight of the cynical masks of the cosmopolitan conspirators. They were amused by the scene. The Holsteiner gained confidence, shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
满屋的脸纷纷转向那扇窗——拉代同另一个人就站在窗前——我的脸也转了过去。拉代形容枯槁，被风霜磨旧，被激情磨旧，一身獾灰色，被激成一种姿态怪诞的、自尊受了伤的狂怒。另一位是个失了国籍、眼光闪烁、面色灰黄、灰须满面的人，格陵兰体系（Système Groënlandais）治下一省的总督；头发剃得精光服帖，公牛脖子，好大一肚皮。他偷偷朝四下里张望，拿不定是逃呢，还是等着轮到他说话。他望了望稍远处围住那扇窗的一圈人；他的脸本已隐约露出一半羞惭，一看见那些世界主义密谋家玩世不恭的面具，便硬了起来。这场面让他们看了个乐。那霍尔斯坦人壮了胆，耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“You have had the fever very badly since you came back,” he said, showing a level row of white teeth. “You did not talk like that out there.”

===Chinese===
“您回来以后，那热病可发得不轻。”他说着，露出一排齐整的白牙。“在外头的时候，您可不是这么说话的。”

===Original===
“No﻿—*pas si bête*﻿—you would have hanged me, perhaps, as you did that poor devil of a Swiss. What was his name? Now you offer me the cross. Because I had the fever, *hein*?”

===Chinese===
“不——我才不那么傻（pas si bête）——您说不定会把我绞死的，就像您结果那个可怜的瑞士倒霉鬼那样。他叫什么名字来着？如今您却要授我十字勋章。就因为我害过热病，嗯？”

===Original===
I had been watching the Duc’s face; a first red flush had come creeping from under the roots of his beard, and had spread over the low forehead and the sides of the neck. The eyeglass fell from the eye, a signal for the colour to retreat. The full lips grew pallid, and began to mutter unspoken words. His eyes wandered appealingly from the woman beside him to me. *I* didn’t want to look him in the face. The man was a trafficker in human blood, an evil liver, and I hated him. He had to pay his price; would have to pay﻿—but I didn’t want to see him pay it. There was a limit.

===Chinese===
我一直留意着公爵的脸；最初的一阵红潮已经从他胡子的根部底下爬了起来，漫过低低的额头和脖颈两侧。单片眼镜从眼睛上跌落，像一声号令，红色随之退却。丰满的嘴唇褪成灰白，开始嗫嚅出无声的字眼。他的目光带着恳求，从他身旁的女人身上游移到我身上。倒是我，不愿看他的脸。这个人是买卖人血的贩子，一个行恶度日的人，我恨他。他必须付出他的代价；终要付出——可我不想看着他付。凡事总有个限度。

===Original===
I began to excuse myself, and slid out between the groups of excellent plotters. As I was going, she said to me:

===Chinese===
我开始告罪脱身，从一组组体面之至的密谋家中间溜了出去。临走时，她对我说：

===Original===
“You may come to me tomorrow in the morning.”

===Chinese===
“你明天上午可以到我这儿来。”

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
I was at the Hôtel de Luynes﻿—or Granger﻿—early on the following morning. The mists were still hanging about the dismal upper windows of the inscrutable Faubourg; the toilet of the city was being completed; the little hoses on wheels were clattering about the quiet larger streets. I had not much courage thus early in the day. I had started impulsively; stepping with the impulse of immediate action from the doorstep of the dairy where I had breakfasted. But I made detours; it was too early, and my pace slackened into a saunter as I passed the row of porters’ lodges in that dead, inscrutable street. I wanted to fly; had that impulse very strongly; but I burnt my boats with my inquiry of the incredibly ancient, one-eyed porteress. I made my way across the damp courtyard, under the enormous portico, and into the chilly stone hall that no amount of human coming and going sufficed to bring back to a semblance of life. Mademoiselle was expecting me. One went up a great flight of stone steps into one of the immensely high, narrow, impossibly rectangular anterooms that one sees in the frontispieces of old plays. The furniture looked no more than knee-high until one discovered that one’s self had no appreciable stature. The sad light slanted in ruled lines from the great height of the windows; an army of motes moved slowly in and out of the shadows. I went after awhile and looked disconsolately out into the courtyard. The porteress was making her way across the gravelled space, her arms, her hands, the pockets of her black apron full of letters of all sizes. I remembered that the *facteur* had followed me down the street. A noise of voices came confusedly to my ears from between half-opened folding-doors; the thing reminded me of my waiting in de Mersch’s rooms. It did not last so long. The voices gathered tone, as they do at the end of a colloquy, succeeded each other at longer intervals, and at last came to a sustained halt. The tall doors moved ajar and she entered, followed by a man whom I recognized as the governor of a province of the day before. In that hostile light he looked old and weazened and worried; seemed to have lost much of his rotundity. As for her, she shone with a light of her own.

===Chinese===
第二天一清早，我就到了吕伊纳公馆（Hôtel de Luynes）——或者叫格兰杰公馆。雾气还萦绕在那高深莫测的圣日耳曼区凄清的高层窗畔；城市的梳洗正在收尾；一辆辆带轮子的小水车在安静的大街上嗒嗒作响地跑动。时辰这么早，我没有多少勇气。我是一时冲动出的门；带着立刻行动的那股冲动，从吃过早饭的乳品店门阶上迈了出去。可我绕起了弯路；还太早，走过那条死气沉沉、高深莫测的街上的一排门房小屋时，我的步子松懈成了信步。我想逃；这冲动强烈得很；可我对那位老得令人难以置信的独眼女门房开了口问话，便烧掉了自己的船。我穿过潮湿的院子，从巨大的柱廊底下，走进那寒气森森的石砌大厅——多少人来来往往，也不足以让它恢复一点生气的模样。小姐正在等我。人拾级登上一大段石阶，进到一间极高、极窄、方正得不像话的前厅——旧剧本卷首插图里见到的那一种。家具看上去不过齐膝高，直到你发现你本人已没有了可观的身量。惨淡的光从窗子极高的顶上成一道道直线斜落下来；一支尘埃的大军缓慢地在阴影里进进出出。过了一会儿，我走过去，怏怏地朝院子里张望。女门房正穿过铺砾石的空地，她的手臂、双手和黑围裙的口袋里，满是大大小小的信件。我想起那邮差（facteur）方才一路跟我走在这条街上。一阵人语从半开的折叠门之间含混地传进我耳朵；这情形叫我想起在德·默施房中等候的那一回。这一回没有等那么久。那些声音渐渐凝出腔调，像一场谈话到了收尾时那样，一句比一句隔得久，终于停成一个长久的静止。高高的门扇错开一道缝，她走了进来，身后跟着一个男人，我认出他就是头一天那位格陵兰体系治下一省的总督。在那片不友善的光线里，他显得苍老、干瘪、满面忧色；仿佛失掉了许多原本的富态。至于她，她焕发着一种她自己独有的光。

===Original===
He greeted me dejectedly, and did not brighten when she let him know that we had a mutual friend in Callan. The Governor, it seemed, in his capacity of Supervisor of the *Système*, was to conduct that distinguished person through the wilds of Greenland; was to smooth his way and to point out to him excellences of administration.

===Chinese===
他垂头丧气地招呼了我；她让他知道我们有一位共同的朋友卡伦时，他也不曾为之振作。看来，总督将以那套体系的监察人（Supervisor of the Système）的身份，领着那位杰出人物穿越格陵兰的莽荒之地；要为他把路铺平，向他指点行政上的种种优异。

===Original===
I wished him a good journey; he sighed and began to fumble with his hat.

===Chinese===
我祝他旅途顺遂；他叹了口气，开始摸索他的帽子。

===Original===
“*Alors, c’est entendu*,” she said; giving him leave to depart. He looked at her in an odd sort of way, took her hand and applied it to his lips.

===Chinese===
“Alors, c’est entendu（那么，就这么说定了），”她说，算是放他告辞。他样子古怪地望了她片刻，拿起她的手，送到自己唇上。

===Original===
“*C’est entendu*,” he said with a heavy sigh, drops of moisture spattering from beneath his white moustache, “*mais*﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“C’est entendu（说定了），”他重重地叹了口气说，白胡子底下溅出几点潮气，“mais …（可是……）”

===Original===
He ogled again with infinitesimal eyes and went out of the room. He had the air of wishing to wipe the perspiration from his brows and to exclaim, “*Quelle femme!*” But if he had any such wish he mastered it until the door hid him from sight.

===Chinese===
他又用那双针尖大的眼睛送了个秋波，走出了房间。他那副神气，像是恨不得抹去额上的汗，喊一声“Quelle femme!（好一个厉害的女人！）”可他纵有这念头，也把它按捺住了，直到门把他遮得看不见为止。

===Original===
“Why the﻿ ﻿…” I began before it had well closed, “do you allow that thing to make love to you?” I wanted to take up my position before she could have a chance to make me ridiculous. I wanted to make a long speech﻿—about duty to the name of Granger. But the next word hung, and, before it came, she had answered:

===Chinese===
“为什么你……”不等门关严我就开了口，“你竟容许那个东西向你求爱？”我想在她有机会把我弄得可笑之前，先站好自己的立场。我本想发表一篇长长的演说——讲对格兰杰这个姓氏的责任。可下一个字悬在半空，还没等它落下，她已经答了话：

===Original===
“He?﻿—Oh, I’m making use of him.”

===Chinese===
“他？——哦，我这是在利用他。”

===Original===
“To inherit the earth?” I asked ironically, and she answered gravely:

===Chinese===
“为了承受大地？”我讥诮地问。她郑重地答道：

===Original===
“To inherit the earth.”

===Chinese===
“为了承受大地。”

===Original===
She was leaning against the window, playing with the strings of the blinds, and silhouetted against the leaden light. She seemed to be, physically, a little tired; and the lines of her figure to interlace almost tenderly﻿—to “compose” well, after the ideas of a certain school. I knew so little of her﻿—only just enough to be in love with her﻿—that this struck me as the herald of a new phase, not so much in her attitude to me as in mine to her; she had even then a sort of gravity, the gravity of a person on whom things were beginning to weigh.

===Chinese===
她斜倚在窗口，摆弄着百叶帘的拉绳，衬在铅灰色的天光前成一剪影。她看上去身体有点倦了；她身形的线条近乎温存地交叠着——照某一派的美学，是“构图”上佳。我对她知道得那么少——刚刚够得上爱上她——以致这一幕在我眼里成了一个新阶段的先声，与其说是她对我的态度进入了新阶段，不如说是我对她的；她那时便有了一种郑重，一种事情开始落到身上来的人的郑重。

===Original===
“But,” I said, irresolutely. I could not speak to her; to this new conception of her, in the way I had planned; in the way one would talk to a brilliant, limpid﻿—oh, to a woman of sorts. But I had to take something of my old line. “How would flirting with that man help you?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我说，迟疑不决。我没法照原先打算的那样对她说话；对这个新的她，没法照我盘算的方式说；没法像同比一个光彩焕发、澄澈见底——哦，同比一个好歹算是女人的女人——那样去说。可我又不得不捡回几分我的老套子。“同那个男人调情，对你能有什么帮助？”

===Original===
“It’s quite simple,” she answered, “he’s to show Callan all Greenland, and Callan is to write﻿ ﻿… Callan has immense influence over a great class, and he will have some of the prestige of﻿—of a Commissioner.”

===Chinese===
“这很简单，”她答道，“他要领着卡伦把整个格陵兰看个遍，而卡伦要写……卡伦对一个庞大的阶层有巨大的影响力，而且他将带上几分——几分特派员的威望。”

===Original===
“Oh, I know about Callan,” I said.

===Chinese===
“哦，卡伦是怎么回事我知道。”我说。

===Original===
“And,” she went on, “this man had orders to hide things from Callan; you know what it is they have to hide. But he won’t now; that is what I was arranging. It’s partly by bribery and partly because he has a belief in his *beaux yeux*﻿—so Callan will be upset and will write an﻿ ﻿… exposure; the sort of thing Callan would write if he were well upset. And he will be, by what this man will let him see. You know what a little man like Callan will feel﻿ ﻿… he will be made ill. He would faint at the sight of a drop of blood, you know, and he will see﻿—oh, the very worst, worse than what Radet saw. And he will write a frightful article, and it will be a thunderclap for de Mersch.﻿ ﻿… And de Mersch will be getting very shaky by then. And your friend Churchill will try to carry de Mersch’s railway bill through in the face of the scandal. Churchill’s motives will be excellent, but everyone will say﻿ ﻿… You know what people say﻿ ﻿… That is what I and Gurnard want. We want people to talk; we want them to believe.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“再有，”她接着说，“这个人本来奉命对卡伦隐瞒种种；你知道他们必须隐瞒的是什么。可如今他不会了；我方才安排的就是这个。一半靠行贿，一半也因为他颇为信赖自己的那双俊眼（beaux yeux）——于是卡伦会心绪大乱，会写一篇……揭发；正是卡伦这个人被彻底搅昏了头之后会写出来的那种东西。而他会被搅昏的，凭这个人放给他看的那些东西。你知道卡伦这样的小个子会有什么感受……他会难受出病来的。你知道，他见了血也要晕过去，而他将看到的——哦，是最不堪入目的，比拉代看到的还要不堪。他会写出一篇骇人的文章，对德·默施便是一声晴天霹雳。……而到那时，德·默施也早已摇摇欲坠。而你的朋友丘吉尔会顶着这桩丑闻，设法让德·默施的铁路法案通过。丘吉尔的动机将是极好的，可人人都会说……你知道人们会怎么说……这就是我和格纳德所要的。我们要人们开口议论；我们要他们相信。……”

===Original===
I don’t know whether there really was a hesitation in her voice, or whether I read that into it. She stood there, playing with the knots of the window-cords and speaking in a low monotone. The whole thing, the sad twilight of the place, her tone of voice, seemed tinged with unavailing regret. I had almost forgotten the Dimensionist story, and I had never believed in it. But now, for the first time I began to have my doubts. I was certain that she had been plotting *something* with one of the Duc de Mersch’s lieutenants. The man’s manner vouched for that; he had not been able to look me in the face. But, more than anything, his voice and manner made me feel that we had passed out of a realm of farcical allegory. I knew enough to see that she might be speaking the truth. And, if she were, her calm avowal of such treachery proved that she *was* what she had said the Dimensionists were; cold, with no scruples, clear-sighted and admirably courageous, and indubitably enemies of society.

===Chinese===
我不知道她的语声里是否真有一丝迟疑，还是我把那读进了里面。她站在那里，拨弄着窗帘绳的绳结，声音低沉而单调地说着话。这一切，这地方的惨淡幽冥，她的语调，似乎都染着一点无补于事的憾意。我几乎已经忘了第四维人那套说法，而且我从来就不曾信过。可现在，我头一回开始犯疑了。我确信她一直在同德·默施公爵的一名部属密谋着什么事。那男人的举止可以为证；他始终没法正眼看我。但最要紧的是，他的声音和举止使我感到：我们已经走出了那闹剧式寓言的国度。我知道得足够多，看得出她说的可能是实话。而倘若果真如此，她把这等背信如此平静地招认出来，便证明了她确是她所说第四维人的那副样子：冷酷，无所顾忌，目光清明，勇敢得令人赞叹，而且是毫无疑问的社会之敌。

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” I said. “But de Mersch then?”

===Chinese===
“我不明白，”我说，“那德·默施怎么办呢？”

===Original===
She made a little gesture; one of those movements that I best remember of her; the smallest, the least noticeable. It reduced de Mersch to nothing; he no longer even counted.

===Chinese===
她做了个小手势；那是我对她记得最清楚的那些动作之一；最小、最不惹眼的一种。它把德·默施化成了乌有；他连数都不算了。

===Original===
“Oh, as for him,” she said, “he is only a detail.” I had still the idea that she spoke with a pitying intonation﻿—as if she were speaking to a dog in pain. “He doesn’t really count; not really. He will crumble up and disappear, very soon. You won’t even remember him.”

===Chinese===
“哦，至于他，”她说，“只是一个细节罢了。”我仍旧觉得她说话带着怜惜的声口——像在对一条痛苦中的狗说话。“他实在算不上数；实在算不上。他很快就会碎掉、消失。你甚至不会记得他这个人。”

===Original===
“But,” I said, “you go about with him, as if you.﻿ ﻿… You are getting yourself talked about.﻿ ﻿… Everyone thinks﻿—”﻿ ﻿… The accusation that I had come to make seemed impossible, now I was facing her. “I believe,” I added, with the suddenness of inspiration. “I’m certain even, that *he* thinks that you﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我说，“你同他招摇来去，仿佛你……人家都在议论你了……人人以为——”……我来时打算提出的那番指控，此刻当着她的面，似乎说不出口了。“我相信，”我又添了一句，来得像灵光一现，“我甚至确信，连他本人都以为你……”

===Original===
“Well, they think that sort of thing. But it is only part of the game. Oh, I assure you it is no more than that.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，他们就好想那种事。可那不过是这局棋的一部分。哦，我向你担保，仅此而已。”

===Original===
I was silent. I felt that, for one reason or another, she wished me to believe.

===Chinese===
我沉默了。我感到，不管出于什么缘故，她是要我相信。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “I want you to believe. It will save you a good deal of pain.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”她说，“我要你相信。这会省去你许多的痛苦。”

===Original===
“If you wanted to save me pain,” I maintained, “you would have done with de Mersch﻿ ﻿… for good.” I had an idea that the solution was beyond me. It was as if the controlling powers were flitting, invisible, just above my head, just beyond my grasp. There was obviously something vibrating; some cord, somewhere, stretched very taut and quivering. But I could think of no better solution than: “You must have done with him.” It seemed obvious, too, that that was impossible, was outside the range of things that could be done﻿—but I had to do my best. “It’s a﻿—it’s vile,” I added, “vile.”

===Chinese===
“你要真想让我少受痛苦，”我坚持道，“你就会同德·默施了断……永远了断。”我有一种感觉：那个解数超出了我的能力。仿佛那些掌控的力量正无影无形地在我头顶上掠来掠去，恰恰在我够不着的地方。显然有什么东西在震颤；有一根弦，在什么地方，绷得极紧，颤个不住。可我想不出比这更好的解数：“你必须同他了断。”同样显而易见的是，那不可能，超出了办得到的事情的范围——可我总得尽我所能。“这是——这是卑鄙的，”我又说，“卑鄙。”

===Original===
“Oh, I know, I know,” she said, “for you.﻿ ﻿… And I’m even sorry. But it has to be gone on with. De Mersch has to go under in just this way. It can’t be any other.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道，我知道，”她说，“对你而言是……我甚至觉得抱歉。可这事必须进行下去。德·默施就得这样垮掉。不可能有别的样子。”

===Original===
“Why not?” I asked, because she had paused. I hadn’t any desire for enlightenment.

===Chinese===
“为什么不可能？”我问，因为她停住了。我可并不渴望得到开导。

===Original===
“It isn’t even only Churchill,” she said, “not even only that de Mersch will bring down Churchill with him. It is that he must bring down everything that Churchill stands for. You know what that is﻿—the sort of probity, all the old order of things. And the more vile the means used to destroy de Mersch the more vile the whole affair will seem. People﻿—the sort of people﻿—have an idea that a decent man cannot be touched by tortuous intrigues. And the whole thing will be﻿—oh, malodorous. You understand.”

===Chinese===
“这甚至还不止是丘吉尔一个人的事，”她说，“甚至不止是德·默施会把丘吉尔一道带倒。而是他必须带倒丘吉尔所代表的一切。你知道那是什么——那一种的正派，整个的旧秩序。而用来毁掉德·默施的手段越是卑鄙，这整桩事看上去就越发肮脏。人们——那一班人们——有一个成见，认为一个正派人是曲折的阴谋伤不到的。而这一切将是——哦，恶臭熏天的。你明白。”

===Original===
“I don’t,” I answered, “I don’t understand at all.”

===Chinese===
“我不明白，”我答道，“我一点儿也不明白。”

===Original===
“Ah, yes, you do,” she said, “you understand.﻿ ﻿…” She paused for a long while, and I was silent. I understood vaguely what she meant; that if Churchill fell amid the clouds of dust of such a collapse, there would be an end of belief in probity﻿ ﻿… or nearly an end. But I could not see what it all led up to; where it left us.

===Chinese===
“啊，不，你明白，”她说，“你明白……”她停顿了许久，我沉默着。我模模糊糊地明白她的意思：倘若丘吉尔在这样一场崩塌扬起的尘雾之中倒下，对正派的信念便告终结……或者说几乎终结。可我看不出这一切究竟引向哪里；又把我们撇在了什么地步。

===Original===
“You see,” she began again, “I want to make it as little painful to you as I can; as little painful as explanations *can* make it. I can’t feel as you feel, but I can see, rather dimly, what it is that hurts you. And so﻿ ﻿… I want to; I really want to.”

===Chinese===
“你要知道，”她又开口了，“我想让这事对你尽量少些痛苦；少到解释所能做到的那种地步。我无法像你那样去感受，可我能看见——朦朦胧胧地——是什么在伤害你。所以……我愿意那样；我真的愿意。”

===Original===
“But you won’t do the one thing,” I returned hopelessly to the charge.

===Chinese===
“可你偏偏不肯做那唯一的一件事，”我绝望地重又发动攻势。

===Original===
“I cannot,” she answered, “it must be like that; there isn’t any way. You are so tied down to these little things. Don’t you see that de Mersch, and﻿—and all these people﻿—don’t really count? They aren’t anything at all in the scheme of things. I think that, even for you, they aren’t worth bothering about. They’re only accidents; the accidents that﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我做不到，”她答道，“必定得这样；没有任何别的路。你被这些小事束缚得太紧了。你难道看不见，德·默施，还有——还有所有这些人——实在都算不得数？在万事的大格局里，他们根本什么也不是。我想，即便为你着想，他们也不值当劳神。他们只是些偶然；那些偶然——”

===Original===
“That what?” I asked, although I began to see dimly what she meant.

===Chinese===
“偶然什么？”我问，虽然我开始隐约明白她的意思了。

===Original===
“That lead in the inevitable,” she answered. “Don’t you see? Don’t you understand? We *are* the inevitable﻿ ﻿… and you can’t keep us back. We have to come and you, you will only hurt yourself, by resisting.” A sense that this was the truth, the only truth, beset me. It was for the moment impossible to think of anything else﻿—of anything else in the world. “You must accept us and all that we mean, you must stand back; sooner or later. Look even all round you, and you will understand better. You are in the house of a type﻿—a type that became impossible. Oh, centuries ago. And that type too, tried very hard to keep back the inevitable; not only because itself went under, but because everything that it stood for went under. And it had to suffer﻿—heartache﻿ ﻿… that sort of suffering. Isn’t it so?”

===Chinese===
“引向必然的偶然，”她答道，“你难道看不见？你难道不明白？我们就是那必然……你拦不住我们。我们非来不可，而你，你只会伤害你自己，若是你抵抗。”一种感觉围住了我：这是真理，唯一的真理。那一刻，已不可能再想别的——想世上任何别的什么。“你必须接受我们，接受我们意味着的一切，你必须退后；或早或迟。你只消环顾一下你的四周，就会更明白些。你正置身于一个类型的宅子里——一个早已不可能存在的类型。哦，几个世纪以前就不可能了。而那个类型，也曾竭力阻拦过必然；不只是因为它自己沉了下去，还因为它所代表的一切都沉了下去。它也不得不受苦——心痛……那一类的苦。是不是这样？”

===Original===
I did not answer; the illustration was too abominably just. It was just that. There were even now all these people﻿—these Legitimists﻿—sneering ineffectually; shutting themselves away from the light in their mournful houses and suffering horribly because everything that they stood for had gone under.

===Chinese===
我没有作答；这个例证恰切得可憎之至。正是这样。甚至此刻也还有这许多人——这些正统派——无济于事地讥嘲着；把自己关在阴郁的宅邸里避开天光，苦痛万状，因为他们所代表的一切都已沉没。

===Original===
“But even if I believe you,” I said, “the thing is too horrible, and your tools are too mean; that man who has just gone out and﻿—and Callan﻿—are they the weapons of the inevitable? After all, the Revolution﻿ ﻿…” I was striving to get back to tangible ideas﻿—ideas that one could name and date and label﻿ ﻿… “the Revolution was noble in essence and made for good. But all this of yours is too vile and too petty. You are bribing, or something worse, that man to betray his master. And that you call helping on the inevitable.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“可就算我信了你，”我说，“这事情也太可怕，你的工具也太下作；那个刚走出去的男人，还有——还有卡伦——这些就是必然的武器？说到底，大革命……”我竭力想退回到摸得着的观念上去——叫得出名字、指得出日期、贴得上标签的观念……“大革命本质高贵，是为了善的。可你的这一切太卑鄙、太琐屑。你在贿赂那个人，或者用更坏的手段，让他背叛他的主人。而你把这个叫作推动那必然。……”

===Original===
“They used to say just that of the Revolution. That wasn’t nice of its tools. Don’t you see? They were the people that went under.﻿ ﻿… They couldn’t see the good.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“从前人们对大革命说的正是这一套：说它的工具不干净。你难道不明白？他们就是沉没下去的那些人……他们看不见善……”

===Original===
“And I﻿—I am to take it on trust,” I said, bitterly.

===Chinese===
“而我——我就得单凭一句话信下去，”我辛酸地说。

===Original===
“You couldn’t see the good,” she answered, “it isn’t possible, and there is no way of explaining. Our languages are different, and there’s no bridge﻿—no bridge at all. We *can’t* meet.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你不可能看见善，”她答道，“这不可能，也没有办法解释。我们的语言不同，中间没有桥——根本没有桥。我们无法相遇。……”

===Original===
It was that revolted me. If there was no bridge and we could not meet, we must even fight; that is, if I believed her version of herself. If I did not, I was being played the fool with. I preferred to think that. If she were only fooling me she remained attainable. If it was as she said, there was no hope at all﻿—not any.

===Chinese===
使我反感的正是这一点。倘若没有桥、我们无法相遇，那我们就只好战斗；这就是说，倘若我相信她对她自己的一套说法。倘若我不信，那我便是被人耍了。我倒宁愿那样想。倘若她不过是在耍我，那她就仍是可到手的。倘若真像她说的那样，那就毫无希望——一丝一毫也没有。

===Original===
“I don’t believe you,” I said, suddenly. I didn’t want to believe her. The thing was too abominable﻿—too abominable for words, and incredible. I struggled against it as one struggles against inevitable madness, against the thought of it. It hung over me, stupefying, deadening. One could only fight it with violence, crudely, in jerks, as one struggles against the numbness of frost. It was like a pall, like descending clouds of smoke, seemed to be actually present in the absurdly lofty room﻿—this belief in what she stood for, in what she said she stood for.

===Chinese===
“我不信你。”我忽然说。我不愿相信她。这事情太可憎了——可憎得言语道不出，而且难以置信。我同它搏斗，如同人同无可逃免的疯狂搏斗，同对它的念头搏斗。它悬在我头顶上，叫人发木，叫人发昏。人只能用蛮力同它斗，粗粝地、一阵一阵地，像人同霜冻的麻木搏斗那样。它像一袭尸衣，像压下来的团团烟云，仿佛当真就在这间高得荒唐的屋子里——这信仰：信她所代表的东西，信她自称所代表的东西。

===Original===
“I don’t believe you,” I proclaimed, “I won’t.﻿ ﻿… You are playing the fool with me﻿ ﻿… trying to get round me﻿ ﻿… to make me let you go on with these﻿—with these﻿—It is abominable. Think of what it means for me, what people are saying of me, and I am a decent man﻿—You shall not. Do you understand, you *shall* not. It is unbearable﻿ ﻿… and you﻿ ﻿… you try to fool me﻿ ﻿… in order to keep me quiet﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我不信你，”我声言，“我不要信。……你在拿我耍笑……想把我绕进去……好让我放你继续干这些——干这些——这太可憎了。想想这于我意味着什么，想想人家正怎么说我，而我是个体面人——你不许。你懂吗，你不许。这叫人无法忍受……而你……你却想糊弄我……好让我安安静静……”

===Original===
“Oh, no,” she said. “Oh, no.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不是的，”她说，“哦，不是的。”

===Original===
She had an accent that touched grief, as nearly as she could touch it. I remember it now, as one remembers these things. But then I passed it over. I was too much moved myself to notice it more than subconsciously, as one notices things past which one is whirled. And I was whirled past these things, in an ungovernable fury at the remembrance of what I had suffered, of what I had still to suffer. I was speaking with intense rage, jerking out words, ideas, as floodwater jerks through a sluice the debris of once ordered fields.

===Chinese===
她的语调里有一种近乎触到悲恸的东西——触到了她所能触到的最近处。我此刻还记得它，如同人记得这一类事情那样。可在当时我把它放了过去。我自己被搅动得太厉害，只在下意识里觉察到它，如同人被卷着疾驰而过时觉察两侧掠过的事物。而我也确是被卷着疾驰而过，卷在一场驾驭不住的狂怒里——想起我已受过的苦，想起我还将受的苦。我带着炽烈的怒气说话，把字句、念头一阵一阵迸出来，像洪水把一度齐整的田亩的残屑从水闸里一阵一阵迸出来那样。

===Original===
“You are,” I said, “you *are*﻿—you﻿—you﻿—dragging an ancient name through the dust﻿—you﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你是的，”我说，“你确实是——你——你——把一个古老的姓氏拖过尘泥——你……”

===Original===
I forget what I said. But I remember, “dragging an ancient name.” It struck me, at the time, by its forlornness, as part of an appeal to her. It was so pathetically tiny a motive, so out of tone, that it stuck in my mind. I only remember the upshot of my speech; that, unless she swore﻿—oh, yes, swore﻿—to have done with de Mersch, I would denounce her to my aunt at that very moment and in that very house.

===Chinese===
我说了些什么，我忘了。可我记得“把一个古老的姓氏拖过尘泥”。在当时，它那份孤凄打动了我，像是向她哀恳的一部分。这个动机渺小得可怜，又那么不合调子，以致它留驻在我心里。我只记得我那番话的结局：除非她起誓——哦，是的，起誓——同德·默施一刀两断，否则我就当场、就在这幢宅子里，把她告发给老姑母。

===Original===
And she said that it was impossible.

===Chinese===
而她说，这是不可能的。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
I had a sense of walking very fast﻿—almost of taking flight﻿—down a long dim corridor, and of a door that opened into an immense room. All that I remember of it, as I saw it then, was a number of pastel portraits of weak, vacuous individuals, in dulled, gilt, oval frames. The heads stood out from the panelling and stared at me from between ringlets, from under powdered hair, simpering, or contemptuous with the expression that must have prevailed in the monde of the time before the Revolution. At a great distance, bent over account-books and pink cheques on the flap of an escritoire, sat my aunt, very small, very grey, very intent on her work.

===Chinese===
我有一种感觉，觉得自己正沿着一条幽暗的长廊走得飞快——几乎是在飞逃——随后是一扇门，通向一间巨大的房间。当时眼中所见、如今还记得的，只有若干色粉肖像，画的是些孱弱、空虚的人物，框在发暗的镀金椭圆框里。那些头脸从护壁镶板上凸出来，瞪视着我——从长卷发的缝隙间，从扑粉的头发底下——或傻笑着，或不屑地摆出一副大革命前时代上流社会（monde）里必定盛行的神情。极远处，老姑母伏在一张斜面书桌翻下的活板上，对着账簿和粉红色支票，极小，极灰暗，极专注于她的活计。

===Original===
The people who built these rooms must have had some property of the presence to make them bulk large﻿—if they ever really did so﻿—in the eyes of dependents, of lackeys. Perhaps it was their sense of ownership that gave them the necessary prestige. My aunt, who was only a temporary occupant, certainly had none of it. Bent intently over her accounts, peering through her spectacles at columns of figures, she was nothing but a little old woman alone in an immense room. It seemed impossible that she could really have any family pride, any pride of any sort. She looked round at me over her spectacles, across her shoulder.

===Chinese===
当年造起这些房间的人，必定身怀某种在场的特质，才能在仆从、听差的眼里显得魁伟——倘若他们当真显得过的话。也许正是那份产业主的意识，给了他们必需的威望。我的老姑母不过是个临时房客，当然一丝一毫也没有。她专注地伏在账目上，隔着眼镜细看一栏栏数字，不过是个独坐巨大房间里的瘦小老妇人。她当真还能有什么家族的自尊、任何一种自尊，这似乎是不可能的事。她从肩膀上方、隔着眼镜回头看了看我。

===Original===
“Ah﻿ ﻿… Etchingham,” she said. She seemed to be trying to carry herself back to England, to the England of her land-agent and her select visiting list. Here she was no more superior than if we had been on a desert island. I wanted to enlighten her as to the woman she was sheltering﻿—wanted to very badly; but a necessity for introducing the matter seemed to arise as she gradually stiffened into assertiveness.

===Chinese===
“啊……埃钦厄姆，”她说。她似乎正竭力把自己带回英国去，带回那个有她的土地代理人、有她精选的拜客名单的英国。在这里，她并不比我们同处荒岛时更高人一等。我想让她认清她收留在屋檐下的那个女人——非常想；可是，随着她渐渐绷紧、摆出说一不二的架势，这件事倒似乎非得挑开不可了。

===Original===
“My dear aunt,” I said, “the woman.﻿ ﻿…” The alien nature of the theme grew suddenly formidable. She looked at me arousedly.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的姑母，”我说，“那个女人……”这个话题的异己性质陡然变得令人生畏。她激动地望着我。

===Original===
“You got my note then,” she said. “But I don’t think a woman *can* have brought it. I have given such strict orders. They have such strange ideas here, though. And Madame﻿—the *portière*﻿—is an old retainer of M. de Luynes, I haven’t much influence over her. It is absurd, but.﻿ ﻿…” It seems that the old lady in the lodge made a point of carrying letters that went by hand. She had an eye for gratuities﻿—and the police, I should say, were concerned. They make a good deal of use of that sort of person in that neighbourhood of infinitesimal and unceasing plotting.

===Chinese===
“这么说，便条你收到了，”她说。“可我想，一个女人总不至于送来吧。我吩咐得那么严。不过这地方的人想法古怪得很。还有那位太太——门房（portière）——是德·吕伊纳先生家的老仆，我说不上能左右她多少。荒唐得很，可是……”看来，门房里那位老太太认定了一桩事：凡靠人手传递的信件，都由她经手来送。她对赏钱有眼力——而且，我该说，警察也与此有关。在那片琐屑而无休止的密谋之地里，这类人是大有用场的。

===Original===
“I didn’t mean that,” I said, “but the woman who calls herself my sister.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我不是说这个，”我说，“我是说那个自称我姐妹的女人……”

===Original===
“My dear nephew,” she interrupted, with tranquil force, as if she were taking an arranged line, “I cannot﻿—I absolutely cannot be worried with your quarrels with your sister. As I said to you in my note of this morning, when you are in this town you must consider this house your home. It is almost insulting of you to go to an inn. I am told it is even﻿ ﻿… quite an unfit place that you are stopping at﻿—for a member of our family.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的侄儿，”她打断我，带着一种平静的强硬，仿佛在照着一条排定好的路线行事，“我不能——我绝对不能让你和你姐妹的争吵来烦扰我。正像我今天早晨便条里对你说的，你在这座城里，就得把这所宅子当你的家。你跑去住客栈，简直近乎侮辱。我听说，你眼下落脚的那个地方，甚至……相当不合适——对我们家族的一员来说。”

===Original===
I maintained for a few seconds a silence of astonishment.

===Chinese===
我惊愕得沉默了好几秒。

===Original===
“But,” I returned to the charge, “the matter is one of importance. You must understand that she.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我重新发动攻势，“这件事非同小可。您必须明白，她……”

===Original===
My aunt stiffened and froze. It was as if I had committed some flagrant sin against etiquette.

===Chinese===
老姑母绷紧了，冷了下来。仿佛我犯下了某种明目张胆的失礼大罪。

===Original===
“If I am satisfied as to her behaviour,” she said, “I think that you might be.” She paused as if she were satisfied that she had set me hopelessly in the wrong.

===Chinese===
“我既然对她的举止感到满意，”她说，“我想你也大可以满意。”她停了停，仿佛对于已把我断送进无望翻身的理亏之地感到满意。

===Original===
“I don’t withdraw my invitation,” she said. “You must understand I *wish* you to come here. But your quarrels you and she must settle. On those terms.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我并不收回我的邀请，”她说。“你要明白，我真心愿意你到这儿来。不过你和你那位姐妹之间的争吵，得由你们自己去解决。条件就是如此……”

===Original===
She had the air of conferring an immense favour, as if she believed that I had, all my life through, been waiting for her invitation to come within the pale. As for me, I felt a certain relief at having the carrying out of my duty made impossible for me. I did not *want* to tell my aunt and thus to break things off definitely and for good. Something would have happened; the air might have cleared as it clears after a storm; I should have learnt where I stood. But I was afraid of the knowledge. Light in these dark places might reveal an abyss at my feet. I wanted to let things slide.

===Chinese===
她带着一副赏赐天大恩典的神气，仿佛相信我这一辈子都在等她的邀请，好跨进那个体面的圈子。至于我，眼见履行义务之路被堵死，倒觉出几分轻松。我并不想把事情告诉老姑母，从而把一切干脆利落、一劳永逸地断掉。本来是会闹出点什么的；空气也许会像暴雨过后那样豁然开朗；我也会知道自己站在何处了。可我害怕知道。这些暗处一旦透进光，也许会照出我脚下的深渊。我只想听任事情就这么滑下去。

===Original===
My aunt had returned to her accounts, the accounts which were the cogwheels that kept running the smooth course of the Etchingham estates. She seemed to wish to indicate that I counted for not very much in the scheme of things as she saw it.

===Chinese===
老姑母已经回到她的账目上去了——那些账目是一副副齿轮，维系着埃钦厄姆田产的顺当运转。她似乎有意示意：在她眼中的万事格局里，我这个人无足轻重。

===Original===
“I should like to make your better acquaintance,” she said, with her head still averted, “there are reasons.﻿ ﻿…” It came suddenly into my head that she had an idea of testamentary dispositions, that she felt she was breaking up, that I had my rights. I didn’t much care for the thing, but the idea of being the heir of Etchingham was﻿—well, was an idea. It would make me more possible to my pseudo-sister. It would be, as it were, a starting-point, would make me potentially a somebody of her sort of ideal. Moreover, I should be under the same roof, near her, with her sometimes. One asks so little more than that, that it seemed almost half the battle. I began to consider phrases of thanks and acceptance and then uttered them.

===Chinese===
“我倒愿意同你进一步相知，”她说，头仍偏着，“这里面有些缘故……”我突然想到，她是在盘算遗嘱上的处分，她觉得自己行将溃散，而我自有我的权利。这档子事我本不甚在意，可是，做埃钦厄姆的继承人这个念头——怎么说呢——到底是个念头。它会让我在那位假姐妹眼里更成为可能。它将是一个起点，可以说是起点，会让我有潜质成为她那一类理想中的一个人物。再者，我将住进同一个屋檐底下，在她近旁，有时同她在一起。人所求的比这多不了多少，于是这似乎几乎就是成功的一半了。我开始斟酌致谢与接受的措辞，随后便说了出来。

===Original===
I never quite understood the bearings of that scene; never quite whether my aunt really knew that my sister was not my sister. She was a wonderfully clever woman of the unscrupulous order, with a sangfroid and self-possession well calculated to let her cut short any inconvenient revelations. It was as if she had had long practice in the art, though I cannot say what occasion she can have had for its practice﻿—perhaps for the confounding of wavering avowers of Dissent at home.

===Chinese===
我始终不曾十分明白那一幕的种种关节；也始终不曾明白，老姑母究竟知不知道我的姐妹并非我的姐妹。她是个精明绝顶的女人，属于不择手段的那一类，那份冷静与自持，恰足以让她把任何不便的揭发半途掐断。那情形仿佛她在这门艺术上久经研习，虽然我说不出她能有什么研习的场合——也许是用来震慑家乡那些摇摆不定的非国教（Dissent）信奉者吧。

===Original===
I used to think that she knew, if not all, at least a portion; that the weight that undoubtedly was upon her mind was nothing else but that. She broke up, was breaking up from day to day, and I can think of no other reason. She had the air of being disintegrated, like a mineral under an immense weight﻿—quartz in a crushing mill; of being dulled and numbed as if she were under the influence of narcotics.

===Chinese===
我一向以为她是知道的，就算不全知，至少也知道一部分；她心上那份无可置疑的重负，不是别的，正是这件事。她在溃散，正一天天溃散下去，此外我想不出别的缘故。她有一种正被碾散的神气，像巨压之下的矿石——碎矿机里的石英；有一种昏沉麻木的样子，仿佛受了麻醉品的影响。

===Original===
There is little enough wonder, if she actually carried that imponderable secret about with her. I used to look at her sometimes, and wonder if she, too, saw the oncoming of the inevitable. She was limited enough in her ideas, but not too stupid to take that in if it presented itself. Indeed they have that sort of idea rather grimly before them all the time﻿—that class.

===Chinese===
倘若她当真随身携着那个无形的秘密，这也就没什么可怪的了。我有时望着她，纳闷她是否也看见了那不可避免者的逼近。她的见识够有限的了，可还不至于蠢到这个摆在眼前也领悟不了。的确，那一类人——那个阶级——是自始至终冷冷地把那种念头摆在眼前的。

===Original===
It must have been that that was daily, and little by little, pressing down her eyelids and deepening the quivering lines of her impenetrable face. She had a certain solitary grandeur, the pathos attaching to the last of a race, of a type; the air of waiting for the deluge, of listening for an inevitable sound﻿—the sound of oncoming waters.

===Chinese===
想必正是那个东西，日复一日、一点一点地压下她的眼皮，加深她那张莫测高深的脸上微微颤动的纹路。她自有一种孤独的庄严，一种系于一个种族、一种类型之末路的哀婉；一副坐等洪水临头的神气，一副谛听着某个无可回避的声响的神气——大水涌来的声响。

===Original===
It was weird, the time that I spent in that house﻿—more than weird﻿—deadening. It had an extraordinary effect on me﻿—an effect that my “sister,” perhaps, had carefully calculated. She made pretensions of that sort later on; said that she had been breaking me in to perform my allotted task in the bringing on of the inevitable.

===Chinese===
我在那座宅子里度过的那段时光很怪诞——不止怪诞——是叫人麻木的。它对我产生了一种异乎寻常的影响——一种我的“姐妹”也许早已精心算定了的影响。她后来果然以此自命；说她一直在驯练我，好叫我在促成那不可避免之事的过程中，尽我分派的职分。
===Original===
I have nowhere come across such an intense solitude as there was there, a solitude that threw one so absolutely upon one’s self and into one’s self. I used to sit working in one of those tall, panelled rooms, very high up in the air. I was writing at the series of articles for the *Bi-Monthly*, for Polehampton. I was to get the atmosphere of Paris, you remember. It was rather extraordinary, that process. Up there I seemed to be as much isolated from Paris as if I had been in﻿—well, in Hampton Court. It was almost impossible to write; I had things to think about: preoccupations, jealousies. It was true I had a living to make, but that seemed to have lost its engrossingness as a pursuit, or at least to have suspended it.

===Chinese===
我在别处从未遇见过像那里那样浓重的孤独，一种把人如此彻底地抛回自身、掷入内心的孤独。我惯常坐在那些高大的镶板房间中的一间里工作，高高凌在半空。我在为《双月刊》、为波尔汉普顿写那组文章。你要记得，我的差事就是把巴黎的气氛摄取下来。那个过程相当离奇。在那么高的地方，我与巴黎隔绝之深，仿佛我身处——唔，身处汉普顿宫（Hampton Court）。写作几乎不可能；我有心事要想：种种牵挂，种种醋意。不错，我是要谋生的，可谋生作为一门营生，似乎已失去了它引人入胜的力量，或者至少是暂时搁下了。

===Original===
The panels of the room seemed to act as a sounding-board, the belly of an immense cello. There were never any noises in the house, only whispers coming from an immense distance﻿—as when one drops stones down an unfathomable well and hears ages afterward the faint sound of disturbed waters. When I look back at that time I figure myself as forever sitting with uplifted pen, waiting for a word that would not come, and that I did not much care about getting. The panels of the room would creak sympathetically to the opening of the entrance-door of the house, the faintest of creaks; people would cross the immense hall to the room in which they plotted; would cross leisurely, with laughter and rustling of garments that after a long time reached my ears in whispers. Then I should have an access of mad jealousy. I wanted to be part of her life, but I could not stand that Salon of suspicious conspirators. What could I do there? Stand and look at them, conscious that they all dropped their voices instinctively when I came near them?

===Chinese===
房间的镶板仿佛起着共鸣板的作用，像一把大提琴的琴腹。宅子里从来没有任何声响，只有从极远处传来的低语——像人往一口深不可测的井里丢下石子，隔了许久许久，才听见搅动的水那微弱的声音。回首那段时光，我总把自己想成永远举着笔坐在那里，等一个不肯来的字眼——那个字眼，我也并不怎么在乎等得着等不着。宅子大门一开，房间的镶板便应和着吱呀一响——最轻微的一声吱呀；人们穿过那巨大的厅堂，往他们密谋的房间去；从容地穿过去，人语与衣料的窸窣，要过好一阵才以低语的形式传到我耳中。这时我便要发作一阵疯狂的醋意。我想成为她生活的一部分，可我受不了那个满是可疑阴谋家的沙龙。我在那儿能做什么呢？站着看他们，同时心里明白，他们一见我走近，便都本能地压低了嗓音？

===Original===
That was the general tone of that space of time, but, of course, it was not always that. I used to emerge now and then to breakfast sympathetically with my aunt, sometimes to sit through a meal with the two of them. I danced attendance on them singly; paid depressing calls with my aunt; calls on the people in the Faubourg; people without any individuality other than a kind of desiccation, the shrivelled appearance and point of view of a dried pippin. In revenge, they had names that startled one, names that recalled the generals and flaneurs of an impossibly distant time; names that could hardly have had any existence outside the memoirs of Madame de Sévigné, the names of people that could hardly have been fitted to do anything more vigorous than be reflected in the mirrors of the *Salle des Glaces*. I was so absolutely depressed, so absolutely in a state of suspended animation, that I seemed to conform exactly to my aunt’s ideas of what was desirable in me as an attendant on her at these functions. I used to stand behind chairs and talk, like a good young man, to the assorted *Pères* and *Abbés* who were generally present.

===Chinese===
那段时期大体上就是这个调子，当然，也不尽然。我偶尔露一露面，满怀同情地陪老姑母进早餐，有时也同她们两个一起坐完一顿饭。我分别随侍着她们两位；陪老姑母出去作些叫人丧气的拜访——拜访圣日耳曼区那些人家；那些人除了一种干缩之外再无个性，外貌和见解都像一颗干瘪起皱的皮平苹果（pippin）。作为补偿，他们的名字倒叫人一惊，让人想起一个遥远得不可想象的时代里的将军们和浪荡子（flaneurs）；那些名字几乎不可能在塞维涅夫人（Madame de Sévigné）的书简之外存在，那些名字的主人，几乎安派不上任何比映在镜厅（Salle des Glaces）的镜子里去更费力气的用场。我是那么彻底地意气消沉，那么彻底地处于一种假死状态，以至于竟似乎恰恰合乎老姑母的心意——在她眼里，我随她出席这类场合，要的正是这么一个随侍。我惯常站在椅子背后，像个好青年似的，同通常在场的那些形形色色的神父（Pères）、司铎（Abbés）攀谈。

===Original===
And then I used to go home and get the atmospheres of these people. I must have done it abominably badly, for the notes that brought Polehampton’s cheques were accompanied by the bravos of that gentleman and the assurances that Miss Polehampton liked my work﻿—liked it very much.

===Chinese===
然后我便回家去，摄取这些人的气氛。我一定写得糟透了，因为随波尔汉普顿的支票一同寄来的短简里，总伴着那位先生的喝彩，还有波尔汉普顿小姐喜欢我的文字——非常喜欢——的种种保证。

===Original===
I suppose I exhibited myself in the capacity of the man who knew﻿—who could let you into a thing or two. After all, anyone could write about students’ balls and the lakes in the Bois, but it took *someone* to write “with knowledge” of the interiors of the barred houses in the Rue de l’Université.

===Chinese===
我想，我是在以“知情之人”的身份展示自己——一个能让你窥见一二内幕的人。毕竟，学生舞会、布洛涅林园（the Bois）的湖水，谁都写得出；可要“内行地”写出大学街（Rue de l’Université）那些装着铁栅的宅邸的内部，那就得是个人物才行了。

===Original===
Then, too, I attended the more showy entertainments with my sister. I had by now become so used to hearing her styled “your sister” that the epithet had the quality of a name. She was “*mademoiselle votre soeur*,” as she might have been Mlle. Patience or Hope, without having anything of the named quality. What she did at the entertainments, the charitable bazaars, the dismal dances, the impossibly bad concerts, I have no idea. She must have had some purpose, for she did nothing without. I myself descended into fulfilling the functions of a rudimentarily developed chaperon﻿—functions similar in importance to those performed by the eyes of a mole. I had the maddest of accesses of jealousy if she talked to a man﻿—and *such* men﻿—or danced with one. And then I was forever screwing my courage up and feeling it die away. We used to drive about in a coupé, a thing that shut us inexorably together, but which quite as inexorably destroyed all opportunities for what one calls making love. In smooth streets its motion was too glib, on the *pavé* it rattled too abominably. I wanted to make love to her﻿—oh, immensely, but I was never in the mood, or the opportunity was never forthcoming. I used to have the wildest fits of irritation; not of madness or of depression, but of simple wildness at the continual recurrence of small obstacles. I couldn’t read, couldn’t bring myself to it. I used to sit and look dazedly at the English newspapers﻿—at any newspaper but the *Hour*. De Mersch had, for the moment, disappeared. There were troubles in his elective grand duchy﻿—he had, indeed, contrived to make himself unpopular with the electors, excessively unpopular. I used to read piquant articles about his embroglio in an American paper that devoted itself to matters of the sort. All sorts of international difficulties were to arise if de Mersch were ejected. There was some other obscure prince of a rival house, Prussian or Russian, who had desires for the degree of royalty that sat so heavily on de Mersch. Indeed, I think there were two rival princes, each waiting with portmanteaux packed and manifestos in their breast pockets, ready to pass de Mersch’s frontiers.

===Chinese===
再说，我也陪着我的“姐妹”出席那些更铺张的社交集会。到这时，听人管她叫“您的姐妹”已经听得如此习惯，这个称呼都有了专名的性质。她成了“您的姐妹小姐”（mademoiselle votre soeur），仿佛她本名可以叫“忍耐小姐”或“希望小姐”（Mlle. Patience or Hope）似的，却毫不具备名字所指的那种品质。她在那些集会——慈善义卖、死气沉沉的舞会、糟糕得无以复加的音乐会——上究竟做些什么，我毫无所知。她必定自有目的，因为她做事从无一件没有目的。我自己呢，沦落到去履行一个发育未全的陪护人（chaperon）的职能——其重要程度，约等于鼹鼠的眼睛所履行的职能。她同哪个男人说话——还是“那样”的男人——或是同谁跳舞，我便要发作最疯狂的醋意。再有，我永远在鼓起勇气，又永远觉出它消散。我们惯常乘一辆双座轿式马车（coupé）出游，那玩意儿把我们无可转圜地关在一处，却又同样无可转圜地毁掉一切可供人所谓谈情说爱的机会。平坦的街上它滑得太溜，铺石路面（pavé）上它又颠簸得不像话。我想向她求爱——哦，非常想——可我从来凑不上情绪，或者说机会从来不肯露面。我惯常发作最狂乱的烦躁；不是疯狂，也不是消沉，只是对小障碍层出不穷的单纯的狂乱。我读不进书，怎么也读不进。我惯常坐着，怔怔地望英文报纸——望任何一份报纸，独独不望《时辰报》。德·默施一时不见了踪影。他的选任大公国出了乱子——他确实有本事把自己弄得在选举人当中大失人心，失心到了极处。我惯常在一份专写这类事情的美国报纸上，读到关于他那场乱子的辛辣文章。倘若德·默施被撵下台，种种国际纠纷将随之而起。另有一位不那么知名的亲王，出自对立的王室——普鲁士的也罢，俄国的也罢——觊觎着那个压得德·默施够呛的王室尊位。的确，我记得对立的亲王有两位，个个行囊都已捆好，胸袋里揣着宣言，随时准备跨过德·默施的边境。

===Original===
The grievances of his subjects﻿—so the Paris-American *Gazette* said﻿—were intimately connected with matters of finance, and de Mersch’s personal finances and his grand ducal were inextricably mixed up with the wildcat schemes with which he was seeking to make a fortune large enough to enable him to laugh at half a dozen elective grand duchies. Indeed, de Mersch’s own portmanteau was reported to be packed against the day when British support of his Greenland schemes would let him afford to laugh at his cantankerous Diet.

===Chinese===
他臣民的怨怼——据那份巴黎出版的美国《公报》（Paris-American Gazette）说——同财政事项牵连甚深；而德·默施的私人财务与他大公国的财务，又同他借以谋利的那几套野猫式投机计划纠缠不清——他要牟的利，得大到足以让他把半打选任大公国都不放在眼里。的确，据报道，德·默施自己的行李箱也已收拾停当，单等英国人对他的格陵兰计划的支持成全他，让他负担得起对自己那个爱闹别扭的议会一笑置之的那一天。

===Original===
The thing interested me so little that I never quite mastered the details of it. I wished the man no good, but so long as he kept out of my way I was not going to hate him actively. Finally the affairs of Holstein-Launewitz ceased to occupy the papers﻿—the thing was arranged and the Russian and Prussian princes unpacked their portmanteaux, and, I suppose, consigned their manifestos to the flames, or adapted them to the needs of other principalities. De Mersch’s affairs ceded their space in the public prints to the topic of the dearness of money. Somebody, somewhere, was said to be up to something. I used to try to read the articles, to master the details, because I disliked finding a whole field of thought of which I knew absolutely nothing. I used to read about the great discount houses and other things that conveyed absolutely nothing to my mind. I only gathered that the said great houses were having a very bad time, and that everybody else was having a very much worse.

===Chinese===
这件事引不起我多少兴致，以致我始终不曾弄通它的细节。我不祝这人好运，可只要他不挡我的路，我也不打算积极地恨他。终于，霍尔斯坦-劳内维茨的事务不再占据报端——事情料理妥当了，俄国亲王和普鲁士亲王拆开了各自的行李，而且，我猜，把各自的宣言付之一炬，或者改写一番，去凑别的公国的需要。德·默施的事务把公共报刊上的位置，让给了银根昂贵的题目。据说有什么人，在什么地方，正捣鼓着什么。我一向努力去读那些文章、掌握那些细节，因为我不喜欢发现自己面对整整一个思想领域却绝对一无所知。我一向读到那些大贴现行，读到别的种种对我的头脑绝对不传达任何意思的东西。我只汇集出一点：上述那几家大商号日子很不好过，而其余所有人的日子还要糟得多。

===Original===
One day, indeed, the matter was brought home to me by the receipt from Polehampton of bills instead of my usual cheques. I had a good deal of trouble in cashing the things; indeed, people seemed to look askance at them. I consulted my aunt on the subject, at breakfast. It was the sort of thing that interested the woman of business in her, and we were always short of topics of conversation.

===Chinese===
果然有一天，这件事落到我自己头上来了：波尔汉普顿寄来的不是往常的支票，而是期票。兑现这几张纸让我费了不少周折；说真的，人们对它们似乎都要侧目而视。早餐桌上，我就此事请教了老姑母。这正是能唤起她身上那个女商人的兴趣的那类事，何况我们向来短缺谈话的话题。

===Original===
We breakfasted in rather a small room, as rooms went there; my aunt sitting at the head of the table, with an early morning air of being en famille that she wore at no other time of day. It was not a matter of garments, for she was not the woman to wear a peignoir; but lay, I supposed, in her manner, which did not begin to assume frigidity until several watches of the day had passed.

===Chinese===
我们在一间按那座宅子的标准而言相当狭小的房间里用早餐；老姑母坐在桌子的上首，带着一副清晨时分一家人似的随意（en famille）的神气——一天里旁的时刻她从不这样。这无关衣着，因为她不是穿晨衣（peignoir）的那种女人；而是——我猜——在于她的举止：那举止要等一天过去好几个时辰，才开始显出冷峻。

===Original===
I handed her Polehampton’s bills and explained that I was at a loss to turn them to account; that I even had only the very haziest of ideas as to their meaning. Holding the forlorn papers in her hand, she began to lecture me on the duty of acquiring the rudiments of what she called “business habits.”

===Chinese===
我把波尔汉普顿的期票递给她，解释说我不知如何把它们变成可用的钱；甚至对它们的含义，也只有极其模糊的概念。她把那几张寒碜的纸拿在手里，开始训诫我，说我有责任学一学她所谓的“生意习惯”的入门功夫。

===Original===
“Of course you do not require to master details to any considerable extent,” she said, “but I always have held that it is one of the duties of a.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“当然，细节你不必掌握到相当深的地步，”她说，“可我一向认为，这是一位……的本分之一。”

===Original===
She interrupted herself as my sister came into the room; looked at her, and then held out the papers in her hand. The things quivered a little; the hand must have quivered too.

===Chinese===
我的“姐妹”走进屋来，她的话头到此打住；看了看她，然后把手里的票据递了过去。那几张纸微微一颤；那只手想必也颤了。

===Original===
“You are going to Halderschrodt’s?” she said, interrogatively. “You could get him to negotiate these for Etchingham?”

===Chinese===
“你要去哈尔德施罗特那儿？”她以询问的口气说。“能不能请他替埃钦厄姆把这几张通融掉？”

===Original===
Miss Granger looked at the papers negligently.

===Chinese===
格兰杰小姐漫不经心地看了看那几张票据。

===Original===
“I am going this afternoon,” she answered. “Etchingham can come.﻿ ﻿…” She suddenly turned to me: “So your friend is getting shaky,” she said.

===Chinese===
“我今天下午去，”她答道。“埃钦厄姆可以来……”她突然朝我转过身来：“这么说，你那位朋友有点摇摇欲坠了。”

===Original===
“It means that?” I asked. “But I’ve heard that he has done the same sort of thing before.”

===Chinese===
“这就是说，到那步田地了？”我问。“可我听说，他以前也干过这类事。”

===Original===
“He must have been shaky before,” she said, “but I daresay Halderschrodt.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“那他以前必定早就摇摇欲坠了，”她说，“不过我敢说哈尔德施罗特……”

===Original===
“Oh, it’s hardly worth while bothering that personage about such a sum,” I interrupted. Halderschrodt, in those days, was a name that suggested no dealings in any sum less than a million.

===Chinese===
“哦，为这么点数目去惊动那尊人物，实在犯不上，”我插嘴道。那时候，哈尔德施罗特这名字意味着：百万以下的款项，概不沾手。

===Original===
“My dear Etchingham,” my aunt interrupted in a shocked tone, “it is quite worth his while to oblige us.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的埃钦厄姆，”老姑母用震骇的口气打断我，“为我们效劳，完全值得他费这份心……”
===Original===
“I didn’t know,” I said.

===Chinese===
“这我不知道，”我说。

===Original===
That afternoon we drove to Halderschrodt’s private office, a sumptuous﻿—that is the mot juste﻿—suite of rooms on the first floor of the house next to the Duc de Mersch’s *Sans Souci*.
