“You have seen her?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“您见过她了？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, I called two or three weeks﻿—no, months﻿—ago. She’s the image of your poor, dear mother,” he added, “at that age; I remarked upon it to your aunt, but, of course, she could not remember. They were not married until after the quarrel.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我去拜望过——是两三个礼拜……不，几个月……以前的事了。她活脱就是你那可怜的、亲爱的母亲，”他补了一句，“就是那个年纪的模样；我对你姑母提起过，可她自然记不得了。他们是吵翻之后才结的婚。”

===Original===
A sudden restlessness made me bolt the rest of my tepid dinner. With my return to the upper world, and the return to me of a will, despair of a sort had come back. I had before me the problem﻿—the necessity﻿—of winning her. Once I was out of contact with her she grew smaller, less of an idea, more of a person﻿—that one could win. And there were two ways. I must either woo her as one woos a person barred; must compel her to take flight, to abandon, to cast away everything; or I must go to her as an eligible suitor with the Etchingham acres and possibilities of a future on that basis. This fantastic old man with his mumbled reminiscences spoilt me for the last. One remembers sooner or later that a county-man may not marry his reputed sister without scandal. And I craved her intensely.

===Chinese===
一阵突然的不宁，让我把剩下的半温不火的晚饭囫囵吞了。随着我回到上层世界，随着一副意志重新回到我身上，某种绝望也回来了。我面前摆着这道题——这桩必要——把她赢到手。一旦同她断了接触，她就变小了，不那么像一个观念，更像一个人——一个赢得到手的人。路子有两条：我要么像追求一个被禁阻的人那样去求她，逼她出走，逼她舍弃、抛开一切；要么就作为一个合意的求婚者到她跟前去，带着埃钦厄姆的田产，带着在这块底子上展开前程的种种可能。这位满口含混旧事的荒唐老头，把后一条路给我毁掉了。人迟早会想起：一个郡望人家的子弟，要娶他名义上的“姐妹”，不闹出丑闻是办不到的。而我对她的渴念，炽烈得很。

===Original===
She had upon me the effect of an incredible stimulant; away from her I was like a drunkard cut off from his liquor; an opium-taker from his drug. I hardly existed; I hardly thought.

===Chinese===
她于我，有一种不可思议的兴奋剂般的效用；离开她，我就像断了酒的酒鬼，断了瘾药的鸦片鬼。我几乎不存在；我几乎不思想。

===Original===
I had an errand at my aunt’s house; had a message to deliver, sympathetic enquiries to make﻿—and I wanted to see her, to gain some sort of information from her; to spy out the land; to ask her for terms. There was a change in the appearance of the house, an adventitious brightness that indicated the rise in the fortunes of the family. For me the house was empty and the great door closed hollowly behind me. My sister was not at home. It seemed abominable to me that she should be out; that she could be talking to anyone, or could exist without me. I went sullenly across the road to the palings of the square. As I turned the corner I found my head pivoting on my neck. I was looking over my shoulder at the face of the house, was wondering which was her window.

===Chinese===
我在姑母宅上有一桩差事：有一句话要传，几句慰问的话要问——我也想见见她，想从她那里探得某种消息；探一探地势；同她讲一讲条件。宅子的外观有了变化，一种外加的亮丽，显出这一家的气运正在上升。在我感觉里，宅子是空的，大门在我身后空空洞洞地阖上了。我的“姐妹”不在家。她竟出去了；竟能同什么人谈着话，竟能没有我也照样存在——这在我看来可憎之极。我阴沉着脸穿过马路，走到广场的围栏边。拐过街角时，我发觉自己的脑袋在脖颈上自转起来。我正回头望着宅子的正面，猜度哪一扇是她的窗。

===Original===
“Like a lovesick boy﻿—like a damn lovesick boy,” I growled at myself. My sense of humour was returning to me. There began a pilgrimage in search of companionship.

===Chinese===
“像个害相思病的毛孩子——像个该死的害相思病的毛孩子，”我冲自己咕哝。我的幽默感正在回来。于是一场寻求人做伴的巡礼开始了。

===Original===
London was a desert more solitary than was believable. On those brilliant summer evenings the streets were crowded, were alive, bustled with the chitter-chatter of footsteps, with the chitter-chatter of voices, of laughter.

===Chinese===
伦敦是一片荒漠，荒凉得超乎可信。在那些明晃晃的夏夜，街上熙熙攘攘，一派生气，喧腾着脚步的嘁嘁喳喳，人声的嘁嘁喳喳，笑声的嘁嘁喳喳。

===Original===
It was impossible to walk, impossible to do more than tread on one’s own toes; one was almost blinded by the constant passing of faces. It was like being in a wheat-field with one’s eyes on a level with the indistinguishable ears. One was alone in one’s intense contempt for all these faces, all these contented faces; one towered intellectually above them; one towered into regions of rarefaction. And down below they enjoyed themselves. One understood life better; they better how to live. That struck me then﻿—in Oxford Street. There was the intense good-humour, the absolute disregard of the minor inconveniences, of the inconveniences of a crowd, of the ignominy of being one of a crowd. There was the intense poetry of the soft light, the poetry of the summer-night coolness, and they understood how to enjoy it. I turned up an ancient court near Bedford Row.

===Chinese===
走路是不可能了，除了踩自己的脚趾，旁的什么也做不成；一张张脸不住地掠过，人几乎被晃瞎了眼。就像置身一片麦田，眼睛恰恰同那些分辨不清的麦穗齐平。人在自己对所有这些脸——所有这些心满意足的脸——的强烈蔑视里孑然独处；人在智性上高踞于他们之上；高踞进空气稀薄的地带。而在底下，他们自得其乐。人更懂得生活；他们更懂得怎么活。这念头是当时击中我的——在牛津街（Oxford Street）上。那里有一种浓烈的兴高采烈，一种对种种小小不便的绝对置之度外——对人群的不便，对沦为人群中一分子的屈辱。那里有柔光浓烈的诗意，有夏夜清凉的诗意，而他们懂得怎样去消受。我拐进了贝德福德街（Bedford Row）附近一条古老的巷子。

===Original===
“In the name of God,” I said, “I will enjoy﻿ ﻿…” and I did. The poetry of those old deserted quarters came suddenly home to me﻿—all the little commonplace thoughts; all the commonplace associations of Georgian London. For the time I was done with the meanings of things.

===Chinese===
“以上帝的名义，”我说，“我要享乐……”而我确实享乐了。那些老而空落的街区，其诗意突然直抵我心——全是些小小的平庸念头；全是乔治王朝伦敦的平庸联想。那一阵子，我同万物的意义两讫了。

===Original===
I was seeking Lea﻿—he was not at home. The quarter was honeycombed with the homes of people one knows; of people one used to know, excellent young men who wrote for the papers, who subedited papers, who designed posters, who were always just the same. One forgot them for a year or two, one came across them again and found them just the same﻿—still writing for the same papers, still subediting the same papers, designing the same posters. I was in the mood to rediscover them in the privacies of their hearths, with the same excellent wives making fair copies of the same manuscripts, with the same gaiety of the same indifferent whiskey, brown or pale or suspicious-looking, in heavy, square, cut-glass stoppered decanters, and with the same indifferent Virginian tobacco at the same level in the same jars.

===Chinese===
我找的是利——他不在家。那片街区蜂窝似的密布着熟人的家；旧日熟人的家——都是些优秀的青年，给报纸写稿，替报纸编稿，设计海报，永远原封不动。人把他们忘上一两年，再撞见时，发现他们还是原样——还在给同样的报纸写稿，还在编同样的报纸，设计同样的海报。我正有兴致到他们炉边的私寓里去重新发现他们：身边还是那些贤惠依旧的太太，誊着同样的稿件；还是同样的威士忌——棕色的、浅色的、或颜色可疑的——盛在沉重的方形雕花玻璃塞的酒瓶里，带着同样无所谓的欢快；还是同样无所谓的弗吉尼亚烟草，在同样的罐子里齐着同样的存量。

===Original===
I was in the mood for this stability, for the excellent household article that was their view of life and literature. I wanted to see it again, to hear again how it was filling the unvarying, allotted columns of the daily, the weekly, or the monthly journals. I wanted to breathe again this mild atmosphere where there are no longer hopes or fears. But, alas!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我正向往这种恒定，向往那件优良的家用什器——那就是他们对生活与文学的见解。我想再见到它，再听听它如何填满日报、周报、月刊上那分派既定、一成不变的栏目。我想再呼吸呼吸这温和的空气——在这里，希望与恐惧都已不再。可是，唉！……

===Original===
I rang bell after bell of that gloomy central London district. You know what happens. One pulls the knob under the name of the person one seeks﻿—pulls it three, or, it may be, four times in vain. One rings the housekeeper’s bell; it reverberates, growing fainter and fainter, gradually stifled by a cavernous subterranean atmosphere. After an age a head peeps round the opening door, the head of a hopeless anachronism, the head of a widow of early Victorian merit, or of an orphan of incredible age. One asks for So-and-so﻿—he’s out; for Williams﻿—he’s expecting an increase of family, and has gone into the country with madame. And Waring? Oh, he’s gone no one knows where, and Johnson who used to live at Number 44 only comes up to town on Tuesdays now. I exhausted the possibilities of that part of Bloomsbury, the possibilities of variety in the types of housekeepers. The rest of London divided itself into bands﻿—into zones. Between here and Kensington the people that I knew could not be called on after dinner, those who lived at Chiswick and beyond were hyperborean﻿—one was bound by the exigencies of time. It was ten o’clock as I stood reflecting on a doorstep﻿—on Johnson’s doorstep. I must see somebody, must talk to somebody, before I went to bed in the cheerless room at the club. It was true I might find a political stalwart in the smoking-room﻿—but that was a last resort, a desperate and ignominious pis aller.

===Chinese===
我在伦敦中心那片阴郁的地段，一家接一家地按铃。你知道那是怎么回事。人在要找的那人名牌底下拉铃钮——拉上三回，或者兴许四回，白拉。人去按管家的铃；铃声空空地回荡，一阵弱似一阵，渐渐被一种地窖般的地下气氛闷熄。过了一个纪元，才有一颗脑袋从开缝的门后探出来——一颗无可救药的时代错乱者的脑袋，一颗维多利亚早期贤德的寡妇的脑袋，或者一颗年纪大得难以置信的孤儿的脑袋。人找某某——他出去了；找威廉斯（Williams）——他家就要添丁，带着太太下乡去了；那么沃林（Waring）呢？哦，他去了个谁也不知道的地方；还有约翰逊（Johnson），从前住在四十四号的，如今只有礼拜二才进城一趟。我把布卢姆斯伯里那一角的可能性用尽了——把管家婆类型的花样用尽了。伦敦的其余部分自分成带——分成地带。从这里到肯辛顿，我认识的人晚饭后便不便再去拜望；住在奇斯威克（Chiswick）及更远的，就属于极北边地了——人被时间的急务捆住了。我在一道门阶上站着出神——在约翰逊的门阶上——已是十点钟了。上床之前——到俱乐部那间凄清的房间里去睡之前——我必须见一见什么人，同一同什么人说话。不错，吸烟室里或许找得到一位政界老将——可那是最后一招，一记孤注一掷、有失体面的下策（pis aller）。

===Original===
There was Fox, I should find him at the office. But it needed a change of tone before I could contemplate with equanimity the meeting of that individual. I had been preparing myself to confront all the ethically excellent young men and Fox was, ethically speaking, far from excellent, middle-aged, rubicund, leery﻿—a free lance of genius. I made the necessary change in my tone of mind and ran him to earth.

===Chinese===
还有福克斯，我该能在报馆里找到他。可要想心平气和地面对同此人相见，先得换一换心绪。我原本是把自己调理好了去会那班品行端方的青年的，而福克斯——就品行而论——远远谈不上端方：人到中年，红光满面，目光油滑——一位自行其是的天才。我把心境作了必要的调整，便循迹把他追到了老巢。

===Original===
The Watteau room was further enlivened by the introduction of a scarlet plush couch of sumptuous design. By its side stood a couple of electric lights. The virulent green of their shades made the colours of the be-shepherded wall-panels appear almost unearthly, and threw impossible shadows on the deal partition. Round the couch stood chairs with piles of papers neatly arranged on them; round it, on the floor, were more papers lying like the leaves of autumn that one sings of. On it lay Fox, enveloped in a Shetland shawl﻿—a good shawl that was the only honest piece of workmanship in the torn-tawdry place. Fox was as rubicund as ever, but his features were noticeably peaked and there were heavy lines under his eyes﻿—lines cast into deep shadow by the light by which he was reading. I entered unannounced, and was greeted by an indifferent upward glance that changed into one of something like pleasure as he made out my features in the dim light.

===Chinese===
那间华托房间又添了一重新热闹：搬进来一张式样豪华的猩红长毛绒沙发。它旁边立着两盏电灯。灯罩那烈性的绿色，把墙上那些绘满牧羊人的板壁的颜色衬得几乎非人间所有，又在杉木隔板上投下不可能的影子。沙发四周摆着椅子，椅上一摞摞文件码放得整整齐齐；四周地上还有更多的文件，像诗里咏叹的秋叶般铺陈着。福克斯躺在沙发上，裹在一条设得兰（Shetland）披巾里——一条好披巾，是这间破旧俗丽的屋子里唯一一件货真价实的做工。福克斯依旧红光满面，可五官分明尖削了，眼睛底下压着两道沉沉的纹路——他借着读书的那盏灯光，把纹路投成深深的阴影。我没等通报便进去了；迎我的是淡淡朝上的一瞥，等他在昏暗中辨认出我的眉目，那一瞥变成了近乎喜悦的一瞥。

===Original===
“Hullo, you old country hawbuck,” he said, with spasmodic jocularity; “I’m uncommon glad to see you.” He came to a jerky close, with an indrawing of his breath. “I’m about done,” he went on. “Same old thing﻿—sciatica. Took me just after I got here this afternoon; sent out one of the messengers to buy me a sofa, and here I’ve been ever since. Well, and what’s brought you up﻿—don’t answer, I know all about it. I’ve got to keep on talking until this particular spasm’s over, or else I shall scream and disturb the flow of Soane’s leader. Well, and now you’ve come, you’ll stop and help me to put the *Hour* to bed, won’t you? And then you can come and put me to bed.”

===Chinese===
“喂，你这老庄稼汉，”他说，带着一阵阵发作似的诙谐；“见到你我高兴得很。”他猛地刹住话头，倒吸了一口气。“我差不多垮了，”他接着说。“还是老毛病——坐骨神经痛。今天下午我刚到这儿它就把我放倒了；我派了个报馆听差出去给我买了张沙发，打那以后就一直待在这儿。唔，你上城来干什么——别回答，我全知道。我得不停地说话，说到这阵痉挛过去为止，不然我就要喊出声来，搅了索恩那篇社论的文气。好啦，你既来了，就留下来帮我把我这份《时辰报》付印，好不好？然后你再过来把我送上床。”

===Original===
He went on talking at high pressure, exaggerating his expressions, heightening his humorous touches with punctuations of rather wild laughter. At last he came to a stop with a half suppressed “Ah!” and a long indrawing of the breath.

===Chinese===
他继续高压地谈着，神情夸张，诙谐的点缀用一阵阵颇狂放的笑声来断句。终于他停了下来，半憋住一声“啊！”，长长地吸了一口气。

===Original===
“That’s over,” he said. “Give me a drop of brandy﻿—there’s a good fellow.” I gave him his nip. Then I explained to him that I couldn’t work for the *Hour*; that I wasn’t on terms with de Mersch.

===Chinese===
“过去了，”他说。“给我来一口白兰地——乖，做个好伙计。”我给了他那一小盅。然后我向他解释，说我不能再为《时辰报》做事了；说我同德·默施闹翻了。

===Original===
“Been dropping money over him?” he asked, cheerfully. I explained a little more﻿—that there was a lady.

===Chinese===
“在他身上蚀了钱？”他兴致很好地问。我又多解释了一句——说这里面有位姑娘。

===Original===
“Oh, it’s *that*,” Fox said. “The man *is* a fool﻿ ﻿… But anyhow Mersch don’t count for much in this particular show. He’s no money in it even, so you may put your pride in your pocket, or wherever you keep it. It’s all right. Straight. He’s only the small change.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是‘那个’缘故，”福克斯说。“那家伙‘确实’是个傻瓜……不过反正，这出戏里默施算不得什么。他在这上头连钱都不占份，所以你尽管把你的自尊揣进口袋，或者搁进你收着它的随便什么地方。没事的。真的。他不过是点找头。”

===Original===
“But,” I said, “everyone says; you said yourself.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我说，“人人都这么说；您自己也曾说过。……”

===Original===
“To be sure,” he answered. “But you don’t think that *I* play second fiddle to a bounder of that calibre. Not really?”

===Chinese===
“说的是，”他答道。“可你总不至于以为，‘我’会给那种成色的粗坯拉第二小提琴吧。不至于吧？”

===Original===
He looked at me with a certain seriousness. I remembered, as I had remembered once before, that Fox was a personality﻿—a power. I had never realised till then how entirely﻿—fundamentally﻿—different he was from any other man that I knew. He was surprising enough to have belonged to another race. He looked at me, not as if he cared whether I gave him his due or no, but as if he were astonished at my want of perception of the fact. He let his towzled head fall back upon the plush cushions. “You might kick him from here to Greenland for me,” he said; “I wouldn’t weep. It suits me to hold him up, and a kicking might restore his equilibrium. I’m sick of him﻿—I’ve told him so. I knew there *was* a woman. But don’t you worry; *I’m* the man here.”

===Chinese===
他带着某种严肃望着我。我想起来了——从前也这样想起过一回——福克斯是个人物——是一股势力。直到那时我才明白，他同我认识的任何别人有怎样的全然——根本——的不同。他惊人得足以另属一个族类。他看我的神情，并不像在计较我给不给他应得的评价，倒像是诧异于我竟看不出这个事实。他把那颗蓬乱的脑袋向后一仰，靠进长毛绒垫子里。“你尽管替我把他从这儿一路踢到格陵兰去，”他说；“我不会掉一滴泪。抬举他于我有用，而挨一顿踢，说不定倒能恢复他的平衡。我烦透他了——我也当面同他这么说过。我早知道那里头‘确实’有个女人。不过你别发愁；这里当家的‘是’我。”

===Original===
“If that’s the case﻿ ﻿…” I said.

===Chinese===
“既然如此……”我说。

===Original===
“Oh, that’s it,” he answered.

===Chinese===
“哦，正是这么回事，”他答道。

===Original===
I helped him to put the paper to bed; took some of the work off his hands. It was all part of the getting back to life; of the resuming of rusty armour; and I wanted to pass the night. I was not unused to it, as it happened. Fox had had several of these fits during my year, and during most of them I had helped him through the night; once or twice for three on end. Once I had had entire control for a matter of five nights. But they gave me a new idea of Fox, those two or three weird hours that night. It was as if I had never seen him before. The attacks grew more virulent as the night advanced. He groaned and raved, and said things﻿—oh, the most astounding things in gibberish that upset one’s nerves and everything else. At the height he sang hymns, and then, as the fits passed, relapsed into incredible clear-headedness. It gave me, I say, a new idea of Fox. It was as if, for all the time I had known him, he had been playing a part, and that only now, in the delirium of his pain, in the madness into which he drank himself, were fragments of the real man thrown to the surface. I grew, at last, almost afraid to be alone with him in the dead small hours of the morning, and longed for the time when I could go to bed among the uninspiring, marble-topped furniture of my club.

===Chinese===
我帮着把手头的报纸付了印；接过了他一部分活计。这全是重新回到生活里去的一部分；是重新披挂起生锈的甲胄；而我也正想把这一夜打发过去。事有凑巧，这于我不算生手。在我供职的那一年里，福克斯这样发作过好几回，大多数时候都是我帮他熬夜撑过去的；有一两回一连三夜。有一回，足足五个夜晚，整份报纸全归我一人掌管。可是那一夜那两三个诡异的钟头，给了我一个全新的福克斯。仿佛我从前从未见过他。夜越深，发作越凶。他呻吟，胡言，说着种种事情——哦，都是些咿咿呀呀、最骇人听闻的话，搅得人的神经和其余一切都乱了套。痛到极处，他唱起圣歌来；随后，一阵阵发作过去，他又跌回一种令人难以置信的头脑清明。我说，这给了我一个全新的福克斯。仿佛在我认识他的全部时日里，他一直在扮演一个角色；只有此时此刻，在他疼痛的谵妄里，在他借酒把自己灌进去的疯狂里，那真人的碎片才被抛上水面。到后来，在凌晨死寂的时辰里同他单独相对，我几乎有些害怕，只盼着早一刻回到俱乐部，在那些毫不鼓动人的大理石面家具中间上床去。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===
At noon of the next day I gave Fox his look in at his own flat. He was stretched upon a sofa﻿—it was evident that I was to take such of his duties as were takeable. He greeted me with words to that effect.

===Chinese===
第二天中午，我到福克斯自己的寓所去探望了他一回。他直挺挺躺在沙发上——显然，他那些可以移交的职务，都要由我来接手了。他招呼我的话，也正是这个意思。

===Original===
“Don’t go filling the paper with your unbreeched geniuses,” he said, genially, “and don’t overwork yourself. There’s really nothing to do, but your being there will keep that little beast Evans from getting too cock-a-hoop. He’d like to jerk me out altogether; thinks they’d get on just as well without me.”

===Chinese===
“别净拿你那些连裤子还没穿上的天才去填报纸，”他和和气气地说，“也别把自己累坏了。其实真没什么事可做，不过有你坐在那儿，就能叫埃文斯那小畜生翘不起尾巴。他巴不得把我整个儿挤出去呢；还觉得没了我，他们照样办得下去。”

===Original===
I expressed in my manner general contempt for Evans, and was taking my leave.

===Chinese===
我在举止间对埃文斯表露了笼统的鄙夷，随即告辞。

===Original===
“Oh, and﻿—” Fox called after me. I turned back. “The Greenland mail ought to be in today. If Callan’s contrived to get his floodgates open, run his stuff in, there’s a good chap. It’s a feature and all that, you know.”

===Chinese===
“哦，还有——”福克斯在身后喊我。我回过身去。“格陵兰的邮件今天该到了。要是卡伦设法把闸门打开了，就把他的稿子登上去，乖，好伙计。这可是篇特稿之类的重头文章，你知道。”

===Original===
“I suppose Soane’s to have a look at it,” I asked.

===Chinese===
“我想这稿子总该先让索恩过目吧？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” he answered; “but tell him to keep strictly to old Cal’s lines﻿—rub that into him. If he were to get drunk and run in some of his own tips it’d be awkward. People are expecting Cal’s stuff. Tell you what: you take him out to lunch, eh? Keep an eye on the supplies, and ram it into him that he’s got to stick to Cal’s line of argument.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是的，”他答道，“不过要告诉他，严格照老卡尔的路线来——把这一点给他死死擦进去。他要是一喝醉，掺进几条他自己的独家消息，那可就难办了。大家盼的是卡尔的东西。这么着吧：你带他出去吃顿午饭，嗯？盯紧酒水，再给他狠狠敲进脑子里去：他必须紧扣卡尔的论证路线。”

===Original===
“Soane’s as bad as ever, then?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这么说，索恩还是一如既往地不成样子？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh,” Fox answered, “he’ll be all right for the stuff if you get that one idea into him.” A prolonged and acute fit of pain seized him. I fetched his man and left him to his rest.

===Chinese===
“哦，”福克斯答道，“只要你把这一个念头灌进他脑袋，出稿子他是不成问题的。”一阵长久而剧烈的疼痛攫住了他。我去唤来他的男仆，留他歇息。

===Original===
At the office of the *Hour* I was greeted by the handing to me of a proof of Callan’s manuscript. Evans, the man across the screen, was the immediate agent.

===Chinese===
到了《时辰报》社，迎接我的是一份卡伦手稿的校样，给塞到了我手里。隔着屏风的埃文斯，就是直接的经手人。

===Original===
“I suppose it’s got to go in, so I had it set up,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我想这稿子横竖总要登的，所以我叫人把它排了版。”他说。

===Original===
“Oh, of course it’s got to go in,” I answered. “It’s to go to Soane first, though.”

===Chinese===
“哦，当然要登，”我答道，“不过得先送索恩过目。”

===Original===
“Soane’s not here yet,” he answered. I noted the tone of subacid pleasure in his voice. Evans would have enjoyed a fiasco.

===Chinese===
“索恩还没到呢。”他答道。我听出他话音里那股酸溜溜的快意。埃文斯巴不得看一场砸锅。

===Original===
“Oh, well,” I answered, nonchalantly, “there’s plenty of time. You allow space on those lines. I’ll send round to hunt Soane up.”

===Chinese===
“哦，没事，”我淡然答道，“时间有的是。版面就照那个篇幅留好。我这就派人去把索恩寻来。”

===Original===
I felt called to be upon my mettle. I didn’t much care about the paper, but I had a definite antipathy to being done by Evans﻿—by a mad Welshman in a stubborn fit. I knew what was going to happen; knew that Evans would feign inconceivable stupidity, the sort of black stupidity that is at command of individuals of his primitive race. I was in for a day of petty worries. In the circumstances it was a thing to be thankful for; it dragged my mind away from larger issues. One has no time for brooding when one is driving a horse in a jibbing fit.

===Chinese===
我觉得非抖擞起全副精神不可。报纸本身我倒不甚在乎，可要是叫埃文斯做了手脚——叫一个犟劲儿发作的威尔士疯子做了手脚——我却有着确切无疑的反感。我知道等着的是什么；知道埃文斯会装出不可思议的愚蠢，那种黑沉沉的愚蠢，是他那个原始种族里的个人招之即来的。我这是注定要熬一天琐碎的烦恼了。处在这般境况里，这倒是件可感激的事；它把我的心神从更大的事情上拖开了。人赶着一匹犯起犟来死活不肯走的马时，是没有工夫愁思的。

===Original===
Evans was grimly conscious that I was moderately ignorant of technical details; he kept them well before my eyes all day long.

===Chinese===
埃文斯阴沉沉地吃准了我对技术细节相当无知；一整天，他偏让这些细节在我眼前晃来晃去。

===Original===
At odd moments I tried to read Callan’s article. It was impossible. It opened with a description of the squalor of the Greenlander’s life, and contained tawdry passages of local colour.

===Chinese===
趁空当儿我试着读卡伦的文章。办不到。它开头描写格陵兰人生活的腌臜，里头尽是俗艳的地方色彩段落。

===Original===
I knew what was coming. This was the view of the Greenlanders of pre-Merschian Greenland, elaborated, after the manner of Callan﻿—the Special Commissioner﻿—so as to bring out the glory and virtue of the work of regeneration. Then in a gush of superlatives the work itself would be described. I knew quite well what was coming, and was temperamentally unable to read more than the first ten lines.

===Chinese===
我知道下文是什么。这是默施时代以前格陵兰的格陵兰人景象，按照卡伦——那位特派专员（the Special Commissioner）——的路数加工铺陈，好衬出复兴大业的荣耀与德性。随后，那项工程本身自会在一阵最高级形容词的倾泻里得到描写。下文我一清二楚，而生性又注定我读不过开头十行。

===Original===
Everything was going wrong. The printers developed one of their sudden crazes for asking idiotic questions. Their messengers came to Evans, Evans sent them round the pitch-pine screen to me. “Mr. Jackson wants to know﻿—”

===Chinese===
事事都不顺。印刷工人忽然犯起他们那种爱问傻问题的痴劲。他们的信差来找埃文斯，埃文斯又把他们打发绕过脂松木屏风来找我。“杰克逊（Jackson）先生想知道——”

===Original===
The fourth of the messengers that I had despatched to Soane returned with the news that Soane would arrive at half-past nine. I sent out in search of the strongest coffee that the city afforded. Soane arrived. He had been ill, he said, very ill. He desired to be fortified with champagne. I produced the coffee.

===Chinese===
我派去寻索恩的信差，回来的是第四个，带回消息说，索恩九点半才能到。我派人出去，搜罗全城所能供应的最浓的咖啡。索恩到了。他说他病了，病得不轻。他要求用香槟给自己固一固元气。我端出了咖啡。

===Original===
Soane was the son of an Irish peer. He had magnificent features﻿—a little blurred nowadays﻿—and a remainder of the grand manner. His nose was a marvel of classic workmanship, but the floods of time had reddened and speckled it﻿—not offensively, but ironically; his hair was turning grey, his eyes were bloodshot, his heavy moustache rather ragged. He inspired one with the respect that one feels for a man who has lived and does not care a curse. He had a weird intermittent genius that made it worth Fox’s while to put up with his lapses and his brutal snubs.

===Chinese===
索恩是一位爱尔兰贵族之子。他五官堂皇——如今略见漫漶——还剩下几分大家气派。他的鼻子是古典工艺的一件奇迹，只是时间的洪水把它泡红了、洒上了斑斑点点——不惹人厌，倒带着反讽；他的头发渐白，眼睛布满血丝，浓重的髭须颇见散乱。他使人起一种敬意——人对一个活过了、什么都不放在心上的人所感到的那种。他有一种间歇发作的诡异天才，害得福克斯值得耐下性子，容忍他的失态和他那粗暴的抢白。

===Original===
I produced the coffee and pointed to the sofa of the night before.

===Chinese===
我端出咖啡，指了指前一晚的那张沙发。

===Original===
“Damn it,” he said, “I’m ill, I tell you; I want﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“见鬼，”他说，“我病着呢，我告诉你；我要的是……”

===Original===
“Exactly!” I cut in. “You want a rest, old fellow. Here’s Cal’s article. We want something special about it. If you don’t feel up to it I’ll send round to Jenkins.”

===Chinese===
“正是！”我插嘴道，“你需要歇一歇，老伙计。这是卡尔的文章。我们要为它配点特别的东西。你要是觉得撑不住，我就派人去叫詹金斯。”

===Original===
“Damn Jenkins,” he said; “I’m up to it.”

===Chinese===
“詹金斯见鬼去，”他说；“我撑得住。”

===Original===
“You understand,” I said, “you’re to write strictly on Callan’s lines. Don’t insert any information from extraneous sources. And make it as slashing as you like﻿—on those lines.”

===Chinese===
“你要明白，”我说，“你务须严格照卡伦的路子写。别往里塞任何外来渠道的消息。火力随你多猛——但只许在那条路子上。”

===Original===
He grunted in acquiescence. I left him lying on the sofa, drinking the coffee. I had tenderly arranged the lights for him as Fox had arranged them the night before. As I went out to get my dinner I was comfortably aware of him, holding the slips close to his muddled eyes and philosophically damning the nature of things.

===Chinese===
他哼了一声，算是应承。我留他躺在沙发上喝咖啡。我体贴地替他布好了灯光，就像前一晚福克斯布的那样。我出门吃晚饭去时，心里还安然记着他的样子：把长条校样凑到那双迷蒙的眼睛跟前，像个哲学家似的咒骂着事物的本性。

===Original===
When I returned, Soane, from his sofa, said something that I did not catch﻿—something about Callan and his article.

===Chinese===
我回来时，索恩从沙发上说了句什么，我没听清——关于卡伦和他的文章的什么。

===Original===
“Oh, for God’s sake,” I answered, “don’t worry me. Have some more coffee and stick to Cal’s line of argument. That’s what Fox said. I’m not responsible.”

===Chinese===
“哦，看在上帝份上，”我答道，“别来烦我。再喝点咖啡，紧扣卡尔的论证路线。这是福克斯说的。出了岔子概不负责。”

===Original===
“Deuced queer,” Soane muttered. He began to scribble with a pencil. From the tone of his voice I knew that he had reached the precise stage at which something brilliant﻿—the real thing of its kind﻿—might be expected.

===Chinese===
“邪门得很。”索恩喃喃道。他开始用铅笔涂写起来。听那话音我就知道，他已恰好到了那个火候——在这个火候上，可以指望出点神来之笔——那一类里的真货。

===Original===
Very late Soane finished his leader. He looked up as he wrote the last word.

===Chinese===
很晚很晚，索恩才写完他的社论。写下最后一个字时，他抬起头来。

===Original===
“I’ve got it written,” he said. “But﻿ ﻿… I say, what the deuce is up? It’s like being a tall clock with the mainspring breaking, this.”

===Chinese===
“写好了。”他说。“可是……我说，这到底是怎么回事？这情形，就像自己成了一座主发条断掉的落地大钟。”

===Original===
I rang the bell for someone to take the copy down.

===Chinese===
我按铃叫人来把稿子送下楼去。

===Original===
“Your metaphor’s too much for me, Soane,” I said.

===Chinese===
“你这比喻我可消受不起，索恩。”我说。

===Original===
“It’s appropriate all the way along,” he maintained, “if you call me a mainspring. I’ve been wound up and wound up to write old de Mersch and his Greenland up﻿—and it’s been a tight wind, these days, I tell you. Then all of a sudden﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“这比喻从头到尾都贴切，”他坚持道，“只要你肯把我算作主发条。我让人上了弦又上弦，好去把老德·默施和他的格陵兰捧上天——告诉你，这些天，这弦上得可紧呐。然后突然之间……”

===Original===
A boy appeared and carried off the copy.

===Chinese===
一个小听差进来，把稿子拿走了。

===Original===
“All of a sudden,” Soane resumed, “something gives﻿—I suppose something’s given﻿—and there’s a whirr-rr-rr and the hands fly backwards and old de Mersch and Greenland bump to the bottom, like the weights.”

===Chinese===
“突然之间，”索恩接着说，“有什么东西松了劲——我想是真有什么断了——只听呜——rr——rr一声，指针倒飞，老德·默施和格陵兰便哐当一声砸到底，跟钟锤一个样。”

===Original===
The boom of the great presses was rattling the window frames. Soane got up and walked toward one of the cupboards.

===Chinese===
大印报机的轰鸣震得窗框格格作响。索恩起身，朝一口橱柜走去。

===Original===
“Dry work,” he said; “but the simile’s just, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“口干的营生。”他说；“不过这比喻是公道的，不是吗？”

===Original===
I gave one swift step toward the bell-button beside the desk. The proof of Callan’s article, from which Soane had been writing, lay a crumpled white streamer on the brown wood of Fox’s desk. I made toward it. As I stretched out my hand the solution slipped into my mind, coming with no more noise than that of a bullet; impinging with all the shock and remaining with all the pain. I had remembered the morning, over there in Paris, when she had told me that she had invited one of de Mersch’s lieutenants to betray him by not concealing from Callan the real horrors of the *Système Groënlandais*﻿—flogged, butchered, miserable natives, the famines, the vices, diseases, and the crimes. There came suddenly before my eyes the tall narrow room in my aunt’s house, the opening of the door and her entry, followed by that of the woebegone governor of a province﻿—the man who was to show Callan things﻿—with his grating “*C’est entendu﻿ ﻿…*”

===Chinese===
我朝书桌旁边的电铃按钮疾跨一步。索恩据以作文的卡伦文章校样，在福克斯书桌的棕色木面上摊成一条揉皱的白色长带。我朝它走去。就在我伸出手去的当口，谜底滑进了我的脑子，来得不比一颗子弹更响；带着全部冲击撞了进来，又带着全部疼痛留了下来。我想起在巴黎的那个早晨，她告诉我，她已邀了德·默施的一名部属去背叛他——办法是不向卡伦隐瞒格陵兰体系（Système Groënlandais）的真实恐怖——被鞭笞、被屠戮的悲惨土人，还有饥荒、恶行、疾病与罪愆。我眼前蓦地浮起老姑母宅中那间高而窄的房间，门开了，她走进来，身后跟着愁眉苦脸的某省总督——就是要领卡伦去看些东西的那个人——带着他那声刺耳的“C’est entendu……”

===Original===
I remembered the scene distinctly; her words; her looks; my utter unbelief. I remembered, too, that it had not saved me from a momentary sense of revolt against that inflexible intention of a treachery which was to be another step toward the inheritance of the earth. I had rejected the very idea, and here it had come; it was confronting me with all its meaning and consequences. Callan *had* been shown things he had not been meant to see, and had written the truth as he had seen it. His article was a small thing in itself, but he had been sent out there with tremendous flourishes of de Mersch’s trumpets. He was *the* man who could be believed. De Mersch’s supporters had practically said: “If he condemns us we are indeed damned.” And now that the condemnation had come, it meant ruin, as it seemed to me, for everybody I had known, worked for, seen, or heard of, during the last year of my life. It was ruin for Fox, for Churchill, for the ministers, and for the men who talk in railway carriages, for shopkeepers and for the government; it was a menace to the institutions which hold us to the past, that are our guarantees for the future. The safety of everything one respected and believed in was involved in the disclosure of an atrocious fraud, and the disclosure was in my hands. For that night I had the power of the press in my keeping. People were waiting for this pronouncement. De Mersch’s last card was his philanthropy; his model state and his happy natives.

===Chinese===
那一幕我记得清清楚楚；她的话；她的神色；我全然的不信。我也记得，那并未能使我免于一阵短暂的憎恶——憎恶那桩绝无通融的背叛图谋，那图谋不过是通向承受大地的又一级台阶。我当初连这念头本身都摒弃了，可如今它来了；它带着全部的意义和后果，当面立在我眼前。卡伦确实叫人给看了些不该给他看的东西，也照他眼见的实情写了出来。他这篇文章本身是件小事，可当初送他出去时，德·默施的号角吹得何等嘹亮。他正是那一位可以取信于人的人。德·默施的支持者们实际上早已说了：“他若谴责我们，我们就真完了。”如今谴责临头，在我看来，这便是毁败——我过去一年里认识的、效力的、见过的、听说过的一切人等的毁败。毁的是福克斯，是丘吉尔，是各位大臣，是火车车厢里高谈阔论的那班人，是店主们，是政府；它威胁着那些把我们系于往昔的制度——那些我们对未来的担保。人所敬重、所信奉的一切的安全，都系于一桩残暴骗局的败露，而这败露就攥在我手里。这一夜，报界的权柄由我保管。人们正等着这篇判词。德·默施的最后一张牌，是他的慈善事业；他的模范国家，和他那些幸福的土人。

===Original===
The drone of the presses made the floor under my feet quiver, and the whole building vibrated as if the earth itself had trembled. I was alone with my knowledge. Did she know; had she put the power in my hand? But I was alone, and I was free.

===Chinese===
印报机的嗡鸣震得我脚下的地板发颤，整座楼都在震，仿佛大地本身颤动了。我独自一人，守着我的秘密。她知道吗；是她把这权柄放进我手里的吗？可我独自一人，而我是自由的。

===Original===
I took up the proof and began to read, slanting the page to the fall of the light. It was a phrenetic indictment, but under the paltry rhetoric of the man there was genuine indignation and pain. There were revolting details of cruelty to the miserable, helpless, and defenceless; there were greed, and self-seeking, stripped naked; but more revolting to see without a mask was that falsehood which had been hiding under the words that for ages had spurred men to noble deeds, to self-sacrifice, to heroism. What was appalling was the sudden perception that all the traditional ideals of honour, glory, conscience, had been committed to the upholding of a gigantic and atrocious fraud. The falsehood had spread stealthily, had eaten into the very heart of creeds and convictions that we lean upon on our passage between the past and the future. The old order of things had to live or perish with a lie. I saw all this with the intensity and clearness of a revelation; I saw it as though I had been asleep through a year of work and dreams, and had awakened to the truth. I saw it all; I saw her intention. What was I to do?

===Chinese===
我拿起校样读起来，把纸页斜向灯光投落的方向。那是一篇热狂的控诉状，可在那人寒碜的修辞底下，有真正的义愤与痛楚。里面有一桩桩令人发指的残虐，施于那些悲惨的、无助的、无告的人；有剥得赤裸的贪婪与营私；可更令人作呕的，是看见那谎言卸下了面具——它一直藏在那一套词句底下，世代以来，正是那些词句鞭策人们去建崇高之业、去自我牺牲、去成就英雄。骇人的是骤然看破：荣誉、光荣、良心，一切传统的理想，早已被押上去支撑一桩滔天而残暴的骗局。谎言悄悄扩散，蛀空了那些信条与信念的心脏——我们在过去与未来之间的跋涉中所倚仗的，正是它们。旧秩序非得同一个谎言一起活，或者一起死。这一切我看得分明，带着天启般的强烈与清晰；我仿佛在劳作与幻梦里沉睡了一年，此刻醒来了，直面真相。我全看见了；我看穿了她的意图。我该怎么办？

===Original===
Without my marking its approach emotion was upon me. The fingers that held up the extended slips tattooed one on another through its negligible thickness.

===Chinese===
不知不觉间，情绪已袭上了我。捏着那摊开长条校样的手指，隔着那薄薄一层微不足道的纸，一下一下彼此叩击起来。

===Original===
“Pretty thick that,” Soane said. He was looking back at me from the cupboard he had opened. “I’ve rubbed it in, too﻿ ﻿… there’ll be hats on the green tomorrow.” He had his head inside the cupboard, and his voice came to me hollowly. He extracted a large bottle with a gilt-foiled neck.

===Chinese===
“够狠的吧，这个。”索恩说。他从打开的橱柜那儿回头望着我。“我也往狠里捶了……明天就要闹翻天了。”他的脑袋探在橱柜里，声音传到我耳中瓮声瓮气。他掏出一支大瓶，瓶颈裹着金箔。

===Original===
“Won’t it upset the apple cart tomorrow,” he said, very loudly; “won’t it?”

===Chinese===
“明天它还不把苹果车整个掀翻，”他放开大嗓门说；“还不掀吗？”

===Original===
His voice acted on me as the slight shake upon a phial full of waiting chemicals; crystallised them suddenly with a little click. Everything suddenly grew very clear to me. I suddenly understood that all the tortuous intrigue hinged upon what I did in the next few minutes. It rested with me now to stretch out my hand to that button in the wall or to let the whole world﻿—“the﻿ ﻿… the probity﻿ ﻿… that sort of thing,” she had said﻿—fall to pieces. The drone of the presses continued to make itself felt like the quiver of a suppressed emotion. I might stop them or I might not. It rested with me.

===Chinese===
他的话音作用于我，就像轻轻一摇满满一瓶静候着的化学药剂；只听咔嗒一响，霎时结了晶。一切陡然对我变得非常清楚。我突然明白，全部曲曲折折的密谋，都悬在我接下来几分钟的作为上。如今全看我：是伸手去按墙上那个按钮，还是听任整个世界——“那……那正派……那一类东西，”她说过——塌成碎片。印报机的嗡鸣还在令人察觉，像一阵被压抑的情绪的震颤。我可以叫它们停，也可以不叫。全在我。

===Original===
Everybody was in my hands; they were quite small. If I let the thing go on, they would be done for utterly, and the new era would begin.

===Chinese===
人人都攥在我手心里；他们实在小得很。我若放任这事照旧进行，他们就得彻底完蛋，而新纪元将开启。

===Original===
Soane had got hold of a couple of long-stalked glasses. They clinked together whilst he searched the cupboard for something.

===Chinese===
索恩已经摸出两只高脚杯。他在橱柜里翻找什么的时候，两只杯子叮当相碰。

===Original===
“Eh, what?” he said. “It *is* pretty *strong*, isn’t it? Ought to shake out some of the supporters, eh? Bill comes on tomorrow﻿ ﻿… do for that, I should think.” He wanted a corkscrew very badly.

===Chinese===
“呃，什么？”他说。“这可真够劲，不是吗？准能抖落掉一批支持者，嗯？法案明天就要上会……我看，这一下就够它受的。”他急需一把开塞钻，急得要命。

===Original===
But that was precisely it﻿—it would “shake out some of the supporters,” and give Gurnard his patent excuse. Churchill, I knew, would stick to his line, the saner policy. But so many of the men who had stuck to Churchill would fall away now, and Gurnard, of course, would lead them to his own triumph.

===Chinese===
可偏偏就是这个——它正要“抖落掉一批支持者”，正好给格纳德送上一个现成的借口。我知道，丘吉尔会守住他的路线，那更稳妥的政策。可是那么多一直拥附丘吉尔的人，如今要溃散了，而格纳德，当然，会率领他们去成就他自己的凯旋。

===Original===
It was a criminal verdict. Callan had gone out as a commissioner﻿—with a good deal of drum-beating. And this was his report, this shriek. If it sounded across the housetops﻿—if I let it﻿—goodbye to the saner policy and to Churchill. It did not make any difference that Churchill’s *was* the saner policy, because there was no one in the nation sane enough to see it. They wanted purity in high places, and here was a definite, criminal indictment against de Mersch. And de Mersch would﻿—in a manner of speaking, have to be lynched, policy or no policy.

===Chinese===
这是一纸刑事判词。卡伦当初是作为特派员出去的——一路锣鼓喧天。这便是他的报告，这一声尖啸。它若响彻各家的屋顶——若我放它响——那就永别了，更稳妥的政策，永别了，丘吉尔。丘吉尔的政策纵然真的更稳妥，也无济于事，因为全国再没有一个头脑够清醒的人看得见这一点。他们要的是高位的清白，而这里正好有一纸明白确凿的刑事控状，控的是德·默施。于是德·默施就——不妨这么说吧——非被私刑处死不可，还讲什么政策不政策。

===Original===
She wanted this, and in all the earth she was the only desirable thing. If I thwarted her﻿—she would﻿ ﻿… what would she do now? I looked at Soane.

===Chinese===
她要的就是这个，而普天之下，她是唯一可欲的东西。我若阻挠她——她会……她如今会怎么样呢？我望着索恩。

===Original===
“What would happen if I stopped the presses?” I asked. Soane was twisting his corkscrew in the wire of the champagne bottle.

===Chinese===
“要是我叫印报机停下来，会怎么样？”我问。索恩正把开塞钻往香槟瓶的铁丝箍里拧。

===Original===
It was fatal; I could see nothing on earth but her. What else was there in the world. Wine? The light of the sun? The wind on the heath? Honour! My God, what was honour to me if I could see nothing but her on earth? Would honour or wine or sun or wind ever give me what she could give? Let them go.

===Chinese===
这是致命的；这世上除她之外，我什么也看不见。世上还有什么别的。酒？阳光？荒野上的风？荣誉！我的上帝，若在这世上我除了她什么也看不见，荣誉于我算得了什么？荣誉、酒、阳光或风，哪一样给得了我她所能给的？都随它们去吧。

===Original===
“What would happen if what?” Soane grumbled, “*D﻿—﻿—﻿n* this wire.”

===Chinese===
“要是怎样就会怎么样？”索恩咕哝道，“这该——死的铁丝。”

===Original===
“Oh, I was thinking about something,” I answered. The wire gave with a little snap and he began to ease the cork. Was I to let the light pass me by for the sake of﻿ ﻿… of Fox, for instance, who trusted me? Well, let Fox go. And Churchill and what Churchill stood for; the probity; the greatness and the spirit of the past from which had sprung my conscience and the consciences of the sleeping millions around me﻿—the woman at the poultry show with her farmers and shopkeepers. Let them go too.

===Chinese===
“哦，我在想件事。”我答道。铁丝啪的一声断了，他开始起那瓶塞。难道我要为了……为了譬如说信得过我的福克斯，就放那道光明从我身旁过去？好吧，让福克斯去吧。还有丘吉尔，还有丘吉尔所代表的一切；那正派；那往昔的伟大与精神——我的良心，连同我周围沉睡的千百万人的良心，都是从那里生出来的——还有家禽展上那位太太，带着她的农场主和店主。也随他们去吧。

===Original===
Soane put into my hand one of his charged glasses. He seemed to rise out of the infinite, a forgotten shape. I sat down at the desk opposite him.

===Chinese===
索恩把一只斟满了的杯子塞进我手里。他仿佛从无穷无尽里升上来，一个被忘掉的形影。我在他对面的书桌前坐下。

===Original===
“Deuced good idea,” he said, suddenly, “to stop the confounded presses and spoof old Fox. He’s up to some devilry. And, by Jove, I’d like to get my knife in him; Jove, I would. And then chuck up everything and leave for the Sandwich Islands. I’m sick of this life, this dog’s life.﻿ ﻿… One might have made a pile though, if one’d known this smash was coming. But one can’t get at the innards of things.﻿—No such luck﻿—no such luck, eh?” I looked at him stupidly; took in his bloodshot eyes and his ruffled grizzling hair. I wondered who he was. “*Il s’agissait de*﻿ ﻿… ?” I seemed to be back in Paris, I couldn’t think of what I had been thinking of. I drank his glass of wine and he filled me another. I drank that too.

===Chinese===
“好得邪门的主意，”他忽然说，“叫这些该死的印报机停下来，耍老福克斯一把。他正打着什么鬼算盘。而且，天在上，我真想往他身上捅一刀；天在上，真想。然后就抛开一切，动身去桑威奇群岛（Sandwich Islands）。这种日子我过腻了，这种狗过的日子。……不过，要是早知道这场崩盘要来，本可以发上一笔的。可是人钻不进事情的脏腑里去。——没那份运气——没那份运气，嗯？”我傻乎乎地望着他；把他那双布满血丝的眼睛、那头揉乱的灰白头发看在眼里。我纳闷他是谁。“Il s’agissait de……？（说的是什么来着……？）”我仿佛又回到了巴黎，想不起自己方才在想些什么。我喝了他那杯酒，他又给我斟上一杯。那杯我也喝了。

===Original===
Ah yes﻿—even then the thing wasn’t settled, even now that I had recognized that Fox and the others were of no account﻿ ﻿… What remained was to prove to her that I wasn’t a mere chattel, a piece in the game. I was at the very heart of the thing. After all, it was chance that had put me there, the blind chance of all the little things that lead in the inevitable, the future. If, now, I thwarted her, she would﻿ ﻿… what would she do? She would have to begin all over again. She wouldn’t want to be revenged; she wasn’t revengeful. But how if she would never look upon me again?

===Chinese===
啊，是啊——即便在那时，这事也还没有定局，哪怕此刻我已认清，福克斯和其余的人都无足轻重……剩下要做的，是向她证明，我不是一件区区动产，不是棋局里的一枚子儿。我正处在这桩事情的最中心。毕竟，是偶然把我放到了那里——是那一切细小事物的盲目偶然，它们一件件引向那不可避免的、那未来。倘若此刻我阻挠她，她会……她会怎么样呢？她就只好从头再来一遍。她不会想报复；她不是记仇的人。可她若从此再也不看我一眼，那又如何？

===Original===
The thing had reduced itself to a mere matter of policy. Or was it passion?

===Chinese===
这事情已归结为一个纯粹的政策问题。要不然，是激情？

===Original===
A clatter of the wheels of heavy carts and of the hoofs of heavy horses on granite struck like hammer blows on my ears, coming from the well of the courtyard below. Soane had finished his bottle and was walking to the cupboard. He paused at the window and stood looking down.

===Chinese===
下面天井里传来重载马车的辘辘车轮、重挽马的铁蹄踏在花岗石上的声响，像一记记锤击敲在我耳上。索恩已喝完了他那瓶酒，正朝橱柜走去。他在窗前停住，站在那里朝下望。

===Original===
“Strong beggars, those porters,” he said; “I couldn’t carry that weight of paper﻿—not with my rot on it, let alone Callan’s. You’d think it would break down the carts.”

===Chinese===
“好壮的家伙，那些搬运工，”他说；“那么重的报纸我可搬不动——光压着我那篇烂文章就够呛，更别说再加上卡伦的了。你会以为非把马车压垮不可。”

===Original===
I understood that they were loading the carts for the newspaper mails. There was still time to stop them. I got up and went toward the window, very swiftly. I was going to call to them to stop loading. I threw the casement open.

===Chinese===
我明白过来，他们是在往车上装报纸，好发邮班。还来得及叫他们停住。我霍地起身，疾步走向窗前。我正要喊他们别装了。我一把推开窗扉。

---

===Original===
Of course, I did not stop them. The solution flashed on me with the breath of the raw air. It was ridiculously simple. If I thwarted her, well, she would respect me. But her business in life was the inheritance of the earth, and, however much she might respect me﻿—or by so much the more﻿—she would recognise that I was a force to deflect her from the right line﻿—“a disease for me,” she had said.

===Chinese===
当然，我没有叫他们停下。谜底随着一口凛冽空气的呼吸，闪进了我的心里。它简单得可笑。我若阻挠她，嗯，她会敬重我。可她毕生的事业是承受大地，而无论她多么敬重我——或者恰恰因此更加敬重——她都会认定，我是一股使她偏离正轨的力量——“一场病——对于我，”她曾这样说。

===Original===
“What I have to do,” I said, “is to show her that﻿ ﻿… that I had her in my hands and that I cooperated loyally.”

===Chinese===
“我要做的，”我说，“是向她表明……表明我曾把她握在手里，而我合作得很忠诚。”

===Original===
The thing was so simple that I triumphed; triumphed with the full glow of wine, triumphed looking down into that murky courtyard where the lanterns danced about in the rays of a great arc lamp. The gilt letters scattered all over the windows blazed forth the names of Fox’s innumerable ventures. Well, he﻿ ﻿… he had been a power, but I triumphed. I had cooperated loyally with the powers of the future, though I wanted no share in the inheritance of the earth. Only, I was going to push into the future. One of the great carts got into motion amidst a shower of sounds that whirled upward round and round the well. The black hood swayed like the shoulders of an elephant as it passed beneath my feet under the arch. It disappeared﻿—it was cooperating too; in a few hours people at the other end of the country﻿—of the world﻿—would be raising their hands. Oh, yes, it was cooperating loyally.

===Chinese===
这事简单得叫我得意洋洋；带着满身酒意的红光得意着，俯瞰进那座昏暗的天井得意着——灯笼在一盏大弧光灯的光柱里舞来舞去。满窗散布的鎏金字母，熊熊照出福克斯那数不清的营生的名号。是啊，他……他曾是一股势力，可我得意了。我已同未来的诸势力合作得很忠诚，虽然我并不想在承受大地中占一份。只不过，我还要朝未来里头挤进去。一辆大货车动了，在一阵声音的骤雨里，那声音绕着天井团团打转，往上飞旋。黑色的车篷摇摇晃晃，像大象的肩膀，从我的脚下、从拱门底下经过。它不见了——它也在合作；再过几个钟头，这个国家另一头的人——世界另一头的人——就要举起双手了。哦，是的，它合作得很忠诚。

===Original===
I closed the window. Soane was holding a champagne bottle in one hand. In the other he had a paper knife of Fox’s﻿—a metal thing, a Japanese dagger or a Deccan knife. He sliced the neck off the bottle.

===Chinese===
我关上窗。索恩一手握着一瓶香槟。另一只手里是福克斯的一把裁纸刀——一件金属家伙，一柄日本短刀，或者一把德干（Deccan）刀。他把瓶颈削了下来。

===Original===
“Thought you were going to throw yourself out,” he said; “I wouldn’t stop you. *I’m* sick of it﻿ ﻿… sick.”

===Chinese===
“还当你要纵身跳出去呢，”他说；“我可不拦你。我是烦透了……烦透了。”

===Original===
“Look at this﻿ ﻿… tonight﻿ ﻿… this infernal trick of Fox’s.﻿ ﻿… And I helped too.﻿ ﻿… Why?﻿ ﻿… I must eat.” He paused “… and drink,” he added. “But there is starvation for no end of fools in this little move. A few will be losing their good names too.﻿ ﻿… I don’t care, I’m off.﻿ ﻿… By the by: What is he doing it for? Money? Funk?﻿—You ought to know. You must be in it too. It’s not hunger with you. Wonderful what people will do to keep their pet vice going.﻿ ﻿… Eh?” He swayed a little. “You don’t drink﻿—what’s your pet vice?”

===Chinese===
“瞧瞧这个……就在今晚……福克斯这手要命的把戏。……我还是帮了忙的。……为什么？……我总得吃饭。”他顿了顿。“……还得喝酒。”他补了一句。“可这一小步挪动里，要数不清的蠢人挨饿。有几个还要赔上好名声。……我不在乎，我走了。……对了：他图的是什么？钱？心里发怵？——你该知道。你一定也入了伙。你又不是为了饿肚子。人为了让自己那门心爱的恶习不断顿，什么都干得出来，真是奇观。……嗯？”他晃了一晃。“你不喝酒——那你心爱的恶习是什么？”

===Original===
He looked at me very defiantly, clutching the neck of the empty bottle. His drunken and overbearing glare seemed to force upon me a complicity in his squalid bargain with life, rewarded by a squalid freedom. He was pitiful and odious to my eyes; and somehow in a moment he appeared menacing.

===Chinese===
他一副挑衅到极处的神气望着我，一手攥着空瓶的瓶颈。他那醉醺醺、盛气凌人的逼视，仿佛硬要拉我入伙他那桩同生活做的卑污交易——报酬是一份同样卑污的自由。在我眼里，他又可怜又可憎；不知怎么，转瞬之间他又显得咄咄逼人。

===Original===
“You can’t frighten me,” I said, in response to the strange fear he had inspired. “No one can frighten me now.” A sense of my inaccessibility was the first taste of an achieved triumph. I had done with fear. The poor devil before me appeared infinitely remote. He was lost; but he was only one of the lost; one of those that I could see already overwhelmed by the rush from the floodgates opened at my touch. He would be destroyed in good company; swept out of my sight together with the past they had known and with the future they had waited for. But he was odious. “I am done with you,” I said.

===Chinese===
“你吓不着我，”我说，回应着他方才激起的那阵莫名的恐惧。“如今谁也吓不着我了。”一种高不可及之感，是到手的胜利的头一口滋味。我同恐惧已经两讫。眼前这可怜虫显得无限遥远。他是完了；可他不过是完了的人里的一个；是我已经看得见的、被我这只手一触即开的闸门里冲出的激流吞没的众人里的一个。他会被毁灭，且有一大群体面的难友作陪；同他们所熟识的过去、同他们所等待的未来一道，从我眼前一扫而光。可他可憎。“我同你也两讫了。”我说。

===Original===
“Eh; what?﻿ ﻿… Who wants to frighten?﻿ ﻿… I wanted to know what’s your pet vice.﻿ ﻿… Won’t tell? You might safely﻿—I’m off.﻿ ﻿… No.﻿ ﻿… Want to tell me mine?﻿ ﻿… No time.﻿ ﻿… I’m off.﻿ ﻿… Ask the policeman﻿ ﻿… crossing sweeper will do.﻿ ﻿… I’m going.”

===Chinese===
“呃；什么？……谁要吓人？……我不过想知道你心爱的恶习是什么。……不肯说？说了也保险——我这就走。……不说。……想先听听我的？……没工夫了。……我走了。……去问警察吧……问扫街口的也凑合。……我这就走。”

===Original===
“You will have to,” I said.

===Chinese===
“你非走不可。”我说。

===Original===
“What.﻿ ﻿… Dismiss me?﻿ ﻿… Throw the indispensable Soane overboard like a squeezed lemon?﻿ ﻿… Would you?﻿ ﻿… What would Fox say?﻿ ﻿… Eh? But you can’t, my boy﻿—not you. Tell you﻿ ﻿… tell you﻿ ﻿… can’t.﻿ ﻿… Beforehand with you﻿ ﻿… sick of it.﻿ ﻿… I’m off﻿ ﻿… to the Islands﻿—the Islands of the Blest.﻿ ﻿… I’m going to be an﻿ ﻿… no, not an angel like Fox﻿ ﻿… an﻿ ﻿… oh, a beachcomber. Lie on white sand, in the sun﻿ ﻿… blue sky and palm-trees﻿—eh?﻿ ﻿… S.S. Waikato. I’m off.﻿ ﻿… Come too﻿ ﻿… lark﻿ ﻿… dismiss yourself out of all this. Warm sand, warm, mind you﻿ ﻿… you won’t?” He had an injured expression. “Well, I’m off. See me into the cab, old chap, you’re a decent fellow after all﻿ ﻿… not one of these beggars who would sell their best friend﻿ ﻿… for a little money﻿ ﻿… or some woman. Will see the last of me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“什么。……把我开革？……把不可或缺的索恩，像挤干了的柠檬似的扔下船去？……你舍得？……福克斯会怎么说？……嗯？可你办不到，小子——偏偏是你，就办不到。告诉你……告诉你……办不到。……我抢在你前头了……烦透了它。……我走了……去群岛——去极乐群岛（the Islands of the Blest）。……我要去当一名……不，不当福克斯那样的天使……当一名……哦，海滩拾荒客（beachcomber）。躺在白沙滩上，晒着太阳……蓝天加棕榈树——嗯？……怀卡托号（S.S. Waikato）。我走了。……你也来吧……乐子一场……把你自己也从这一整套里开革出去。暖和的沙子，暖和的，你记住……你不肯？”他一脸受了委屈的表情。“好吧，我走了。送我上马车，老伙计，你到底是条好汉……不像这班家伙，肯把自己顶好的朋友卖掉……为几个小钱……或是为了哪个女人。眼看就要见我最后一面了。……”

===Original===
I didn’t believe he would reach the South Seas, but I went downstairs and watched him march up the street with a slight stagger under the pallid dawn. I suppose it was the lingering chill of the night that made me shiver. I felt unbounded confidence in the future, there was nothing now between her and me. The echo of my footsteps on the flagstones accompanied me, filling the empty earth with the sound of my progress.

===Chinese===
我不信他真到得了南太平洋，可我还是下了楼，望着他在灰白的黎明里带着几分踉跄沿街走去。我想，是夜里残存的寒气害我打了个寒噤。我对未来感到无边的信心；如今，她和我之间再无隔碍。我的脚步落在石板路上的回声一路陪着我，用我前行的声响，填满这空空荡荡的大地。

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
I walked along, got to my club and upstairs into my room peaceably. A feeling of entire tranquillity had come over me. I rested after a strife which had issued in a victory whose meaning was too great to comprehend and enjoy at once. I only knew that it was great because there seemed nothing more left to do. Everything reposed within me﻿—even conscience, even memory, reposed as in death. I had risen above them, and my thoughts moved serenely as in a new light, as men move in sunshine above the graves of the forgotten dead. I felt like a man at the beginning of a long holiday﻿—an indefinite space of idleness with some great felicity﻿—a felicity too great for words, too great for joy﻿—at the end. Everything was delicious and vague; there were no shapes, no persons. Names flitted through my mind﻿—Fox, Churchill, my aunt; but they were living people seen from above, flitting in the dusk, without individuality; things that moved below me in a valley from which I had emerged. I must have been dreaming of them.
===Chinese===
我一路走着，回到我的俱乐部，安安静静地上楼进了自己的房间。一种全然的平静之感笼罩了我。一场厮杀之后我歇息下来，那场厮杀以胜利收场，而胜利的意义太大，一时既领会不了，也消受不起。我只知道它伟大，因为似乎再没有什么剩下的事要做了。一切都安歇在我体内——连良心，连记忆，都像在死亡里一样安歇着。我已升到它们之上，我的思想安详地移动着，仿佛行在一片新的光里，像人在阳光里走过被遗忘的死者的坟茔之上。我觉得自己像一个人初逢一个漫长假日的开端——一段没有尽头的闲散，尽头处有某种巨大的至福——那至福大到言语装不下，欢乐也装不下。一切都美妙而朦胧；没有形体，也没有人。一个个名字掠过我的心头——福克斯、丘吉尔、老姑母；但他们都是些从高处俯瞰下去的活人，在暮色里掠动，没有个性；是些在我下方、在我已经走出的那道山谷里移动的东西。想必我方才一直在梦见他们。

===Original===
I know I dreamed of her. She alone was distinct among these shapes. She appeared dazzling; resplendent with a splendid calmness, and I braced myself to the shock of love, the love I had known, that all men had known; but greater, transcendental, almost terrible, a fit reward for the sacrifice of a whole past. Suddenly she spoke. I heard a sound like the rustling of a wind through trees, and I felt the shock of an unknown emotion made up of fear and of enthusiasm, as though she had been not a woman but only a voice crying strange, unknown words in inspiring tones, promising and cruel, without any passion of love or hate. I listened. It was like the wind in the trees of a little wood. No hate﻿ ﻿… no love. No love. There was a crash as of a falling temple. I was borne to the earth, overwhelmed, crushed by an immensity of ruin and of sorrow. I opened my eyes and saw the sun shining through the window-blinds.
===Chinese===
我知道我梦见了她。唯有她在这些形体中间是清晰的。她显得光彩灼人，焕发着一种辉煌的平静，我振作起来，迎受爱的冲击——那我所识得的爱，人人所识得的爱；只是更伟大，超凡的，近乎可怖的，配得上拿整个过去作牺牲来抵偿。忽然她开口了。我听见一种像风穿过树间的沙沙声，随即感到一种未知的情绪的震撼，由恐惧和热忱合成，仿佛她不是一个女人，只是一个声音，用鼓动人心的声调喊出奇异而未知的言语，又是许诺又是残酷，没有一丝爱或恨的激情。我听着。像一片小树林里的风声。没有恨……没有爱。没有爱。接着轰然一响，像一座神庙倒塌。我被压倒在地，被浩大的废墟与哀伤压得粉碎。我睁开眼睛，看见阳光正从窗板间照进来。

===Original===
I seem to remember I was surprised at it. I don’t know why. Perhaps the lingering effect of the ruin in the dream, which had involved sunshine itself. I liked it though, and lay for a time enjoying the﻿—what shall I say?﻿—usualness of it. The sunshine of yesterday﻿—of tomorrow. It occurred to me that the morning must be far advanced, and I got up briskly, as a man rises to his work. But as soon as I got on my legs I felt as if I had already overworked myself. In reality there was nothing to do. All my muscles twitched with fatigue. I had experienced the same sensations once after an hour’s desperate swimming to save myself from being carried out to sea by the tide.
===Chinese===
我仿佛记得，我当时对此感到意外。我不知道为什么。也许是梦里那场废墟的余波，连阳光本身也牵连了进去。不过我喜欢它，躺着享受了一会儿那——该怎么说呢？——那寻常的滋味。昨天——也是明天——的太阳。我想到早晨想必已经不早，便利落地起了身，像一个人起来去上工。可双腿一站定，我就觉得自己仿佛已经操劳过度了。实际上无事可做。我全身的肌肉都困倦得抽动。这种滋味我从前领略过一回——那次为了不让潮水把自己卷进海里，绝望地游了一个钟头之后。

===Original===
No. There was nothing to do. I descended the staircase, and an utter sense of aimlessness drove me out through the big doors, which swung behind me without noise. I turned toward the river, and on the broad embankment the sunshine enveloped me, friendly, familiar, and warm like the care of an old friend. A black dumb barge drifted, clumsy and empty, and the solitary man in it wrestled with the heavy sweep, straining his arms, throwing his face up to the sky at every effort. He knew what he was doing, though it was the river that did his work for him.
===Chinese===
不。无事可做。我走下楼梯，一种全然无所事事的感觉把我推出大门，大门在我身后无声地荡拢。我转向河边，在宽阔的堤岸上，阳光裹住了我，友好、熟稔而温暖，像老友的关切。一条黑黢黢的哑巴驳船漂着，笨拙而空荡，船上那个孤零零的人搏弄着沉重的大橹，绷紧手臂，每使一把力就把脸仰向天空。他知道自己是在干什么，虽说替他干活的其实是这条河。

===Original===
His exertions impressed me with the idea that I too had something to do. Certainly I had. One always has. Somehow I could not remember. It was intolerable, and even alarming, this blank, this emptiness of the many hours before night came again, till suddenly, it dawned upon me I had to make some extracts in the British Museum for our *Cromwell*. Our Cromwell. There was no Cromwell; he had lived, had worked for the future﻿—and now he had ceased to exist. His future﻿—our past, had come to an end. The barge with the man still straining at the oar had gone out of sight under the arch of the bridge, as through a gate into another world. A bizarre sense of solitude stole upon me, and I turned my back upon the river as empty as my day. Hansoms, broughams, streamed with a continuous muffled roll of wheels and a beat of hoofs. A big dray put in a note of thunder and a clank of chains. I found myself curiously unable to understand what possible purpose remained to keep them in motion. The past that had made them had come to an end, and their future had been devoured by a new conception. And what of Churchill? He, too, had worked for the future; he would live on, but he had already ceased to exist. I had evoked him in this poignant thought and he came not alone. He came with a train of all the vanquished in this stealthy, unseen contest for an immense stake in which I was one of the victors. They crowded upon me. I saw Fox, Polehampton, de Mersch himself, crowds of figures without a name, women with whom I had fancied myself in love, men I had shaken by the hand, Lea’s reproachful, ironical face. They were near; near enough to touch; nearer. I did not only see them, I absolutely felt them all. Their tumultuous and silent stir seemed to raise a tumult in my breast.
===Chinese===
他的劳作让我想到，我也有事情要做。当然有。人总有一件什么事要做。可不知怎么，我就是想不起来。这真叫人受不了，甚至叫人惊心——眼前这一片空白，从现在直到夜幕再临的许多钟头的一片空虚——直到忽然，我恍然想起：我得去大英博物馆（British Museum），为我们的《克伦威尔传》做些摘录。我们的《克伦威尔传》。克伦威尔已经没有了；他活过，为未来工作过——如今他已不复存在。他的未来——我们的过去，已经到了头。那条驳船，连同船上还在绷着胳膊摇橹的人，已经消失在桥拱底下，像穿过一道门进了另一个世界。一种古怪的孤独感悄悄袭上我，我背过身去不再看河，它同我的这一天一样空空荡荡。双轮马车、布鲁厄姆马车川流不息，车轮连绵低沉地滚着，蹄声笃笃。一辆大板车加进一记雷鸣和铁链的铿锵。我发觉自己竟莫名其妙地想不通，还有什么可能的目的留下来，使它们继续奔跑。造就它们的那个过去已经完结，它们的未来已被一个新的构想吞噬了。那么丘吉尔呢？他也曾为未来工作；他会继续活下去，可他已经不复存在了。我在这尖锐的思绪里把他召唤了出来，而他来时并非孤身。他携着一长列人马而来——这场为巨额赌注而进行的悄无声息、无形无影的角力中所有的战败者，而我，是胜利者中的一员。他们向我拥来。我看见福克斯、波尔汉普顿、德·默施本人、成群没有名字的身影、那些我自以为爱过的女人、那些我握过手的男人、利的那张责备而讥诮的脸。他们近；近得触得到；更近了。我不仅是看见他们，我实实在在感到了他们全体。他们那喧腾而无声的骚动，似乎在我胸中也掀起一场骚动。

===Original===
I sprang suddenly to my feet﻿—a sensation that I had had before, that was not new to me, a remembered fear, had me fast; a remembered voice seemed to speak clearly incomprehensible words that had moved me before. The sheer faces of the enormous buildings near at hand seemed to topple forwards like cliffs in an earthquake, and for an instant I saw beyond them into unknown depths that I had seen into before. It was as if the shadow of annihilation had passed over them beneath the sunshine. Then they returned to rest; motionless, but with a changed aspect.
===Chinese===
我猛地跳起身——一种从前有过的感觉，并不新鲜，一种记得起来的恐惧，紧紧攫住了我；一个记得起来的声音，仿佛清清楚楚地说着听不懂的、却曾打动过我的话。近处那些巍峨建筑的笔直墙面仿佛要向前倾倒，像地震中的悬崖，刹那间我看见了它们背后那片我从前见过的未知深处。仿佛毁灭的阴影在阳光下从它们身上掠过。随后它们复归于静止；不动了，却换了一副面目。

===Original===
“This is too absurd,” I said to myself. “I am not well.” I was certainly unfit for any sort of work. “But I must get through the day somehow.” Tomorrow﻿ ﻿… tomorrow.﻿ ﻿… I had a pale vision of her face as it had appeared to me at sunset on the first day I had met her.
===Chinese===
“这太荒唐了，”我对自己说。“我是病了。”我确实什么工作都担不起了。“可我总得想法子挨过这一天。”明天……明天。……我眼前淡淡浮起她那张脸——我遇见她的头一天，日落时分她在我眼里的模样。

===Original===
I went back to my club﻿—to lunch, of course. I had no appetite, but I was tormented by the idea of an interminable afternoon before me. I sat idly for a long time. Behind my back two men were talking.
===Chinese===
我回到我的俱乐部——自然是去吃午饭。我没有胃口，可一想到面前还横着一个没完没了的下午，我就备受煎熬。我无所事事地坐了许久。我背后有两个人在交谈。

===Original===
“Churchill﻿ ﻿… oh, no better than the rest. He only wants to be found out. If I’ve any nose for that sort of thing, there’s something in the air. It’s absurd to be told that he knew nothing about it.﻿ ﻿… You’ve seen the *Hour*?” I got up to go away, but suddenly found myself standing by their table.
===Chinese===
“丘吉尔……哦，不比其余的人强。他巴不得被人揭穿呢。这种事我要是还有几分鼻子，风里就有点什么味道。说他对此一无所知，未免荒唐。……你看过《时辰报》了吗？”我起身要走，却忽然发觉自己已站在他们桌旁。

===Original===
“You are unjust,” I said. They looked up at me together with an immense surprise. I didn’t know them and I passed on. But I heard one of them ask:
===Chinese===
“你们不公道，”我说。他们一齐抬头看我，满脸极大的错愕。我不认识他们，便走了过去。但我听见其中一个问：

===Original===
“Who’s that fellow?”﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
“那家伙是谁？”……

===Original===
“Oh﻿—Etchingham Granger.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
“哦——埃钦厄姆·格兰杰。……”

===Original===
“Is he queer?” the other postulated.
===Chinese===
“他有点古怪吧？”另一个设问道。

===Original===
I went slowly down the great staircase. A knot of men was huddled round the tape machine; others came, half trotting, half walking, to peer over heads, under armpits.
===Chinese===
我缓步走下大楼梯。一小群人围着纸带机（tape machine）挤作一团；又有几个半跑半走地过来，从头顶上、腋下探头张望。

===Original===
“What’s the matter with that thing?” I asked of one of them.
===Chinese===
“那玩意儿出什么事了？”我问其中一人。

===Original===
“Oh, Grogram’s up,” he said, and passed me. Someone from a point of vantage read out:
===Chinese===
“哦，格罗格拉姆（Grogram）起立发言了，”他说着从我身边挤了过去。一个占着好位置的人高声念道：

===Original===
“The Leader of the House (Sir C. Grogram, Devonport) said that.﻿ ﻿…” The words came haltingly to my ears as the man’s voice followed the jerks of the little instrument “… the Government obviously could not﻿ ﻿… alter its policy at﻿ ﻿… eleventh hour﻿ ﻿… at dictates of﻿ ﻿… quite irresponsible person in one of﻿ ﻿… the daily﻿ ﻿… papers.”
===Chinese===
“下院领袖（Leader of the House）（德文波特〔Devonport〕选区C·格罗格拉姆爵士）说，……”那些字句一停一顿地传进我耳朵，那人的声音追着小机器的抽动走：“……政府显然不能……在……最后关头更改……政策……而听凭……一家……日报……上一个全然不负责任的人……摆布。”

===Original===
I was wondering whether it was Soane or Callan who was poor old Grogram’s “quite irresponsible person,” when I caught the sound of Gurnard’s name. I turned irritably away. I didn’t want to hear that fool read out the words of that.﻿ ﻿… It was like the warning croak of a raven in an old ballad.
===Chinese===
我正在琢磨，可怜的老格罗格拉姆嘴里那个“全然不负责任的人”，到底是索恩还是卡伦，这时我听到了格纳德的名字。我烦躁地转过身去。我不想听那个蠢货把那篇东西念出来。……那声音像古老歌谣里乌鸦示警的哑鸣。

===Original===
I began desultorily to descend to the smoking-room. In the Cimmerian gloom of the stairway the voice of a pursuer hailed me.
===Chinese===
我信步朝吸烟室走下去。楼梯间那冥府般的幽暗里，一个追赶者的声音叫住了我。

===Original===
“I say, Granger! I say, Granger!”
===Chinese===
“我说，格兰杰！我说，格兰杰！”

===Original===
I looked back. The man was one of the rats of the lower journalism, large-boned, rubicund, asthmatic; a mass of flesh that might, to the advantage of his country and himself, have served as a cavalry trooper. He puffed stertorously down towards me.
===Chinese===
我回头一看。那人是下等新闻界的一只老鼠，骨架很大，面色红润，患着哮喘；一大团肉，这团肉本可以为他祖国的利益和他本人的利益计，去当一名骑兵的。他打着响亮的呼噜，朝我这边一路喘下来。

===Original===
“I say, I say,” his breath came rattling and wheezing. “What’s up at the *Hour*?”
===Chinese===
“我说，我说，”他气息里带着呼噜和喘鸣。“《时辰报》出什么事了？”

===Original===
“I’m sure I don’t know,” I answered curtly.
===Chinese===
“我确实不知道，”我简慢地答道。

===Original===
“They said you took it yesterday. You’ve been playing the very devil, haven’t you? But I suppose it was not off your own bat?”
===Chinese===
“他们说那是你们昨天干下的。你们这回可是掀起了轩然大波，是不是？不过我想，这总不是出自你自己的主意吧？”

===Original===
“Oh, I never play off my own bat,” I answered.
===Chinese===
“哦，我做事从来不自作主张，”我答道。

===Original===
“Of course I don’t want to intrude,” he said again. In the gloom I was beginning to discern the workings of the tortured apoplectic face. “But, I say, what’s de Mersch’s little game?”
===Chinese===
“当然啦，我并不想打探，”他又说。幽暗里，我开始分辨出那张中了风似的、痛苦扭动的脸上肌肉的运作。“可是，我说，德·默施玩的是什么小小把戏？”

===Original===
“You’d better ask him,” I answered. It was incredibly hateful, this satyr’s mask in the dim light.
===Chinese===
“你最好去问他，”我答道。微光里这张萨提尔（satyr）面具，可恨得难以言表。

===Original===
“He’s not in London,” it answered, with a wink of the creased eyelids, “but, I suppose, now, Fox and de Mersch haven’t had a row, now, have they?”
===Chinese===
“他不在伦敦，”那张面具答道，起了皱褶的眼皮眨了一眨，“不过我想，呃，福克斯和德·默施总没有闹翻吧，呃，没有吧？”

===Original===
I did not answer. The thing was wearily hateful, and this was only the beginning. Hundreds more would be asking the same question in a few minutes.
===Chinese===
我没有回答。这东西可恨得叫人厌倦，而这还只是开头。再过几分钟，还会有成百的人来问同一个问题。

===Original===
The head wagged on the mountainous shoulders.
===Chinese===
那颗头在山岳般的双肩上摇来晃去。

===Original===
“Looks fishy,” he said. I recognised that, to force words from me, he was threatening a kind of blackmail. Another voice began to call from the top of the stairs﻿—
===Chinese===
“看着有猫腻，”他说。我看出，他这是拿一种变相的讹诈作要挟，好逼我吐口。另一个声音又从楼梯顶上叫起来——

===Original===
“I say, Granger! I say, Granger.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
“我说，格兰杰！我说，格兰杰。……”

===Original===
I pushed the folding-doors apart and went slowly down the gloomy room. I heard the doors swing again, and footsteps patter on the matting behind me. I did not turn; the man came round me and looked at my face. It was Polehampton. There were tears in his eyes.
===Chinese===
我推开折叠门，顺着那间幽暗的房间慢慢往里走。我听见门又荡回来，脚步声在我身后的地席上啪嗒作响。我没有回头；那人绕到我前头，看了看我的脸。是波尔汉普顿。他眼里含着泪。

===Original===
“I say,” he said, “I say, what does it mean; *what* does it mean?” It was very difficult for me to look at him. “I tell you.﻿ ﻿…” he began again. He had the dictatorial air of a very small, quite hopeless man, a man mystified by a blow of unknown provenance. “I tell you.﻿ ﻿…” he began again.
===Chinese===
“我说，”他说，“我说，这是什么意思；这到底——是什么意思？”我很难正眼看他。“我告诉你……”他又开了腔。他带着一种非常渺小、全然绝望的人所特有的专横神气，一个被来历不明的打击砸得莫名其妙的人。“我告诉你……”他又开了腔。

===Original===
“But what has it to do with me?” I said roughly.
===Chinese===
“可这跟我有什么相干？”我粗声说。

===Original===
“Oh, but *you*﻿ ﻿… you advised me to buy.” He had become supplicatory. “Didn’t you, now?﻿ ﻿… Didn’t you.﻿ ﻿… You said, you remember﻿ ﻿… that.﻿ ﻿…” I didn’t answer the man. What had I got to say? He remained looking intently at me, as if it were of the greatest moment to him that I should make the acknowledgment and share the blame﻿—as if it would take an immense load from his shoulders. I couldn’t do it; I hated him.
===Chinese===
“哦，可是你……是你劝我买的呀。”他变得苦苦哀告起来。“是不是，嗯？……是不是。……你说过，你记得的……说……”我没有回答这个人。我还有什么可说的？他仍旧死死盯着我，仿佛只要我认下这句话、分担这份罪责，对他就成了天大的事——仿佛那能从他肩上卸下千斤重担。我做不到；我恨他。

===Original===
“Didn’t you,” he began categorically; “didn’t you advise me to buy those debentures of de Mersch’s?” I did not answer.
===Chinese===
“难道不是吗，”他斩钉截铁地开了腔；“难道不是你劝我买下那些德·默施的债券的？”我没有回答。

===Original===
“What does it all mean?” he said again. “If this bill doesn’t get through, I tell you I shall be ruined. And they say that Mr. Gurnard is going to smash it. They are all saying it, up there; and that you﻿—you on the *Hour*﻿ ﻿… are﻿ ﻿… are responsible.” He took out a handkerchief and began to blow his nose. I didn’t say a single word.
===Chinese===
“这到底是什么意思？”他又说。“要是这项法案通不过，我告诉你，我就完了。他们说格纳德先生要把它毁掉。他们上头人人都这么讲；还说你——《时辰报》上的你……要……要负责任。”他掏出一块手帕擤起鼻子来。我一个字也没有说。

===Original===
“But what’s to be done?” he started again; “what’s to be *done*.﻿ ﻿… I tell you.﻿ ﻿… My daughter, you know, she’s very brave, she said to me this morning she could work; but she couldn’t, you know; she’s not been brought up to that sort of thing﻿ ﻿… not even typewriting﻿ ﻿… and so﻿ ﻿… we’re all ruined﻿ ﻿… everyone of us. And I’ve more than fifty hands, counting Mr. Lea, and they’ll all have to go. It’s horrible.﻿ ﻿… I trusted you, Granger, you know; I trusted you, and they say up there that you.﻿ ﻿…” I turned away from him. I couldn’t bear to see the bewildered fear in his eyes. “So many of us,” he began again, “everyone I know.﻿ ﻿… I told them to buy and﻿ ﻿… But you might have let us know, Granger, you might have. Think of my poor daughter.”
===Chinese===
“可是怎么办呢？”他又开了头；“到底怎么办……我告诉你……你知道，我女儿，她很勇敢，今天早上她对我说她可以去工作；可她不行的，你知道；她从小没受过那种教养……连打字都不会……所以……我们全完了……我们每一个人。再说连利先生算上，我手底下还有五十多个人手，到时候全都得散。太可怕了……我信过你，格兰杰，你知道；我信过你，可他们上头的人都说你……”我背过身去，不看他了。我受不了看他眼里那种茫然无措的恐惧。“我们这么多人，”他又开了腔，“我认识的每一个人……是我叫他们买的，可是……可你总该给我们透个信儿啊，格兰杰，你总该啊。想想我那可怜的女儿。”

===Original===
I wanted to say something to the man, wanted to horribly; but there wasn’t anything to say﻿—not a word. I was sorry. I took up a paper that sprawled on one of the purple ottomans. I stood with my back to this haggard man and pretended to read.
===Chinese===
我想对这个人说点什么，想说想得要命；可是无话可说——一个字也没有。我很抱歉。我拿起一份摊在紫色软榻上的报纸，背对着这个形容枯槁的人站着，假装读报。

===Original===
I noticed incredulously that I was swaying on my legs. I looked round me. Two old men were asleep in armchairs under the gloomy windows. One had his head thrown back, the other was crumpled forward into himself; his frail, white hand just touched the floor. A little further off two young men were talking; they had the air of conspirators over their empty coffee cups.
===Chinese===
我难以置信地注意到，我的双腿正在打晃。我环顾四周。两个老人在幽暗的窗子底下睡着了。一个头向后仰着，另一个向前蜷缩成一团；他那只纤弱的白手刚好碰到地板。稍远处，两个年轻人正在交谈；对着空咖啡杯，一副密谋家的神气。

===Original===
I was conscious that Polehampton had left me, that he had gone from behind me; but I don’t think I was conscious of the passage of time. God knows how long I stood there. Now and then I saw Polehampton’s face before my eyes, with the panic-stricken eyes, the ruffled hair, the lines of tears seaming the cheeks, seeming to look out at me from the crumple of the paper that I held. I knew too, that there were faces like that everywhere; everywhere, faces of panic-stricken little people of no more account than the dead in graveyards, just the material to make graveyards, nothing more; little people of absolutely no use but just to suffer horribly from this blow coming upon them from nowhere. It had never occurred to me at the time that their inheritance had passed to me﻿ ﻿… to us. And yet, I began to wonder stupidly, what was the difference between me today and me yesterday. There wasn’t any, not any at all. Only today I had nothing more to do.
===Chinese===
我觉察到波尔汉普顿已经离开了我，已经从我身后走掉了；可我想我并没有觉察时间的流逝。天知道我在那里站了多久。波尔汉普顿的脸不时浮现在我眼前——惊惶的眼睛，蓬乱的头发，一道道泪痕纵横脸颊——仿佛从我手里那份皱报纸的皱褶里向外望着我。我也知道，这样的脸到处都是；到处都是，一张张惊惶失措的小人物的脸，同墓园里的死者一样无足轻重，不过是造墓园的材料罢了，仅此而已；绝对派不上别的用场的小人物，只会被这不知从何处落到头上的打击可怕地折磨。当时我从未想到，他们的遗产已经传到了我手里……传到了我们手里。然而，我开始愚钝地纳闷：今天的我和昨天的我，究竟有什么分别。没有分别，丝毫没有。只是今天，我再也没有什么事可做了。

===Original===
The doors at the end of the room flew open, as if burst by a great outcry penetrating from without, and a man appeared running up the room﻿—one of those men who bear news eternally, who catch the distant clamour and carry it into quiet streets. Why did he disturb me? Did I want to hear his news? I wanted to think of Churchill; to think of how to explain.﻿ ﻿… The man was running up the room.
===Chinese===
房间尽头的门猛地开了，像被外面透进来的一声巨呼撞开似的，一个人顺着房间跑进来——那种永远传递新闻的人，他们捕捉远处的喧嚣，把它带进安静的街道。他为什么打扰我？我想听他的新闻吗？我想的是丘吉尔；想的是怎么解释……那人顺着房间跑过来。

===Original===
“I say﻿ ﻿… I say, you beggars.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
“我说……我说，你们这帮家伙……”

===Original===
I was beginning to wonder how it was that I felt such an absolute conviction of being alone, and it was then, I believe, that in this solitude that had descended upon my soul I seemed to see the shape of an approaching Nemesis. It is permitted to no man to break with his past, with the past of his kind, and to throw away the treasure of his future. I began to suspect I had gained nothing; I began to understand that even such a catastrophe was possible. I sat down in the nearest chair. Then my fear passed away. The room was filling; it hummed with excited voices. “Churchill! No better than the others,” I heard somebody saying. Two men had stopped talking. They were middle-aged, a little gray, and ruddy. The face of one was angry, and of the other sad. “He wanted only to be found out. What a fall in the mud.” “No matter,” said the other, “one is made a little sad. He stood for everything I had been pinning my faith to.” They passed on. A brazen voice bellowed in the distance. “The greatest fall of any minister that ever was.” A tall, heavy journalist in a white waistcoat was the centre of a group that turned slowly upon itself, gathering bulk. “Done for﻿—stood up to the last. I saw him get into his brougham. The police had a job.﻿ ﻿… There’s quite a riot down there.﻿ ﻿… Pale as a ghost. Gurnard? Gurnard magnificent. Very cool and in his best form. Threw them over without as much as a wink. Outraged conscience speech. Magnificent. Why it’s the chance of his life.”﻿ ﻿… And then for a time the voices and the faces seemed to pass away and die out. I had dropped my paper, and as I stooped to pick it up the voices returned.
===Chinese===
我开始纳闷，为什么我竟如此确信自己是独自一人；而正是在那时，我相信，在这份降临到我灵魂上的孤独之中，我仿佛看见了一个渐渐逼近的报应之神涅墨西斯（Nemesis）的轮廓。没有人被允许同自己的过去、同自己族类的过去决裂，把未来的珍宝随手抛开。我开始疑心自己一无所得；我开始明白，连这样一场大灾祸都是可能的。我在最近的一把椅子上坐下。随后我的恐惧退去了。房间里渐渐坐满了人；兴奋的人声嗡嗡作响。“丘吉尔！不比别的家伙强，”我听见有人说。两个人停下了交谈。他们人到中年，鬓发微灰，脸色红润。一个的脸是愤怒的，另一个的脸是悲哀的。“他一心只想被人揭穿。摔得一身泥啊。”“不要紧，”另一个说，“人只是有点儿难过。他代表着我一直寄托信念的一切。”他们走过去了。远处一个大嗓门声若洪钟地吼着。“有史以来大臣中最惨的一跤。”一个穿白背心的高大笨重的记者成了一群人的中心，那群人围着自身慢慢旋转，越滚越大。“完了——撑到了最后一刻。我亲眼看见他上了他的布鲁厄姆马车。警察可是费了番劲。……底下简直成了一场骚乱。……白得像鬼。格纳德？格纳德威风极了。十分冷静，又正处在巅峰状态。把他们甩了，连眼皮都没眨一下。一篇义愤填膺的良心演说。漂亮。嘿，这可是他一生难得的机会。”……随后有一阵子，那些声音和面孔仿佛退去、消逝了。我的报纸掉在了地上，当我俯身去拾时，那些声音又回来了。

===Original===
—“Granger﻿ ﻿… Etchingham Granger.﻿ ﻿… Sister is going to marry Gurnard.”
===Chinese===
——“格兰杰……埃钦厄姆·格兰杰。……他妹妹要嫁给格纳德了。”

===Original===
I got on to my hands and knees to pick up the paper, of course. What I did not understand was where the water came from. Otherwise it was pretty clear. Somebody seemed to be in a fit. No, he wasn’t drunk; look at his teeth. What did they want to look at his teeth for; was he a horse?
===Chinese===
我自然是以双手双膝着地，去拾那份报纸的。我不明白的只是，那水是从哪儿来的。除此之外都相当清楚。像是有人发了病。不，他不是喝醉了；瞧他的牙齿。他们要瞧他的牙齿做什么？他是匹马吗？

---

===Original===
It must have been I that was in the fit. There were a lot of men round me, the front row on their knees﻿—holding me, some of them. A man in a red coat and plush breeches﻿—a waiter﻿—was holding a glass of water; another had a small bottle. They were talking about me under their breaths. At one end of the horseshoe someone said:
===Chinese===
发病的一定就是我。我周围围了许多人，前排的人跪着——有几个在按着我。一个穿红号衣、丝绒及膝裤的男人——是个侍者——端着一杯水；另一个拿着一只小瓶子。他们压着嗓子谈论我。马蹄形人群的一端，有人说：

===Original===
“He’s the man who.﻿ ﻿…” Then he caught my eye. He lowered his voice, and the abominable whisper ran round among the heads. It was easy to guess: “the man who was got at.” I was to be that for the rest of my life. I was to be famous at last. There came the desire to be out of it.
===Chinese===
“他就是那个……”说到这里他撞上了我的目光。他放低了声音，那声可憎的耳语便在一颗颗头颅中间传开。不难猜到：“那个被收买的人。”我后半辈子就要做这个了。我终于要出名了。接着涌起一阵想要脱身的渴望。

===Original===
I struggled to my feet.
===Chinese===
我挣扎着站了起来。

===Original===
Someone said: “Feel better now?” I answered: “I﻿—oh, I’ve got to go and see.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
有人说：“现在好些了吗？”我答道：“我——哦，我得去看看……”

===Original===
It was rather difficult to speak distinctly; my tongue got in the way. But I strove to impress the fool with the idea that I had affairs that must be attended to﻿—that I had private affairs.
===Chinese===
要口齿清楚地说话相当困难；我的舌头碍事。可我竭力想让那蠢货记住一个意思：我有非料理不可的事务——我有私事。

===Original===
“You aren’t fit. Let me.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
“你这副样子可不行。让我……”

===Original===
I pushed him roughly aside﻿—what business was it of his? I slunk hastily out of the room. The others remained. I knew what they were going to do﻿—to talk things over, to gabble about “the man who.﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
我把他粗鲁地推开——关他什么事？我仓皇地溜出房间。其余的人留了下来。我知道他们要干什么——把事情议论一番，叽里呱啦地谈论“那个……的人”。

===Original===
It was treacherous walking, that tessellated pavement in the hall. Someone said: “Hullo, Granger,” as I passed. I took no notice.
===Chinese===
门厅里那方格镶嵌的石板地，走起来步步艰险。我经过时，有人说：“喂，格兰杰。”我没有理会。

===Original===
Where did I wish to go to? There was no one who could minister to me; the whole world had resolved itself into a vast solitary city of closed doors. I had no friend﻿—no one. But I must go somewhere, must hide somewhere, must speak to someone. I mumbled the address of Fox to a cabman. Some idea of expiation must have been in my mind; some idea of seeing the thing through, mingled with that necessity for talking to someone﻿—anyone.
===Chinese===
我想到哪里去呢？没有一个人能抚慰我；整个世界化成了一座辽阔空寂的城，家家闭户，门门上锁。我没有朋友——一个也没有。可我总得去个地方，总得在什么地方藏身，总得找个人说说话。我朝马车夫咕哝了福克斯的地址。我脑子里想必存着某种赎罪的念头；某种把这事情看到底的念头，掺杂着那种非找个人说话不可的需要——随便什么人都行。

===Original===
I was afraid too; not of Fox’s rage; not even of anything that he could do﻿—but of the sight of his despair. He had become a tragic figure.
===Chinese===
我也害怕；不是怕福克斯发怒；甚至不是怕他能做出什么来——而是怕看见他的绝望。他已经变成一个悲剧人物了。

===Original===
I reached his flat and I had said: “It is I,” and again, “It is I,” and he had not stirred. He was lying on the sofa under a rug, motionless as a corpse. I had paced up and down the room. I remember that the pile of the carpet was so long that it was impossible to walk upon it easily. Everything else in the room was conceived in an exuberance of luxury that now had something of the macabre in it. It was that now﻿—before, it had been unclean. There was a great bed whose lines suggested sinking softness, a glaring yellow satin coverlet, vast, like a sea. The walls were covered with yellow satin, the windows draped with lace worth a king’s ransom, the light was softened, the air dead, the sounds hung slumbrously. And, in the centre of it, that motionless body. It stirred, pivoted on some central axis beneath the rug, and faced me sitting. There was no look of inquiry in the bloodshot eyes﻿—they turned dully upon me, topaz-coloured in a bloodred setting. There was no expression in the suffused face.
===Chinese===
我到了他的寓所；我那时说过一声“是我”，又说过一声“是我”，他没有动过。他躺在沙发上一条毯子底下，一动不动，像一具尸体。我曾在房间里踱来踱去。我记得那地毯的绒那么长，走在上面想从容都不可能。房间里其余的一切，都构想于一种恣肆的奢华，如今那奢华里已经透出几分阴森可怖。此刻它是阴森了——从前，它是龌龊。有一张大床，线条透着下陷般的柔软；一幅刺目的黄缎床罩，铺展无边，像一片海。四壁蒙着黄缎，窗上垂着抵得上国王赎身的蕾丝帘，光线是柔化的，空气是死寂的，种种声音都悬着睡意。而在这正中央，是那具不动的身体。它动了动，绕着毯子底下某根中轴一转，坐着面向我。充血的眼睛里没有探问的神色——它们呆呆地转向我，在血红的眶底里泛着黄玉色。那张浮肿的脸上没有任何表情。

===Original===
“You want?” he said, in a voice that was august by dint of hopelessness.
===Chinese===
“你要什么？”他说，声音因其绝望而显得庄严。

===Original===
“I want to explain,” I said. I had no idea that this was what I had come for.
===Chinese===
“我想解释一下，”我说。我原先并不知道，我为的正是这个。

===Original===
He answered only: “You!” He had the air of one speaking to something infinitely unimportant. It was as if I had no inkling of the real issue.
===Chinese===
他只答了一声：“你！”那副神气，像是在对一个无比无足轻重的东西说话。仿佛我对于真正的症结一无所知。

===Original===
With a bravery of desperation I began to explain that I hadn’t stumbled into the thing; that I had acted open-eyed; for my own ends﻿ ﻿… “My own ends.” I repeated it several times. I wanted him to understand, and I did explain. I kept nothing from him; neither her coming, nor her words, nor my feelings. I had gone in with my eyes open.
===Chinese===
带着绝望激出的一股勇气，我开始解释：说我并不是失足跌进这件事里来的；说我行事是睁着眼睛的；为了我自己的目的……“我自己的目的。”我把这话重复了好几遍。我要他明白，而我也确实解释了。我什么都没有瞒他；她的到来、她的话、我的感情，一概没有。我是睁着眼睛走进去的。

===Original===
For the first time Fox looked at me as if I were a sentient being. “Oh, you know that much,” he said listlessly.
===Chinese===
福克斯第一次看着我，仿佛我是个有知觉的生物。“哦，这个你是知道的，”他有气无力地说。

===Original===
“It’s no disgrace to have gone under to her,” I said; “we *had* to.” His despair seemed to link him into one “we” with myself. I wanted to put heart into him. I don’t know why.
===Chinese===
“败在她手里并不丢人，”我说；“我们是不得不败。”他的绝望似乎把他也拴进了我口中的那个“我们”。我想给他打气。我不知道为什么。

===Original===
He didn’t look at me again.
===Chinese===
他没有再看我。

===Original===
“Oh, *that*,” he said dully, “I﻿—I understand who you mean.﻿ ﻿… If I had known before I might have done something. But she came of a higher plane.” He seemed to be talking to himself. The half-forgotten horror grew large; I remembered that she had said that Fox, like herself, was one of a race apart, that was to supersede us﻿—Dimensionists. And, when I looked at him now, it was plain to me that he *was* of a race different to my own, just as he had always seemed different from any other man. He had had a different tone in triumph; he was different now, in his despair. He went on: “I might have managed Gurnard alone, but I never thought of her coming. You see one does one’s best, but, somehow, here one grows rather blind. I ought to have stuck to Gurnard, of course; never to have broken with him. We ought all to have kept together.﻿—But I kept my end up as long as he was alone.”
===Chinese===
“哦，那个啊，”他木然地说，“我——我明白你指的是谁。……我要是早知道，也许还能有点办法。可她出身的层面更高。”他像是在自言自语。那半已淡忘的恐怖涨大起来；我想起她说过，福克斯和她一样，也属于一个行将取代我们的异种——第四维人。而此刻我望着他，便看得分明：他确实属于与我不同的族类，正如他一向显得不同于任何旁人。他得胜时是另一种声口；如今绝望了，又是另一种。他继续说：“格纳德一个人，我原本对付得了，可我没想到她会来。你知道，人总是竭尽所能，可是不知怎么，到了这一步，人就变得相当盲目了。我当然本该始终咬住格纳德；根本不该同他闹翻。我们本该全都抱成一团。——不过，只要他还是孤家寡人，我倒还守得住阵脚。”

===Original===
He went on talking in an expressionless monotone, perhaps to himself, perhaps to me. I listened as one listens to unmeaning sounds﻿—to that of a distant train at night. He was looking at the floor, his mouth moving mechanically. He sat perfectly square, one hand on either knee, his back bowed out, his head drooping forward. It was as if there were no more muscular force in the whole man﻿—as if he were one of those ancient things one sees sunning themselves on benches by the walls of workhouses.
===Chinese===
他继续说着，一种毫无表情的单调，也许是对自己，也许是对我。我听着，像人听无意义的声音——像夜里听远处的火车。他望着地板，嘴机械地动着。他端端正正地坐着，一手按一边膝盖，背向外弓，头向前垂。仿佛他浑身上下再没有一丝气力——仿佛他已是那种老朽的东西，就是人们在济贫院墙边的长凳上见过的、晒着太阳的那种。

===Original===
“But,” I said angrily, “it’s not all over, you can make a fight for it still.”
===Chinese===
“可是，”我气恼地说，“还没有全完，你还可以搏一搏。”

===Original===
“You don’t seem to understand,” he answered, “it *is* all over﻿—the whole thing. I ran Churchill and his conscious rectitude gang for all they were worth.﻿ ﻿… Well, I liked them, I was a fool to give way to pity.﻿—But I did.﻿—One grows weak among people like you. Of course I knew that their day was over.﻿ ﻿… And it’s all *over*,” he said again after a long pause.
===Chinese===
“你好像不明白，”他答道，“就是全完了——整个儿完了。我驱着丘吉尔和他那伙自觉正直的人马，把他们的一切价值都用足了。……唉，我是喜欢他们的，我心软让了步，真是个傻瓜。——可我让了。——同你们这样的人处久了，人就变软弱。我当然知道他们的日子早就完了。……是全完了。”隔了半晌，他又说了一遍。

===Original===
“And what will you *do*?” I asked, half hysterically.
===Chinese===
“那你到底打算做什么呢？”我问，近乎歇斯底里。

===Original===
“I don’t just know,” he answered; “we’ve none of us gone under before. There haven’t been enough really to clash until she came.”
===Chinese===
“我还真不知道，”他答道；“我们这些人从前谁也没有垮过。在她来之前，我们的人数实在还多不到真正碰撞起来的地步。”

===Original===
The dead tranquillity of his manner was overwhelming; there was nothing to be said. I was in the presence of a man who was not as I was, whose standard of values, absolute to himself, was not to be measured by any of mine.
===Chinese===
他举止中那种死寂的平静势不可当；再没有什么可说的了。我面对的是一个与我不同的人，他的价值标准，只对他自己是绝对的，根本无法用我的任何标准去衡量。

===Original===
“I suppose I shall cut my throat,” he began again.
===Chinese===
“我想我会割了自己的喉咙，”他又开口道。

===Original===
I noticed with impersonal astonishment that the length of my right side was covered with the dust of a floor. In my restless motions I came opposite the fireplace. Above it hung a number of tiny, jewelled frames, containing daubs of an astonishing lewdness. The riddle grew painful. What kind of a being could conceive this impossibly barbaric room, could enshrine those impossibly crude designs, and then fold his hands? I turned fiercely upon him. “But you are rich enough to enjoy life,” I said.
===Chinese===
我怀着一种置身事外的惊讶注意到，我右半边身子的整个长度上，都沾着一种地板上的灰。在我烦躁的踱步中，我走到了壁炉对面。炉台上方挂着好几只小小的、镶着珠宝的相框，里面装着一些惊人淫秽的涂抹画。这谜变得叫人痛苦了。怎样一种生灵，才构想得出这间荒唐绝伦的蛮野房间，供奉得起这些粗鄙绝伦的图样，然后竟拱起双手、袖手不管？我凶猛地转向他。“可你有的是钱，尽可以享受人生，”我说。

===Original===
“What’s that?” he asked wearily.
===Chinese===
“你说什么？”他倦怠地问。

===Original===
“In the name of God,” I shouted, “what do you work for﻿—what have you been plotting and plotting for, if not to enjoy your life at the last?” He made a small indefinite motion of ignorance, as if I had propounded to him a problem that he could not solve, that he did not think worth the solving.
===Chinese===
“看在上帝份上，”我吼道，“你工作到底为了什么——你算计了又算计，到底为了什么，若不是为了到头来享受人生？”他做了一个细小而含混的、表示不知的手势，仿佛我向他提出了一道他解不开、也不认为值得去解的难题。

===Original===
It came to me as the confirmation of a suspicion﻿—that motion. They had no joy, these people who were to supersede us; their clear-sightedness did nothing more for them than just that enabling them to spread desolation among us and take our places. It had been in her manner all along, she was like Fate; like the abominable Fate that desolates the whole length of our lives; that leaves of our hopes, of our plans, nothing but a hideous jumble of fragments like those of statues, smashed by hammers; the senseless, inscrutable, joyless Fate that we hate, and that debases us forever and ever. She had been all that to me﻿ ﻿… and to how many more?
===Chinese===
那个动作，向我证实了一个猜想。这些将要取代我们的人，没有欢乐；他们的明察秋毫，于他们自己的用处，也不过仅止于此——让他们得以在我们中间散布荒芜，取我们的位置而代之。这一点从来就在她的举止里；她像命运；像那可憎的命运，把我们整整一生从头到尾都变成荒漠；把我们的希望、我们的计划，只留下一堆丑陋的碎片，像被铁锤砸碎的雕像的残块；那没有知觉、不可索解、没有欢乐的命运，我们恨它，它却永远永远地贬损我们。她之于我，就是这一切……而她之于多少人，还将是这一切？

===Original===
“I used to be a decent personality,” I vociferated at him. “Do you hear﻿—decent. I could look a man in the face. And you cannot even enjoy. What do you come for? What do you live for? What is at the end of it all?”
===Chinese===
“我从前是个体面人，”我冲他大声嚷道。“你听见没有——体面。我能正眼看一个人的脸。而你们，连享受都不会。你们来干什么？你们为什么活着？这一切到头来到底是什么？”

===Original===
“Ah, if I knew﻿ ﻿…” he answered, negligently.
===Chinese===
“啊，我要是知道……”他漫不经心地答道。

## XIX / 第十九章

===Original===
